# Public-semver archive

Git-tag releases off the `v0.0.x` train. Most recent first. See [ADR-0008](../../docs/adr/0008-versioning-and-release-tags.md) (versioning) and [ADR-0009](../../docs/adr/0009-tag-organized-roadmap.md) (roadmap shape).

## v0.2.0 → v0.2.29 — Brain M1 through the Launch Cycle (Shipped 2026-07-16 → 2026-09-03)

Moved verbatim from `ROADMAP.md ## Next` on 2026-09-04. 133 dated entries covering the
`v0.2.x` train from Brain M1's thin wedge slice through the launch cycle's public surfaces
and `brain_facts.published_by`. `## Next` had accumulated seven weeks of shipped work in a
section [ADR-0009](../../docs/adr/0009-tag-organized-roadmap.md) defines as the current
in-flight tag; this is that section's contents, unedited.


**Shipped 2026-09-03 — the launch cycle's surfaces, and the sentence they print made true** ([#5606](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5606), [#5607](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5607), [#5608](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5608), [#5609](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5609), [#5635](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5635); PRs [#5628](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5628), [#5629](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5629), [#5630](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5630), [#5631](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5631), [#5632](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5632), [#5634](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5634), [#5636](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5636); released as `v0.2.29`) — the landing fold, the README's first screen, the MCP-first docs, five competitor comparison pages, the generated Open Graph card, `@useatlas/mcp@0.0.4`, and `brain_facts.published_by` with a scoped approve verb and the approver served in `searchAtlas`.

⭐ **A claim rendered on six public surfaces was two-thirds backed, and the surfaces are why nobody noticed.** The sentence — *every one carries its source, its date, and the name of the person who approved it* — was on the README, the landing H1, the docs, both `llms.txt` surfaces and the OG card. Source and date were real columns. The approver was not: `provenance.attribution` names who **said** a thing, and nothing recorded who **stood behind** it. Measured against `us` prod before the migration: no result in the anonymous demo's payload carried an approver on any of six results, and `history.changedBy` was NULL on all of them. The copy was verified by `check-launch-sentence.sh`, which proves the six surfaces agree with `launch-cycle.md` — it cannot prove any of them is true. **Generalizable: a claim rendered identically on every surface is evidence the surfaces were copied from one another, not that the claim is backed — a drift gate over public copy checks agreement, and agreement is the property a false claim has most reliably.**

⭐ **The latent bug was found by adding a second column to a partitioned write, not by exercising it.** `WIDEN_AND_PROMOTE_FACTS_SQL` sets `status = 'published'` and had never stamped `published_at`; only `PROMOTE_FACTS_SQL` did. The two statements partition one publish — a draft whose grant widens takes one arm, everything else the other — so a widened fact has been publishable-without-a-date since #5591. It had not bitten: `us` prod read 40 published, 27 stamped, and all 13 unstamped predate the column, so the arm has never fired on a serving region. Both arms now write both columns and `brain-facts-approver.test.ts` asserts that as one property. **Generalizable: when two statements partition one operation, the partition is the review unit, not the statement — every column added to one arm is a defect in the other until someone checks, and a latent arm produces no evidence of its own absence.**

⚠️ **Forward-only means the existing rows are finished, not pending — and that is an operational fact, not only a schema one.** `published_by` is never backfilled ([0216](../../packages/api/src/lib/db/migrations/0216_brain_facts_published_by.sql) carries the argument: a partial backfill makes "approver unknown" mean two different things on rows that look identical, and a rogue write manufactures evidence of a review that never happened *with a named person on it*). The consequence surfaced immediately at the prod demo re-seed: **re-running the seed could not put a name on the 27 existing demo facts.** `seedDemoCorpusApprove` promotes only `status = 'draft'` and the demo had zero drafts; `seedDemoCorpusIngest` dedupes on source id and would have inserted nothing. The seed would have reported success and changed nothing. Getting the approver onto the demo required deleting the facts so the gate could create them again. **Generalizable: a forward-only column leaves existing rows permanently NULL, so a surface that must display it needs its rows re-created, not its job re-run — and an idempotent seed is precisely the shape that will report success while doing so.**

**The re-extraction was not a risk that materialized.** Deleting 27 facts / 13 episodes / 28 edges and re-running ingest → extract → approve against the live model returned `missingExpectedClaims: []` — every curated corpus claim reproduced — 28 facts promoted, 0 refused, and the Finance/Support contradiction's `in-tension-with` edge **minted at write time** rather than needing an admin's tension sweep, which the previous seed had required. Backup taken first (7 seed-owned `brain_*` tables); `demo_anonymous_sessions` deliberately excluded, since it is the launch-cycle gate's counter and not seed data.

⚠️ **A static asset can ship and still be wrong at its canonical URL for hours.** The regenerated OG card deployed to `www` at `c8ddc48c2` and the origin served it immediately, while `https://www.useatlas.dev/og.png` returned the old hand-made card with `cf-cache-status: HIT` and `cache-control: max-age=14400`. Social scrapers read the canonical URL, so a share posted in that window renders the superseded headline. **Generalizable: for an asset whose only consumers are other people's crawlers, "deployed" and "served" are different facts — verify with a cache-buster and purge the edge before the asset's audience arrives, because the cached copy is what the launch actually shows.**

**Shipped 2026-09-02 — #5338 AC 8: the triaged-out count reaches the coverage surface, and the recorded-measurement store that AC 4's gate was missing** ([#5338](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5338) stays open — AC 6 is blocked on #5337, and the scoring set is now the only thing between here and a number) — `BrainCoverage.triage` on the wire, composed in `lib/brain/coverage.ts`, rendered as the last half of the composed statement, plus `lib/brain/triage-measurements.ts`. `coverage.ts` had **zero** triage awareness before this, so ADR-0041's statement of what Atlas can see silently excluded every episode stage-0 routed out before any model call.

**The arm is REQUIRED, and that is the criterion's word "unconditionally" made structural.** An optional field would let a deploy that never wired the read up render identically to one filtering half its intake — opposite statements, one shape — so `BrainCoverageSchema` refuses a response without it. It is also its **own** arm rather than a per-class number, and the argument is the mechanism's rather than the page's: the availability ratios are counted in channels, mailboxes and enrolled pairs so a triaged episode moves none of them, and stage 0's rules are body-shape rules that know nothing about source class. **Generalizable: when a new count does not move any existing denominator, that is the signal it needs its own arm — folding it into one it cannot move produces a number that is unchanged for the wrong reason.**

⭐ **AC 4's gate had a hole nobody could have seen from the gate.** `checkTriageDefaultGate` reads a `records` array, and its only caller was a test passing `[]` — so the half of the criterion about *a recorded measurement below threshold* was unreachable: a landed failing run could not have arrived at the gate at all, and the check would have stayed green through exactly the event it exists for. Records now live in a committed module (a code change, reviewed — the same argument the gate itself makes for living in a test rather than a boot path), and both the gate's test and the page read the one store. **Generalizable: a pure function over injected state is only as real as its production caller — a gate whose only caller is a test with a literal argument is a gate over nothing, and it looks identical to a working one.**

⚠️ **The count is a live gauge, not a tally, and the wire says so.** #5534's re-queue clears both triage columns, so a re-queued episode leaves the count and the table retains no record it was ever there. That is the correct present-tense statement AND the flattering direction, so it is spelled out on the type and the sentence is present tense throughout ("is not reading", never "has dropped"). The recall half ships **unmeasured** and names the specific harm rather than hedging: a triage false negative is invisible by construction, because nobody notices a fact that was never proposed, so an admin reading a bare count would reasonably assume the gap surfaces elsewhere on the page. It does not.

### ⭐ Recorded as NOT achievable: prod cannot produce the scoring set, and it is not a matter of time

The acceptance criteria say prod is the smoke test and the number is set on a synthetic set. The alternative that kept being restated — *"or prod accumulates ~110 reviewed positives"* — is **dead**, and the reason is an order of magnitude sharper than the "roughly a year at 9 positives/month" estimate it replaces. Measured against the `us` region's internal DB on 2026-09-02 (`VgazIn9VLcR1wkqouZxZ759cBEuvg0Nq`, the only serving region):

| | |
|---|---|
| Episodes, **lifetime** | **36** (21 slack, 9 warehouse, 6 human) |
| …of which **triage-eligible** | **27** — `notAWarehouseEpisodeSql` drops warehouse episodes from scope |
| Extracted | 36 — nothing pending |
| Earliest episode | 2026-08-03 |
| Facts | 13 published, 24 draft |

The recall denominator counts **episodes**, so 27 is not the current total — it is the **ceiling**, and it is 4× below the Wilson floor of 110. Reviewing every one of the 24 outstanding drafts does not move it, because those drafts come from the same episodes.

⚠️ **The eligible figure is 27, not 36, and the difference was nearly reported the flattering way.** The manifest's window holds 33 episodes (19 slack, 8 warehouse, 6 human) and its `entries` number 25 with `excluded: 0` — an eight-row gap that looks like a cutter bug and is not: `heldoutClassifySql`'s `scoped` CTE applies `notAWarehouseEpisodeSql` **before** anything is counted, so warehouse episodes never enter the population that `excluded` describes. The manifest is correct and its counts are internally consistent. What was wrong was the headline: quoting the lifetime episode count as the ceiling overstates the reachable denominator by a third, in the direction that makes the prod path look more survivable than it is. **Generalizable: when a population is filtered before it is counted, the count's denominator is not the table's — check what the query scoped out before quoting a total as a limit.** The bottleneck was assumed to be review throughput (the issue records the denominator as *"partly a measure of review throughput"*); it is **ingest volume**, and no amount of reviewing reaches the number. **Generalizable: before pricing a data-collection path in calendar time, check whether the population has a ceiling — "9 per month" and "36 ever" recommend completely different plans, and the first reads as patience while the second reads as a redesign.**

The consequence is that the evaluation set must be **labelled text that is not prod's**, and the corpus lane is less blocked than it appears: `.claude/research/extractor-corpus-acquisition.md`'s two decision cards are about **training and shipped weights**, while an evaluation set produces no weights — so it needs only that document's *"store in our infra"* column, not its *"commercial derived weights"* column, and ADR-0044's prohibition (fact content into model weights) is not engaged at all. The data-protection axis still is. What remains genuinely unsolved is the **labels**: public text carries no Atlas review decision, so each episode's class has to be produced by running the real extraction path over it and a human applying the review gate — roughly 300 labelled episodes at the observed ~36% positive rate to clear 110. That is finite and it is hours, versus a prod path that does not converge.

**Shipped 2026-09-02 — #5338's measurement harness, and the acceptance criteria's set-size arithmetic is ~20% low** ([#5338](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5338) stays open — the scoring set does not exist yet) — `lib/brain/triage-measure.ts` (the arithmetic), `lib/brain/triage-measure-record.ts` (what a measurement is *allowed* to claim) and `scripts/measure-triage.ts` (the DB-free harness), 34 assertions. Closes AC 3's mechanism, both halves of AC 4, AC 5, the synthetic half of AC 7, AC 9 and AC 10.

⭐ **The threshold's own set-size figures do not survive being computed.** The ACs say *"by the rule of three, zero observed misses clears a 95% lower bound only at n ≥ 60, and tolerating one miss needs n ≥ ~100"* — but the rule of three approximates an **exact** (Clopper-Pearson-style) bound while the criterion is written against a **Wilson** bound, which is stricter here. At a perfect score Wilson reduces to `n/(n + z²)`, so the floor is `z²·0.95/0.05 ≈ **73**`, and one miss needs **110**, not ~100. At the issue's own numbers the bound comes back **0.9398** (n=60, 0 misses) and **0.9455** (n=100, 1 miss) — both failing the 0.95 they were chosen to clear. The tests DERIVE the minima rather than asserting literals, so whoever cuts a set reads the number the gate enforces rather than the number the issue estimated. **Generalizable: when a criterion names a specific statistic, the sample size it quotes has to be computed FROM that statistic — a figure borrowed from a neighbouring approximation reads as rigour and is off by a fifth.**

⭐ **Measuring the stage-0 baseline is worth doing BEFORE stage 1 exists, and the reason is structural rather than convenient.** The yield half of the threshold is *relative* — the composed layer must drop strictly more than stage 0 alone at no worse recall — so stage 0's number is a prerequisite of the comparison, not a by-product of it. Measured now, stage 1 lands against a figure already on the record instead of one invented alongside it, which is `practices.md`'s *"the actor that builds a check may not be its only judge"* applied to a number rather than to a person. On the smoke fixture stage 0 drops **38.10%** at recall **1.0000** — and the run demonstrates the point of the whole statistic, because that perfect recall carries a 95% lower bound of **0.6756** at n=8 and clears nothing.

⚠️ **The harness reports an ABSENT composed measurement as absent.** With no stage-1 adapter it prints the baseline and stops, rather than composing a no-op stage 1 and printing the resulting yield failure — a layer that ties the baseline fails by construction, and rendering that as a verdict would dress an absent result as a measured one. Same discipline in the fixture format: a set declares `role: smoke | evaluation`, `assertCanGate` **refuses** a gating verdict from a smoke set, and an `evaluation` set must carry `provenance { labelsFrom, cutAt }` — because the structural rule has no teeth if a hand-authored file becomes the scoring set by having one field edited. The scoring set still does not exist; the instrument is built and the data is named, which is the honest deliverable when one is ready and the other is not — the same finding this file already records against #5420.

**AC 4's gate is written so it can fail, and its failing direction is tested.** `checkTriageDefaultGate` turns red if the registry default for `ATLAS_BRAIN_EXTRACTION_TRIAGE_ENABLED` becomes `true` while the latest recorded run did not clear the pair — a TEST, not a boot check, because *"enabled by default"* is a code change and a boot-time check would fail closed in a region that never ran the harness. It is vacuous today (the dial defaults off), and a vacuously-green check is worth nothing until someone has watched it go red, so the suite drives the `on` arm with no records, with a failing record, and with a superseded failure. The budget (AC 9) is data rather than a paragraph: three attempts per **set** — counted per set, so a second candidate spends the same allowance, because the budget is a property of the set's independence and not of any one candidate's patience — and `verifyRecordedVerdict` recomputes each record's verdict from its own numbers so a hand-edited `"passed": true` is caught rather than trusted.

**Shipped 2026-09-02 — #5338's manifest cutter: acceptance criteria 1 and 2, and the one that could not have been met by the probe it named** ([#5338](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5338) stays open — the harness, the smoke test and the numbers remain) — `lib/brain/heldout-manifest.ts` + `atlas-operator ops heldout-manifest`, 41 unit assertions, 13 against real Postgres, 13 on the operator surface. The set freezes as a **manifest** — `(episodeId, class)` plus the cut date, the window and the dial evidence — so it can be committed under `packages/api/scripts/heldout/` where a `gate-export` bundle must not be: bodies are re-read live at measurement time, which turns a purged row into a **loud** `--verify` failure instead of a committed file quietly still serving content a tenant asked us to delete.

⭐ **AC 2 could not have been established by the signal it named, and the reason is a feature we shipped ourselves.** *"Cut from a window in which the triage dial was off"* reads as a query over `brain_episodes.triaged_out_at` — and [#5534](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5534)'s re-queue sets `triaged_out_at` and `triage_reason` back to NULL, so after one re-queue **there is no query over that table that can establish triage ever ran**. The mark is therefore one of three probes, not the probe: the immutable `brain.extraction_cycle` audit row (`skipped.triaged`) survives a re-queue, and the platform `settings` row says whether the window has already closed. Generalizable: **a precondition proven by a mark that another shipped feature can clear is not proven** — check for the immutable record of the act, not for its erasable side effect. ⚠️ And when **no** cycle audit rows exist between the window's start and the cut, that half is reported **UNATTESTED** rather than passed: "0 cycles reporting triage" out of zero cycles observed reads exactly like a clean bill of health unless the denominator is printed beside it. Refusing on missing audit rows was rejected — it would fire hardest on the deployments with the shortest retention, which has nothing to do with whether triage ran.

⭐ **The load-bearing test is an AGREEMENT test, not a correctness test.** The manifest's class is per-EPISODE (triage drops episodes) and `gate-export`'s is per-DECISION (a reviewer who published one claim and retracted another produced two rows); #5338's recall denominator is the first and its corpus is the second. So the pg suite seeds one workspace, collapses the *bundle's* rows independently of the cut's SQL, and asserts the two maps are equal — a disagreement would compute the number over a population the corpus does not contain, and would be invisible in either suite alone. Two other divergences are deliberate and pinned: the cut **refuses** an oversized window where `gate-export` truncates-and-warns (a prefix bundle is still worth eyeballing; a prefix manifest is sampled by sort order, which is exactly the authorship a mechanical window removes), and `gate-export`'s unrepresentable-grant refusal is **absent**, because that rule is about content leaving and a manifest carries none.

⭐ **A two-axis review of my own branch found three defects, and the sharpest is a general one: the path that PRINTS an alarm must be gated as tightly as the path that arms it.** `--verify` re-resolves a manifest against the live DB, and its whole value is that an id which no longer resolves is a loud purge signal — but it took no containment check, so pointing a `us` manifest at `--region eu` reported **every row as purged**: the design's loudest alarm, at full volume, from a flag typo. The cut path had refused to cross a boundary since the first commit; the read path that renders the consequence had not. Second: `checkCutWindow`'s docstring called `to <= now` a hard boundary that made a row's class settled, and it is only **necessary** — an episode ingested a second before `to` may still be un-drained at `cutAt`, landing in `excluded` rather than on the arm it is about to reach, so the negative arm's size varies with the `to`→`cutAt` gap. A drain-lag margin is the obvious fix and the wrong one (batch turns around in hours, a quarantined episode may never arrive, and any constant either lies or lets one stuck row block every evaluation forever), so the shortfall is **measured** into `counts.stillDraining` instead of gated — same posture as the unattested `cyclesObserved: 0`. Third: AC 2 says *"off in every region"* and every probe reads one, which ADR-0024 makes unavoidable — a process cannot read another region's tables — so the manifest now records `dialEvidence.attestsRegion` and the console prints `<region> ONLY` rather than letting a one-region pass be read as a fleet-wide one. **Generalizable: when a criterion asks about a fleet and the architecture confines you to one node, state the scope you actually checked in the artifact — an unqualified pass is the lie, not the narrow check.**

Two smaller ones worth carrying. The dial's audit probe is deliberately **not** workspace-scoped — the extraction fiber is process-wide, so a triage drop in any workspace is proof the dial was on in the region — which correctly makes those rows shared state between pg tests in one schema, so the tests that seed a dirty row clean up after themselves rather than the probe being narrowed to suit them. And the evidence query compares `coalesce(a.metadata->'skipped'->>'triaged', '0') <> '0'` rather than casting: a `::numeric` on a malformed audit row raises instead of counting, and the string form makes *absent* read as no-triage (true for every cycle row predating [#5336](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5336)) while *unparseable* reads as triage. ⚠️ The window is on `ingested_at` and the audit span runs to the **cut**, not to the window's end — an episode ingested inside the window can be drained at any time up to the cut, so the dial has to have been off for the longer period.

**Corrected 2026-09-02 — three regions are BUILT, one is SERVING, and I asserted otherwise three times in a day the repo already said so** ([#5582](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5582) parked `eu` and `apac` on 2026-09-01) — Railway production, read today: `api-eu` and `api-apac` `offline` with **no active deployment**; `eu-int-postgres` and `apac-int-postgres` still **online** with volumes, so the regional data is retained and still bills. ⭐ **Nothing was missing from the tree.** [`docs/development/parked-regions.md`](../../docs/development/parked-regions.md) already carried the cost measurement ($16.65/mo, 43% of a $38.87 bill, because Railway bills memory per GB resident per minute and an idle region costs what a busy one does), what parked means mechanically (`selectable: false` + `requestable: true` in `deploy/api/atlas.config.ts`), and the seven steps un-parking takes against a config comment claiming it is one. The signup flow already models it — `requestableRegions` with a request-access affordance and, by construction, no `apiUrl` for a parked arm. What failed was **orientation**: `CLAUDE.md` said *"3-region SaaS residency"* with no pointer to any of it, and this file's `## Today` said the SaaS *"runs across three regions"*, so a present-tense claim of three live regions was the reading the tree handed you. Both now say one serves and point at the runbook. ⚠️ **The trap that makes this worth an entry rather than a typo fix: the region DBs stay online, so `atlas-operator --region eu|apac` still answers.** A verification that queried eu and got rows would look exactly like a verification of a live region — **the DB replying is not the region being live**, and every "verified in all three regions" claim written after 2026-09-01 was run against one. Generalizable: **when a capability is switched off at a layer above its data, the data layer keeps confirming the capability exists** — park the process and the database will still tell you yes.

**Designed 2026-09-02 — #5338's stage-1 recall threshold, and four defects in the acceptance criteria that asked for it** ([#5338](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5338) stays open; no code, ACs rewritten) — the milestone said *"needs a grill before the stage-1 recall threshold is set"*, and the grill's first output is that the criteria as written could not have produced a number. ⭐ **AC2 was satisfiable by a no-op: a triage layer that drops nothing scores 100% recall.** Stage 0 with all three rules disabled passes the criterion perfectly and delivers exactly none of what [#5334](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5334) exists for, so a recall-only threshold cannot fail in the direction that matters. The threshold is therefore a **pair**: recall **≥ 99% observed with a 95% Wilson lower bound ≥ 95%**, and a **relative** yield gate — the composed layer must drop strictly more episodes than stage 0 alone at no worse recall. The lower-bound clause is what makes the number non-arbitrary rather than chosen: by the rule of three, zero observed misses clears a 95% lower bound only at **n ≥ 60**, and tolerating one miss needs **n ≥ ~100**, so held-out set size falls out of the threshold instead of being picked separately. 99% rather than 100% because 100% is unfalsifiable in practice and any single ambiguous item fails it — which is precisely the pressure that gets a held-out set quietly regenerated. Yield stays relative because no one has measured what fraction of a real channel is noise, and an absolute *"≥30%"* would be a number wearing a measurement's clothes; *"beat the rules you already shipped"* is a bar that can genuinely fail. ⚠️ **The threshold is a cost threshold, not a safety one, and only because of a design constraint now written onto [#5336](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5336) stage 1: mark, never discard** — the same `triaged_out_at` + `triage_reason` posture stage 0 shipped, with the requeue surface [#5534](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5534) closed. A miss becomes a visible backlog rather than a lost fact; without that constraint the honest threshold is near-1 and the cascade is not worth building.

⭐ **The corpus can only be read counterfactually, and that makes the cut time-sensitive.** `gate-export`'s negative arm requires `e.extracted_at IS NOT NULL` and an ingested-but-unextracted episode *"must not appear at all"* (pinned, `gate-export-pg.test.ts:193`) — but triage runs **before** extraction, so **a triaged-out episode never appears in any bundle.** Recall cannot be read off a corpus directly; it has to be measured by replaying triage over a frozen set of episodes that did reach extraction. Sound — and it means the frozen set must come from a window where the dial was **off**, because the first region to enable triage makes every later bundle pre-filtered by the thing under test. `ATLAS_BRAIN_EXTRACTION_TRIAGE_ENABLED` is default-off everywhere, so prod's history is currently clean and the cut is cheap; its value only decays. ⚠️ **Corrected 2026-09-02: "all three regions" was wrong** — `eu` and `apac` were parked by #5582 the day before this was written, so only `us` serves. The window to cut is one region's, which makes the argument simpler rather than weaker. At two workspaces / 37 facts there is nothing to window — the window is everything up to the cut date, and `GATE_EXPORT_ROW_MAX = 5000` is two orders of magnitude away.

⚠️ **Three more defects, each of which the next lane would otherwise have rediscovered.** (1) **AC1's *"frozen, is versioned"* contradicts `gate-export.ts:139` verbatim** — `EVALUATION_ONLY_NOTICE` says *"cut it for a named evaluation and destroy it afterwards — do not accumulate bundles"*, and a frozen versioned set is an accumulated bundle. Resolved by freezing the **manifest, not the bundle**: `(episode_id, class, cut_date)` and no tenant text, re-resolved against the live DB at each run, with a row that no longer resolves being a loud purge signal instead of a silent staleness. The bundle carries `episode.body` **verbatim with no redaction or hashing** — exclusions are by explicit enumeration, not scrubbing — so this is the difference between a durable file of customer Slack/mail/transcript content and a list of ids. (2) ⭐ **The manifest has to own the label because an approval leaves no timestamp of its own** (`gate-export.ts:288`): publish stamps nothing per-claim, `updated_at` also moves on grant widening, and only retraction dates itself. A mechanical time window can therefore window on `occurred_at`/`ingested_at`/`extracted_at` but **never on decision time**, so decisions landing after the cut would drift a set that was supposed to be frozen. A `decided_at` column on the publish path is the correct long-term fix and is not this slice. (3) **AC4's *"a single command"* conflicts with where the work belongs** — the number-producing run is pure and synthetic and belongs in `packages/api/scripts/`, while replaying triage over real episode bodies is the same class of thing as `gate-export` and belongs behind its region check and `ATLAS_..._OK=1` + `--confirm` in `atlas-operator`. Two commands, and the AC says so now rather than being quietly reinterpreted. AC2's *"stage-1 recall"* is also now *composed-layer* recall: an admin turns on a layer, not a stage, and per-stage thresholds invite stages that are individually fine and jointly terrible.

**The number is set on a synthetic set; prod is the smoke test, and the entry that said so is two entries up.** 37 facts carries a 95% interval roughly ±10 points wide — quoting that as a gate would be the *"number produced by loosening what counts as real"* this file already warns about for [#5420](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5420). ⭐ **Public corpora for *evaluation* are a materially weaker ask than the open training-corpus decision cards** — ADR-0044's argument is that weights cannot unlearn, whereas an evaluation reads a corpus once and discards it, which is what `EVALUATION_ONLY_NOTICE` already contemplates. So Ubuntu IRC + Apache lists (licence-verified in `.claude/research/extractor-corpus-acquisition.md`) supply the set without touching decision card 1, with generated Slack-idiom items filling the gap they do not cover and provenance labelled per item. Denominator is positives only (`status='published' AND invalidated_at IS NULL`), with positives+rejected reported beside it as an ungated diagnostic carrying one named alarm: diagnostic recall running materially **below** gating recall means the filter learned **reviewer taste** rather than **claim presence**, which reads as a win and will not generalize to a new workspace or a new reviewer. ⚠️ **Recorded as a known limit rather than solved: a fact is published only if a reviewer reached it**, so with a deep backlog the positives denominator is partly a measure of review throughput, not extraction value.

**What enforces it, and what happens when it fails.** The gate is a test that fails if the triage dial's default is `on` while the recorded measurement is below threshold — *"enabled by default"* is a code change, so the gate belongs where code changes are caught rather than on a boot path that would fail closed in a region that never ran the harness. A recall failure means keep the cascade and never default it on; a recall **and** yield failure means abandon it and the question is answered. ⭐ **And a measurement budget, because the issue forbids regenerating the set but says nothing about re-measuring against it:** candidates are declared before the cut, each is measured once, and needing more than three attempts means cutting a second set. Retune-and-remeasure erodes a held-out set just as surely as regenerating it, one reasonable-seeming step at a time. Coverage ([ADR-0041](../../docs/adr/0041-the-coverage-surface-counts-what-it-can-see.md)) gets the triaged-out **count** unconditionally now — `triaged_out_at` is queryable today and an episode deliberately not looked at belongs in a statement of what Atlas can see — upgrading to a recall caveat once a number exists.

**Demonstrated 2026-09-02 — the ingest lane recognizes a contradiction as it ARRIVES, and running it found the lane had been dead for an unknown period** ([#5449](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5449) closed; [#5588](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5588) filed) — every anchor-arm `in-tension-with` edge in prod until today was minted by a human-triggered sweep over rows that already existed, and the sweep lane always requires a human to have curated the predicate first. So condition 4's *"nobody had to intervene"* had **sweep-lane evidence only**. Two contradicting messages were posted to `C0BLVJPD8DC` from two accounts, with the prediction registered on the issue **before** posting, per the #5438 precedent. Result: `b8ed0d8d` (`Onboarding` / `takes` / `5 business days end to end`, key `onboarding`) and `c4c5c628` (`onboarding process` / `has target of` / `10 business days`, key `onboarding process`), both `cardinalityHint: single`, and edge `85d1e343` `in-tension-with` `c4c5c628` → `b8ed0d8d`. **Anchor arm, not exact slot** — subject keys differ and predicate keys differ, so no exact-slot arm can reach a pair with no shared slot. **That it was ingest is established four ways, and the fourth does not depend on timestamps:** the edge's `created_at` equals the newer fact's `ingested_at` to the microsecond (`01:39:27.386643+00`, one transaction); `admin_action_log` filtered to non-`system:` actors returns **0 rows** across 01:23→01:41; the last `brain_fact.tension_sweep` was an hour earlier and minted 0; and ⭐ **`has target of` has no `brain_predicate_cardinality` row at all, so `TENSION_SWEEP_SQL`'s approved-entry gate makes the retroactive lane structurally incapable of having produced this edge.** The first anchor-arm edge in production that no human act produced.

⚠️ **The run was blocked mid-flight by a prod outage nobody could have known about, and that is the more general finding.** The first extraction cycle after the messages landed failed on `A positive credit balance is required for all requests, including BYOK` — the AI Gateway account was empty. The refund-and-requeue behaviour worked exactly as designed (one strike forgiven per episode, quarantine at 3, probing rather than absorbing) and both episodes survived to extract cleanly once credit was restored. **What did not work is that nothing knew.** The last successful extraction in prod was 2026-08-25T14:09Z; in the eight days since, the fiber ran ~2,300 cycles and **every one logged `status: "success"`** — all with `inspected: 0`, because an empty queue cannot fail. A query for `failed > 0 OR outageRefunded > 0` across the entire `admin_action_log` returns exactly one row, today's: not "the failures were quiet", but **no attempt had been made to fail.** ⭐ **A green cycle counter on a queue-driven fiber measures that the fiber woke up, not that its dependencies work — and health observable only under load is not health monitoring.** On a real customer this fails silently at their first message. #5588 carries it, with two constraints written in: nothing added may write a fact, edge or episode (ADR-0037 §7 refused an unattended autonomous writer of `brain_edges`, and a health probe must not become one by accident), and the refund-and-requeue behaviour stays as-is because it is what made this run recoverable. ⭐ **The demonstration found it only by being the first thing to use the lane in eight days** — which is an argument for demonstrations against prod over green tests, one more time.

**Recorded as NOT established.** Segmentation held here and is not reliable — the extractor keyed the subjects apart as hoped, which is what made this the anchor arm at all; ADR-0037 records segmentation as a model behaviour that holds statistically, and a rerun could key both the same and test nothing. Recognition stays partial by construction: the anchor arm is a whole-token prefix test, so `onboarding` against `the ramp-up process` is still silence. ⭐ **The model gate was open for the second time in prod, which is two data points and not a rate** — the extractor answered `single` on both claims against a prompt ADR-0037 §3 describes as biased toward `multi`, as it did for the #5438 fixture; the prediction deliberately called this even, and a `multi` would have been an equally publishable demonstration of the gate biting, which remains unobserved. Adjudication was not exercised — `object_cmp` is NULL on both, so the pair sits in the abstain band and stays there. `us` only, 37 facts, one workspace, two human authors, one source vendor.

**Filed 2026-09-02 — the gateway shortlist, as the cost half of #5338's AC3, deliberately not as a swap** ([#5338](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5338) stays open) — `lib/gateway-catalog.ts` already pulls the unauthenticated `https://ai-gateway.vercel.sh/v1/models` on a 30-minute TTL and `token-pricing.ts` already prefers its live per-token rates over the static family table, so the catalog is a standing asset rather than something to build. Read today: **363 models, 227 of them language + structured output** (non-negotiable — `llmFactExtractor` calls `generateObject`), **145 cheaper** than the `anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5` extraction default. ⚠️ **`no_training` is a hard gate here, not a preference** — #5339 prohibits training on customer episodes and ADR-0044 makes it structural — and it removes the entire free tier (`minimax-m2.7-free`, `minimax-m3-free`, `laguna-s-2.1-free`, all `no_training: none`) plus `zai/glm-4.7-flashx`. ⭐ **`some` is not a passing grade: it means the guarantee holds for a subset of routes, and an extraction fiber does not get to know which route it drew.** Filtering to `no_training: all` **and** `zdr: all` leaves **47**, led by `alibaba/qwen3.7-flash` at ~$2/mo against Haiku 4.5's ~$82 on #5337's measured shape. **Nothing moves `WORKSPACE_EXTRACTION_DEFAULTS` until #5338's numbers exist:** `providers.ts` still carries *"the gate-agreement number is deferred"* from when #5353 shipped the Haiku tier without one, and swapping again on price alone repeats that one model further down — a cheaper extractor changes what the gate lets through, which changes what reaches a human, and condition 4 requires that queue stay bounded. ⚠️ **And the saving is projected, not observed** — $82/mo is a 100-employee workspace at 100k episodes/month; prod holds two workspaces, 37 facts, and the lane has completed twice in eight days, so actual extraction spend rounds to zero and 97% of zero is zero. ⭐ **One thing it does change: #5337's "a fixed cost replaces an unbounded variable one" argument weakens considerably at $2–5/mo** — a workspace adding Teams, email and Drive multiplies a $3 bill rather than an $82 one. That is an argument about ordering and not about cancelling #5337, whose residency trigger is untouched by any gateway price and is the one with revenue attached; recorded so the harness measures the hosted candidates and the distilled model against the same held-out set rather than deciding between them twice.

**Measured 2026-09-02 (UTC) — the week elapsed clean: condition 4 holds, and the field that proved it was erased four minutes before the read** ([#5425](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5425) closed) — the observation window opened 2026-08-25T19:36:30Z and closed 2026-09-01T19:36:30Z; the re-read ran read-only against us prod at 2026-09-02T00:46Z, five hours after it closed. All four checks the 2026-08-25 entry left to be re-run pass without qualification: edge `fa9935b7` still exists as `in-tension-with` `547c7e55` → `eb2f9afb` with `created_at` unmoved at 19:36:30.598834+00, and both facts are live — `valid_to` NULL, `invalidated_at` NULL on each. **Nobody intervened, established three ways rather than one:** the admin log holds exactly two human acts in the window, both #5426's condition-5 work one fact-family over (`brain_fact.retract` on `219f5a09`, `brain_fact.correct`/`supersede` on `b568b707`), and a scan for either fact id in `target_id` **or** in `metadata::text` returns zero rows across the whole window; every `brain.extraction_cycle` filtered on `factsCreated`/`factsCorroborated`/`factsProvisional`/`extracted` `> 0` returns zero rows, so no machine wrote either; and only **two episodes** were ingested in the workspace at all, both being those same two corrections. **Neither was picked** — three `in-tension-with` edges exist in the workspace and the only one between the pair is the advisory one, with no `supersedes` edge, no `valid_to`, and `object_cmp` NULL on both, so `$25M` against `$30M` still sits in the abstain band exactly as designed. Attribution reads as a human name since #5440: **Matt Sywualk ($25M) against Gregg ($30M)**.

⚠️ **A `mode.publish` at 2026-09-02T00:42:53Z — 5h06m AFTER the window closed, four minutes before the read — promoted BOTH facts of the pair `draft` → `published`** (`promotedBrainFacts: 2`, both ids named in `promotedRows`; 20 warehouse drafts refused on `OBSERVATION_NOT_PUBLISHABLE`). It is outside the window on both ends of the clause and does not touch the verdict. Two consequences that outlive it: ⭐ **the pair is now a contradiction between two AUTHORITATIVE claims, neither picked** — a strictly stronger state than the demonstration was written against, which ran entirely on drafts, and `supersededFacts: []` / `supersessionsHeldBack: 0` on the same log row say the publish picked nothing. And ⚠️ **`updated_at` on both rows is now 00:42:53, so the corroborating check the 2026-08-25 entry leaned on is gone** — that entry recorded *"`updatedAt` on both rows is still 2026-08-25T14:09Z"* as independent evidence that the sweep touched no fact row. Any later write erases it and one has, so the non-intervention proof now rests entirely on `admin_action_log` and `brain_episodes`. ⭐ **Second sighting of the same property**: #5424 found `updated_at` last-write-wins and useless for recovering a publisher. **A field whose evidentiary value depends on it not having been written is not evidence you can bank — it is evidence with an expiry nobody sets.** Where non-intervention must be provable later, it has to be provable from an append-only table.

⭐ **#5450's zero-forecast confirmed against the write, not just against the read.** At 00:44:03–00:44:07Z the four pending warehouse-proposed cardinality entries — `is active`, `region`, `name`, `plan tier` — were approved, and a tension sweep at 00:44:18Z minted **`{"minted": 0}`**. [#5463](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5463)'s `tension-forecast` endpoint predicted zero for all four on 2026-08-26 for a structural reason — the warehouse producer writes one episode per run and one row per `(subject, predicate)` slot, so the anchor arm's different-episode requirement and the exact-slot arm both come up empty on every warehouse subject. The counterfactual held when it became a real write. The workspace now carries 5 approved entries, 1 `human` and 4 `warehouse_structural`.

**Recorded as NOT established.** Recognition is still partial by construction — the anchor arm is a whole-token prefix test, so `Series B` against `the round` remains silence, and that remainder closes on human authoring (ADR-0037 §6), not on this demonstration. The recognition was **retroactive, not at ingest**: the ingest lane has still never minted an anchor-arm edge in prod, which is [#5449](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5449) and still owed. A human authored the cardinality entry and a human ran the sweep, and **neither operation has a UI** — *"nobody had to intervene to keep it that way"* is satisfied; *"nobody had to intervene to make it happen"* was never the claim and is false. Only `us` was read; eu and apac are parked (#5582). The corpus is unchanged in shape at **35 facts, one workspace, two human authors, one source vendor** (13 published / 22 draft), so nothing here generalizes to a corpus of another size. And the attribution is **present and resolved, not verified** — nobody checked that the two people said what the claims say.

**Condition 4 is the fifth of eight to close** (2, 4, 5, 6, 7). The three open are **1** (cold start — [#5374](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5374), the only one gated on a real customer), **3** (tiers unmistakable — [#5375](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5375), [#5473](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5473)) and **8** (self-hoster — [#5377](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5377), inheriting the other seven). The reachable ceiling without a customer is unchanged: seven of eight.

**Shipped 2026-09-01 — two idle regions stop costing 43% of the bill, without un-building them** (PR [#5582](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5582); no issue) — measured against prod on 2026-09-01 (Railway CLI + dashboard, billing period Jul 28 – Aug 28): project bill $38.87, of which memory was 90% ($35.17), and `api-eu` + `api-apac` + their int-postgres + their backup-scratch came to **$16.65/mo — 43% of the bill**. Both regions held **0 conversations** (`eu` 1 seeded user, `apac` 2). Railway bills memory per GB resident per minute, so an idle always-on API process (~0.6 GB) costs what a busy one costs. Parked via the #3948 **existence ≠ selectability** seam rather than a new one: both arms stay in the residency map, `RegionGuardLive` still boots an `ATLAS_API_REGION=eu|apac` process, residency routing still resolves them, and un-parking is deleting one flag. A second flag `requestable: true` splits the two reasons a region can be non-selectable — `selectable: false` **alone** means *internal* (the `staging` arm; must never be shown to a customer), `+ requestable` means *parked and advertisable* — and `isRegionRequestable` requires **both**. Parked arms render in the signup picker as plain rows, deliberately not cards (a card is a selectable affordance), linking to `/pricing?residency=<id>`, which pre-fills the existing Turnstile-protected talk-to-sales dialog so the resulting Twenty Note names the region. No new endpoint. ADR-0024 is unaffected: this is not the "dark-launch US-only" option that ADR rejected, which deferred the *engineering* — this defers only the *spend*, after the engineering shipped, and an EU customer is one flag plus a redeploy away. ⭐ **Inferring a second meaning from the absence of a first one advertises whatever the absence also covers** — requestability read off `selectable: false` alone would have put the internal `staging` hostname into the public signup funnel the moment it shipped; two reasons for one negative need two flags, not a cleverer read of one. ⚠️ With a single selectable arm the US region-map takes `resolveRegion`'s `map.regions.length === 1` short-circuit and **never fans out an existence probe**, so the two dormant eu/apac accounts cannot reach the login front-door while parked — acceptable only because neither region holds data, and pinned as a test rather than left to a comment; **re-check that precondition before parking a region that does hold data**. ⚠️ Deploy ordering is load-bearing: prod must serve this config **before** the services are scaled down, or the live picker still offers Europe and a prospect picks a dead host. Shipped in v0.2.25.

**Shipped 2026-09-01 — a dependency's schema bump stops being able to block a release** ([#5580](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5580) closed; PR [#5581](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5581)) — better-auth 1.6.25 -> 1.7.1 adds a required `account.issuer`; its migrator refuses to add a required column with no default to a POPULATED table and throws, which set `_migrationError` -> `INTERNAL_DB_UNREACHABLE` -> `hasInternalDbError && isSaas` -> `/api/health` 503 -> the 300s Railway healthcheck failed in all three regions and v0.2.23 rolled back. Prod kept serving v0.2.22 throughout, so there was no outage — but the release could not land, and `web` HAD deployed, leaving prod split. `preflightUnsafeColumns()` mirrors Better Auth's own predicate and adds such columns as NULLABLE before `runMigrations()` — never backfilling and never adding the NOT NULL, because only the schema's author knows a correct value for an existing row — and refuses loudly for foreign keys, indexed/unique fields and types it cannot place. Shipped in v0.2.24. ⭐ **A pre-flight that reads a predicate more loosely than the gate it front-runs reintroduces that gate's failure, silently** — the first cut asked `defaultValue !== undefined` where the migrator asks `!hasStaticColumnDefault`, which is false for `json`/`date` and for any function-valued default, and the `continue` fired before the `skipped.push` so nothing named the column; the general form is that a guard standing in front of a stricter check must copy that check, not paraphrase it. ⭐ **Repairing the schema does not fix a running pod** — `_migrationError` is set once at boot and never re-evaluated, so staging stayed 503 against an already-repaired database until it was redeployed; the recovery order for this class is ALTER **then** redeploy, and anyone doing only the ALTER will reasonably conclude the fix failed. ⚠️ CI cannot see this bug class at all: a fresh `account` table makes the refusal unreachable by construction — Better Auth creates an absent table whole, NOT NULL and all, never taking the ALTER path — which is why the new suite runs against real Postgres with a POPULATED fixture, and why `migrate.test.ts` (mocking both the pool and the auth instance, asserting only that a THROWN error sets the flag) stayed green throughout. ⚠️ `api-staging` had **no healthcheck at all**: it hit the identical failure on 2026-08-31 in `saas` mode and was marked SUCCESS in 65 seconds, so the soak environment gated nothing and its managed auth sat broken for 32 hours unnoticed. Now configured with prod's `/api/health` + 300s timeout — but a Railway *redeploy* reuses the prior snapshot's deploy config, so the gate has not yet been observed firing; the next source-triggered staging deploy is the one to watch ([#5583](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5583)).

**Shipped 2026-08-31 — a half-filled Admin form stops being able to shadow a working env credential** ([#5564](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5564) closed; PR [#5579](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5579)) — `missingRequired`, the predicate deciding whether a workspace's action credentials are complete, had **no caller on the write path**: neither the store's save nor the admin PUT checked the merged result, so a half-filled Admin form freely created a row that — under ADR-0046's all-or-nothing rung rule — *shadowed* the environment rung instead of being topped up by it, silently breaking a self-hosted target that had been working from `process.env`. The route now validates the merged bundle (stored row, plus incoming `fields`, minus `clearFields`) against the resolver's own `unsatisfiedRequiredFields` — exported here for its first write-path caller — and returns 400 naming the unsatisfied fields before persisting anything; `DELETE` stays ungated on completeness so the refusal traps nobody. `configured` and `resolvedFrom` are removed outright, no shim and no deprecation window, for a five-way `state` discriminant — `unconfigured` · `workspace` · `env` · `partial-row` · `partial-row-shadowing-env` — the last being the damaging one the old flag pair could not express, since "no row" and "a row missing a required field" both reported `configured: false`. `mayHaveStoredRow` is gone with them: it inferred removability from resolver precedence, an inference over an absence, and offered a destructive action on a "might". ⭐ **A predicate with no caller on the write path is not a guard, and a status API cannot report a state its write path never refused** — the two halves are one defect seen from either end, and asking the same function of the exact object the next line writes is what keeps "complete enough to save" and "complete enough to execute" from drifting. ⚠️ Per-field `stored` was added beyond the brief because `present`/`source` answer only "what would execute" and in a partial state *nothing* executes — every field reads `unset` even when the row holds it, which would have forced an admin to re-type every required field and so disabled the one save that repairs the row. One review finding was **declined** with the reason recorded at the field: `stored` sitting beside `present`/`source` without the three forming a discriminant predates this change and reshaping the per-field read-back contract is outside #5564's scope — tracked as [#5584](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5584).

**Shipped 2026-08-31 — four deepening passes, then the five follow-ups they filed** ([#5568](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5568), [#5569](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5569), [#5570](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5570), [#5571](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5571), [#5572](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5572) closed; PRs [#5567](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5567), [#5573](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5573), [#5574](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5574), [#5575](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5575), [#5576](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5576), [#5577](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5577)) — a three-agent survey over the last ~100 commits' hot spots produced four passes, one commit per plate: an **encryption-axis completeness tripwire** (the rotation registry was the one table-lifecycle axis — vs purge / bundle / cleanup / residue-sweep / both Better-Auth lists — with no completeness guard, and the drift was live twice: `workspace_model_config` rotated only via a hand-copy inside `rotate-encryption-key.ts`, `email_outbox.payload`'s skip recorded only as prose; both are declarations in `db/integration-tables.ts` now, enumerated against the Drizzle schema in both directions); the **triage seam** promoted from a hard-imported sync function to `BrainExtractionDeps.triage: Triager` beside the ten adapters the fiber already injects, with `composeTriagers` as milestone 98's stage-1 mount point and every adapter fault failing open; **action targets made one-entry-truly**, where `ActionCredentialsOf` derives each target's credential type from its `as const` spec and the four `toXCredentials` narrowers, four byte-identical resolver copies, six `as unknown as` casts and three hand-maintained name lists are deleted (−311 lines); and **`approveActionAsUser` / `denyActionAsUser`** taking authorization, CAS and executor lookup behind tagged outcomes, deleting bulk's `preClassify` and its TOCTOU window. Each deferral was then filed and closed in turn: the review gate named as a facade with an id-scoped promotion arm ([#5568](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5568)), the `lib/vendor-http` spine extracted once [ADR-0045](../../docs/adr/0045-hand-rolled-vendor-http-clients.md)'s own stated deferral trigger fired ([#5569](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5569)), the executor registry re-keyed by `action_type` at module load with an explicit human re-dispatch verb ([#5570](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5570)), the staged-write verify→burn ordering written once ([#5571](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5571)), and the Salesforce action bounded by hand-rolling its two calls ([#5572](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5572)). ⭐ **An invariant that exists only as a copy-pasted comment is not an invariant** — `verifyAndBurnStagedConfirm` exposes no signature by which a caller can obtain a verified token and burn it later, so a third staged verb inherits the no-`await`-between-verify-and-burn ordering rather than being trusted to repeat a comment; the general form is that a rule enforced by reviewer attention is enforced by nothing, and the fix is to make the wrong sequence unrepresentable rather than documented. ⚠️ [#5575](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5575) closed a silent drop: an approval landing on a process that never took the request — after a restart, or on a sibling instance — found no executor in the id-keyed in-process `Map`, yet the CAS had already moved the row out of `pending`, so nothing would ever retry it and the approver got a 200 whose entry status quietly said nothing ran. `approved_not_executed` survives, narrowed to its one residual case. Re-dispatch is deliberately a human verb and not a boot sweep — an approval is a decision about a side effect in someone else's system, and firing it hours later with nobody watching is the failure being avoided. ⚠️ [#5576](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5576)'s bound is one 15s budget across **both** legs of the Salesforce create (client-credentials token POST + REST `sobjects`), not one per leg — two budgets would mean a 30s worst case, which is not a fixed bound — and the deadline tracks which leg it fired on, because a timeout while authenticating provably created nothing while one mid-create genuinely may have. Deepening detail for the four passes and the three seams they filed — before/after state, the non-scope each new module declares, and what was deleted rather than tested — is in [`architecture-wins.md`](./architecture-wins.md) #106–#109.

**Shipped 2026-08-31 — the action-credential seam pays out: three targets and an Admin UI, no per-target branches** ([#5553](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5553), [#5554](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5554), [#5555](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5555), [#5556](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5556) closed; PRs [#5559](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5559), [#5561](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5561), [#5562](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5562), [#5563](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5563), [#5566](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5566)) — [ADR-0046](../../docs/adr/0046-per-workspace-action-credentials.md) shipped the API with Jira as its pilot and recorded the rest as deliberate deferrals ([#3766](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/3766) → PR [#5550](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5550)); this is all four of them, and the seam's claim held. Linear, GitHub App and Salesforce each cost **one `ACTION_TARGETS` entry plus a credential-agnostic action module** — the diffs touch none of `credentials/resolver.ts`, `credentials/store.ts` or `routes/admin-action-credentials.ts`. `/admin/action-credentials` generates the entire page from the field specs the API serves out of that same registry: no target slug appears in `page.tsx` or `credential-form.ts`, and `generic-rendering.test.tsx` is what pins it — it serves the page a synthetic target the code has never seen (`widgetron`) and asserts a complete render, save payload and all, so a page that grew a per-target branch renders an empty card and fails the suite. Blank is not "clear": a field left empty is omitted from `fields`, so an admin editing only the base URL keeps the API token they cannot read back, and `clearFields` is the one explicit unset. ⭐ **Each target's env rung is deliberately name-disjoint from the operator-tier vars for the same vendor** — `SALESFORCE_ACTION_*` not `SALESFORCE_CLIENT_*`, `GITHUB_ACTION_*` not `GITHUB_APP_*`, each test-pinned. A shared name would let the self-host rung report a target "configured" out of credentials that can never write to the tenant's system — the all-or-nothing failure mode one level up — and would silently arm a target on any box that had only ever configured the datasource. The general form is that two tiers reading one name is a coupling no amount of documentation makes safe. ⭐ The Salesforce action authenticates as a Connected App on client credentials and deliberately does **not** reach for the workspace's existing Salesforce OAuth bundle: that is a user-delegated, refresh-driven read grant, and reading it here would put two lifecycles on one natural key and let a datasource disconnect silently revoke a write path. ⚠️ [#5561](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5561) found a live cross-tier hazard on the way: `lib/github/installation-token.ts` cached minted tokens keyed by installation id **alone** — harmless while operator env was the only caller, wrong the moment a second caller brings its own App credentials, since an installation token carries the permissions of the App that minted it. ⚠️ [#5566](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5566) is the cross-PR gap the post-burst verification pass caught: #5561 added `multiline` to the field spec for the PEM, but the Admin page — written before it — stripped the attribute at its zod mirror, so a 1,700-character key rendered as a single-line input, the exact paste-mangling the attribute exists to prevent. It renders unmasked by design: HTML has no masked textarea, a CSS `-webkit-text-security` mask renders plain on Firefox, and a mask that sometimes lies is worse than none — the write-only contract is the real protection.

**Shipped 2026-08-31 — `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` stage 4: `packages/api`, src and tests as one stage** ([#5522](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5522) stays open — one stage remains; PR [#5565](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5565)) — graduates `packages/api/src` and everything api's module graph drags with it (`plugins/twenty`, `plugins/chat`, `plugins/yaml-context`, `packages/mcp`, `ee/`): **792 errors triaged to zero**, with no new `@ts-expect-error`, `oxlint-disable` or `any`. Two corrections to the staging map on #5522, both worth carrying into the last stage. First, api's tests are **not** a separate stage: unlike every package graduated so far, api's tests live *inside* `src`, and the manifest gates directories — naming `packages/api/src` graduates both (measured split: 416 src, 374 tests). ⭐ Second, the stage was 790 errors and not the ~207 an earlier measurement saw, for one nameable reason: `db/{migrate,internal}.ts` declare a structural mirror of pg's `NoticeMessage` as `{ readonly message?: string }` with the listener in a **contravariant** position, so under the flag the real `pg.PoolClient` stops satisfying our own `MigrationClient` / `InternalPoolClient` — a single mismatch cascading to **163 errors across 40+ files**, and matching the driver's declared contract (`?: string | undefined`) retires all of them at once. The general form is that an error count under a strictness flag is not additive, and one contravariant mirror of a third-party type can dominate the total — so measure a staged migration after fixing the type boundaries, not before.

**Shipped 2026-08-30 — the refusal payload columns get their first reader** ([#5557](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5557); PR [#5560](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5560)) — [#5533](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5533) / PR [#5549](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5549) persisted `refusalDetails` for the vocabulary-proposal and predicate-cardinality import sections, but `cleanup.ts`'s delete-time audit still selected the EDGE column alone — so until now the two new payload columns had **no reader at all**: an operator running source-row cleanup was warned about unexported edge refusals while, in the same sweep, a refused vocabulary decision lost its last copy's context in silence. The eligibility re-check now reads all six refusal columns off the **same `FOR UPDATE` row lock** — no extra query, still under the lock that pins the verdict to the deletes — and reports **per section**, each with its own three-state operator message. Per section rather than one summed verdict is the whole argument, and the same reason #5533 made `migrate.ts`'s pre-cutover disclosure per-section one phase earlier: a migration that recorded every edge payload and no cardinality payload is COMPLETE for one section and EMPTY for the other, so a summed line reads "partial" — true of neither — and sends an operator chasing a cardinality decision to a column holding none. `REFUSAL_DISCLOSURE` is exported so the pre-cutover disclosure and the delete-time warn carry one spelling of each section's `sourceTable`, payload `column` and `decisions` noun; they describe the same loss seven days apart, and a second spelling is a pair that can disagree about where an operator should look. ⭐ **The column list is derived from the section table, not written out** — a section listed in the table but missing from the SELECT reads back `undefined`, which the module's `typeof === "number"` narrowing turns into "count unknown, nothing recorded": the exact silence this issue existed to end, reintroduced one layer down and with no symptom. The general form is that a hand-maintained projection of a registry fails toward that registry's own failure mode, so derive the projection or test it in the failing direction.

**Shipped 2026-08-30 — action credentials become the workspace's own: ADR-0046, design + seam** ([#3766](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/3766) closed, and umbrella [#3765](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/3765) with it; PR [#5550](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5550)) — action targets stop reading one global `process.env`: a two-rung ladder (encrypted workspace row → self-host env, and *"there is no operator rung, deliberately"* stated at the resolver — a shared operator Jira is exactly the multi-tenant confusion the issue existed to end), resolved at the handler seam via `ActionExecutionContext` so individual actions go credential-agnostic; Jira is the pilot entry in the `ACTION_TARGETS` registry, migration 0213 on the ADR-0005 encrypted pattern with masked status-only read-back and purge/residency/rotation coverage from birth. All-or-nothing per rung: a partial workspace row throws rather than back-filling from env — the [#2850](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/2850) per-field leak direction, closed structurally — and a store/decrypt failure propagates rather than degrading to env. `requiredCredentials: []` on the Jira action retires the global-env health check's SaaS `degraded` flip for good. ⭐ Approved actions resolve the REQUESTER's stamped `action_log.org_id`, not the approver's active org — pinned by a cross-workspace-approver test, because an approval must not silently re-aim a ticket at the approver's Jira. Admin UI page + per-target children (Linear/GitHub App/Salesforce) deliberately deferred, recorded in [ADR-0046](../../docs/adr/0046-per-workspace-action-credentials.md) and filed: [#5553](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5553) (Admin UI), [#5554](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5554)/[#5555](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5555)/[#5556](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5556) (targets).

**Shipped 2026-08-30 — the triage re-queue gets its surface, and the number an admin reads is the number the verb can move** ([#5534](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5534) closed; PR [#5551](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5551)) — `GET/POST /api/v1/admin/brain-triage`: per-rule backlog counts plus an audited re-queue, `REQUEUE_TRIAGED_SQL` composed into a counting CTE rather than re-typed (the existing shape pins stay load-bearing for the only caller), the backlog sharing the re-queue's exact predicate. Decision recorded in the route header: an admin route, NOT an `atlas-operator` subcommand and not both — re-queueing NULLs both triage columns, so afterwards the admin-action row is the only durable answer to "who, which scope, how many rows", and the operator binary's `system:atlas-operator` is not an answer to "who"; the audit write is therefore awaited, and its failure returns a 500 saying the re-queue COMMITTED with its count rather than inviting a retry. Counts and rule ids only on the wire (strict schemas); the backlog lives beside the verb because `oversight.ts`'s own header forbids unscoped `brain_episodes` reads — argued on both sides of that seam.

**Shipped 2026-08-30 — the last `unreached` Better Auth table: apikey rows die with the user and the workspace** ([#5525](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5525) closed; PR [#5552](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5552)) — two DELETE arms because the two scopes live in different columns and neither subsumes the other: the workspace arm removes keys whose `metadata.orgId` names the purged org — including one whose owner survives elsewhere, the recorded delete-no-retention decision on `scimUser`'s reasoning (the row carries RLS claim values, is a credential at rest, and a non-FK org id would let a re-created org make a dead key live) — and the orphan arm removes an erased user's keys wherever their metadata points. The workspace predicate is evaluated in TypeScript, not SQL: `metadata` is `text`, a cast meeting one malformed row 22P02-aborts the whole erasure, and guarding that needs Postgres 16, which self-host doesn't require. ⭐ The tripwires learned to tell the two statements apart — previously either DELETE satisfied both checks, so removing an arm passed; `BETTER_AUTH_DUAL_ARM_TABLES` now demands two distinguishable statements, and the pg falsifier's fixture declares NO foreign key so no cascade can stand in for either. Pinned unreached set: `[]`.

**Shipped 2026-08-30 — refused vocabulary-memory decisions outlive cutover: the #5112 shape, two sections over** ([#5533](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5533) closed; PR [#5549](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5549)) — all six refusal arms in the proposal/cardinality import sections persist capped `refusalDetails` to the source's `region_migrations` row (migration 0212, additive; `refusalDetails.length < refused` stays the ONLY truncation signal), so a refused human review decision no longer evaporates with source cleanup plus target log rotation. One screen parameterized by schema — three hand-copied screens would re-create the #5303 drift — and `recordMigrationRefusals` writes all six columns in ONE statement whose abort threshold SUMS across sections, so a migration refusing only cardinality decisions no longer carries on past a failed write. Both concurrent arms re-read the destination row so `existingStatus` is a statement rather than a stale value — falsified under genuine interleaved concurrency. Both `bundle-scope.ts` RECORDED DECISION sentences now point at the shipped mechanism. ⚠️ `cleanup.ts`'s delete-time audit still reads only the EDGE column — the deliberate out-of-scope, recorded at `migrate.ts`'s `jsonbPayload` doc and filed as [#5557](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5557).

**Shipped 2026-08-30 — `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` stage 3: okf-bundle + fumadocs-okf, and the map that reorders the rest** ([#5522](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5522) stays open — later stages remain; PR [#5548](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5548)) — 18 errors, all triaged by making absence real; the two deliberate loose-optional boundaries are documented at their declarations (DocSourcePage's lazily-supplied metadata — a getter always makes the key present — and `IngestCapOverrides`, where `| undefined` is genuinely load-bearing), zero new suppressions. ⭐ **The issue's suggested order does not survive measurement**: `@atlas/api` exports raw `src/*.ts`, so "cli + mcp (~93)" is really a 306-error stage that graduates api's production code without saying so — and api itself is one 726-file mutually-recursive core carrying 207 errors, indivisible under a directory-granular manifest, so it ships as one stage or the mechanism grows a per-file gate. The measured map and revised order are on [#5522](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5522) and in the manifest's comments. ⭐ The conditional-spread idiom double-reads its subject — over a lazy getter, `...(page.title !== undefined ? { title: page.title } : {})` silently doubles the underlying file reads; read once into a local. A test caught it here, and api has lazier seams than okf-bundle.

**Shipped 2026-08-30 — three arch-backlog decisions become shipped artifacts in one pass** ([#4975](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4975), [#3777](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/3777), [#5469](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5469) all closed; PR [#5544](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5544)) — ADR-0045 ratifies hand-rolled vendor HTTP clients (the four load-bearing properties: non-throwing `ReadError` contract, engine-owned backoff, SSRF guards on vendor-supplied URLs, no cross-tenant token caching; the shared-half extraction declined-for-now, revisitable without reopening the decision); `plugin_grant_revocation_failures` (migration 0211) gives failed `onUninstall` revocations a durable operator worklist with platform list/resolve routes and an OTel counter — a record and an alert, deliberately never a retry queue, because retrying needs credentials the uninstall just destroyed; and ADR-0038 Layer 2 shipped standalone — `searchBrain` → `searchAtlas`, wire tiers `fact`/`raw-episode` → `attested`/`on-record` — reversing the "never a standalone rename PR" clause by dated amendment after its carrier left the cut and a qualifying tool change ([#5462](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5462)) went by unclaimed. Read-side tolerance everywhere the old spellings persist: `RENAMED_TOOLS` at the config seam, replay normalization in `transformMessages`, renderer fall-throughs and tier aliases mirrored into the published widget; MCP registration is a clean break, per the standing two-names-is-worse rule. ⭐ The new table tripped every new-table tripwire in CI (purge-scope, bundle-scope, residency cleanup rules, migration-count pins) — a registry that enumerates the schema catches exactly the omission it exists for, on the first table to arrive after it. ⚠️ `messages.content` replays persisted tool names and tier values verbatim — an unversioned persisted contract makes every wire rename a read-side migration, and skipping the normalization seam re-opens #5451's gray box for all history.

**Shipped 2026-08-30 — the bun-types patch re-rolled: async matcher argument types restored** ([#4441](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4441) stays open for the upstream filing; PR [#5543](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5543)) — the mapped-type patch's `infer A` captured only the LAST overload of each matcher pair, so all 1,382 `.resolves`/`.rejects` sites had unchecked matcher arguments since it landed, recorded nowhere; the replacement threads a return-type parameter `R` (default `void`) through all 92 `MatchersBuiltin` signatures, measured against both alternatives (stock: 753 `await-thenable` false positives; mapped type: a bogus `toEqual` property silently accepted; `R`: both columns green, verified by the same in-tree counterfactual under each patch). ADR-0031's Wave 3 entry corrected in passing: bun 1.4's runtime fails an un-awaited async assertion anyway, so the "silently disabled assertion" half of the original rationale is retired while the type inaccuracy stands. ⭐ `infer A` over an overloaded signature silently keeps one overload — a type-level workaround needs a counterfactual that must still error, or it trades an error you see for one you don't. ⚠️ The patch grew 51 → 798 lines and re-rolls on every `bun-types` bump; the ADR carries the recipe and `bun run type` is the gate that catches a bad re-roll.

**Shipped 2026-08-30 — the untimed pg hook that read as a migration bug** ([#5545](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5545); PR [#5546](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5546)) — `migrate-vocab-memory-pg.test.ts` was the only real-Postgres suite running the migration set in a hook without `PG_TEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, so it inherited bun's 5 s default; under CI load the hook was killed mid-migration and `afterAll`'s `DROP SCHEMA … CASCADE` deadlocked against the still-running INSERT, surfacing as `Migration 0005 … no schema has been selected to create in` — load-dependent, not diff-dependent (red twice on `main`, red on a types-only PR). The fix matches the 30 s convention its 94 sibling suites already follow; nothing skipped or quarantined. Also re-synced `bun.lock` with #5542's version bumps — CI's install is not `--frozen-lockfile`, so the drift failed nothing and just sat there. ⭐ The error a killed hook leaves behind is downstream of the kill, not the cause — a setup timeout masquerades as the code under test failing, and the tell is load-dependence across unrelated diffs.

**Shipped 2026-08-30 — BYOC Python egress on e2b + daytona: `unsupported` → `enforced`** (side-item of [#4666](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4666), which stays open for the region spike; PR [#5542](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5542)) — #5500's declaration was stale within a month: both pinned SDKs (`@daytonaio/sdk` 0.201.0, `e2b` 2.45.0) expose per-sandbox deny-all and allowlists, read off the SDKs' own type definitions rather than vendor pages. Both plugins now apply the host's per-request allowlist between `pip install` and agent code — the only ordering under which a deny-all bound and a working install are both true — and a refused policy (a Daytona Tier 1/2 org, an SDK below the new peer floor) fails the run closed rather than degrading to an unbounded sandbox, because `executePython` ships raw query-result rows. `enforcePythonEgress` in the plugin SDK resolves `allow-all`/absent/empty before any provider sees a policy, so a provider mapping has no branch that can read an empty allowlist as "allow". ⭐ A capability declaration is a claim about a moving vendor — it goes stale silently while reading as a security posture, so re-verify against the pinned SDK's type definitions, not the marketing page.

**Shipped 2026-08-30 — the CONTEXT.md split completes: eighteen contexts, one front door** ([#5302](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5302); PR [#5541](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5541)) — the remaining fifteen contexts moved to `docs/contexts/<name>/CONTEXT.md`, one commit per context with its `CONTEXT-MAP.md` row, the move proven lossless mechanically (`comm -23` of the sorted line sets is empty); the root file went 70,077 B → 1,539 B and is kept as the front door for the vocabulary rule and the many inbound references. The extraction was mechanical; the inbound-pointer sweep was the work — ten `see below/above` references, 21 navigational pointers across 17 ADRs, four downstream docs, and one public docs-site deep link to an anchor the split deletes, which no gate could catch because `check-docs-links.ts` puts external URLs out of scope. ⭐ Moving a section silently turns its same-file pointers into cross-file ones — a doc move is priced by who links IN, not by what moves, and the external links are the ones nothing checks.
