**Shipped 2026-08-29 — Lane C's training side gets its repo, and the pipeline's plumbing lands tested before any data exists** ([#5381](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5381) closed — both decision cards recorded on the issue: card 2 signed off as-is, card 1 full consent with a deletion-triggered withdrawal rule and derived-artifact survival stated for the register; [#5536](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5536), [#5538](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5538), [#5539](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5539) closed; `AtlasDevHQ/llm` PRs [#1](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/llm/pull/1), [#2](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/llm/pull/2), [#4](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/llm/pull/4), [#5](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/llm/pull/5)) — the private `llm` repo now carries the ADR-0044 side of the cascade: the extract contract vendored **by execution, not parsing** and sha256-pinned with a CI gate (atlas `d382398`; the UTF-16 cap and strip-then-truncate recorded as measured fixtures, so a Python port cannot silently re-read "8000 characters"); stdlib-only manifest tooling where the seal refuses `heldout/` on every read path and "was this in training?" answers by hash; a provisioning runbook + erasure procedure + a **measurable** bucket seal (refused ≠ not-found — canaries and paired probes, because an empty prefix and a working policy fail a read identically); and the Modal job + eval harness dry-running end to end on fixtures, the wrapper's thinness asserted from the syntax tree and GBNF *run* against outputs rather than grepped. ⚠️ Nothing downloads until [#5537](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5537)'s bucket exists — its erasure-procedure row is ticked, the account/bucket/policy/DPA rows are the human remainder, and Modal put the new account in signup review (RunPod/Lambda the recorded fallback; nothing on the critical path needs a GPU before acquisition + labelling complete). ⭐ **A green CI run must mean the checks actually checked** — pytest's exit-5-on-nothing-collected and `--strict-config`'s keys-only blind spot both shipped green-on-nothing shapes here before being closed from the other side (`filterwarnings = ["error::pytest.PytestConfigWarning"]`), and the general form is that a gate must be tested in its failing direction before its green means anything. *(This supersedes the 2026-08-28 entry's "#5381 stays open — two human decisions remain".)*

**Shipped 2026-08-28 — the vocabulary's memory rides the bundle: proposals + cardinality reclassified `exported`** ([#5113](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5113) closed; PR [#5532](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5532)) — the two deferrals `bundle-scope.ts` parked on #5036: export projections minus the region-local claim state (`applying`/`claimed_at`), import sections placed after the edge merge so the cardinality key screen reads the POST-merge closure, and merge lattices on the unordered pair / shared key — a decision outranks `pending`, contradictions keep the destination's and surface as `refused`, and a re-canonicalized key is refused outright, never silently re-keyed. Falsifications pin the #4507 arm (a re-run producer's reversed/display-form re-proposal is refused by the imported memory) and — added by independent review — the ordering itself: FALSIFICATION 2b ships the re-canonicalizing edge **in** the bundle against an alias-free destination, red if the screen ever runs pre-merge. ⭐ **The original falsification passed under either section ordering**, because the destination's alias pre-existed and the closure was identical before and after the merge — a falsifier must vary the thing the safety argument depends on, not merely reach the refusal arm. ⚠️ Refused decisions in the two new sections survive as counter + target-region log only — a recorded decision at both registry entries, the persisted-`refusalDetails` slice tracked as [#5533](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5533) — and the next `@useatlas/types` publish is breaking (two new required `ImportResult` sections, the #5440 precedent).

**Shipped 2026-08-28 — stage-0 deterministic triage ahead of the extraction fiber, off by default** (stage 0 of [#5336](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5336), which stays open for stage 1; PR [#5531](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5531)) — Lane C's first gate lever: three enumerable rules in one exported list (single-character bodies, pure emoji/`:code:` reactions, an exact-match ack set), biased hard toward pass-through — answer-shapes ("yes"/"no"/"agreed") are excluded on review and test-pinned, because a bare "no" is a complete answer to a factual question and a triage rule must not pre-empt a thread-aware extractor. `ATLAS_BRAIN_EXTRACTION_TRIAGE_ENABLED` is platform-scoped, default off, read per tick, with the gate-off equivalence test-pinned, so the merge changes nothing in the three prod regions until the dial turns. Migration 0210 marks routed-out episodes (`triaged_out_at` + `triage_reason`, CHECK-paired, never `extracted_at` — 0180's visible-backlog posture); `REQUEUE_TRIAGED_SQL` re-admits at the original drain position, its operator surface being the recorded scope cut ([#5534](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5534)). ⚠️ A mark-write failure fails OPEN into extraction — an episode goes to the model rather than being dropped uncounted.

**Shipped 2026-08-28 — the corpus-acquisition legwork for the distilled extractor, licence-checked against primary sources** ([#5381](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5381) stays open — two human decisions remain; PR [#5530](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5530)) — `.claude/research/extractor-corpus-acquisition.md`: per-corpus licence + redistribution terms with the load-bearing phrase quoted (recommended: Apache lists + `apache/*` threads on the ALv2 §1 theory, Ubuntu IRC/Dialogue for chat shape, AMI/ICSI held for transcripts; ruled out with quoted grounds: Stack Exchange, GitHub-wide/GH Archive, LKML prose), the two decision cards framed and not made, teacher labelling pinned to the deployed extract-contract with per-batch model-id provenance, a thread-level held-out split, and 30k–100k sizing on cited distillation practice. ⚠️ **The independent review added the axis the first draft lacked: data protection is separate from copyright** — the ASF archive refuses contact-info removal, GitHub's research allowance covers only "non-personal" information, and ADR-0044's weights-cannot-unlearn argument reaches a third party's erasure request; the counsel card now includes it. ⭐ 13 of 14 external quotes verified verbatim at their cited URLs and the one failure replaced — a research doc's warrant is its quote fidelity, so reviewing one means re-verifying relayed quotes against the primary pages, not trusting the summarizer.

**Shipped 2026-08-28 — `PERMISSIONS` consumed from `@useatlas/types`: the four-step sequence closes** ([#5194](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5194) — the step-1 entry below says it deliberately stayed open; it closed with this merge; PR [#5528](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5528)) — steps 3+4: both `create-atlas` template refs bumped `^0.7.0` → `^0.11.0`, `lib/auth/permissions.ts` re-exports the tuple from `@useatlas/types/auth` (retiring the two-copies interim risk step 1 named), `PERMISSION_LABELS` made exhaustive over `Permission` via `satisfies Record` so a flag with no label fails `bun run type`, and `permission-grouping.test.ts` stops hand-copying the tuple. ⭐ The npm-gate falsifier whose absence reverted attempt 1 was run first — `npm pack @useatlas/types@0.11.0` ships `PERMISSIONS` in `dist/auth.js` — because Deploy Validation builds against the published artifact: verify npm, not the source tree, before any consumer swap.

**Shipped 2026-08-28 — the 1.7 `scim*` catalog dies with the workspace** ([#5515](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5515); PR [#5527](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5527)) — `hardDeleteWorkspace` gains phase 3f: domain-keyed `scim*` deletes, child-before-parent, **all statuses** — a decommissioned connection's audit row does not survive a GDPR purge (the recorded no-retention decision). The headline case: a multi-workspace user's `scimUser` projection is unreachable by the user-FK cascade (the orphan arm deliberately spares users with other memberships), so only the domain-keyed delete removes it; `scimSubject.profileSourceId` — a plain-string pointer with no declared FK — is NULLed when its target dies. ⚠️ Decommission runs through `SCIMProvenance.deleteConnection` before the cascade, and its failure warns but never flips `complete` — unlike a Stripe warning, a decommission failure leaves nothing behind, because the transaction deletes every row regardless.

**Shipped 2026-08-28 — `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` stage 2: sdk, plugin-sdk, webhook-publisher** ([#5522](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5522) stays open — later stages remain; PR [#5524](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5524)) — the smallest slice in the issue's suggested order, by the PR-5519 mechanism unchanged. 16 errors, not the map's ~15: `@atlas/oauth-helper` exports raw `src/*.ts`, so the sdk's imports pull its source into the stage program transitively and the gate checks it flag-on whether the manifest names it or not — rather than leave it graduated invisibly, it is graduated fully, src and its out-of-src tests. ⭐ A package that ships source instead of dist graduates with its first consumer, not on its own schedule — a staging map should count transitive source-exporters with the package that imports them.

The built `dist/auth.js` was verified to actually export `PERMISSIONS` — the artifact whose absence (*"Export PERMISSIONS doesn't exist in target module"*) reverted attempt 1 — and the api copy pinned byte-identical. ⚠️ **Merging this chose the public-contract arm with eyes open**: `@useatlas/types` publishes, so removing or renaming a flag stops being an internal refactor and becomes a breaking change — mitigated by the ids being semi-public already, persisted in customer `custom_roles` rows. **Shipped 2026-08-28 — `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` on for `types` + `schemas`, with the staging map for everything else** ([#4955](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4955); PR [#5519](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5519)) — measured at the branch point: the flag at root surfaces **1,056 errors, 728 in `packages/api` alone**, so it lands per package. `tsconfig.exact-optional.json` extends the root config with the flag on, its `include` list is the staging manifest, and `bun run type` runs it right after the root program; when every package graduates, the file deletes into the root config. ⭐ **Every error resolved by making absence real, not by widening** — the one deliberate widening is the boundary whose doc comments already said *"Absent/undefined ≡ …"*, and the new `WithLooseOptionals` widens only the `satisfies z.ZodType` targets, so Zod's `p?: T | undefined` inference stops fighting exact domain interfaces without loosening them for any consumer. The #4943 `tools: maybeUndefinedVar` rejection was verified live by mutation. ⚠️ The remaining stages (sdk/plugin-sdk/webhook-publisher → cli/mcp → api/ee/plugins → web/www/docs, largest-consumer-first) are recorded in the PR body — and nowhere else, since the issue closed at stage 1.

**Shipped 2026-08-28 — the opt-in, off-by-default, draft-only autonomous suggester: M6's last route child** ([#5488](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5488); PR [#5520](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5520)) — ADR-0036 §T9 lock 1's permitted autonomy is now intact **by construction instead of by absence**: `brain_suggester`, a periodic fiber that per opted-in workspace scans up to five recently-idle conversations and files finds through `reconcileFacts` — draft-only is structural (the module writes no `brain_facts` SQL; the #5483 gate stays the only exit from draft), and disabling stops production without retroactively deleting drafts already raised. ⭐ **The precedent's footgun was copied as a negative**: `promote-decay-scheduler.ts` documents that a platform-scope `true` enrolls every workspace, so on SaaS this dial reads explicit workspace overrides only — a platform-scoped `true` enrolls nobody, pinned by a test. A violet "Suggested" chip keeps a machine's guess distinguishable from a person's testimony, and claims attribute to the owner's principal so a later human proposal of the same claim counts as one distinct source, not two (lock 5's self-echo discount). **All six route children of the M6 write-back grill (#5468) are now shipped.**

**Shipped 2026-08-28 — the hero's globe→chart morph staged off the titan** (no issue; PR [#5513](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5513)) — the lift beat: before anything flattens, the globe rises off the titan's shoulders by an amount measured from the real mesh bounds at load, so the unroll happens in clear air and he reads as holding the chart the way he held the globe. The clearance is a y-separation, which holds from every orbit angle — no scroll position or drag can put geometry through him. Label continuity through the fold window (dots ride their regions, leaders thin, words return as the chart settles), and the beat-4→5 white-on-white washout is gone. Verified with a dense t-sweep (0.30–0.70 in 0.025 steps) and six adversarial orbit angles under headless Chromium; ⚠️ Safari remains unverified — no WebKit build in the container, the #5512 caveat carried forward.

**Shipped 2026-08-28 — lazy session-episode materialization at propose-time** ([#5486](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5486); PR [#5511](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5511)) — ADR-0036 §T9 lock 3's genuinely unbuilt half. A chat session becomes a tier-3 raw episode only when a proposal from it is confirmed, never eagerly — by-reference per §T3 (`locator`, NULL `body`, 0180's XOR check), idempotent on the dedupe key, with `extracted_at` stamped at insert so the human's proposal is never handed to the extraction fiber to be re-derived as a second, machine-produced claim. The grant seed is lock 3's sentence as code: the actor plus what the source episode already carried, **never a silent `[org]`** — the function cannot introduce the workspace token for an authenticated actor. ⭐ **The lineage edge is `derives-from`, not `provenance`, deliberately** — the proposal episode already carries the vouch through `reconcileFacts`, and a session episode feeding the corroboration count too would make one act of testimony count as two distinct sources, the exact inflation #5487 exists to discount. A new evidence path must ask which counts it feeds, not just which edges it draws. The confirm token binds the session in its single hash, and the ADR-0020 boundary holds: the three durable modules keep zero brain references, with a scan to keep it that way.

**Shipped 2026-08-28 — the hero becomes the five-beat scroll story, backported from the live artifact** (no issue; PR [#5512](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5512)) — iteration on the published artifact outgrew the repo: titan → descent → the globe unrolling flat into a chart of sources → regions condensing into plates → the Coverage Plate itself as the product draws it (#5422), on a 560vh scroll track driving a sticky stage. The template is backported so the repo is the source of truth again; the payload is untouched. ⭐ **Verified as a backport, not a rewrite**: the three bundle, GLB and all four textures in the live page are sha-identical to `src/`, and `build.sh` over the extracted template reproduces the published page byte-for-byte — the build was structured so artifact iteration could only touch the template, and the round-trip proves that held.

**Shipped 2026-08-28 — the review gate `proposeFact` exits through** ([#5483](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5483); PR [#5510](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5510)) — the draft→published transition for proposal-class facts: a new *class* through the existing review gate, not a new gate, with the single-writer guarantee untouched. The genuinely missing piece was the origin label — `BRAIN_PROPOSAL_PRODUCER` in `@useatlas/schemas` as the one spelling of the `provenance.producer` discriminator (`source` alone cannot carry it: a correction-authored replacement is `human` too), rendered as a Proposed badge and a testimony note in the queue and sheet. The four §T9 exit properties are pinned against real Postgres: an ordinary member's proposal reaches the reviewer's queue labelled and counted; approval publishes; rejection is `retract` — an `invalidated_at` tombstone plus a correction episode recording the reviewer's reason verbatim, never a silent delete; and ⭐ **widening is the reviewer's act at the gate and only there** — a proposal corroborating a narrowly-granted draft moves `visible_to` not one byte, the coming widening is disclosed to the reviewer before it happens, and publish performs it with the pre-widening grant captured.

**Shipped 2026-08-28 — better-auth 1.7: the last two advisories closed by a SCIM redesign and an OAuth resource-binding migration** ([#5493](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5493); PR [#5505](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5505)) — `bun audit` 2 → 0, and #5493's "not a version bump" finding held: core, SCIM and oauth-provider move together or not at all. The high-severity advisory closes *structurally* — 1.7 withdraws the four HTTP management routes our `providerOwnership` patch held shut (the operations survive as `serverOnly` endpoints with no path), so the patches retire and the same `canGenerateSCIMToken` predicate moves outward to `admin-scim.ts`, in front of an endpoint no longer reachable from the wire. ⭐ **Seven breaks that typecheck clean and fail in production, every one found against real Postgres** — including `tsc` proposing `verifyJwsAccessToken` as the rename fix, whose scope-free signature silently stops enforcing `mcp:read` with green types *and* green tests, because the 403 branch stops being reachable. ⚠️ **The fail-open worth reading twice**: narrowing `isSCIMProvisioned` to the new `scimUser` projection fails OPEN mid-upgrade — `false` means "mutation proceeds", so every already-provisioned user reads unmanaged until the IdP re-syncs; both models are consulted, each guarded on its own table existing. Migration 0209 carries connections and **rotates** credentials by decision: decrypting live customer secrets inside a migration to rewrite them under a new scheme fails silently, re-issuing through the audited route does not. Still open from the PR: the staging soak (AC 6), and the new `scim*` tables sit outside the GDPR purge scope — the #5160 shape again, filed as [#5515](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5515).

The headline drift: stability.mdx was falsified by the window — seven documented data-plane endpoints removed under ADR-0034/0035 with no acknowledgment; it now carries a removals ledger, and the new `scripts/check-openapi-removals.ts` fails CI on an unledgered data-plane removal against the last `v*` tag. The other gate: `check-docs-links.ts` now fails a self-hosted-tree page hard-linking a root-mount-only path (26 had accumulated silently); #5508 finished the follow-up — eleven audience-branched pages moved to `content/shared/`, each branched against verified behavior rather than assumption, which itself caught two stale claims. **Shipped 2026-08-27 — `proposeFact`: the draft-only agent write ADR-0036 §T7 has always named** ([#5482](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5482); PR [#5504](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5504)) — asserts a net-new claim, which all four correction verbs structurally cannot (they presuppose an existing tier-2 fact). A new entry point, not new machinery: `lib/brain/proposal.ts` enters through `reconcileFacts` as the fourth writer and issues no `brain_facts` SQL at all, so draft-only is structural rather than asserted — the seam never names `status`, migration 0180's `DEFAULT 'draft'` applies, and `check-brain-fact-promotion.sh` refuses a second writer. It sits behind #5496's confirm gate, and not as belt-and-braces: "draft-only" is true of *novel* claims and false of *agreeing* ones, which write an unreviewed `provenance` edge that feeds the corroboration count and resets the staleness anchor with no draft for anyone to catch. ⚠️ §T9 lock 3 applied literally (no session episode exists to inherit from) would truncate the disclosure decision — `widenGrantFromEvidence` unions evidence grants at the review gate, so a narrowly-granted proposal later published becomes a company fact only its author and reviewer can read. The grant is `org`, stated on the confirm card, recorded as a dated ADR amendment: a lock written for one mechanism does not transplant to another by its letter.

**Shipped 2026-08-27 — the 3D Atlas hero: bronze titan on brand tokens** (no issue; PR [#5503](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5503)) — `brand/atlas-hero-3d/`, a self-contained WebGL page emitted as one file by `build.sh`; 1.9 MB of brand assets, no product code touched. The purchased mesh ("Atlas statue", Fab Standard Licence, Personal tier) is deliberately not in the repo — redistributing the source asset from a public AGPL repo would purport to grant downstream rights we don't hold — so what's committed is geometry-only glTF plus optimized maps, with provenance, re-download links and the licence-threshold caveat in the asset's README. ⚠️ The Artifact CSP fails `blob:`/`data:` texture loads **silently** — the mesh arrives, every map is missing, and metalness-1 renders as a blank mirror ball that reads as a lighting bug; textures live outside the GLB and decode via `createImageBitmap` from a `Blob` with no URL of any kind, and the page reports a failed map rather than rendering silently wrong.

**Shipped 2026-08-27 — `correct_fact` stages for confirmation instead of firing in the agent loop** ([#5496](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5496); PR [#5501](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5501)) — the write now runs only at `POST /api/v1/brain-corrections/confirm` after a human clicks Confirm, and that endpoint re-runs the whole gate rather than trusting the staged payload; previously the human's intent to make *this* correction was assumed by a sentence in the tool description — the exact hole #5485 recorded. #3007's confirm mechanism moved to `lib/confirm-token.ts`, both gates specializing it by `typ` domain separator, tested in both directions — one signing key and one nonce store mean the separator is all that separates them. The widget loses the verb deliberately: `buildRegistry({ rendersConfirmations })` defaults false, so staging a correction nobody can confirm (#5495's shape) is unreachable, and the divergence `registry.ts` predicted got its split signal instead of a re-globalized tool. ⚠️ `x-atlas-surface` is a UX capability hint, never authorization — forging it yields a staged correction and nothing else, stated at every site so nobody grows a permission check on it.

**Shipped 2026-08-27 — corroboration weights distinct sources, not distinct episodes** ([#5487](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5487); PR [#5499](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5499)) — the reviewer's number was `COUNT(DISTINCT episode)` rendered as "Sources" in five surfaces, so two messages from one person read as two corroborating sources over one voice. The unit is now the episode's authoring principal, and only when it names a person; machine principals count per-episode, because the warehouse producer stamps every snapshot with one constant actor, and a principal-keyed count with no machine arm would report one corroboration for a workspace's entire warehouse history, permanently. ⭐ **The weighting changed; the evidence did not** — `INSERT_PROVENANCE_EDGE_SQL` is byte-for-byte unchanged, after the first design (suppress the duplicate edge) was killed by two independent findings: a real-Postgres CI suite the local pre-flight structurally cannot run, and a spec trace showing the re-vouch verbs would lose their only observable effect, the decay-anchor reset. The edge set is also the decay anchor, the grant-widening input, and the audit record. Generalizable: fix a counting bug in the count, never by suppressing the evidence the count reads.

**Shipped 2026-08-27 — the plugin SDK gains a Python execution surface, implemented for e2b and daytona** ([#3414](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/3414), [#4665](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4665); PRs [#5500](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5500), [#5502](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5502)) — a BYOC org's `executePython` fell through the provider chain and ran on the operator's Vercel account in `iad1`: the tool that ships raw query-result rows bypassed exactly the provider the customer chose for region control. `createPython` presence is the capability; the host hands plugins its own wrapper source so the in-sandbox AST import guard has one copy instead of drifting ones; and a selected-but-broken provider now **fails closed** naming the package to upgrade, because silently demoting to the operator chain is the defect being removed. Neither provider exposes a per-sandbox host allowlist, so both declare `pythonEgressControl: "unsupported"` rather than letting an unenforced policy read as an enforced one. #5502 published both at 0.0.6 and wired `check-publishable-peers.ts` into their publish steps — run by hand before the SDK publish, it named the wrong-order failure nothing else in the pipeline would have said. ⚠️ Review caught per-request sandboxes never released on the happy path — every Python call left a live VM on the org's own bill until the provider's reaper arrived; a resource acquired per request needs its release on the same seam, in a `finally`. *(Superseded in part 2026-08-30 — [#5542](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5542): both SDKs expose per-sandbox egress control at the pinned versions, so "neither provider exposes a per-sandbox host allowlist" is no longer true; both plugins now enforce the bound and declare `pythonEgressControl: "enforced"`. Entry above.)*

**Shipped 2026-08-27 — REST writes gate on a surface that can confirm them** ([#5495](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5495); PR [#5498](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5498)) — exactly one thing in the tree renders the confirm-before-write banner, and it lives in `packages/web`; the embeddable widget hits the same route and resolves the same registry with no such card, so a widget user got *"This will permanently delete 3 people — confirm?"* and no banner to confirm with — and so did SDK, Slack, MCP and the scheduler. A surface now declares it can finish a write via `x-atlas-write-confirm-ui`; undeclared means the write is refused at the point it would stage — no confirm token minted, no nonce burned — ordered *after* the allowlist verdict so the refusal's advice ("run it from the Atlas web app") is never false. ⭐ **The default is the fix** — the gate is server-side and fails closed, so every `@useatlas/react` version already on npm is corrected without upgrading; and the header is a capability hint, not a security control, because `/confirm` re-runs datasource resolution, the allowlist and the single-use token server-side, so a client that lies only re-earns the dead-ended turn.

**Shipped 2026-08-27 — `/preview` and `/cardinality` resolve one predicate to one slot** ([#5466](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5466); PR [#5479](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5479)) — the cardinality preview keyed its blast-radius count with `identityKey(predicateSurface)` (normalization only) while the write keyed with `slotKey(surface, vocabulary.predicate)` (normalization **and** the alias closure). They agree for an unaliased predicate and diverge for an aliased one — and `/surfaces` offers aliased norms, so the divergent case was exactly what the picker put in front of an operator: a count for one slot beside a write that lands in another, which defeats the only property that justifies offering a retroactive flip.

⭐ **Resolved once at the boundary, and the old spelling dropped from the type.** `structurallyEmptyReason` and `planCounterfactual` each derived the key independently — free while the derivation was pure, and no longer free once it reads the store, because two reads straddling an approval gate on one slot and count in another. The internal `ResolvedRequest` carries `canonicalKey` and **not** `predicateSurface`, so the divergent form no longer compiles rather than merely no longer appearing. Generalizable: **when a derivation starts reading shared state, every independent copy of it becomes a race — delete the input, not just the duplicate.**

**Shipped 2026-08-27 — `proposeFact` is a second agent write, not the only one** ([#5468](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5468); PR [#5481](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5481)) — the M6 write-back route's `wayfinder:map`, recorded as a dated amendment on ADR-0036 §T9. The mechanism §T9 named as the agent's single write-back verb had **already shipped** as `correct_fact` (#4915) before M6 was scoped, so the ADR's own framing understated what the milestone inherits.

**Shipped 2026-08-27 — a gate for template-shipped deps, and how to read a preempted shard** ([#5421](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5421), [#5395](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5395); PR [#5490](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5490)) — a new API runtime dependency could reach a scaffolded project's *source* without reaching its `package.json`, and no gate noticed; and `api-tests` shard 1/4 is lost to runner preemption in a way a timings rebalance cannot touch, so the shard now reads as preempted rather than as a failure.

⚠️ **The better-auth 1.7 bump was attempted inside this PR and refused.** `^1.6.25 → ^1.7.1` is two migrations wearing one version number — a SCIM redesign and an OAuth resource-binding migration — and the peer pins make core, SCIM and `oauth-provider` move together or not at all. Split out with the full measured scope as [#5493](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5493) so it is not rediscovered.

**Shipped 2026-08-27 — gate decisions export as an evaluation corpus** ([#5335](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5335); PR [#5491](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5491)) — the schema already stored the pairs; this makes them readable as a corpus, via the `atlas-operator` `gate-export` subcommand.

**Shipped 2026-08-27 — the write-back entry gate is the human's act, verified server-side** ([#5485](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5485); PR [#5494](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5494)) — recorded as an ADR-0036 §T9 amendment beside #5481's block. §T9 lock 1's *"explicit-act-to-enter"* named the **agent's** tool call as the explicit act; it should be the **human's**, and server-verifiable. `correct-fact.ts:81` tells the model *"Confirm the user actually wants the brain changed before calling"* — **prose in a tool description, not a gate**; the only hard check is that the calling user holds owner/admin. Under prompt injection, the threat this was decided against, the agent's act carries no information, because a compromised loop calls the tool "explicitly" every time.

⭐ **Atlas already had the pattern, and `correct_fact` was the one side-effecting agent write exempt from it.** `lib/openapi/rest-write-confirm.ts` (#3007) stages an allowlisted write, returns `needs_confirmation` **without firing**, and executes only at a confirm endpoint that re-validates server-side and burns a single-use signed token binding the exact staged operation. Its stated purpose is verbatim this problem — making the human-in-the-loop guarantee *"server-verifiable"* against *"a replayed request, an XSS/CSRF against the SPA, **a looping agent**."*

⚠️ **"Draft-only" is not true of the corroboration path, and that is why the ENTRY gate carries the weight.** `reconcile.ts:951` deliberately corroborates a re-observed claim against the published fact rather than minting a draft duplicate. So `proposeFact` (#5482) would be draft-only for a **novel** claim and an immediate, unreviewed write for an **agreeing** one. Injected content that merely agrees is a slow poisoning attack raising no draft to reject and no tension to surface — worse alongside #5487's per-episode idempotence. An exit gate cannot absorb an entry threat it never sees. ⚠️ **This reorders M6**: the decision binds both verbs, so it blocks #5482. New order **#5485 (decided) → #5496 (build) → #5482 → #5483**.

⚠️ **Recorded as a capability removal**: the embeddable widget (`@useatlas/react`) hits the same `/api/v1/chat` and can invoke `correct_fact` today with no confirm card of its own. Registration narrows to surfaces that can confirm; the widget loses the verb until it learns the card.

**Shipped 2026-08-27 — `/ship`: issue to ready-for-merge in one lane** (PRs [#5480](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5480), [#5489](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5489), [#5492](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5492)) — a runbook that pins an issue, opens a fresh worktree, implements, opens a **draft** PR early so remote CI runs during the two-axis review, batches one fix pass over both sets of findings, then stops and asks. **It never merges**; fork PRs are refused outright. Filed as a skill rather than a twelfth `.claude/commands/` runbook because that file count is a gate-registered claim.

⚠️ **Two follow-ups, both from real incidents in the same week.** #5489: with four lanes live, one entered a worktree another session owned, branched over it, and deleted that branch on the way out, orphaning a commit (recovered — `1d965f0c0`). The cause was naming: four schemes, two directories carrying no issue number, **not one directory equal to its branch**, so no lane could answer *"is this issue already claimed?"*. Now one string names both (`<type>-<N>-<slug>`), a claim check runs before create, `.claude/worktrees/.lanes/<lane>.json` records the owning session id, and three prohibitions are absolute — never `git branch -d/-D`, never enter or remove a worktree you did not create, never improvise past a failed `worktree add`. **A failed add is the guard working.**

⭐ **#5492: a lane that cannot perform one of its own steps must say so at step 1, not step 8.** #5468's lane could not spawn the review sub-agents, self-checked instead, and reported the gap *after* the PR was ready to merge — the one moment that information is worth nothing. Step 1's stop table had four rows, all for spec-shaped problems, and none for a lane-capability one. The row is deliberately narrow: a standing guardrail against sub-agents is **not** this row, because invoking `/ship` *is* the user requesting the review. Generalizable: **a capability gap is a stop condition, and its value decays to zero the moment the work exists.**

**Shipped 2026-08-26 — the test guard's escape hatch stops granting itself** (PR [#5478](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5478)) — the project memory states the hatch as TWO conditions: *"if a full local run is ever genuinely necessary, **ask first**, and cap the workers (`bun test --parallel=6`)"*. `guard-bun-test.sh` enforced only the cap and silently converted "ask first" into a standing self-grant, on the ONE path in the whole guard that is permissive. `bun test --parallel=6 packages/` — the entire monorepo — was ALLOW: inside the memory's letter, and exactly the run that should stop to ask.

⭐ **`permissionDecision: "ask"` is real, and it is NOT a prompt everywhere — that second half is the finding.** The hooks reference documents `allow/deny/ask/defer` and *"`ask` prompts the user to confirm"*. But **measured in a live session on this box**: with `permission_mode: "bypassPermissions"`, a PreToolUse hook returning `ask` did **not** prompt — the command ran, no notice, no log line. A `deny` control on a sibling sentinel blocked in the same session, so the hook was loaded and firing; bypass swallows `ask` specifically. **Under bypass, `ask` IS `allow`** — and all three of this guard's recurrences happened in agent sessions, which is where bypass is used. A hatch built on a plain `ask` would have looked closed and been open in precisely the sessions that crashed the box.

So the hook reads `permission_mode` (a PreToolUse common input field — confirmed by dumping the hook's own stdin) and **allowlists** the modes: `default`/`acceptEdits`/`auto` ask, everything else — `bypassPermissions`, `dontAsk`, `plan`, an unknown mode, an absent field — denies. Unlisted means shut. The change is **monotonic**: every shape that was ALLOW is now ASK or DENY, so if `ask` turned out to be silently permissive in a listed mode too, the hatch would be exactly as permissive as it was before and never more.

⚠️ **An invalid `permissionDecision` fails OPEN**, which is why the value was verified before it was written: the reference says a parsed object that fails schema validation is *"a non-blocking error: the action proceeds"*. A typo on this path would have made the guard weaker on the one shape it exists to gate.

**Falsifiers, all observed:** 11 new cases red against the pre-change hook (each returned nothing and scored `allow`). Four mutations applied and observed: the harness verdict collapsed from three-way to `deny`-vs-`allow` → 5 red (an `ask` becomes indistinguishable from a grant); the mode allowlist widened to every mode → 6 red; the hatch reverted to an outright `continue` → 11 red; `[ "$cap" -le 6 ]` → `-le 7` → 1 red. **That last mutation SURVIVED the whole 41-case suite before this work** — the cap value 6 was not pinned by anything, `--parallel=32 <dir>` only catches a raise past 32 and `--parallel=8` denies for having no target. An invented cap of 8 is the mistake this guard's own history records, so the boundary is now load-bearing. 41 → 56 cases.

**Audit of the existing suite, since three of its falsifiers had previously passed for the wrong reason:** seven rules mutated, six reddened the case that claims them (`set -f`, the all-positionals quantifier, brace expansion, the `TEST_DATABASE_URL` deny, the max-cap `sort | tail`, the cap bound). One mutation initially "survived" and was **my own bad mutation, not a bad case** — a first-match-only `perl -0p` substitution hit the identical text in a comment eight lines above the code. Worth recording: on a file that documents its own rules in prose, a mutation harness that matches text can silently mutate the documentation instead of the rule and report the gate as unfalsifiable.

**Built in the same PR after Matt set the budget: the fleet ceiling.** `count_bun_workers()` counts processes where **`comm == "bun"` AND the cmdline carries `--test-worker`**, and denies when `live + cost > 12`. It sits **above every allowance** — `--changed`, named files and the capped hatch all `continue` out of the loop, so any of them placed above it would be a way to skip it. Cost is the declared `--parallel=N`, else 1; `--changed` is charged 1 deliberately, because charging it the 32 workers bun advertises would deny the sanctioned pre-flight whenever anything else was running, and a gate that cries wolf on the everyday case is one people route around.

**12 is Matt's number, given as *"I'd budget for 15 probably just in case maybe a few less"*** — the cautious end of his range, not a measurement, and the hook's comment says so in as many words rather than dressing it up. It has a clean meaning: at the sanctioned cap of 6, two concurrent breadth runs fit and a third does not. ⚠️ **Do not later cite the record as the authority for 12** — the memory names 6 as a *per-command* cap and says nothing about a fleet total. That circular warrant is the cap-of-8 mistake and it is one page above this one.

⚠️ **The predicate is "the executable is bun", NOT "the command line says worker", and that distinction cost a shell.** The Bash tool wraps every command in `bash -c '<command text>'`, so a cmdline-text scan counts the process running the scan: a `pkill -f -- '--test-worker'` during design killed this session's own shell (exit 144). Verified both directions — a shell whose argv carries the flag counts **0**, three real bun processes carrying it count **3**.

⚠️ **A fourth inert falsifier, caught by mutation rather than by review.** The case pinning that predicate used `bash -c 'sleep 30' '--test-worker'` as its fixture. **bash execs a lone simple command in place**, so that process is `comm=sleep, cmdline=[sleep 30]` — the flag is gone, and the case passed with the `comm` rule deleted. `bash -c 'sleep 30; :'` defeats the exec optimisation and keeps the argv. The suite now asserts its own fixture carries the flag, and the spawner blocks until the counter can see its workers and **fails loudly** rather than letting the dependent cases run on an empty box. **Two of the mutations in this session were themselves broken** — a first-match `perl -0p` that hit a comment instead of the code, and one where perl interpolated `${ATLAS_GUARD_WORKER_BUDGET}` as a perl variable and blanked the line. A mutation that "survives" is a claim about the mutation before it is a claim about the test.

**Known limit, stated rather than papered over:** only the parent `bun test` exists for the first moments of a run, so two invocations landing in the same sub-second can both see zero. This closes the minutes-apart case that actually happened, not the simultaneous one. A counter file would close it and was rejected — it leaks on the very crash the guard exists to prevent, and a leaked counter denies every future run, which is how a gate becomes something people disable. Counting real processes self-heals.

⚠️ **The residual hole moved, and it is now reachable.** The memory records fleet concurrency — three agents each at a legal `--parallel=6` is 18 workers — as out of reach for a per-command hook. **That is now measured false: PreToolUse hooks DO fire for subagent Bash calls, and DO enforce.** A subagent-issued sentinel was blocked by a `deny`, and the hook's stdin carried `agent_id`, `agent_type: "general-purpose"`, and the **same `session_id` as the parent**. A `pgrep`/loadavg or session-scoped concurrency check is therefore buildable. **Built here after all**, once Matt named the budget — see the fleet-ceiling paragraphs above.

**Shipped 2026-08-26 — the correction lane's anchor arm now needs the curated entry the sweep needs** ([#5467](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5467); PR [#5476](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5476)) — `correction.ts` hard-coded `predicateCardinality: "single"` on the claim a correction authors. That was correct against a **predicate** arm, where the tension scan reached only the slot the human corrected; the anchor arm ([#5438](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5438)) widened the scan to every live claim sharing the subject's whole-token prefix. **The reach moved, the hard-code did not**, and one spurious prod edge — `e78de65d`, a raise target flagged against a post-money valuation — exists because of it.

⭐ **The decision, re-derived from scratch after the original author's reasoning was lost: the verb keeps the EXACT SLOT and loses the ANCHOR ARM.** A human replacing `8M` with `10M` asserts *"this slot holds one value"* directly, by the act. They assert nothing about the subject's whole predicate fan, and [#5027](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5027) already settled that only a curated approved entry may call a predicate single-valued — `correction.ts` concedes the verb is a proposer that writes `pending`. So the gate goes exactly where the licence was never earned, and nowhere else. Generalizable: **when a scan's reach widens, every hard-coded assertion written against the old reach silently becomes a claim its author never made.**

⚠️ **The counter-argument the issue left open does not hold on this lane.** *An advisory edge is cheap, so keep the width* — except `TENSION_SWEEP_SQL` mints the identical pair the moment a human curates, so the wide reach buys **earliness only**, priced at a measured 1-of-3 precision corpus-wide and 0-of-1 here. The extract lane is untouched and its model-guess licence stands, carried as a named PRD limit.

⚠️ **`e78de65d` STAYS, untouched, until after ~2026-09-01.** It sits on `series a`, which is [#5425](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5425)'s condition-4 clock — the demonstration that a disagreement survives a week with nobody intervening. Writing to those rows destroys it and costs another week. It is advisory meanwhile: nothing merges, supersedes or ranks on it.

⭐ **Three mutation tables regenerated, and only suite sizes moved — not one kill count changed**, so nothing on this branch weakened a falsifier. Recording that the counts *held* is what makes a regeneration a check rather than a refresh. Also corrected in the same pass: a PRD sentence claiming the verb *"licenses the exact slot only"* (the gate binds the incoming predicate, so an approved predicate still flags the fan — bounded by curation, not eliminated), a test header claiming four cases over three blocks, and the pg-suite census, 89 → 90.

**Shipped 2026-08-26 — the corrector is named, and so is the machine that produced a claim** ([#5454](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5454); PR [#5475](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5475)) — `identityFor` rendered *"cannot name this person"* for a `user:` handle whose payload after the colon **is** a `"user".id` — a false statement Atlas could answer with a local join. Resolved at **read** time (option 2), with no stored row. This closes the hazard three entries below carried as live.

⭐ **An independent re-derivation reached the same decision and supplied the argument the original author never recorded.** Option 1 cannot work as specified without a second change nobody scoped: `AUTHORING_PRINCIPALS_SQL` composes `human:<id>` while `correctFact` stamps `user:<id>`, so the two handles never join — a capture-per-source fix writes a row nothing reads while the claim stays opaque forever. It also would not run on a deployment with no chat install, and ADR-0036 §T5's amendment does not reach a lane with no vendor and no directory call, so a stored row would widen a privacy reversal for nothing. Generalizable: **when a session dies and takes its reasoning with it, re-deriving the decision is what separates a right answer from a lucky one — the diff cannot tell you which it got.**

⚠️ **The `machine` arm was DEAD on every real row, and both its tests passed because they hand-built the handle.** It tested the ADR-0036 class off the handle's prefix, assuming `warehouse:system:warehouse-producer`; production writes the bare `system:warehouse-producer`, because `reconcile.ts` short-circuits on an explicit principal before composing a prefix and `system` is not an episode source. Every machine claim rendered opaque exactly as before the arm existed. Thirteen fixtures store the bare string; none store the prefixed form. ⭐ **A test that constructs its own input cannot discover that production constructs a different one.** The primary test is now the `system:` prefix — this codebase's spelling for a non-person principal — which generalises to all nine of them.

⚠️ **The derived read had no workspace scope, and its comment argued a containment it does not have.** `admin-migrate.ts` binds an imported bundle's provenance verbatim behind a check that only asserts a non-empty string, so `user:<foreign-id>` would have disclosed a name and email to any reader entitled to `actor` here. Now joined through `member`, pinned against a real database: a person in the global `"user"` table who is not a member is never named, with a real member beside them as the control — and dropping the join reddens it with that person's actual name. The agent contract taught three identity states and now teaches four; both shipped `brain-rows.ts` copies, including the published `@useatlas/react`, gained the arm they were missing.

**Shipped 2026-08-26 — seven holes in the test guard, and the one that took the box down was a quantifier** (no issue; PR [#5474](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5474)) — the guard from [#5459](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5459) was in place and still let the machine go down three times in one session, taking two background agents' worktrees with it. Seven holes found across three rounds of adversarial review, every one measured against the pre-fix version before being fixed. 41 cases, all failing directions observed against the commit before them.

⭐ **The one that crashed it: the guard asked whether ANY argument looked like a test file, not whether they ALL did.** One trailing `.test.ts` laundered two directory globs into a 32-worker run. Bare directory globs were already denied — the mixed argument list was the whole hole, which is why the fix is a quantifier. Generalizable: **an allowlist that matches on "any argument" is an allowlist with an escape hatch appended to it.**

⚠️ **Then the fix itself shipped holes, and the sharpest finding is that two of the author's own falsifiers passed without the rule working at all.** They used paths that do not exist, and the glob check only fired on globs matching *nothing* — `for tok in $args` is unquoted, so bash expands before the `case` arm sees a `*` and a glob matching real files arrives as literal filenames. `set -f` fixes it. ⭐ **A falsifier built from non-existent paths tests the guard's parser, not the guard.**

⚠️ **A quote-scrubbing "improvement" turned a DENY on `main` into an ALLOW.** The `\x01` quote-join paired two apostrophes across a newline and deleted the invocation between them. Reverted to per-line scrubbing, on the asymmetry that decides it: **over-scrubbing is a false ALLOW, and that is the failure that costs the machine.** Four more: redirections counted as positional arguments, so `bun test one.test.ts 2>&1` denied — **a gate that cries wolf on the everyday case gets routed around**; a `TEST_DATABASE_URL` fix over-corrected and denied a single named pg suite, the exact command its own deny text instructs; a repeated `--parallel=` took the first match rather than the max; and brace expansion, carrying no glob metacharacter, became 500 files.

⚠️ **The cap is 6 — the only number the record names.** 8 was invented here and then written into project memory as though the memory sanctioned it. ⭐ **A circular warrant: a note that records your own guess, cited later as the authority for it.** Corrected in the same pass.

⚠️ **NOT gated, and stated rather than implied: concurrent agents each running their own capped suite.** Three at `--parallel=6` is 18 workers with every command individually legal. A stateless per-command hook cannot see it — **and that is the shape the incident actually turned on.**

**Shipped 2026-08-26 — the Coverage Plate: condition 6 at a glance, with the demonstration pinned so it cannot rot silently** ([#5422](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5422); PRs [#5470](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5470), [#5471](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5471), [#5472](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5472)) — `arms.tsx` already made condition 6's statement correctly. The plate is the bet that the same facts are legible **at a glance** to someone who has never used Atlas — which is condition 3, and [#5375](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5375) is its test. It **supplements** the arms rather than replacing them: statement → plate → arms, each quad an anchor into its own class card, so the one interaction lands on the sentences rather than on a second wording of them.

⭐ **It renders from counts, not from `arm.units` — and drawing from the unit list was the natural implementation and would have been a disclosure leak in a picture.** That array is clipped at `COVERAGE_UNITS_MAX` and holds only units a clause admitted, so a workspace whose mailboxes are all withheld would have rendered an **empty quad over a card reporting twelve**. A sounding is anonymous, so ADR-0041's two-clause label policy never applies to it, and counts are always disclosable. Generalizable: **a visual encoding inherits every disclosure rule of the field it reads, and none of the prose that would have explained the omission.**

⭐ **`not-surveyable` is drawn in the MARGIN, outside the neatline — not hatched.** Hatch means ground a survey could cover; `human` is *"correctly absent from every ratio, forever; not a gap"*, and hatching it would draw an affirmative refusal as a hole. AC6 holds in **every** visual encoding: no ring, no arc, no wedge, no fill fraction, no percentage. Tests assert the sheet is digit-free and arc-free, and that the page still contains no `%`.

⚠️ **Fade is NOT shipped, and that is a finding rather than an omission: it cannot be built on this wire without breaking ADR-0041.** Fade by evidence age contradicts the ADR in text — *"a source that hasn't moved is current, however old its newest evidence"* — so a quiet-but-current unit would dim to look exactly like a stale one, the collapse #5422 forbids. Fade by measured lag is honest, but `lagMs` exists **per named unit only**: withheld and clipped stale units would render at full brightness while named ones dimmed, **making the label policy visible as brightness**. So the three freshness states ship as three distinct marks, and a test asserts no gradient and no `opacity` on any mark. The field that would make the second route buildable is a lag distribution on `BrainCoverageFreshnessCounts` — named here, not added.

⭐ **The day-one state was built first and not padded, and it is the most persuasive render on the page**: one filled sounding in Chat, 281 hollow rings in Warehouse with nothing filled, two hatched quads, People in the margin. *The ground is enumerated and almost none of it is surveyed* — said better by the drawing than by any sentence on the page. The test asserts the day-one sheet draws exactly `7 + 281` marks.

⚠️ **The summary block had been stuttering in prod the whole time, and every existing test was green through it** — *"surveys 2 of 7 chat channels **of the channels** Atlas's chat credentials can see"*. Both strings are correct, and both read correctly in the detail block where they sit on separate lines; the summary concatenated one finished phrase onto another. ⭐ **The old tests checked each half separately, so asserting on the COMPOSED sentence is the whole fix** — and the second new regression checks for a noun repeating *across a connector*, because `channels of the channels` is two words apart and a back-to-back check misses it entirely. Both were verified red against the exact prior composition. `UNIT_CAPTION` is now derived from `UNIT_CAPTION_PARTS` rather than concatenated onto, byte-identical, so caption and paragraph can no longer drift even in principle.

⭐ **A legibility finding is invalidated by the VOCABULARY changing, not by the layout changing — which is what made the condition pinnable at all.** #5427's browser read closed at 12:41 UTC; #5470 merged a new claim-bearing layer onto that same page at 20:27 UTC, and the read was stale within eight hours with **nothing anywhere knowing**. *"Re-verify once the UI settles"* is not a plan, because the UI does not settle. So `PLATE_READER_VOCABULARY` and `PLATE_QUAD_RENDERS` are exported and pinned: green means a recorded finding still describes what ships, however much the page has been redrawn around it; red means re-run the demonstration before citing it again. Four mutations observed red — a renamed mark, two collapsed blank states, a fifth quad render and a sixth mark kind, the last two reddening the count check as well as the set check, which is why the count is there. Colours, geometry, spacing, legend order and every gloss are deliberately **not** pinned: **a gate that fires on ordinary polish is one that gets deleted.**

⚠️ **Two deliberate departures from the mockup, and the mockup file now says where it was overridden.** Forest, not the mockup's teal — ADR-0023 §4 commits this product to forest, *"never teal"* — with the single-hue ordinal ramp re-stepped onto the brand hue and revalidated in both modes; and no *Day one / Month six* toggle, because the product has one state, the real one, and a control that faked another would be the sample data AC4 forbids. The committed mockup's header had said *"where the two disagree, the ADR wins"* without saying **where**, which is the same failure committing the mockup existed to end.

**Shipped 2026-08-26 — the cost of arming the anchor arm, asked before it is paid** ([#5450](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5450); PRs [#5463](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5463), [#5465](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5465)) — `forecastTensionEdges` / `POST /api/v1/admin/brain-facts/tension-forecast`: the sweep's own statement with the INSERT replaced by a `count(*)` and a counterfactual predicate carried as a bind, so *"how many edges would approving `plan tier` mint?"* is a request rather than a paste into `psql`.

⭐ **The standing precondition #5450 wrote had no code to run.** Both measurements on this arc were taken by pasting `TENSION_SWEEP_SQL`'s CTEs with the cardinality gate replaced by `TRUE` — an unversioned second spelling of a rule the module owns, which the issue then made a *permanent* obligation. `tensionCandidateCteSql` is now the one spelling; `TENSION_SWEEP_SQL` is byte-identical to before, verified by capturing it pre-refactor and diffing. Generalizable: **a precondition that names a query nobody ships will be skipped or will drift, and an issue that writes one is asking for the query to exist.**

⚠️ **The spurious pair #5450 called "one approval away" was MINTED — by the correction lane, with no cardinality entry anywhere in the loop.** `correction.ts:2135` hard-codes `predicateCardinality: "single"` on the claim a correction authors, argued correctly for the predicate arm it was written against; with the anchor arm ([#5438](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5438)) that same scan reaches every live claim sharing the subject's whole-token prefix from a different episode. `has target raise of` has **no row at all** in `brain_predicate_cardinality`, and edge `e78de65d` exists. So the issue's reframing — *the bound is approved-predicate coverage, not history* — is right about the **sweep** lane and needs a second clause: **on the correction lane there is no coverage bound at all.** **Acted on 2026-08-26 (#5467).** The trade was re-made with the arm's current width in view, and it came out differently on this one lane: the verb keeps the exact slot, the anchor arm now requires the same approved entry the sweep requires. The extract lane is untouched and its model-guess licence stands, carried as a named PRD limit. `e78de65d` itself stays until after ~2026-09-01 — it sits on `series a`, which is #5425's condition-4 clock, so removing it now would destroy that demonstration; it is advisory in the meantime and costs a glance.

⭐ **All four pending warehouse predicates forecast ZERO, for a structural reason rather than an incidental one.** `plan tier`, `name`, `region` and `is active` each mint 0 read-only against prod, because the warehouse producer writes **one episode per run** and **one row per `(subject, predicate)` slot** — so the anchor arm's different-episode requirement and the exact-slot arm both come up empty. #5450's expectation that approving those *"arms the sweep across every warehouse subject at once"* does not hold on this corpus, and the bound `segmentation.ts` already names is doing more work in practice than it reads like.

⚠️ **One-of-three, then one-of-two, on 35 facts in ONE workspace with two producers is not a precision rate and does not generalize** to a corpus of any other size or shape. It is evidence of exactly one thing: the cost `segmentation.ts` named in advance is real and lands where it predicted.

⚠️ **`FORECAST_MAX_CONCURRENT` argued against a cap and then set one, in the same paragraph.** Its docstring rejected an advisory lock because it *"refuses the 2nd, 3rd and 4th of a legitimate four-predicate render"* — and capped at **2**, refusing the 3rd and 4th of exactly that render. The excess now queues (FIFO, bounded at 10s), matching the render semaphore the module already cited, and `forecast-busy` survives as the wait-expired arm so the bound stays reachable. ⭐ Generalizable: **the paragraph that names a failure mode is the one most likely to ship it**, because the argument has already spent the author's attention on it.

⭐ **Four checks that were not checking, all shipped green** — a tautological assertion comparing complement-set lengths (true for a regex matching nothing, while claiming to catch exactly that); a guard keyed on `code:` alone that could be silenced by *omitting* `code:`; a "cannot report `reconcile-lock`" test asserting a literal about itself; and four tests sharing one module counter with only an end-of-test assertion. Each is now mutation-verified. Generalizable: **a test that cannot fail is worse than an absent one**, because the absent one does not report coverage.

**Shipped 2026-08-26 — cardinality authoring and the tension sweep get surfaces** ([#5447](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5447); PR [#5464](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5464)) — the two operations that close the recognition loop were API-only. `POST /brain-vocabulary/cardinality` had no caller anywhere in `packages/web/src` and nothing called `POST /brain-facts/tension-sweep` at all, so **every prod write of a `single` entry — including the ones that demonstrated finish condition 4 — was a hand-written `fetch` from a browser console.**

⭐ **The In-force pane already rendered `entry.cardinality` as a badge, and that is what made the gap easy to miss: the surface displayed a state it could not author.** Generalizable, and the exact counterpart of the `supersedes` finding above: **a read path is not evidence of a write path, and a rendered field reads as a supported operation.**

⚠️ **An API asymmetry this works around and does not fix.** `/preview` keys its cardinality arms with `identityKey` (normalization only); `/cardinality` keys its write with `slotKey` (normalization **and** the alias closure). They agree for an unaliased predicate and diverge for an aliased one — and `/surfaces` offers aliased norms, because it groups by the norm of the observed *surface* with no closure applied. Both endpoints are correct in isolation; together they let a page show a count for one slot and write to another, which defeats the single property that justifies offering a retroactive flip at all. Resolved client-side with a cycle guard and a depth bound that *refuse* rather than answer, the fold disclosed and the resolved norm displayed. **The better repair is for `/preview` to apply the closure itself** — `packages/api` work, tracked at [#5466](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5466).

⭐ **Two review findings were this area's own invariant inverted — something unknown rendered as something clean.** `useAdminFetch` returns `data: null` for the whole initial fetch, not only on failure, and `/in-force` is several round trips where `/surfaces` is one. Picking a predicate quickly put a destructive *"could not read … unknown, not clear. Reload before deciding"* over a request that was in flight and about to succeed — with a remedy that restarts the load. Fixed by replacing `edges[]` + `vocabularyKnown: boolean` with a discriminated union, so the loading state is unrepresentable as a failure.

**Shipped 2026-08-26 — an answer that changed says so, in the answer** ([#5461](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5461); PR [#5462](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5462)) — the non-admin surface #5426 was originally filed for, built the day its blocker cleared. A reader asking an ordinary question whose answer changed is now told, in the answer, that it changed, what it said before, and who changed it — without visiting `/admin`, and without asking for history.

⭐ **The finding that decided the shape: `supersedes` had no read path at all.** All three references in the tree were write- or gate-side — the cardinality repeat gate, vocabulary supersession, insert-time dedupe. The edge that exists precisely to preserve a previous answer had never been read back to anyone. So this was never "expose an admin thing to non-admins": **there was no admin thing**, and the read is born non-admin. Generalizable: *a record can hold a fact perfectly and still answer nobody, and "the machinery exists" is the claim that hides it* — the same error that rated this condition "Yes at the record level" twice.

⭐ **There is exactly one non-admin path into brain data** — the `searchBrain` result on the chat stream — so `history` rides it as a sibling of `tensions`. No new endpoint, no new auth lane, no new page, and the embeddable widget gets it on the same terms. `api/index.ts` mounts no brain route outside `/api/v1/admin/*`, so a fetch-on-click design would have been web-only and would have failed *"someone asks a question"* for every widget and chat adapter.

⚠️ **"Who changed it" needed no `admin_action_log` join.** It is a re-read of the live claim's OWN provenance — the correction lane mints the replacement, so the corrector is its author, already on the wire and already gated by #4836. That is the 2026-08-26 census's falsified-prediction entry below paying off: because the correction lane attributes the fact it mints, the answer was already in the record.

⚠️ **`changedBy` discriminates on the replacement's `producer`, never its `actor`, and that is a correctness property rather than a nicety.** Two producers write `supersedes` — the correction verbs and the publish gate — and on the gate path the replacement's actor is whoever the NEWER claim was extracted from, a person who never touched the old claim. ⭐ Generalizable: **a field that answers one question is not a field that answers the adjacent one**, which is the same shape as *"who made this claim" ≠ "who changed this claim"* recorded below. Reading `actor` uniformly would render an accusation the record does not support, so the promotion arm names nobody and the schema's `z.strictObject` makes an actor on that arm unrepresentable.

⚠️ **The retracted predecessor is excluded from the COUNT, not merely from the content.** `retract` is the GDPR-erasure path (#4916), and a `priorCount` that survived an erasure would re-disclose the erased claim on the very surface built to show history — while being indistinguishable from a predecessor withheld by ACL. The walk stops at a tombstone rather than counting through it, pinned against real Postgres and mutation-verified.

⭐ **The half that satisfies the condition is the tool prose, not the card.** Most people read the sentence. `searchBrain`'s description now requires the change to be stated unprompted, pinned by tests the way #5458 pinned *"names neither verb"*. Generalizable for every surface on this board: **when an LLM is the renderer, the tool description IS the UI, and copy that is not pinned is copy that is optional.**

⚠️ **Reachable, not read.** The record holds exactly one changed claim, so this fires on one row in thirty. The next census should read `supersedes` count alongside how often `history.prior` was non-null on a SERVED page — if the first grows and the second stays at zero, the surface works and nobody met it, which is a different failure from the one this fixed.

**Fixed 2026-08-26 — the correction options render as two columns, not one sentence** ([#5426](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5426); PR [#5460](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5460)) — found by reading `/admin/brain/facts` in prod on `v0.2.21`. The feature worked in every respect the tests describe — published-claim title, plain-language options, withdrawal preselected, neither verb word in the copy — and each option rendered as two narrow columns. shadcn's `Label` base class is `flex items-center gap-2`, so the bold lead and its explanation became flex **items** rather than inline text.

⚠️ **No test was added, deliberately: every assertion in `correction-dialog.test.tsx` passes on the broken output.** Text content, accessible label and radio wiring were all correct; only the rendered layout was wrong. Asserting on utility classes would pin the implementation rather than the property, and still would not prove the sentence reads as a sentence. ⭐ Generalizable, and the same lesson condition 6 recorded the same day on a different surface: **a skipped pixel read is not a passed one** — this shipped with 7 green render tests and was visible in the first screenshot anyone took of the real page.

**Measured 2026-08-26 — the arc runs `supersede` for the first time, and condition 5's RECORD half now holds** ([#5426](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5426), [#5454](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5454)) — a human corrected a published claim through the new surface on `us` prod at 16:25:09Z. `brain_fact.correct` **0 → 1**, `supersedes` edges **0 → 1**, `valid_to` set **0 → 1**, `valid_from` set **22 → 23**.

```
6M   draft      invalidated_at 2026-08-03      <- retracted: unreadable, by design
8M   published  valid_to 2026-08-26 16:25:09   <- SUPERSEDED: still readable
10M  published  valid_from 2026-08-01 00:00Z   <- today's answer
```

⭐ **The clause that failed on 2026-08-25 now passes.** That census found the one claim in prod whose answer had ever changed was retired with `retract` — a tombstone every fact-serving read filters, `asOf` included — so *"can see the previous answer"* failed on the only claim capable of testing it. The `8M` row now carries `valid_to` and **no** `invalidated_at`, so an `asOf` read returns it. The finding was never that the machinery was wrong; it was that **the arc had never once run the verb that makes the past legible.** It has now.

⭐ **`valid_from` is `2026-08-01 00:00:00+00`, from a typed `2026-08-01`** — the date-only → UTC-midnight widening verified in prod rather than only in unit tests, and the first exercise of the valid-time axis on this pair, which the previous census recorded as NULL on both rows.

⚠️ **A prediction made hours earlier was FALSIFIED, and is kept rather than deleted because it set a priority.** [#5454](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5454) says actor-identity capture never reaches the correction lane, and this entry's own earlier note argued the new surface moved that from latent to exposed — predicting the replacement would render *"cannot name this person"*. It does not: the `10M` row carries `provenance.actor = user:3AaGbea…` with `source: human`, and the audit row independently names the correcting admin. **The correction lane attributes the fact it MINTS.** What has no answer is the **superseded** row — `8M` names its original Slack author and nothing on the claim records who retired it. So the durable form is the one #5426 recorded: **"who made this claim" and "who changed this claim" are different questions, and only the first is stored on the claim** — the replacement gets an author because it is a new claim, while the retirement leaves no mark on the claim it retired. **The rendering half is fixed as of 2026-08-26 (PR [#5475](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5475)); the second question — who RETIRED this claim — is still answered only from the purgeable audit table.**

⚠️ **The condition is still NOT met, and the gap is the whole original scope.** Everything above is admin-side and read from the database. Condition 5 says *"**Someone** asks a question"* — not an admin — and #5426's body forbids satisfying it with a history tab in `/admin/brain`, which would meet the machinery half that already held. The blocker the issue was re-scoped onto is cleared; the demonstration it was originally filed for is now unblocked and unstarted.

⚠️ **Two claims were retracted rather than superseded in the same sitting, and Atlas now has no published Business tier price at all** — `499 a month` (2026-08-03) and `599 a month` (2026-08-26), the second through the new dialog's withdrawal arm. ⭐ Generalizable, and it is the affordance question this surface exists to answer: **the dialog offers both answers, and a human still has to know which one happened.** Neither the record nor the UI can tell a claim that was never true from one that changed; only the person can, and the cost of choosing withdrawal is that the previous answer stops being readable. The two price claims also never collided — predicates `priced at` vs `is priced at`, different keys — which is [#5438](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5438)'s segmentation shape surviving in prod for 23 days.

**Measured 2026-08-26 — condition 5's clock starts: the surface is live in prod and the record still holds zero of what it needs** ([#5426](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5426); tag `v0.2.21`) — the correction surface deployed to all four prod services, verified per service by its **active deployment's commit hash** rather than by a health check. Census against `us` prod immediately after: **30 live facts** (22 draft, 8 published), **0** `supersedes` edges, **0** `brain_fact.supersede` rows in `admin_action_log`, `brain_fact.retract` unchanged at 4.

⭐ **Eight published claims now render a `Correct` button, and before this release it existed on no surface at all.** They are the rows where `canSupersede` is true — published, open window, not an observation. That makes criterion 3 *reachable* for the first time, and it makes this row a real baseline rather than a restatement: **any non-zero `supersedes` count from here came through the surface, because nothing else writes one.**

⚠️ **Two numbers from the 2026-08-25 census are corrected here, by measurement rather than inference.** The corpus is **30 live facts, not 34** — the earlier figure counted the 4 retracted rows, and both are true of different populations, but the live count is the one that governs what can be corrected. And **`valid_from` is set on 22 of 30**, so the original finding (*both Series A rows carry NULL*) is correct as written but must not be read as *"prod has never exercised the valid-time axis"* — it has, on most rows; the pair that matters has not.

⚠️ **The criterion must not be satisfied by a manufactured correction.** `Series A / has target raise of / 8M` is the natural test — the one claim whose value has already changed once, and whose retirement by `retract` is what made condition 5 fail. But superseding a claim that is not genuinely out of date would write a false value into the record to turn a criterion green, corrupting the very history this condition exists to make legible. ⭐ Generalizable, and it applies to every demonstration on this board: **a finish condition measured by writing to the record can be faked by writing to the record, so the demonstration has to be a real event or it is worth less than an open criterion.** A criterion that waits is a legitimate outcome.

⚠️ **And the release moved [#5454](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5454) from latent to exposed.** That issue is scoped on the `supersede` arm being *"unexercised in prod — latent, not realized"*, which was true only while nothing routed humans to it. The correction lane drops actor identity, so a claim Atlas can name renders *"cannot name this person"* — and a correction is the one write where *who changed this* is the entire point. The surface built to make the past legible feeds the path that cannot attribute it. **Fixed 2026-08-26 (PR [#5475](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5475)), at read time and with no stored row — see the entry at the top of this section.**

**Shipped 2026-08-26 — a reviewer correcting an outdated claim reaches `supersede`, and never learns the word** ([#5426](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5426); PR [#5458](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5458)) — prod had run `brain_fact.retract` **four times** and `supersede` **zero**, and that was not adoption drift. `supersede` was reachable only from the `correct_fact` agent tool: `POST /brain-facts/:id/correct` shipped with no web caller, so the only button in front of a human stamped the tombstone — and `retract` is the verb whose purpose is to make the past unreadable (`invalidated_at`, filtered by every fact-serving read including `asOf`, deliberately, per [#4916](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4916)). **The record could not accumulate legible history no matter what surface was built over it.**

⭐ **The surface already existed; only the ACTION was missing.** `facts/?status=published` had listed exactly this population since the Published chip shipped, and the two fields that decide admissibility — `status` and `validTo` — were already on the wire. So the fix needed no endpoint, no field and no admissibility service. Reading `correction.ts`'s refusals against the queue's own filters shows why: observations cannot reach this surface at all (`notAnObservationSql` sits ABOVE the status arm in `candidates.ts`), retracted rows are excluded by `invalidated_at IS NULL`, and what remains is **one** row state admitting both verbs — published, with no decided `valid_to`. Generalizable: **when a verb has a rich refusal set, the "which verb?" question is usually already answered by the record, and the real design question is the one case where it is not.**

⚠️ **`supersede` refuses ANY decided end date, a FUTURE one included** (`VALIDITY_ALREADY_CLOSED`) — a second arbitration of the same claim is the thing it must not permit. The obvious predicate, *"is the window open right now"*, offers the verb on a claim with a scheduled end and takes a 409; the test is **presence, not order**, and that arm is pinned.

⚠️ **The dialog kept one claim's answer on the next claim, and the failure was silent.** Radix fires `onOpenChange` only for changes IT initiates; the success path closes by nulling `target` from the parent, a controlled prop change that never invokes it, so the reset hung off that callback never ran. Correcting one claim to `10M` then opening a different claim left the changed arm selected with `10M` still typed — one careless confirm from superseding the wrong claim with the wrong value. Found in review, reproduced by test before the fix. ⭐ Generalizable: **a controlled-`open` dialog cannot hang its form reset off `onOpenChange` if anything but the user can close it**, and the stale state reads as a filled-in form rather than an error.

⚠️ **This makes `supersede` reachable, not chosen**, so #5426 stays open. Its third criterion wants a `supersedes` edge in prod arrived at *through* the surface; if the 4/0 split was habit rather than affordance, this will not move condition 5 and the next census will say so. Closing on a shipped affordance is the same mistake that rated the condition *"Yes at the record level"* to begin with.

**Shipped 2026-08-26 — the trust tier reaches a person, as a rendered label rather than the model's prose** ([#5451](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5451); PR [#5456](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5456)) — ADR-0036 makes the tier a permanent product invariant on *"every retrieval result AND every UI surface"*.
