The first clause held from M1; **the second was met by nothing** — `raw-episode` appeared **0 times** across `packages/web/src`, `packages/react/src`, the SDK and every plugin, and `searchBrain` fell through the chat surface's `default:` arm to a gray *"Tool: searchBrain"* box. The label reached a user only through generated prose, at the model's discretion, phrased differently each turn. ⭐ Generalizable, and `lib/brain/segmentation.ts` names the same shape one layer down: **an invariant delegated to model prose holds statistically and reports nothing on the turn it is simply omitted** — which is indistinguishable, from outside, from holding. `@useatlas/schemas/trust-tier` is now one table (four tiers, labels, plain-language meanings, ADR-0036's rank), in schemas rather than types for the reason `schemas/brain.ts` already gives, with a compile pin that fails if a `BrainResultTier` exists no surface can label. ⚠️ **No badge path renders nothing** — an unrecognized tier draws a loud *"unknown tier"* chip, because a badge that silently disappears reproduces this bug one wire change later.

**Shipped 2026-08-26 — every claim-vocabulary write leaves an audit row, and the cardinality flip records what it erased** ([#5448](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5448); PR [#5455](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5455)) — measured in prod 2026-08-25: `POST /cardinality` and `POST /tension-sweep` ran back to back as the same admin and **only the sweep left a row**. ⭐ **Attribution was not missing, it was SPLIT across two stores — and the split fell in the wrong place.** The sweep is advisory and additive; the cardinality entry is the input that *arms* it and is retroactively blast-radius-bearing, so the write with the larger consequence was the one absent from the log. All four writes on `/api/v1/admin/brain-vocabulary` now log, because every one changes *what would collide*. ⚠️ **`declarePredicateCardinality` is `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` on one row per `(workspace, predicate)`**, so a later flip overwrote `proposed_by`/`reviewed_by` and the earlier decision and its author were gone without trace — the same last-write-wins shape [#5424](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5424) found on `brain_facts.updated_at`, fixed the same way: a data-modifying CTE reads the prior row inside the write's own snapshot, so two concurrent curators cannot both audit the same already-replaced decision. `PriorCardinalityEntry` is three states rather than a nullable value, for the reason [#5440](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5440) gives one table over.

**Shipped 2026-08-26 — the whole-suite test shapes are refused at the tool boundary, and the guard denied its own commit twice** (no issue; PR [#5459](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5459)) — CLAUDE.md has long said remote CI on the PR is the gate and the local pre-flight is the cheap subset, but nothing enforced it, and two of the offending shapes are **not reachable by reading the command text**: `bun run test:api` and `bun run --filter <pkg> test` expand to a whole-suite run *through* the package script. `.claude/hooks/guard-bun-test.sh` refuses them, plus the bare run (one global shared across every file) and `--no-isolate` (`agent-compaction.test.ts` leaks module state across same-process runs today). A single named file and `--changed=<ref>` stay allowed.

⭐ **The interesting part is that it blocked itself.** The first version matched `.tool_input.command` directly — but that field carries the command verbatim, so a plain grep **cannot tell a MENTION from an INVOCATION**. It denied the commit that introduced it (the message names the command), then denied the patch that fixed that. Generalizable: **a PreToolUse guard that greps raw command text is matching a string, not an action, and its false positives land on exactly the people documenting the rule.** The fix scrubs data before matching — heredoc bodies first, since they may contain quotes, then quoted spans.

⚠️ **`.claude/rules/testing.md` and `docs/development/testing.md` both still said to run the full suite before a PR** — the exact shape the hook denies. Those lines were **already stale against CLAUDE.md**, which makes remote CI the gate and names the local subset; the hook only made the disagreement reachable. Both now say what CLAUDE.md says. `guard-bun-test.test.sh` carries **16 cases**, five of them mention-vs-invocation, each of which blocked a real command while this was being written — a hook nobody can exercise is one edit from silently inverting, and this one had already inverted once.

**Shipped 2026-08-26 — a human name on every authoritative claim, and the prod census that closes condition 2** ([#5440](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5440), [#5424](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5424); PRs [#5452](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5452), [#5453](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5453); tag `v0.2.20`) — migration 0208's `brain_actor_identity` resolves a vendor handle in three states: `atlas` (a **live** join to `"user"`, so a rename reaches every surface with no re-ingest and nothing is snapshotted), `directory` (a **dated** snapshot, inverting that rule because for someone who has left both the vendor and the company a name captured at ingest is the only record that will ever name them), and `opaque` (a **positive** record that Atlas looked and could not name this person, which is why it is a stored row rather than an absent one). Capture rides the audience sync and is bounded to principals who **authored an ingested episode** — people who spoke into the Atlas, not a copy of the customer's directory, which ADR-0036 refuses by name. The name renders inside the existing `visible: true` attribution arm, so [#4836](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4836)'s gate covers it structurally rather than by a second check.

**The census, us prod, 2026-08-26 after `v0.2.20` deployed 0208** (boot log: `Applying migration 0208_brain_actor_identity.sql` → `applied=1 total=209`; two audience-sync cycles since, both `authors=2 atlas=2 directory=0 opaque=0 refused=0`). Corpus unchanged from #5424 — 34 facts, one workspace:

| scope | facts | named live | named snapshot | opaque | no identity row |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **published (authoritative)** | **8** | **8** | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| published (retracted) | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 2 |
| draft | 22 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 20 |
| draft (retracted) | 2 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

All 8 authoritative claims: **Matt Sywualk / matt@useatlas.dev**, source `slack`, dated 2026-08-03. Person, source and date on every one, with the name coming from the live join rather than a snapshot. **Condition 4's attribution clause clears with it** — the observation pair now reads as a named disagreement (Matt $25M against Gregg $30M), which was the failure its own comment pinned. The read was read-only, so the condition-4 window to ~2026-09-01 is intact.

⭐ **An issue's stated CAUSE can be wrong while the defect it reports is real, and the wrong cause is the part that propagates.** #5440's condition-4 comment asserted both actors were *"Slack users with no Atlas account, which is this issue's own measured majority case"*. Both resolve to Atlas accounts and took the `atlas` arm immediately. The defect was real and the fix holds — but that reading is what sized the whole three-state design and what the prediction two blocks down was built on. Generalizable: **a symptom verified in prod does not verify the mechanism offered for it**, and a design scaled to the mechanism inherits an error the symptom never had.

⚠️ **The `directory` arm is unexercised in prod — 0 rows — and this read could not exercise it.** Every author in us prod has an Atlas account, so #5440's AC1 (*a claim authored by someone with no Atlas account shows a directory-sourced name, labelled as a snapshot with its date*) is demonstrated **by test only**. The one unresolved principal is `USLACKBOT`, a bot that authored nothing. The majority case the three-state design exists for is the case prod has no instance of, so *"condition 2 demonstrated"* means demonstrated on the `atlas` arm, in one region, with two human authors. Stored `opaque` is likewise unexercised; only the absent-row path was observed.

⚠️ **Capture never reaches the correction lane** ([#5454](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5454)) — `audience/sync.ts` calls capture with `source: SLACK_HISTORY_SOURCE`, hardcoded, so `human`-source episodes get no row and `identityFor`'s `?? OPAQUE_IDENTITY` renders them *"cannot name this person"*. The handle is `human:<atlasUserId>`: the payload after the colon **is** a `"user".id`, nameable by a local join with no directory call at all. Latent, not realized — all four `human` episodes in prod are `retract` and mint 0 facts, reproducing #5424's control 2 unchanged — but a `supersede` mints. ⭐ Same shape as #5424's region-import finding: **not a broken gate, an unreached one.** Note it also defeats the usual reassurance that correction attributes well; #5424 measured it as the best-attributed path in the product, and both of those attributions still hold. What regresses is the *rendered* identity, and only since a rendering capable of saying "cannot name" exists. **Fixed 2026-08-26 (PR [#5475](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5475)): resolved at READ time by a local join, so no capture row was added — and the re-derivation found that the capture-per-source fix this bullet assumed could not have worked, because `AUTHORING_PRINCIPALS_SQL` composes `human:<id>` while `correctFact` stamps `user:<id>` and the two never join.**

⚠️ **A machine renders as an unnameable person.** `system:warehouse-producer` has no row and so renders `opaque` — asserting Atlas looked for a person and could not name one, where there is no person. The three states have no arm for *"this actor is not a human"*, and a reader cannot tell a machine producer from an author whose capture has not run. Out of scope for *authoritative* (all 22 warehouse claims are `draft`, refused at publish by `OBSERVATION_NOT_PUBLISHABLE`), so it costs nothing today; recorded because the guard that keeps them non-authoritative landed 2026-08-21, after two of them had already been published.

**Recorded as NOT established:** that the attribution is **true** — this is presence and resolution, not verification, and nobody checked that `slack:U0AQW6KF2EM` said what the claims say. Nothing here speaks to **eu or apac**. Scale is unchanged at **34 facts, one workspace, two human authors, one source vendor**.

**Measured 2026-08-25 — condition 4's clock starts, #5000's close is NARROWED not removed, and the prod pair's model gate was open the whole time** ([#5425](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5425), [#5000](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5000), [#5438](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5438)) — condition 4 is the only wall-clock item on the [Finish Conditions board](https://github.com/orgs/AtlasDevHQ/projects/3): *"a week later it has still picked neither, and nobody had to intervene to keep it that way."* Today is criteria 1–3 and the start of the clock; the verdict is due ~2026-09-01.

### Criterion 1 — the qualifier was NARROWED, twice, and never removed

The [PRD snapshot](../../docs/prd/company-atlas.md) rated condition 4 *"**Yes**, for claims that are recognized as contradicting. Recognition itself is the arc's open bug"*, and #5425 asks whether #5000 closing removes that. **It does not, and the reason is on #5000's own close comment.**

#5000 set itself a prod-verification close condition — *"the live `Business tier` 499/599 pair acquires an `in-tension-with` edge"* — and closed 2026-08-12 without meeting it, by explicit decision, on the demonstrated *mechanism* instead. `d947a3cd` (`Business tier` / `priced at` / `499 a month`) carries `invalidated_at = 2026-08-03 15:25:27+00`: it was retracted ~35 minutes after ingest, on the day the bug was filed, so there was no live pair left to edge and `authorAliasEdge` refused with `empty-population` — correctly, since ADR-0037's rule is that a vocabulary decision with no population rots silently. Verified still true in prod today: `d947a3cd` invalidated, `219f5a09` (the `599` claim) live, no edge between them.

⭐ **An issue that closes on a mechanism rather than on its own stated evidence condition does not retire the qualifier the evidence condition was written to test.** The stand-in was a staging rehearsal, and #5000's closing comment already records that it *"was **not** faithful on the axis that decided prod"* — staging's `499` row was published where prod's was a retracted draft. So what closing bought was: the machinery exists and the authoring surface exists. What it did not buy was any evidence about prod, and the qualifier simply moved — from *recognition is exact-string and broken* to *recognition works once a human authors an alias and a cardinality entry*.

**#5438 then falsified that narrowing too**, three weeks later, on a pair **no vocabulary entry can reach**: the extractor segmented the two sentences differently, so an alias at the predicate leaves the subjects apart and one spanning both positions is what ADR-0037 §6 forbids. That needed machinery, not authoring. And after #5438 recognition is still partial by construction — the anchor arm is a whole-token prefix test, so `Series B` against `the round` remains silence. **Narrowed twice, removed never.**

### Criteria 2–3 — the pair is live in prod, from two people, and was NOT recognized

The #5438 fixture pair, posted through the real Slack ingest path and still live in `VgazIn9V…`:

| id | subject / predicate / object | subject_key | episode | actor | ingested |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `eb2f9afb` | `Series B` / `target raise` / `$25M` | `series b` | `dd0087e3` | `slack:U0AQW6KF2EM` | 14:09:29Z |
| `547c7e55` | `Series B fundraise` / `has goal of` / `$30M` | `series b fundraise` | `0fe47d2d` | `slack:U0BT8MMFANL` | 14:09:31Z |

Both `draft`, both live (`invalidated_at` and `valid_to` null on each), different episodes, different authors, messages 31 seconds apart. **Neither picked** — and, until today, **neither surfaced**: no `in-tension-with` edge existed. Prod's only such edge was still `8934f202`, the Series A pair from 2026-08-03 whose rival arm was retracted the same day.

⭐ **The largest named blocker was not the blocker here, and the record proves it: `cardinalityHint: "single"` is stored in `provenance` on BOTH facts.** The PRD calls the extractor's cardinality guess *"the largest remaining blocker"* for condition 4 — the rival scan runs only for a claim labelled `single`, against a prompt that says *"when unsure answer 'multi'"*. For this pair the extractor answered `single` on `has goal of`, so the gate was **open**, and the two claims still never met because both key arms diverged before any matching rule ran. The blocker was segmentation alone. That does not make the model gate safe in general — it makes this pair a case where it happened not to bite, and the distinction was only visible because the hint is persisted.

⚠️ **And the fix was not running where the evidence lives.** PR [#5442](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5442) merged 2026-08-25T17:46Z; the pair was ingested at 14:09Z; `origin/prod` and all four Railway prod services were still serving `3e16d54a2` (v0.2.18) until `v0.2.19` was cut today. Reconcile only ever wires a tension edge for a claim it is *creating*, so the ingest lane could not have reached these rows even after the deploy. **"The fix shipped" and "the fix is running where the pair is" are different statements**, and #5438's own ROADMAP entry did not distinguish them.

### The reach, measured against prod read-only before any write

The post-#5438 candidate scan, run by hand as a `SELECT` against us prod with the cardinality gate lifted, so both the target pair and the warned spike could be counted without minting anything:

| newer | older | arm |
|---|---|---|
| `547c7e55` `series b fundraise` / `has goal of` / `$30M` | `eb2f9afb` `series b` / `target raise` / `$25M` | anchor |
| `219f5a09` `business tier` / `is priced at` / `599 a month` | `5d1090e4` `business tier` / `discounted for launch` / `no` | anchor |
| `b568b707` `series a` / `has target raise of` / `8M` | `124734f9` `series a` / `has post money valuation of` / `30M` | anchor |

**Three pairs over the whole corpus, every one of them anchor-only, and two of the three spurious** — exactly the shape `segmentation.ts` predicts by name (*"a subject carrying several `single`-cardinality predicates will see its claims flagged against each other across episodes"*). One-in-three precision on a 34-fact corpus is not a rate that generalizes; it is a demonstration that the predicted cost is real and lands where predicted.

⭐ **The warned one-off review-queue spike is bounded by CURATION, not by history — which is the opposite of how it reads.** The PRD warns that the first sweep after #5438 *"mints anchor-only advisory edges across ALL of history at once"*. True in principle, but `TENSION_SWEEP_SQL` gates every candidate on `cardinalitySingleSql("a")`, which requires an entry that is `single` **and** `status = 'approved'`. Prod holds **4 cardinality rows, all `pending`, zero approved**, all four proposed by `warehouse:v1` and never reviewed — and neither `target raise` nor `has goal of` has a row at all. So the sweep's blast radius is not the corpus, it is *the set of predicates a human has approved*, and on a workspace that has never curated it is empty. The spike is real for a workspace that has curated broadly, and is one edge here.

⚠️ **The gate is on the NEWER side only.** `cardinalitySingleSql("a")` reads `a`, and `(b.ingested_at, b.id) < (a.ingested_at, a.id)` makes `b` the older row — so it is `has goal of` (the `$30M` claim, ingested 1.2s later) that must be curated. Curating `target raise` instead sweeps nothing, silently, and a `{minted: 0}` is documented as not identifying a cause.

### The clock started 2026-08-25T19:36:30Z

`v0.2.19` was cut and rolled out first (prod `api` SUCCESS at `af18a2209`, the v0.2.18 deployment `REMOVED`). Then two authenticated admin acts, both as `admin@useatlas.dev`:

| | value |
|---|---|
| cardinality entry | `has goal of` = `single`, **`approved`**, `source_class = human`, `proposed_by` = `reviewed_by` = `5GbsmPM72gWL2Npad17Vf7uPD4S5sJOs`, at 19:36:15.182953Z |
| sweep | `{minted: 1, truncated: false}` |
| **the edge** | **`fa9935b7-028e-42eb-b079-ea3dc984adee`**, `in-tension-with`, `547c7e55` → `eb2f9afb`, **19:36:30.598834Z** |
| audit | `brain_fact.tension_sweep`, `actor_email = admin@useatlas.dev`, `{"minted": 1, "workspaceId": "VgazIn9V…"}` |

**Exactly one edge minted, and it is the predicted pair** — the read-only forecast said one, named which, and the sweep agreed. Both facts re-read after the sweep are still `draft` with `valid_to` and `invalidated_at` null: **the write was additive and no fact row was touched**, as the route documents.

⚠️ **The cardinality write emits NO `admin_action_log` row** — only the sweep audits. Its attribution lives on the `brain_predicate_cardinality` row's own `proposed_by`/`reviewed_by`. Not wrong, but it means "who curated this predicate" and "who swept" are answered from two different places, and only one of them is the audit log.

⭐ **Neither operation has a UI, and both are what #5000's close leaned on.** The web app calls 7 of the 8 `brain-vocabulary` endpoints — `author`, `decide`, `in-force`, `pending`, `preview`, `remove`, `surfaces` — and **not `/cardinality`**; it never calls `/tension-sweep` at all. So the two acts that actually close the recognition loop for this pair were reachable only by hand-written `fetch` against the API origin. #5000 closed on *"#5025's surface exists"*; the surface exists for aliases and for the pending queue, and the declaring-cardinality half and the look-again half are API-only.

### Criterion 3 proved on the SURFACE, not inferred from the tables

`GET /api/v1/admin/brain-facts?tension=true` after the sweep returned `"total": 2`, `"tensionsTruncated": false`. Both facts appear in their own right and each carries the other inside `tensions[]` with opposite `edgeDirection` — `loadTensionClusters`' both-directions walk doing what its docstring promises, since *"a `from`-only walk would hide from the reader exactly the older claim whose trust is now in question."* Each `tensions[]` entry carries the counterpart's own `provenance.attribution` (`visible: true`, actor, sourceId, occurredAt), re-decided per counterpart row rather than inherited, plus the episode body — so a reviewer reads the two sentences that disagree. `status: "draft"`, `validTo: null`, `invalidatedAt: null`, `promotionBlock: null` and `corroborationCount: 1` on both: **no rank, no score, no order favouring either.**

⭐ **`updatedAt` on both rows is still `2026-08-25T14:09Z`** — the ingest time, unmoved by a sweep at 19:36. Independent confirmation that the edge write touched no fact row, from a field nobody set out to use as evidence. The same field #5424 found is last-write-wins and useless for recovering a publisher; here its *not* moving is the whole point.

### What makes the week meaningful, checked in the shipped code rather than hoped

Two structural facts, because *"nobody had to intervene"* is worth nothing if something automatic was going to act anyway:

- **Nothing re-runs the sweep.** `tension-sweep.test.ts` mechanically asserts the admin route is its ONLY caller and fails if it is reachable from `scheduler/`, `registerPeriodicFiber` or `boot` — ADR-0037 §7 refused an unattended autonomous writer of `brain_edges`.
- **The observation reaper cannot touch this pair.** `observationSql` matches `provenance->>'source' = ANY(warehouse sources)`; both facts carry `source: "slack"`, `producer: "extraction:v1"`, so they fail that arm and the warehouse-episode arm. The first production DELETE against `brain_facts` is fenced away from them three ways.

**Re-read on ~2026-09-01**, needing no rediscovery: facts `eb2f9afb-2dfa-40fb-be83-7a2bcc740b20` and `547c7e55-489d-41d1-a722-92221cfcc2c6`, edge `fa9935b7-028e-42eb-b079-ea3dc984adee`, workspace `VgazIn9VLcR1wkqouZxZ759cBEuvg0Nq`, region `us`. The condition holds if the edge still exists, both facts are still live with `valid_to` and `invalidated_at` null, and no `admin_action_log` row between 2026-08-25T19:36Z and the re-read touched either.

### Recorded as NOT established

- ⚠️ **The week has not elapsed.** Everything above is a point-in-time check, which #5425 says explicitly does not satisfy this condition. The verdict, the PRD's condition-4 line and the board row's final state all wait for the re-read. Condition 4 is **not** demonstrated yet.
- ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-08-26 — "attributed" now means a human name, and the stated cause of the failure was wrong.** As written on 2026-08-25 this read: *"Both actors are `slack:U0AQW6KF2EM` and `slack:U0BT8MMFANL`… not a vendor id to a person. **Condition 4 cannot be fully met while condition 2 is not**."* The blocker was real and is cleared — after [#5440](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5440) shipped in `v0.2.20`, the pair surfaces as **Matt Sywualk ($25M) against Gregg ($30M)**, both `atlas` state, named by a live join. ⚠️ **But the diagnosis was false and is kept here rather than deleted:** the same comment asserted both actors were *"Slack users with no Atlas account, which is this issue's own measured majority case"*. Both resolve to Atlas accounts and took the `atlas` arm on the first capture pass. ⭐ **A correct symptom does not make the cause behind it correct**, and this one sized a whole design (see the condition-2 block below).
- **The pair is flagged and will never be adjudicated, by design.** `object_cmp` is NULL on both — `$25M` refuses to parse because currency symbols are ambiguous — so it sits in the abstain band until a human settles it. That is correct behaviour and it also means **this demonstration exercises recognition and surfacing, not adjudication.**
- **The recognition was RETROACTIVE, not at ingest.** The sweep reached rows that already existed; the ingest lane has still never minted an anchor-arm edge in prod, and cannot for this pair. A demonstration that the ingest path recognizes a segmented-apart contradiction as it arrives is still owed.
- **A human authored the cardinality entry.** Criterion 2 forbids an *alias* for the specific pair and none was written — but *something* still had to say the predicate is `single`, and here it was a person, not the model. The extractor's own `single` hint is on the row and the sweep deliberately ignores it (#5027).
- **Only `us` was read.** Nothing here speaks to eu or apac, and `api-eu` was still building at the time of the sweep.
- **Two spurious pairs are now one approval away.** `business tier` and `series a` each sit on an anchor-only candidate pair; curating either predicate `single` mints an edge a reviewer must dismiss. The precision cost is not hypothetical, it is queued.
- **34 facts, one workspace, two producers.** One-in-three anchor precision at this size says nothing about a real corpus.

**Measured 2026-08-25 — condition 5's RECORD half does not hold, and the one claim in prod that could test it was retired by the verb designed to hide it** ([#5426](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5426)) — the PRD snapshot rates condition 5 *"**Yes** at the record level; not yet on any surface a non-admin sees"*, and #5426's first criterion gates the surface work on that being true: *"Establish first whether the record can actually answer 'what did this say three months ago, and who changed it' for a real claim in prod. If the record half is thinner than the snapshot claims, that is the finding and the surface work is premature."* **It is thinner.**

The census, us prod — 34 facts, oldest `2026-08-03`, so the corpus is **22 days old** and *"three months ago"* is not askable at all yet, independently of any of the below:

- `valid_to` set on **0 of 34**. `supersedes` edges: **0**. `brain_fact.supersede` in `admin_action_log`: **0 rows** — the only brain correction verb ever run on prod is `brain_fact.retract` (4).
- Exactly **one** `(subject_key, predicate_key)` group holds more than one fact: `series a` / `has target raise of` — `6M` (draft, created 14:50:01, retracted 15:25:16) displaced in substance by `8M` (published, 14:55:22), which carries an `in-tension-with` edge back to it.

⚠️ **The one answer that ever changed was retired with `retract`, not `supersede` — so the previous answer is not merely unsurfaced, it is unreadable through the temporal API.** `retract` stamps `invalidated_at`, and every fact-serving read AND-s `f.invalidated_at IS NULL` (`search.ts:490`, `candidates.ts:629`) — `asOf` included, and deliberately so ([#4916](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4916)), because retract is the route a GDPR erasure takes and *hiding history is what the verb is for*. `supersede` is the verb that stamps `valid_to` and writes the `supersedes` edge that would have preserved it, and **it has never run.** Condition 5's second clause — *"can see the previous answer"* — therefore fails on the only claim in the record capable of testing it, and fails by design rather than by omission.

⭐ **The third clause holds only OUTSIDE the brain, which is condition 2's defect one table over.** *"who changed it and when"* is answerable — `admin_action_log` carries the four `brain_fact.retract` rows, attributed to `matt@useatlas.dev` with timestamps — but **the fact row does not carry it**: `provenance.actor` still names the claim's ORIGINAL author (`slack:U0AQW6KF2EM`), never the person who retired it. The retractor is recoverable only by joining `admin_action_log` on `target_id` — a table that is per-org purgeable (`admin_action_retention_config`, nullable, **no rows in prod today so nothing is expiring**) and whose workspace-purge scrub **replaces `target_id` with a sentinel by design** ([`purge-scope.ts`](../../packages/api/src/lib/db/purge-scope.ts), `admin_action_log: anonymized`). Generalizable: **"who made this claim" and "who changed this claim" are different questions, and only the first is stored on the claim.** [#5440](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5440) is the first question; this is the second, and the `provenance.actor` field that looks like it should answer both answers only one.

⚠️ **`valid_from` is NULL on BOTH Series A rows**, so today's answer has no valid-time window either — an `asOf` read against this pair is answering from transaction time, not valid time. The bi-temporal machinery is present and correct; prod has never exercised the valid-time axis at all.

**The verdict #5426 asked for: the record half does not hold — two of three clauses fail — so the surface work is premature by the issue's own first criterion.** Nothing here is a defect in the correction layer: `retract` behaving as a tombstone is #4916 working as designed. The finding is that **the arc has never once run the verb that makes the past legible**, and a condition rated *"Yes at the record level"* was rated on the machinery existing rather than on the record containing an example.

**Measured 2026-08-25 — condition 6 holds at 0%, 1.4% and 29% coverage in the same prod, with the rendered page named as the one part NOT re-read** ([#5427](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5427)) — the condition claims a **standing** property (*"every part of that statement is correct… at 4% as clearly as at 80%"*) and [#5216](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5216) verified it once. This is the re-read #5427 asks for.

Prod state, us, 2026-08-25 — the coverage cycles ran at 17:59 UTC the same day, all three rows with `last_success_at` set, `last_error` empty and `degraded_arms = {}`, over 479 `brain_coverage_snapshot` rows:

| workspace | class | surveyed / units | |
|---|---|---|---|
| `VgazIn9…` | chat | 2 / 7 | 29% |
| `VgazIn9…` | warehouse | 4 / 281 | 1.4% |
| `HcaUY6…` | warehouse | **0 / 191** | 0% |
| `HcaUY6…` | chat | — | never enumerated |

⭐ **The low-coverage regime the condition is written for is the regime prod is actually in.** One read spans a real **0%** carrying a real dated denominator, a 1.4%, and a class with **no cycle on record at all** — the three shapes that a comfortable number would have hidden, and exactly what *"4% as clearly as 80%"* was written to cover. `email` and `transcript` hold zero snapshot rows in either workspace, so the never-enumerated arm is live in prod rather than only in fixtures.

Each of the six rendering rules in `arms.tsx` is stated in its docblock as a **DECISION, not styling**, and each is pinned by test: **91 green** across `coverage.test.ts` + `coverage-enumeration.test.ts` + `coverage-error-text-writers.test.ts` (70), `coverage-statement.test.ts` (15) and `admin-brain-coverage.test.ts` (6). The two load-bearing at prod's actual state are pinned by name — *"a class with no attempt on record gets no date at all"* (`HcaUY6`'s chat) and *"a MEASURED empty roster reads differently from never having looked"*, which asserts `not.toContain("0 of 0")` while still requiring the sentence carry its `as of` date. ⭐ **A real `0 of 191` is a number the page SHOULD print** — its denominator is real and dated, and suppressing it would be the dishonest move; what the page may never print is a *fabricated* denominator. The two failures are opposite and a single "hide zeros" rule would commit the second while fixing the first. `coverage.ts` additionally cross-checks the cycle aggregate against the roster rows and logs *"the ratio is not trustworthy"* rather than serving the flattering one.

⚠️ **One part was not re-verified at the time of that reading, and was named rather than assumed: the rendered page.** The reading above covers the data, the composition layer, and the rendering rules with their tests — **not** `/admin/brain` loaded in a browser as an admin, which is how #5216 closed. A skipped step is not a passed step, so it was recorded as open rather than folded into the verdict.

**Closed 2026-08-26 — the browser read was done across BOTH prod workspaces, and condition 6 holds in full** (PR [#5457](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5457)). `/admin/brain` on `app.useatlas.dev`, admin session, read as prose rather than as a checklist:

- **`VgazIn9…`** — chat `2 of 7`, warehouse `4 of 281`. ⭐ **1.4% is BELOW the condition's own 4% benchmark**, and it renders with its caption and date intact. `transcript` and `email` both read *"Never enumerated … so there is no denominator to show — not a zero"*, with **no date at all**.
- **`HcaUY6…`** — ⭐ **the real `0 of 191` IS printed**, carrying `of the entity–dimension pairs your semantic layer defines · as of Aug 26, 2026` and `191 are visible to Atlas and unsurveyed`. This is the failure a *"hide the zeros"* rule would commit while fixing the opposite one, and the page commits neither. The whole workspace is at total zero — 0 claims, 0 published, 0 channels, three never-enumerated classes — and the statement stays honest at every part.

⭐ **The refusal is in the COPY, not only in the code**, which is what makes it citable by a reader rather than only by a reviewer: *"There is no single coverage percentage here — channels, mailboxes and warehouse entities are not the same kind of thing, so any one number would need weights nobody measured."* And the direction of honesty is stated outright — *"connecting more can make a ratio go down — that is the number getting more honest, not worse"* — which is ADR-0041's forecast pressure answered in advance, on the page itself.

Three renderings the test-level reading did not surface, all load-bearing: **`People — Not a surveyable class`** (*"correctly absent from every ratio rather than missing from one"* — the one class where inventing a denominator would be easiest); **`unverified since … (this source publishes no activity metadata, so no lag can be measured)`**, which refuses to fabricate a staleness judgement rather than defaulting to `stale`; and the unsurveyed remainder named per-row as *"visible to Atlas, not in scope"* rather than omitted.

⚠️ **One blemish found, and it is condition 3's, not this one's.** The summary block concatenates each class's label and caption into one sentence and the unit noun lands twice — *"surveys 2 of 7 chat channels **of the channels** Atlas's chat credentials can see"*. Both strings read correctly in the detail block, where they render on separate lines, so neither is wrong; the concatenation is. **This condition claims *correct*, and it is correct** — legibility is [#5422](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5422), filed there rather than resolved here, per #5427's own warning against reopening the Coverage Plate under condition 6. The caption is load-bearing and pinned by test, so the fix composes the two strings rather than shortening either.

**Shipped 2026-08-25 — a contradiction the extractor segmented apart is recognized, and condition 4 gains its named limits** ([#5438](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5438), [#5425](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5425); PR [#5442](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5442)) — two people contradicted each other in writing, **in prod**, and Atlas did not notice. `TENSION_CANDIDATES_SQL` required `subject_key = $2 AND predicate_key = $3`, and the extractor had absorbed *"fundraise"* into one message's **subject**, so the pair diverged at *both* arms before any matching rule ran (`Series B` / `target raise` / `$25M` against `Series B fundraise` / `has goal of` / `$30M`).

⭐ **No vocabulary entry can reach this pair, which is why it needed machinery rather than authoring** — an alias at the predicate leaves the subjects apart, and one spanning both positions is what [ADR-0037](../../docs/adr/0037-claim-identity-in-the-brain.md) §6 forbids. Three other mechanisms were designed against the pair and **falsified against the shipped code first**; `segmentation.ts` records all three, including that `$25M` yields no comparable value at all and the pair is therefore flagged but never adjudicated. So the tension scan drops the predicate: `tensionReachSql` keeps the exact slot as one arm and adds *"same subject anchor, different episode"*. ⚠️ **The licence is the CONSUMER, not the pair** — tension is the only identity consumer whose errors are recoverable in both directions, so corroboration and supersession keep the exact slot and both halves are pinned in `identity-consumers-pg.test.ts`.

⚠️ **The widening had to be stopped from SUBTRACTING edges, which is the failure mode nothing would have reported.** `TENSION_EDGE_CAP` is 10 and both statements are `ORDER BY … LIMIT`, so the cap bites on a candidate set the anchor arm makes strictly *larger*: a subject anchor with more than ten live claims could fill the cap with newer anchor-only rivals and push out an exact-slot rival that had earned an edge before. **A missing advisory edge is indistinguishable from agreement** — which is the exact failure this issue is about, reintroduced by its own fix. `exactSlotFirstSql` ranks the exact slot first in both statements. ⭐ Generalizable: **widening a candidate set under a fixed cap is a silent narrowing of whatever the set already contained**, and the direction of the loss is invisible wherever absence and agreement render the same.

The cost is precision and it lives in the corpus rather than in a comment: `predicate-differs` (a tier's price vs its renewal price) now earns an advisory edge and moves to a new `anchor-flagged` relation whose other two cells stay false. `paraphrase-identity.test.ts` still forbids closing that pair by lexical proximity — what it forbids is the **stamp**, which did not move. Condition 4 in [the PRD](../../docs/prd/company-atlas.md) gains its named limits in full: **recognition is partial by construction**, it is gated on a model's `single`-cardinality guess, and the remainder still closes on human authoring. ⚠️ The widened reach applies to the admin tension sweep too, so **the first sweep after this ships mints anchor-only advisory edges across all of history at once** — bounded per fact, not per run.

⚠️ **And the mutation gate caught its own anchor going dead.** `identity-corpus.mutations.ts` anchored the *"TENSION_CANDIDATES_SQL repointed at the surface columns"* mutation on four literal lines, two of which this branch replaced with a `tensionReachSql(…)` call. The anchor stopped matching and the gate reported the mutation as **MEASURING NOTHING** — and for as long as that stood, nothing verified that the rival scan matches on slot keys rather than surfaces. Re-anchored on the two smallest unique spans, deliberately not spanning the `ORDER BY`, since `exactSlotFirstSql` is a ranking term rather than a matching arm. ⭐ **That is the `--check` lane working as designed: a mutation that stops matching is a coverage hole, and it reads as a passing gate.**

**Shipped 2026-08-25 — the invite endpoint 500s AFTER the row is committed and the email is sent** ([#5436](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5436); PR [#5437](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5437)) — `POST /api/auth/organization/invite-member` returned 500 on **every** invite on prod `us`, confirmed 2026-08-25T12:14Z. `afterCreateInvitation` read `inviter.user.id`, but Better Auth's `routes/crud-invites.mjs` passes the User **flat** (`inviter: session.user`) while `sendInvitationEmail`, *in the same file*, gets an envelope (`inviter: { ...member, user: session.user }`). The hook had copied the envelope form.

⚠️ **The throw lands after the commit and after the send, so the failure was invisible where it mattered and loud where it did not:** the recipient got a working invite, the admin saw *"Failed to send invitation."*, and the `user.invite` audit row plus the "invite team" nudge never fired. An admin who retried stacked duplicate pending rows against the seat cap. `afterCancelInvitation` carried the identical bug and is fixed with it — unexercised only because revoke takes a different UI path.

⭐ **TypeScript could not catch either, and the type is why:** both hooks type the field as `User & Record<string, any>`, so `.user` resolves to `any` and `.user.id` type-checks against a payload that has no such property. Generalizable: **an index signature widened onto a precise type disables checking for every property the precise half does not name** — the annotation reads as documentation while behaving as `any`. The regression test therefore drives each composed hook with Better Auth's **actual flat payload** and asserts the `admin_action_log` row lands attributed to the actor; verified to reproduce the exact prod `TypeError` against the old code, and it fails equally if a future Better Auth switches to the envelope.

The second commit fixes the message that hid it. The bare fallback named the one step that **had** worked and pointed at a retry, which is the action that stacks duplicates. `inviteErrorMessage` resolves four arms — server message, 5xx (names the status and warns the invitation may already exist), 4xx (status only), and no-status (the request never reached the server, where retrying is safe). Both invite dialogs share it. ⚠️ No request ID is available on these: the 500s come from Better Auth's own handler rather than `runHandler`, so the repo's *"request IDs on all 500s"* rule does not reach them.

**Shipped 2026-08-25 — condition 2's two real edges fixed; the condition itself is NOT met, and now says why** ([#5424](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5424), [#5439](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5439)) — *"Every authoritative claim has a human name on it… no exceptions, including for claims that arrived by import, correction, or migration."*

**The gate holds.** A census of every claim in us prod — 34 facts, 31 episodes, not a sample — found **zero** missing `actor`, `source`, `sourceId`, `episodeId` or `occurredAt`. Plus a seeded random draw (`setseed(0.5424)`), because the condition says *pick any claim at random* and an exhaustive count does not answer that question in its own words. `classifyEpisodeForReconcile`'s `SOURCE_PRINCIPAL_UNRESOLVED` is why: an episode naming nobody produces no row at all.

**The PRD snapshot rated this "Close — import and correction paths are the open edges." Both halves were wrong**, and the two real edges were ones it did not name:

- **Correction is the BEST-attributed path in the product**, not an open edge — the correction episode's `source_actor` plus an attributed `admin_action_log` row carrying the actor's email. `re-authority` goes further and stamps the person onto the claim's own `provenance`.
- **Import holds** on both shipped lanes (connector, warehouse producer).
- **MIGRATION was open.** The region import validated `provenance` with `Object.keys(...).length > 0` and nothing else, then bound it verbatim — so `{"note":"x"}` imported as a **`published`** claim naming nobody, with no log. The analogue of `sources.ts`'s documented `source` fail-open, and wider: that one is argued in a header, logged at the site, and closed at two downstream gates; this had none of the three. The same validator refuses a blank `brainEnrollments[].enrolledBy` ~1,900 lines up, with the reasoning *"authority nobody can be shown to have granted"* — the condition-2 argument, applied to the act that authorizes claims and not to the claims.
- **PUBLISH was open, and is not an arrival path at all** — which is why no wording of the snapshot could have caught it. Publish is the verb that CONFERS the authority the condition is about. Nothing recorded which facts it promoted: `mode.publish` carried `promotedBrainFacts: <count>` while carrying **ids** for `widenedGrants` and `refusedDrafts`. The publisher of a given claim was recoverable only by joining `brain_facts.updated_at` to the audit timestamp, and `updated_at` is last-write-wins. **Two published prod facts had already lost it** — `47d83d30`, `a6d9ede7`, published 2026-08-18, retracted 2026-08-24, `updated_at` overwritten.

**Both fixed, not filed.** The import demotes an unattributed `published` fact to `draft` and logs it — it does not refuse the bundle, because refusing is what `sources.ts` rejected and the reasoning transfers unchanged (all-or-nothing validation strands the whole workspace at cutover). `PromotionReport.promotedIds` now carries the ids into the audit row.

### Recorded as NOT established

- ✅ **MET 2026-08-26 — superseded by the census below.** As written on 2026-08-25: *"THE CONDITION IS NOT MET… The stored actor is an opaque vendor id: 12 of 34 prod claims attribute to `slack:U0AQW6KF2EM`, the record holds no mapping to a person… condition 2 is open until it lands."* [#5440](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5440) landed and the mapping exists. The question this bullet raised for the PRD — *does a resolvable handle satisfy "a human name"?* — keeps its answer: **no**, and the fix supplies the name rather than redefining the question.
- ⚠️ **The prediction that sized the three-state design was FALSIFIED in this workspace — kept, because the prediction and its refutation are both load-bearing.** As written: *"Resolving to an Atlas user id is NOT the fix… It attributes the minority and leaves the majority where they started."* The measurement it cites is still accurate history (4 Atlas users, 1 resolved audience member, 2 distinct Slack actors). **The inference from it was wrong.** Both actors producing claims resolved to Atlas accounts on the first capture pass: `atlas` = 2, `directory` = **0**, `opaque` = **0**. There was no majority to leave behind here. ⭐ **A measurement of the population and a prediction about which arm it lands in are different claims, and the second does not inherit the first's authority.** The three-state shape is not thereby wrong — it is *unexercised*: us prod contains no author the `directory` arm was built for, so this workspace could never have told you either way, and a real customer still can.
- **Only `us` was read.** Nothing here speaks to eu or apac.
- **34 facts, one workspace, two producers.** An exhaustive census at 34 says nothing about 34,000.
- **Presence, not truth.** Nobody verified that `slack:U0AQW6KF2EM` said what the claim says.
- **`pin` is covered by neither instrument** — it shares `applyVouch` with `re-authority`, so the mechanism is tested and that verb's own wiring is not.

**Shipped 2026-08-24 — `brace-expansion` and `js-yaml` clear on a lockfile refresh, not an override** (partially [#4903](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4903); PR [#5435](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5435)) — #4903's Group 2 called the 9 `brace-expansion` alerts blocked: *"bun `overrides` are top-level only — path-scoped keys are silently ignored — so a single entry cannot pin one package at three majors."* Both sentences are true. **The conclusion does not follow**, because it assumes an override is the only lever.

⭐ **For a purely transitive dependency, an override is the lever of LAST resort — check the parent's declared range first.** `brace-expansion` is declared in no `package.json` in this repo, so its version is chosen by whatever range its parent asks for, and every parent asked with a caret that already admitted the patched release: `minimatch@3.1.5` (×3 paths) `^1.1.7` → `1.1.18`, `minimatch@5.1.9`/`@9.0.9` `^2.0.1`/`^2.0.2` → `2.1.4`, `minimatch@10.2.5` `^5.0.5` → `5.0.9`. All three published 2026-07-30, 25 days clear of `bunfig.toml`'s 48h `minimumReleaseAge`. The lockfile was pinned to stale-but-in-range versions; nothing structural was blocking, and no override, parent bump or `exceljs`/`archiver` work was needed. Generalizable: **a caret that already admits the patch means the lockfile is stale, not that the tree is blocked.** `js-yaml` is the same shape and the last of Group 4 — the flagged copy is the nested `4.2.0` under `cosmiconfig` + `fumadocs-core`/`-mdx`/`-openapi`, all declaring ranges that admit `4.3.1`.

⚠️ **11 of the 13 open alerts closed, and two of them postdate #4903's body and are named nowhere in it** — GHSA-rgw5-rvv9-x895 (2026-08-04) and GHSA-5p4m-2wfm-xmqj (2026-08-06). That is the standing cost of an advisory list kept as prose. The two `better-auth` alerts stay open: #4903 defers them as blocked on a `1.7.0-beta.4` prerelease and 1.7.1 is stable now, so **that constraint has expired** — but a `^1.6.25` → `^1.7.1` minor across 10 declaring sites including `packages/mcp` is not this change's class.

⚠️ **The root `js-yaml` bump was incidental and still went red.** 5.x is in neither advisory's range, but `bun update` bumps the *declared* range and #4903's own syncpack criterion then fired exactly as written: `deps:lint` reported `8x js-yaml … DiffersToHighestOrLowestSemver`, because bun could not reach the other 7 declaring sites — both `create-atlas` templates among them. `bun run deps:fix` moves all 8 together (`syncpack fix-mismatches` is deprecated in v14).

**Shipped 2026-08-24 — the pg-suite 60s timeout is EXPLAINED: 47 of 87 suites put `public` on their search_path, migration 0020 then takes `AccessExclusiveLock` on the ONE shared `public.organization`, and the convoy is the missing 55s** ([#5430](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5430)) — the open half of #5410, closed with evidence rather than inference. All five acceptance criteria met.

⚠️ **THE INSTRUMENT HAD TO BE SHAPED AROUND THE FAILURE, NOT AROUND THE CODE.** #5430's whole warning is that this failure decays before instrumentation lands (55 → 15 → 0 → 0 → 0 on an unchanged tree), so anything switched on after it is seen will never see it. Two consequences drove the design in `packages/api/src/lib/db/migration-breadcrumb.ts`:

- **A completion-time log is the one shape that cannot work.** A hook killed at 60,000ms never returns from the call holding the budget, so an "elapsed Nms" line written after the await is precisely the line the failing run does not produce. The report is therefore driven by a **watchdog timer** that names whichever phase is open *while* it is open.
- **Armed by default wherever the failure lives** — on whenever `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is set, off in production. An instrument that must be enabled by someone who already suspects the answer is not armed.

### The measurement

Measured 2026-08-24, WSL2 / 32 cores / bun 1.4.0, stock Postgres 16.12, 87 `-pg` suites at `--parallel=32`. Three sweeps of the SAME tree:

| run | antagonist | wall | 60,000ms hook failures |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 (control) | none | 116s | **5** |
| 2 (control, re-run) | none | 165s | **0** |
| 3 | 32 busy loops on 32 cores | 119s | **23** |

Run 3 is the acceptance criterion that mattered: the failure is now **produced on demand** rather than waited for, and run 2 reproduces the decay in the same sitting.

### What the breadcrumb said, from runs that actually timed out

Every timed-out hook left a full tick trail — 10s, 20s, 30s, 40s, 50s, 60s — and **all 252 ticks read `STILL IN apply`**. Not one landed in `pool.connect` or `advisory-lock`. Two numbers settle what five hypotheses could not:

- `phase_ms ≈ total_ms` on every failing run, so taking a connection and taking the advisory lock together cost **~9ms**. The connection cap and the advisory lock are refuted from *inside* the process, matching #5410's refutation of each from outside.
- `drift_ms` — scheduled versus actual tick — stayed at **9–22ms on the control and ≤40ms under a 32-way CPU antagonist**. The event loop was free the entire 60s, so **the test process was never CPU-starved**. It was waiting on Postgres. That distinction is what the drift field exists for and it is what removes starvation from the list.

### The blocking call, named

Sampling `pg_stat_activity` every 1s across a failing sweep, then the blocked→blocker graph via `pg_blocking_pids()`:

- **1,451 of 1,504 blocked-lock samples are on `public.organization`** — 1,090 `AccessExclusiveLock`, 361 `AccessShareLock`. Not a scratch schema. **One shared table.**
- Only **3 samples on `advisory`**, and peak connections **44 of 300** — the two refuted hypotheses, re-refuted from the server side.
- The waiting statements are migrations **0020** (499 samples), **0000_baseline** (476) and **0106** (173).
- Second-largest wait class is `IO/DataFileImmediateSync` (904 samples): ~190 relations × 87 schemas of `CREATE TABLE`/`CREATE INDEX`, each fsync'd immediately — an fsync storm on a WSL2 filesystem.

**The mechanism.** 47 of the 87 suites connect with `options: -c search_path="<scratch>",public`. `organization` is Better-Auth-managed and therefore among the 18 of 208 migrations skipped, so it does **not** exist in the scratch schema — and `0020_plan_tier_rename.sql`'s `to_regclass('organization')` guard, which exists precisely to scope that lookup to the caller's search_path (#2820), resolves it down the path to `public.organization`. The migration then runs nine `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN`, a `CREATE INDEX`, two `ALTER TABLE … CONSTRAINT` and two `UPDATE`s against it, holding `AccessExclusiveLock` until that suite's migration transaction commits. Forty-seven suites doing that against one table is a convoy, and the convoy is the missing 55s.

⚠️ **AND THIS IS THE DECAY, WHICH WAS THE THING THAT WAS NOT A MECHANISM.** On a database where `public.organization` has not yet been through 0020, every one of those statements does real work under an exclusive lock. Once it has, `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, `DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS` and `UPDATE … WHERE plan_tier = 'team'` are all no-ops that take the same lock and release it immediately. So the first sweep after a fresh database convoys and later ones do not — until enough extra load re-lengthens the hold, which is exactly what run 3's antagonist did. *"It decays"* now has a cause.

### Recorded as NOT done

**No fix is attempted here, deliberately.** The repair is to stop 47 test suites putting `public` on their search_path — a behavioural change across 47 files where some may depend on the fallthrough, and folding it into the diagnosis is how a 47-file change gets reviewed as a footnote. #5430 asked for the mechanism; the mechanism is what is delivered. The follow-up is scoped by this entry, and the breadcrumb is left armed so the fix can be verified against run 3's reproduction rather than against a green run that was already green.

**Shipped 2026-08-24 — a custom AC statement that omits a resource denies it to everyone, `owner` included** (no issue — found while setting up prod fixtures for [#5376](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5376); PR [#5434](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5434)) — inviting a user to an organization failed for **every role** on prod (us) with Better Auth's *"You are not allowed to invite users to this organization"*. `org-permissions.ts` builds a custom statement and custom owner/admin/member roles, and `server.ts` passes both to `organization({ ac, roles })` — which **replaces** the stock `defaultStatements`/`defaultRoles` rather than extending them. The custom statement never carried `invitation`.

⭐ **An absent resource is not an absent restriction.** `role.authorize()` returns `unknownResourceResponse` for a resource missing from the role's statements, so omitting the line did not leave invitations unrestricted — it made them impossible, `owner` included. The general form: **where a permission model lets you replace its defaults, every resource you fail to copy becomes a silent deny, and the omission is invisible at the call site that needs it.**

⚠️ **It was half a fix until review found the web mirror.** `packages/web/src/lib/auth/org-permissions.ts` is a hand-copy of the server module (web cannot import from `@atlas/api`) fed to `organizationClient({ ac, roles })`, and nothing in CI compared the two — so the server-side fix left a UI-side reproduction of the same bug waiting for the first client-side permission gate. A test now normalises both sources and requires them identical, so future drift fails generally rather than for `invitation` alone. ⚠️ **And the coverage guard was resource-level, so it could pass while the route 403s** — a resource present but MISSING AN ACTION denies exactly like an absent one (`unauthorizedResourceResponse` vs `unknownResourceResponse`). It now checks every stock action on every stock resource, and pins the deny-on-unknown semantic it depends on, since a future Better Auth that default-allowed unknown resources would leave the guard passing while meaning nothing.

**Shipped 2026-08-24 — the mandatory local release gate is demoted to advisory, the silently-hollow default path is made loud, and the reported failure does not reproduce** ([#5410](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5410)) — all four acceptance criteria met, with AC1 met *as written* — it required the migration-cost hypothesis be **measured before being written down as the answer**, and the measurement refuted it as sufficient, so the cause is recorded as partly open rather than closed. The headline defect could not be reproduced on the machine that filed it; four hypotheses were tested and refuted, **one of them generated and nearly shipped during this very investigation**.

⚠️ **THE REPORTED FAILURE DOES NOT REPRODUCE, AND IT WAS CHECKED ON BOTH TAGS THE ISSUE NAMES.** Full `bun test --parallel` for `@atlas/api` with `TEST_DATABASE_URL` set, stock Postgres, same box, 2026-08-24:

| SHA | reported in #5410 | measured |
|---|---|---|
| `f3f32a7c7` (`main`) | — | **22,266 pass · 0 fail** · 147s |
| `a81f7729a` (`v0.2.17`) | 27 fail, 17 errors | **22,224 pass · 1 fail** · 148s |
| `33f45fccd` (`v0.2.16`) | 16 fail, 10 errors | **22,187 pass · 0 fail** · 143s |

The single `v0.2.17` failure is `ai-saas.test.ts` — a TTL-cache timing assertion, not a Postgres suite and not a 60s timeout. So the premise *"it cannot go green on this machine, and it could not at the previous tag either"* is **false as stated today**, which does not make the report wrong — it makes the failure **load-dependent and nondeterministic**, which is a different and worse thing to gate a release on.

⚠️ **THE 60s TIMEOUTS ARE REAL BUT THEY DECAY ACROSS IDENTICAL RE-RUNS, WHICH IS WHY NO SINGLE RUN IS EVIDENCE.** They were reproduced only by running the 87 `*-pg.test.ts` files *alone* at full parallelism. Five identical invocations, same tree, same settings, nothing changed between them: **55 fail (39 at exactly 60,000ms) → 15 fail (11 at 60s) → 0 → 0 → 0.** A gate whose verdict depends on how recently you last ran it will be overridden, and should be. ⭐ Generalizable: **"it failed for me" and "it passes for me" are the same measurement taken at different points on a decay curve** — neither settles a flake, and a rule that reads one run as a verdict is the defect, not the run.

⚠️ **PER-SUITE MIGRATION COST IS MEASURED AND IS NOT A SUFFICIENT CAUSE — AND THE REST OF THE ANSWER IS DELIBERATELY WITHHELD.** #5410 named it the next hypothesis and required it be measured before being written down as the answer. It was: each `-pg` suite's `beforeAll` creates a scratch schema and applies **190 of 208 migrations**, costing **5.2s at 1-way concurrency, 6.5s at 4-way, 8.4s at 16-way** — ~5s of a ~7s suite, and 87 suites × 5.2s ≈ **452 CPU-seconds of migration work against one Postgres**. It is by far the largest thing a `beforeAll` does, so it is the plausible occupant of the 60s budget. ⚠️ **That last step is an INFERENCE, not a measurement, and is flagged rather than promoted**: no stack was captured at the 60,000ms mark, so *"the hook is timing out inside `runMigrations`"* is exactly where evidence stops and reasoning begins. What IS measured is that cost alone does not cross 60s — at 32 workers the same sweep finishes **green in 148s**. The honest statement is therefore the negative one: **cost looks necessary and is demonstrably insufficient; the trigger is additional load; the precise blocking call is unproven.** AC1 asked that the hypothesis be measured before being written down as the answer — the measurement is done and the answer is withheld, which is the criterion's own instruction rather than a shortfall against it.

⚠️ **FOUR REFUTATIONS, EACH WITH THE NUMBER THAT KILLED IT.** (1) **Connection cap** — `pg_stat_activity` sampled every 2s through a full sweep peaked at **43 connections (33 active, 9 idle) against a cap of 300**; the `87 × max:4 = 348` arithmetic never happens because `--parallel` bounds concurrency at the CPU count. 33 active is exactly `nproc`: the suite is CPU-bound. (2) **Catalog bloat** — **68 leaked scratch schemas and 109,430 `pg_class` rows were present during the green runs**, re-refuting what #5407 already refuted. (3) **Cold Postgres cache** — the leading candidate, since the only failing runs followed a restart; tested by restarting and running immediately: **0 fail, 136s.** (4) **Advisory-lock serialization** — plausible because `runMigrations` takes one, but the key is per-`current_schema()`: **87 concurrent runs produced 87 distinct keys.**

⭐ **AND THE FIFTH HYPOTHESIS WAS MANUFACTURED BY THE INVESTIGATION ITSELF, LOOKED CAUSAL, AND WOULD HAVE SHIPPED A `docker-compose.yml` CHANGE THAT FIXES NOTHING.** A synthetic probe driving 87 truly-concurrent `runMigrations` hit **`53200 out of shared memory`, HINT: increase `max_locks_per_transaction`** — 74/87 failing at stock `64`, and 30/32 failing at only 32-way. Raising it to `256` took 32-way from **30 failures to 0**: a real limit, a real mechanism, a one-line lever, and a clean dose-response. It is still not the bug. The real suite never drives 87 concurrent migrations — `bun test --parallel` caps at the core count — and at stock `64` the sweep is green at **8, 16 and 32 workers alike**. ⭐ The generalizable part is the shape, not the setting: **a probe is a model, and a model built to stress one component will find that component's limit whether or not the system ever reaches it.** This is the third log-shaped decoy in this area in three weeks — after `pg pool exhausted` (a fixture) and `killed by signal SIG…` (a fixture, #5407) — and the first one the investigator wrote themselves. The `max_connections=300` line keeps its disclaimer and gains the measured 43.

⚠️ **THE DANGEROUS HALF WAS THE REAL DEFECT, AND IT HAS A NUMBER NOW.** Without `TEST_DATABASE_URL`, every `*-pg.test.ts` self-skips — that is the suites' own `describeIfPg` line — and `ci-local.sh` reported `RESULT: PASS — all 42 gates green`, exit 0. Same tree, same command, only the variable differing: **22,266 pass / 147s set, versus 20,834 pass / 27s unset — 1,432 assertions across 87 files silently not run**, and the faster, emptier run is the *default*. `test` now returns **3 (DECLINED)** in that case, rendering `SKIP` with an up-front warning, joining `mutation-tables` in the vocabulary this wrapper already had. A real failure still returns its own non-zero — a decline must never mask a red, which is the one case with a negative-control fixture. There is deliberately **no opt-out flag**: `CI_LOCAL_NO_TEST=1` already covers a chosen gates-only run, and a second flag whose only effect is restoring the false green hands back exactly what this removes. The predicate lives in `lib/ci-local-report.sh` rather than in `g_test`, because `g_gate_fixtures` runs every `scripts/__tests__/*.test.sh` and a fixture invoking `ci-local.sh` would recurse — the same constraint that put the verdict logic there in #5151.

⚠️ **AND `/release` STEP 3'S "REFUSE TO TAG IF ANYTHING FAILS" IS REPLACED, BECAUSE THE GATE IT NAMED CANNOT PASS.** On an unchanged `main` with the DB URL set, `scripts/ci-local.sh` fails **2 of 42 gates**: `gate-fixtures` (7 pre-existing fixture failures in `check-mutation-tables.test.sh` and `check-docs-brain-snippets.test.sh` — present with the URL set *or* unset, so unrelated to this issue) and `test` (1 of 22,276, `identity-consumers-pg.test.ts`, which passes **76/76 in isolation**). Remote CI is now named as the release gate and local `/ci` is advisory. ⚠️ **The rule had FOUR copies, not two, and the first cut of this change found only two of them** — `release.md` step 3 and the two statements in `release-process.md` were updated, while `docs/development/branch-protection.md` ("`/release` refuses to tag if `/ci` fails", load-bearing for *why no required checks on `prod`*) and `CLAUDE.md` ("run the full `ci-local.sh` … before tagging a release") were left contradicting them. Both are now updated, and each copy names the others. ⭐ **Nothing gates prose copies of a rule against each other, so the count is discovered by searching, not by remembering** — and a stale copy of a *release* rule is read by whoever is mid-release, which is the worst moment to meet two answers. Remote is the honest gate for a structural reason, not a convenient one: it shards `api-tests` **four ways against a dedicated Postgres service**, so each shard runs ~22 of the 87 pg suites rather than all 87 against one local server contended by 32 bun workers — which is why it was green on all 37 checks for the SHA a local run was red on. ⭐ **A mandatory gate that cannot pass is not a gate; it is a step everyone learns to override, and it trains the override that will one day be used on a real red.**

⭐ **AND THE FIX FOR THE FALSE GREEN SHIPPED A WORSE FALSE GREEN, CAUGHT ONLY BY READING THE BYTES BACK.** The first cut of `g_test` was `bun run test` / `local rc verdict` / `rc=$?` — and **`local` is a command that succeeds, so it resets `$?`.** The capture read the `local`, not the suite: every genuinely failing run would have been recorded rc=0 and, with `TEST_DATABASE_URL` set, reported **PASS**. A gate that silently skips 87 suites is bad; a gate that reports a red suite as green is worse, and the second arrived inside the fix for the first. ⭐ The transferable part is *why the tests could not see it*: all five new fixtures drove `ci_local_test_verdict` directly, the predicate was handed a `0` and correctly returned `0` — **the defect was in the CAPTURE, one layer above the thing under test, and a correct unit can be fed a wrong input all day without complaining.** The guard added for it is therefore static — it asserts `rc=$?` is the *very next line* after `bun run test`, since `g_test` cannot be sourced (ci-local.sh runs 42 gates at source time) — and it was verified by **reintroducing the bug and watching it go red**, because a guard never seen to fail is not yet a guard. Same lesson as #5401's detector, which passed every fixture and failed every real log.

⭐ **THE COUNT THE WHOLE ARGUMENT RESTS ON IS NOW GATED, AND REGISTERING IT EXPOSED A SILENT-NON-MATCH TRAP IN THE GATE THAT DOES THE CHECKING.** `87` was a literal in five files that cannot derive it, so one new pg suite would have falsified every copy while `ci-local.sh` — which derives it at runtime — stayed right; the number is the entire persuasive content of "your green run verified nothing", so an ungated one is the wrong kind of claim to make in an issue about ungated claims. It is now `"real-postgres suites|count_pg_suites"` in `check-agent-doc-paths.sh`, taking that gate from 5 registered phrases over 17 occurrences to **6 over 23**, verified by stating a wrong number and watching it name file, line, stated and derived. ⚠️ **The first registration was spelled `real-Postgres suites` and matched NOTHING** — `count_scan` matches its regex against `tolower(win)`, so any capital in a registered phrase makes it unmatchable however plainly the prose states it, and it fails as a *silent non-match* rather than an error. The only reason it surfaced is that gate's own refuse-on-zero-occurrences guard (a #5299 fixture, written for exactly this). ⭐ Generalizable: **a checker whose failure mode is "matches nothing" needs a floor that fails when it matches nothing — otherwise adding a check and adding a no-op are indistinguishable**, which is the same defect as a green run that skipped 87 suites, one level up. `1,432` is deliberately NOT registered — it cannot be derived without running the suite — so it is written as a dated measurement rather than a live invariant.

> **Left open, deliberately:** the 7 `gate-fixtures` failures are pre-existing and out of scope here — they are not about Postgres, they reproduce with the DB URL unset, and folding them in would have hidden them inside a CI-flake issue. Worth their own issue. **— CLOSED 2026-08-24 as [#5429](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5429), fixed in PR [#5433](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5433): the gate was the stale side, not the seven fixtures. bun 1.4 colorizes a PIPE, so the fixtures' expected output stopped matching what the gate read back.**

**Shipped 2026-08-24 — a 12-message thread is one message's worth of novel text, not twelve** ([#5354](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5354); PR [#5419](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5419)) — `composeEmailBody` stored an RFC header block plus Exchange's plain-text conversion of the **whole** message, quoted history included, and nothing in `ingest/outlook/` stripped it. Episode 12 of a thread therefore contained the text of episodes 1–11, and the same claim was read on every reply. Slot-key dedupe collapses the results onto one fact, so this was never corruption — it was **~12× the model spend and ~12 provenance edges on a claim made once, quadratic in thread depth**, landing on exactly the cost [#5334](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5334) exists to reduce.

⭐ **Strip, then truncate — the order is the whole acceptance criterion.** Cap first and a deep thread spends its entire 8k budget on history the model has already been shown, with the newest message falling off the end. Reversed, the cap is reached by real content, and a truncation warning becomes evidence about a long message rather than about thread depth. Generalizable: **where a pipeline both filters and bounds, bounding before filtering spends the budget on what the filter was going to discard.**

⚠️ **A view, not an edit.** `brain_episodes` is append-only evidence (migration 0180) — evidence that can be rewritten cannot back a provenance claim — so the episode keeps its full body and `quoted-reply.ts` produces what the model reads. A parser bug is recoverable by re-extracting; a bad strip at ingest is not recoverable at all. ⚠️ **The issue's own first-choice library was four years dead** (`node-email-reply-parser`, last published 2022-06-21), as were the next two candidates; `email-reply-parser` 2.3.9 (2026-07-16) was chosen instead — MIT, exposing `isQuoted()` and `isSignature()`, shipping its own `.d.ts`. **Half of #5354's criteria is deliberately open as [#5420](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5420)** — disclaimer footers are not stripped.

**Built 2026-08-25 — the instrument for #5420's gating measurement, and NO reading taken** ([#5420](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5420)) — the issue's first criterion gates the other three: *"the token share of disclaimer text in real extracted mail is measured and recorded, not estimated."* `lib/brain/boilerplate-tail.ts` + `scripts/measure-disclaimer-share.ts` can now produce that number. **Nobody has run them against real mail, because there is none to run them against.** The local Postgres holds **zero `brain_episodes` rows** — the main `atlas` DB does not even have the table — no fixture or seed anywhere ships a mail body, every mail fixture in the tree is hand-written and says so, and [ADR-0044](../../docs/adr/0044-fact-content-never-enters-model-weights.md) bars the one public corpus from ever being committed ("Enron as a local development fixture — yes … under no circumstances committed to this repository"). Acquiring it is [#5381](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5381), which the completion plan calls *"procurement, not code — the only item in this lane whose duration does not shrink with effort applied to it"*.

⭐ **A measure-first criterion is only as available as its corpus, and this one was written as though the corpus existed.** #5420 reads as ready work — three options, a clear gate, a named file to edit — and every path through it dead-ends on data that no one has. The arc that would supply it is itself unbuilt: [#5338](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5338) is blocked by [#5335](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5335), and #5335's export is *gate decisions*, not mail, so it would not have answered this anyway. Generalizable: **"measure it first" defers a decision to a future that must be separately funded — when the measurement has no instrument AND no data, the honest deliverable is the instrument plus the name of what is missing, not a number produced by loosening what counts as real.** The `ready-for-human` label on #5420 was correct and remains correct.

⚠️ **Do not read "the instrument is written" as "the cost is known."** Both `quoted-reply.ts`'s limitations block and the module header say so in as many words, because this is the exact substitution the issue exists to prevent — and the failure would be invisible in the direction that matters, since a plausible-looking harness is more persuasive than the absence of one. The script separates the two answers at the process boundary: **exit 0 is a measured share, exit 2 is "nothing to take a share of"**, never a zero. ⭐ *"the share is 0%"* and *"there was nothing to measure"* are the two readings this issue is most likely to confuse, and an empty corpus that exits 0 with `share: 0` would close #5420 as WONTFIX on the strength of having read nothing.

⭐ **AND THE ISSUE'S SECOND ARGUMENT FOR DOING THE WORK IS FALSE — CAUGHT BY REVIEW, AFTER THE FIRST CUT SHIPPED IT AS A MEASURED FIELD.** #5420 says the disclaimer *"eats into `MAX_BODY_CHARS` on exactly the messages already closest to the cap"*, which reads as though a footer pushes real content off the end. It cannot. `extractionExcerpt` keeps a **front** slice (`text.slice(0, MAX_BODY_CHARS)`) and the tail is at the **back**, so with `L` the length and `T` the tail, real content delivered is `min(cap, L - T)` **both before and after a strip — identical for every `L` and every `T`.** A trailing tail costs **tokens only, never a claim**. ⚠️ The first cut of this work not only believed the issue, it shipped a field called `overCapRescuedByTail` counting `L - T <= cap` — a predicate true precisely when the cut lands *inside* the tail, i.e. when nothing but boilerplate was discarded. **It reported its own best case as a saving**, and the surrounding comment asserted the opposite of the arithmetic directly beneath it. The field is deleted rather than corrected, because the quantity it named does not exist; `claimsRecoverableByStripping: 0` is emitted in its place, stated rather than omitted, so a reader who knows the issue's text finds a result instead of an apparent oversight. ⭐ Generalizable: **when a filter and a bound act on the same axis, which END each one cuts from decides whether they interact at all** — #5354's own ⭐ above is the same axis one level up ("bounding before filtering spends the budget on what the filter was going to discard"), and this is its complement: *a filter that acts on the end the bound already discarded buys nothing, however large it is.* The honest number is now `sentShare` — boilerplate as a fraction of what the model **reads** — printed beside the stored-text `share`, and **the gap between the two is the finding**.

⚠️ **AND THE MEASUREMENT SCRIPT WAS WRITING ITS LOGS TO THE CHANNEL ITS OUTPUT IS PARSED FROM.** `createLogger` defaults to **fd 1**, and `strippedForExtraction` emits a `log.warn` on every message the mail parser throws on — above the default level — so `--corpus … | jq` broke on any corpus containing one such message, which a real corpus certainly does. Fixed with the #5126/#5146 mechanism: an import-time shim with no imports of its own, pinned as the script's first import, because `rootLogger` is a module-scope `const` whose destination pino resolves once and which on the dev branch lives in a `pino-pretty` worker thread. ⭐ Same shape as #5126's own lesson and worth restating: **count fd-1 writers by EXECUTION, not by reading the success path** — this script's own logging was never the problem; a dependency's warning path was. Three further review findings in the same pass were all *"the tally that tells you whether to trust the corpus is the tally that lies"*: one unreadable sub-directory threw out of the walk and discarded the entire scan at exit 1 **while printing the path**, which in a maildir is `maildir/<lastname-f>/…` — a person's name, in a script whose whole posture is that it never names anything; and symlinks, which `withFileTypes` reports as neither `isFile()` nor `isDirectory()`, were skipped while being counted in **neither** `filesSeen` **nor** `unreadable`. Measured on a fixture with a symlinked file and a symlinked subtree: 19 messages found after the fix, 12 before, with a clean-looking report either way.

⚠️ **The detector matches no English, because #5420 forbids it** — *"a regex here would be matching English legal boilerplate … it silently drops real content the day someone writes a sentence containing 'confidential'."* So it keys on the structural property the issue itself names when it argues the cost is linear: a disclaimer is **a fixed per-message tail**. Reversed lines per sender domain into a trie; a node at depth *d* with *count* messages IS a *d*-line tail shared verbatim by *count* of them. The consequence is stated rather than hidden: this measures repeated tails, of which disclaimers are a subset, so the number is an **upper bound** on what a perfect stripper could recover — which is the bound a WONTFIX decision needs, and the safe direction for one that argues for work. Two guards push the same way: a tail consuming the *whole* message is counted as a duplicate contributing **zero** (stripping there leaves nothing, and the fix would be dedupe), and results are banded by tail length so a share made of one-line sign-offs cannot pass as legal boilerplate. Four named mutations were each observed to turn the suite red — dropping the newline in the char count, loosening the repeat threshold, removing the whole-message guard, and grouping globally instead of per sender.
