**Shipped 2026-08-24 — an observation is not a belief at the last two surfaces: the oversight counters and publish-time grant widening** ([#5416](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5416), [#5391](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5391); PR [#5417](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5417)) — ⭐ **An exclusion is a property of a QUESTION, not of a table.** That is why these two split out of #5415 rather than being swept into it: `STATE_COUNTERS` has five arms and only three may take `notAnObservationSql` — `awaiting_review`, `provisional` and `in_tension` lose observations, while `published` and `retracted` must keep them, because those are the ADR-0042 stragglers `GET /retirable` exists to enumerate ([#5403](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5403)). ⚠️ **Narrowing the reader-scoped half alone would have *inflated* the hidden-backlog delta by the observation count**, reporting them as backlog federated to somebody else — false, and more actionable-looking than the over-count it replaced; on prod `us` the panel read *"12 awaiting review"* over a review queue showing none, a restatement that stopped being true at #5341 and said so in its own comment. #5391's exclusion is not on `brain_facts` at all but on the **episode** whose grant a publish may widen with: a warehouse reading corroborating a PRIVATE belief unioned `org` into its grant, so the body reached the whole org. The ~50-line deferral comment at `oversight.ts` is **replaced by the fix**, not left describing a defect that no longer exists.

**Shipped 2026-08-24 — the ADR-0042 exclusion reaches every disclosure surface, not just the gate** ([#5411](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5411), [#5409](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5409), [#5412](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5412), [#5413](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5413); PR [#5415](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5415)) — the publish gate has refused warehouse-derived drafts since #5342 and the review queue has excluded them since #5341, but three surfaces over the same rows never composed the rule; on prod `us` that told an operator twelve observations were pending publish, above a button that refused all twelve. ⚠️ **An exclusion enforced at the GATE but not at the DISCLOSURE is only half-enforced** — the data stays safe, which is the important half, but the preview is what an operator *plans* from, and a plan built from an unfiltered list is wrong before it is executed. The count and the projection had to move **together**: narrowing the projection alone re-renders every excluded observation as *"and 12 more you cannot see"*, a worse statement than listing them was. ⭐ **A description may state a RULE about regions; it may not state an INSTANCE about where data sits** — #5409's sentence sent an operator mid-retirement to `eu-prod`/`apac-prod` when every straggler was on `us-prod` and both other regions held zero brain facts, on the one surface that exists to find them. One falsifier, four surfaces (`promotion-pg.test.ts`), red before the fix with `Expected: 1  Received: 3`, with the review-queue arm as the **control** — it composed the rule all along, so if it goes red the fixture stopped being warehouse-derived and the other assertions were passing vacuously.

**Shipped 2026-08-24 — the Producer Stops Publishing milestone closes on a verified prod read, and two of its five statements had to be MADE true** ([#5345](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5345), [#5331](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5331); tag `v0.2.17`) — the two stranded observations retracted on `us-prod` and read back, and the comparison surface reaped down to exactly the 5 live organizations. ⚠️ **The milestone's own stated fear was half-realised, and only a prod read found it.** Statement 5 was FALSE at first check: the surface still held two churned orgs **four days after the capability to exclude them shipped**, because `filter` could not actually be declared — the structured entity editor silently stripped it on save ([#5402](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5402), fixed in the same `v0.2.17`). ⭐ Generalizable: **a capability and the tool for using it can ship in different releases, and nothing fails loudly in between** — the feature was present, tested and green for four days while being unusable, and every green test was telling the truth about the code. ⭐ And **a statement that asserts an ABSENCE cannot detect a dead system**: four of the five statements would still have passed if the tension path had broken too, so statement 3 (disagreement still works) was the only load-bearing one, and it is the one prod could not answer — prod carries no `chat` episode source, so the falsifier at `search-pg.test.ts:552` stands in its place, **verified RED under an injected regression** rather than trusted for being green. Three defects surfaced from the verification itself, all in the disclosure layer rather than the storage layer: [#5411](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5411) (publish-preview offers the 12 observations the gate refuses), [#5412](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5412) (a 409 that still writes a draft), [#5413](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5413) (that 409's advice names a parameter location that does not work). All three shipped the same day in PR [#5415](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5415), with #5409 alongside them.

**Shipped 2026-08-24 — the local-suite abort is measured, made legible, and its one plausible cause refuted** ([#5401](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5401); PRs [#5405](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5405), [#5407](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5407)) — all four acceptance criteria met; the crash itself is unexplained and stays upstream, per the issue's own framing.

⚠️ **THE LOCAL SUITE ABORTS ON A NATIVE SIGNAL IN 40% OF RUNS — MEASURED, 6 OF 15, SIX DIFFERENT FILES.** Measured 2026-08-23 at `2778475be` on WSL2 / bun 1.4.0 with `TEST_DATABASE_URL` set, closing [#5401](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5401)'s first acceptance criterion (PR [#5405](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5405)). All six were **SIGABRT**, and the crash site was **distinct every time** — `tenant-pool`, `chat-cap-pg`, `object-cmp-pg`, `backfill-crm-leads`, `persist-form-install-pg`, `grant-sweep-pg`. Five of six are `*-pg.test.ts` and the sixth is a migrations-script suite, so *every* crash was in a worker touching real Postgres. **This is what makes "the named file is a victim, not a suspect" a measurement rather than a hunch**: six crashes, six files, zero repeats. The estimate the issue was filed on (~50%, n=5) survives contact with n=15.

⚠️ **AND THE DETECTOR WRITTEN TO REPORT IT FAILED ON EVERY REAL LOG WHILE PASSING EVERY TEST.** The same PR's `ci_local_abort_stats` classified all six real crashes as *no crash*. Two independent causes, both invisible to every synthetic fixture: (1) it read the log into a shell variable and tested it with `printf … | grep -qF`; `grep -q` exits on first match, `printf` takes **SIGPIPE**, and under `set -o pipefail` the pipeline returns 141, so the `|| return 1` fired — reproducible only once the log is big enough that printf is still writing, i.e. never at the few-KB fixture scale and always at the real 4.8 MB scale; (2) bun stamps **three** marker wordings, not one — measured in a single run: 243 × `aborted: worker panicked`, 31 × `aborted: sibling worker panicked`, 1 × `worker crashed: SIGABRT` — so keying on the "sibling" wording alone counted 31 of 275 and would have reported **244 failures that did not exist**, which is the exact misread the change was written to remove, arriving from inside the fix. ⭐ Generalizable, and the reason this is worth a paragraph: **a fixture suite samples the shapes its author already imagined, so a detector's first contact with real data is a different kind of test — and here it was the only one that could fail.** Both defects now have fixtures, including a deliberately multi-megabyte log, because the size *was* the bug.

⚠️ **THE CAUSE-SIDE LEVER WAS TRIED AND IT IS REFUTED — CATALOG BLOAT IS NOT IT.** The tempting story was accumulation: crashes clustered late (runs 1–7 clean, 6 of the last 8 crashed) while the local Postgres went from **224 to 451** non-`public` schemas, since each `*-pg` suite creates a random scratch schema and a worker killed by SIGABRT never reaches the `afterAll` that drops it. So the DB was swept to **0** and the identical 15-run protocol re-run against it. **The rate did not move: 4 of 10 (40%), against 6 of 15 (40%) at baseline** — and the very first crash arrived at run 2 with **31** schemas in the database, where the baseline's first came around ~340. The clustering was the confound reading backwards: crashes leak schemas, not the other way round. ⭐ Worth keeping because the refutation was cheap and the hypothesis was *good* — it had a dose-response shape, a mechanism, and a one-command lever, and it was still wrong. **A correlation that survives only because you never removed the suspected cause is not evidence; removing it costs one afternoon and settles it.** A clean run leaks ~5 schemas of its own, so the sweep is still worth doing as hygiene — it just does not touch the crash.

⚠️ **AND TWO CLAIMS IN THE ORIGINAL REPORT ARE NOW FALSIFIED BY THE COMBINED 25 RUNS.** (1) *"Every crash so far has been in a worker touching real Postgres"* — the swept batch crashed in `lib/__tests__/semantic.test.ts` and `lib/__tests__/agent-runagent-call-sites.test.ts`, neither a `*-pg` suite. (2) The abort is **not observed on CI runners at all**: 12 recent `ci.yml` runs (11 green, 1 red) carry **zero** panic sentences and **zero** aborted markers, and the scan is non-vacuous — those logs hold 26 real bun summaries, and the 9 `killed by signal SIG…` lines per run are a *fixture* (`file.test.ts`, one line per signal), not real retries. The standing difference is shape, not luck: CI shards `api-tests` four ways, so each runner drives a far smaller worker pool than one local `--parallel` over 1123 files. ⭐ **Across both batches: 10 crashes, 10 distinct files, zero repeats** — which is as strong a statement as this kind of data makes that the file bun names is a victim and quarantining it would only move the crash.

⚠️ **A COMMENT THAT IS FALSE ABOUT THE CODE CAN OUTLIVE EVERY REVIEW ROUND, AND TWO OF THEM DID — ONE IN A PUBLISHED NPM PACKAGE.** Recorded 2026-08-23 as a note under [`practices.md`](../../docs/agents/practices.md)'s rule 3, closing [#5078](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5078); the mechanism it named — `/review-panel`'s rule deferring `comment-analyzer` to whichever round turns out to be final — went away with the 2026-08-17 deletion, so there is no rule left to change and nothing here gates work. The measurement survives the machinery. [#5032](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5032) (PR [#5076](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5076)) ran **five rounds**; the comment sweep ran once, in round 5, and returned **4 HIGH — comments the tree falsifies**. Two were stale prose left behind by round 4's own fixes, and **two had been wrong since round 1**: `packages/schemas/src/brain.ts` and `packages/types/src/brain.ts` both described `added` as `.nonempty()` and concluded the `satisfies` passes vacuously, when the field had been `z.tuple([z.string()], z.string())` from round 1 *precisely so that it does not* — a maintainer acting on the docstring deletes a working type guard, and the `types` copy ships to npm. ⭐ **The deferral was defensible on its own terms and still had this cost, which is the shape worth keeping**: comment findings mostly *are* consequences of code findings, so an early sweep re-describes code the round is about to change — but that argument only covers style and rot. The four findings here were all **absolute claims or stated measurements that disagreed with the code**, and that class is not invalidated by pending fixes; it is the class a long diff buries deeper each round. Generalizable, and the reason this is a note rather than a rule: **a claim a comment makes about the code is checkable at any moment, so deferring the check is a choice about when to pay, not about whether the finding is real** — and a false comment that survives to merge is worse than no comment, because the next maintainer acts on it.

⚠️ **`brain_facts.extracted_at` IS NOT THE FACT'S ANSWER TO `brain_entity.snapshot_at`, AND BUILDING [#5344](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5344)'S REAPER ON IT EMPTIES THE COMPARISON SURFACE.** The ticket asks the corpus reaper to reuse the entity store's reach rule (#5321) — *reap rows whose timestamp predates that entity's last N successful runs* — and the store's left-hand side is a column every committing run rewrites. The obvious transfer is `extracted_at`, and it is wrong in the one direction that deletes data: a row whose value never changes is **corroborated** on every later run, and `writeCandidate`'s corroboration arm deliberately writes nothing to the fact ("nothing about the fact itself changes — not its grant, not its review state, not its validity"). So a perfectly current observation's `extracted_at` stays pinned at the instant it was FIRST seen, for as long as the row lives, and on run three every entity's whole comparison surface predates its own window. The signal that actually moves is the **evidence**: the newest warehouse episode still hanging off the observation by a `provenance` edge. Generalizable, and it is the shape a reused rule fails in: **transplanting a rule means transplanting BOTH sides of its comparison, and the side that looks analogous by name is not necessarily the side that moves.** The store's column is rewritten wholesale each run; the fact's is written once and never again, and only reading the corroboration path says so.

⭐ **The same reap is trivial where the equivalent for a belief was called "the irreversible half".** #5329 flagged the already-minted observations as deserving an explicit answer because retiring a published fact means retraction or supersession — human arbitration nobody could automate. [ADR-0042](../../docs/adr/0042-warehouse-material-is-an-observation-never-a-published-belief.md) is what collapses it: nobody approved an observation, nothing serves it, no human decision is overturned, so the answer is *stop emitting and reap* with no correction verb, no `valid_to` stamp and no tombstone. **A classification decision made for one reason (what may be served) paid out somewhere unrelated (what may be deleted)** — which is the return an ADR that draws a population boundary is supposed to have, and it is worth noticing when it arrives.

**Shipped 2026-08-24 — `atlas login` had been 404ing on every prod host and staging for five weeks, and the retirable listing named the wrong regions** ([#5404](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5404), [#5403](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5403); PR [#5406](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5406)) — Better Auth's `getEndpoints` merges every plugin's endpoints into one flat map keyed by the endpoint's **object key** and builds its router from that; `@better-auth/agent-auth`'s `deviceCode` (path `/agent/device/code`) is pushed after the core `deviceAuthorization` plugin's `deviceCode` (path `/device/code`), so `/device/code` was **never registered**. ⚠️ **Better Auth's own `checkEndpointConflicts` compares *paths*, so a key collision across two different paths is invisible to it** — and the winning key served nothing through the slot either, since the agent-auth surface is platform-gated off by default. Regression dates to [#4417](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4417) (2026-07-07); fixed by re-keying agent-auth's endpoint in our own wrapper, path untouched — reordering `buildPlugins()` was rejected because it restores one path by unregistering the other, the same defect with a different victim. ⭐ Generalizable: **a registry keyed on one field but conflict-checked on another has a collision class its own guard cannot see.** What isolated it was that `GET /api/auth/device` returned `400` — route present, validation failed — proving the plugin was mounted and healthy, which is why it read as one broken route rather than a missing plugin, and why it survived five weeks.

**Shipped 2026-08-22 — the producer stops publishing, and prod says so for one of five statements** ([#5340](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5340), [#5341](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5341), [#5342](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5342); PR [#5382](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5382) @ `5ec9e841a`, tagged `v0.2.15`) — [ADR-0042](../../docs/adr/0042-warehouse-material-is-an-observation-never-a-published-belief.md) Lane A. A warehouse reading is an **observation**: stored, compared against, never served. `readStoredSource` (`lib/brain/observation.ts`) answers *"what does this stored row's provenance say it IS"* once, in three answers — `observation` / `belief` / `unclassifiable` — for the correction refusal, the serving exclusion and the publish gate, so the three cannot spell the question differently. The exclusion is on the **source**, not the status, which is what makes it cover both arms of `brainFactStatusClause`: developer mode is `status IN ('published','draft')`, so *"never published"* alone would have served the entire comparison surface under the `/ee` overlay. Tension clusters are deliberately untouched — comparison keeps working, serving stops.

**Verified on prod 2026-08-22, and the verification is narrower than the milestone needs** ([#5345](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5345#issuecomment-5377359992) carries the queries and their output) — `searchBrain` over the fact store returns `matched: 8, truncated: false`, all eight `provenance.source = "slack"`, zero warehouse; `query: "plan_tier"` returns `matched: 0`. **Non-vacuous**: the same browse returns real published facts with attribution, so the read path works and the warehouse rows specifically are gone. The five warehouse snapshot episodes survive with the producer's own SQL in `locator`, newest `warehouse:organization@2026-08-19T20:26:07.450Z` — the same window as the deleted organization. **Hidden, not retired**, which is precisely the state [#5331](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5331)'s `retract` has to reach.

⭐ **The developer arm and the review queue were then verified by operator reads, closing the gap this entry first recorded as test-backed only** ([second comment](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5345#issuecomment-5377428358)) — `/admin/brain/facts` shows `slack` on every row under **draft**, **published** and **all**; and in developer mode a quantitative question answered **entirely from live SQL** (`4 rows · 1.5s` off `organization` across `apac-prod`/`eu-prod`/`us-prod`) citing **no stored fact**, with server logs showing **no `searchBrain` invocation on the turn**. **The control that makes it non-vacuous is the banner**: developer mode reports **12 brain facts** visible against the published arm's 8, so the overlay demonstrably widens what the reader sees and still surfaces no warehouse row. ⭐ `Dharma / trial` — ADR-0042's own worked example — appears in that answer *from the warehouse, live*, and the organization deleted 2026-08-19 does not appear at all: live data says it is gone and the fact store no longer says otherwise. Statement 1 now holds on **both arms**; statement 2 holds for the queue half.

⚠️ **A `200` ON A STREAMING ENDPOINT PROVES ONLY THAT THE HEADERS WENT OUT.** The first developer-arm attempt rendered no assistant message and read as a regression in the release. `POST /api/v1/chat 200 7065ms` said otherwise until the deploy log did: `agent turn stopped by user` / `[chat] Agent stream failed — assistant response not available`, ~3s in, after `Semantic index built` and before any tool ran — a client-side abort. The retry answered correctly and unchanged. **This is the milestone's own verification trap one layer up** — *health `ok` does not prove the deploy landed* becomes *a success status on a streamed response is not a successful response* — and both were resolved the same way, by reading a signal the surface does not own.

⚠️ **Three of #5345's five statements remain, and the release does not close the milestone.** Statement 2's *gate* half is unverified on prod because exercising it means attempting a publish, which is a write, not a read. Statement 3's mechanism is proven with a chat-sourced rival but its **warehouse-sourced** counterpart is not — this workspace holds no live claim in tension with an enrolled warehouse value. Statement 1's regional coverage is **one region**: the chat answer fans out across all three regional *datasources*, which is not three independent brain reads. Statements 4 and 5 are outstanding by design ([#5331](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5331), [#5329](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5329)/[#5344](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5344)). The rows are hidden, not retired; **nothing here substitutes for the retract**.

⚠️ **A MUTATION STOPPED APPLYING AND THEREFORE STOPPED TESTING ANYTHING, AND ONLY THE GATE NOTICED.** `alias-proposal.mutations.ts` patches one exact line of source text; #5341 moved that line out of `alias-proposal.ts` into `observation.ts`, byte-identical. The `oldString` then matched nothing, and `check-mutation-tables` reported the spec **stale or unmeasurable** — not a drifted count. The row it killed is the one whose entire purpose is proving `unclassifiable-source` catches the allowlist→denylist inversion, so a guard against a fail-open had itself failed open. Re-pointed at `observation.ts`; re-measured with `--only` as **1 | 0 | 0 | 0**, identical to the committed table, so `alias-proposal.md` needed no edit. **The table was never wrong — the instrument had come loose from it.** Generalizable: *a text-anchored check is coupled to a file path AND a line's bytes, so moving code silently retires it, and only the gate that re-runs it can say so.*

⭐ **TWO SHIPPED ASSERTIONS COULD NOT FAIL, AND THE SECOND JUDGE IS WHAT FOUND THEM.** `candidates-pg.test.ts` carried `expect([draftObservation, publishedObservation]).toHaveLength(2)` — true for any two values — and a `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(1)` floor on the queue vitals that passed **with the exclusion deleted**, leaving `loadFactCandidateSummary`'s new clause untested. Both were written by the same actor that wrote the clause; both were caught by the `/code-review` Standards axis, not by the author. Replaced with an isolated workspace and exact totals (`[draftTotal, publishedTotal] === [1, 1]`), then **verified by mutation**: deleting `notAnObservationSql` from the summary aggregate now reddens it. `docs/agents/practices.md`'s structural rule — the actor that builds a check may not be its only judge — held here because a second judge was actually run.

⚠️ **`/release`'s version inference would have cut the wrong tag, and tags are immutable** ([#5384](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5384)). Step 2 says `git tag -l 'v*.*.*' --sort=-v:refname | head -1`; that glob matches `vercel-sandbox-v0.0.5`, a different tag train, so the auto-bump infers from it rather than from `v0.2.14`. Caught by reading the output before using it — nothing in the runbook would have. Worked around by passing `v0.2.15` explicitly. **The failure is unrecoverable by the runbook's own rules** (*"tags are immutable, don't retag; ship a forward patch"*), which is what makes an inference step with no validation the wrong shape here. ⭐ **CLOSED — the inference is now code with fixtures, and closing it found a second blind spot one level down.** `scripts/next-release-tag.sh` anchors the glob (`'v[0-9]*.[0-9]*.[0-9]*'`) and applies `^v[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$` to the survivors, because the glob's `*` matches dots and letters and would still admit `v0.3.0-rc.1`; both the inferred and the explicitly-passed version clear the **same** `emit()`, which is the asymmetry the defect turned on — only the argument was checked, only the inference was wrong. The fixture suite carries a **falsifier**: it runs the OLD glob against its own fixture repo and requires `vercel-sandbox-v0.0.5` back, so it cannot pass on a fixture that has stopped reproducing the defect. ⚠️ **The second blind spot was found by the review, not the author, and the author's first fix for it was wrong** — `git tag -l` is the only oracle, so a clone that never fetched tags reports an *empty* train and then validates the `v0.0.1` it recommends against the same blind list, tagging over a tag that exists on the remote. The first cut refused on `--is-shallow-repository`, which measures COMMIT depth and has nothing to do with tags: **this session's own checkout is shallow with a complete tag list**, so the guard refused the very tree the acceptance criterion was about. Narrowed to positive evidence (`remote.origin.tagOpt=--no-tags`) plus shallowness *only where the train reads empty*, with a counter-case pinning that a shallow clone which CAN see the train still answers. Generalizable: **a proxy signal that is cheap to check is not the property you care about**, and the fixture that catches it is the one asserting the guard does NOT fire.

⚠️ **`api-tests (1/4)` failed on three of four CI runs of #5382 and once on `main` at the release SHA, never for a code reason** — twice `The runner has received a shutdown signal` (exit 143), once `The operation was canceled`, once two tests timing out at 6965ms and 8460ms that pass locally on the branch *and* on the stashed baseline. Filed as [#5383](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5383): `scripts/test-timings.json` was committed once at #2802 and never regenerated, so the shard balancer packs by durations two thousand commits old — shard 1/4 runs 2m27s against ~1m20s for its siblings on a clean `main`. ⚠️ **Correcting an overstatement made in flight: 476 of 862 files on one shard is NOT the defect** — the balancer packs many fast files together on purpose, and file count is the wrong unit. The drifted durations are. ⭐ **A local regeneration was attempted and reverted**: `--update-timings` hung on the same Postgres contention that makes a full local `-pg` sweep unreliable, and still wrote a file — finished files carrying this machine's numbers, unfinished ones keeping #2802's. **A half-measured balancer is worse than either input alone**, and the flag's own docs say the intended shape is one timings file per CI shard, merged. Left for #5383 with that evidence attached rather than shipped from a dev box.

**Shipped 2026-08-23 — `test-timings.json` regenerated on runners at last, and the starvation rate measured rather than estimated** ([#5383](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5383), [#5396](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5396); PR [#5397](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5397)) — `test-timings-refresh.yml` shipped in #5394 and had never been run: `gh workflow run` 404s until the workflow is on `main`, which is why AC1/AC2 stayed open through the merge that added it. Run 32650149461 is the first real refresh since #2802 — 1122 entries, 1113 of the 1115 common durations moved.

⭐ **The refresh was CHECKED rather than trusted, because the way it fails produces a well-formed file.** A run without a database skips the 60 `-pg` suites in milliseconds and records them as the *fastest* files in the suite — a balancer actively worse than the stale one, shipped by a green workflow. Two reads settle it and both are now in `ci.yml` beside the regeneration date: 88 `-pg` suites carry durations in the seconds (median 4.7s), and **none appears among the hundred fastest files**. Generalizable: *when a job's failure mode is a plausible-looking artifact rather than an error, the artifact needs an acceptance test of its own — the workflow going green is not one.*

⚠️ **THE STARVATION DATAPOINT I RECORDED WAS A SAMPLE OF ONE AND THE POPULATION SAYS THE OPPOSITE.** [#5395](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5395) noted that on run 32648850288 the failure hit shard 4/4 (~94s) while the 137s long pole 1/4 passed, and read that as evidence the preemption is not selecting for shard length. Measured over **120 `ci.yml` runs across five days** (2026-08-18 → 08-23; 22 failed, `api-tests` failing in 14 of them), **11 of the 14 land on shard 1/4** — 79%, against one each on 2/4 and 3/4. Shard length is strongly selective and the one 4/4 failure was noise. The inference was not wrong given its evidence; it was drawn from one run, and the correction cost only a query that had been proposed and not run.

⭐ **The same measurement inverts which signal is crying wolf, which is the part that changes what to do.** #5395's framing treats `mutate-core.test.ts`' 5s budget as a noisy-but-necessary leading indicator whose cost is training people to re-run without reading. The counts: **9 of 14 are outright runner loss** (`shutdown signal` / `operation was canceled`, mostly with zero `(fail)` lines), and **exactly 1** is a time-budget assertion alone. A canary firing once in 120 runs is not noisy — **so the "should we raise the budgets" question dissolves rather than being decided**: there is nothing to fix, and both budgets stay exactly as they are. The re-runs are bought by the cancellations.

⚠️ **4 of the 14 are recorded UNKNOWN rather than assigned.** Their `api-tests` logs are ~35KB and end in Postgres service-container teardown, so a tail read cannot reach the failing assertions and a job-by-job listing costs ~30k tokens each. Named on the issue so the next reader starts from four run ids rather than from scratch; one (32535451657) repeats `Role "test" does not exist`, which looks like a config fault and not starvation. **A measurement with a stated hole is worth more than one that guessed to fill it**, and the hole is 4/14 — large enough that the 79% concentration is the finding and the 9/14 split is a floor.

⚠️ **#5388 closed three of four warn-don't-refuse paths and the fourth was left STATED, which is how a narrowing survives a review that read it.** A WHOLESALE reconcile block landed in the reap's `blind` arm; a PARTIAL one had no dimension-shaped answer available because `report.blocked` is keyed by REASON, so a dimension whose every candidate reconcile refused aged out and lost its observations — and their `in-tension-with` edges against live human beliefs — because reconcile declined to WRITE, not because a row left. Closed in #5396: `reconcile.ts` reports the same refusals keyed by predicate at all three sites that increment `blocked`, and `reapStandDown` holds back a dimension that built candidates and had every one of them refused. The two counts are deliberately **not** collapsed into one — a refusal with a blank predicate names no dimension and appears only on the total, and deriving the total from the map would undercount exactly those, in the direction that reaps. ⭐ The pinning test `"a PARTIAL block still reaps"` **flipped meaning rather than being deleted**, so the case stays on the record as a decision that changed rather than a case that vanished.

**Milestone CLOSED 2026-08-20 — the Coverage Surface closes on a prod read, and the prod read found a defect** ([#5216](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5216), [milestone #96](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/96) 5/5) — an admin read `/admin/brain` on a real workspace and checked every component claim against ground truth, per ADR-0041's rule that the milestone closes on the verification and not on the merge. Chat **2 of 7** channels (2 surveyed + 5 enumerated, agreeing with the independent Slack ingest card), freshness **2 current / 0 stale / 0 unverified**, the quiet-but-current arm rendering *quiet ≠ stale* as specified (evidence Aug 3, source asked Aug 20, had not moved); Warehouse **4 of 281** pairs with `no activity metadata` as the correct unverified reason for a class that declares it cannot ask; Transcripts and Mail carrying *"never enumerated … not a zero"* and no count; **12 awaiting review / 10 published**, matching the nav badge and the Facts queue; no `%` anywhere on the page.

⚠️ **THE VERIFICATION'S FIRST CLAIM DID NOT HOLD, ON THE FLAGSHIP CARD — and no gate could have caught it.** `v0.2.13`'s Warehouse card read *"4 of 281 **enrolled** entity–dimension pairs"* over a list where **277 rows said "visible to Atlas, not in scope."** Both cannot be true. The arithmetic was self-consistent so the schema refinements passed, the types were sound, every test was green; it took a human reading the sentence next to the rows underneath it. **A page can be right in every number and wrong in the sentence that says what the numbers are of**, and the wrong half is the one a person actually reads.

⭐ **The load-bearing checks were the ones against sources OUTSIDE the page.** The arithmetic identities in the verification table are the ones `BrainCoverageSchema` already enforces server-side, so their passing shows the producer is *self-consistent*, not that it is *true*. What caught the denominator was the Slack scope, the Facts queue and the semantic layer — three sources the page does not own. **An internal-consistency check re-reads the producer's own opinion; only an outside source can falsify it.**

**Shipped 2026-08-20 — the warehouse denominator is the semantic layer, not the enrolled subset** (no issue — found by hand on prod at `v0.2.13` while verifying #5216; PR [#5358](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5358) @ `d665734d7`) — `coverage-warehouse.ts:410` walks every `(entity, dimension)` pair the semantic layer defines and sets `inPerimeter = enrolled.has(unitId)` **per unit**, so 281 is the universe the admin's own semantic layer describes and enrollment ([ADR-0039](../../docs/adr/0039-the-warehouse-producer-emits-only-what-a-human-enrolled.md)) selects the *numerator* out of it. `CLASS_COPY.warehouse` named the denominator after the numerator's own filter. Now: *"4 of 281 entity–dimension pairs · of the entity–dimension pairs your semantic layer defines."* The data never moved — only the words did.

⚠️ **[ADR-0041](../../docs/adr/0041-the-coverage-surface-counts-what-it-can-see.md) puts the caption on the honesty side of the line** — *"every denominator is credential-relative … the honest phrasing is 'of what Atlas's credentials can see'"* — so a caption that misdescribes its own universe is a **false statement on a page built to have none**. Not a styling nit: the copy that names a denominator is load-bearing wherever the number itself is.

**Shipped 2026-08-20 — the Coverage Surface: `/admin/brain` states what Atlas covers, in parts** ([#5215](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5215); PR [#5356](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5356) @ `8a86f1bc5`) — ADR-0041's page half, on top of #5214's wire shape. PRD condition 6 asks for **one** surface from which an admin states what Atlas knows, how much of the company it covers and what it does not, every part correct at 4% as clearly as at 80%. The overview *becomes* that surface rather than gaining a sibling, because building coverage beside an overview splits the statement across two pages. The top is a paragraph assembled from true parts — one sentence per class, **including the three arms that carry no counts**, in a fixed order so it cannot rearrange itself to lead with its best class.

⭐ **There is no percentage anywhere on the page and no field one could live in.** The layers are incommensurable, and a headline number would reward blindness twice over: excluding units from the perimeter *raises* it, widening scopes *lowers* it. `composeStatement` is a pure function so that claim is falsifiable without rendering a tree, and both the component suite and the Playwright spec assert it. **A summary statistic that improves when you look at less is worse than no statistic.**

⚠️ **Filed rather than fixed in flight: [#5357](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5357)** — the Warehouse unit list renders 200 rows of `table.column` and buries the rest of the page. Nothing is wrong (counts exact, clipping disclosed) and it is unreadable at 281 pairs, which is the shape a correctness-first surface fails in. It carries two candidate shapes and the denominator questions a per-entity grouping would have to answer first, because **regrouping a unit list silently redefines what the denominator counts.**

**Shipped 2026-08-20 — the counts check could not see a claim written in bold, or one that wrapped** (no issue — found while running `/sitrep`, fixed in the same session; PR [#5355](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5355) @ `847125674`) — `check-agent-doc-paths.sh` had been green over **two live count claims in this repo's own docs** since the gate landed. Both were found by hand. The check scanned `<number>[[:space:]]+<phrase>` with `git grep`, and prose defeats that twice over: CLAUDE.md bolded `operational` *inside* the registered phrase, so `**` sat between the number and the rest; `practices.md` ended one line with the number and opened the next with the phrase, so no single line held both halves. The scan now strips `*`/`_`, joins each line to its predecessor, and reports on the line the phrase ends on.

⚠️ **THE OLD GATE, WITH BOTH CLAIMS MUTATED TO A WRONG NUMBER, PRINTED "no doc names a path, command or registered count that does not exist."** That is the whole finding, and it was measured rather than reasoned about: stash the new gate, break both counts, run it, read the line. A check that reports clean on the defect it names is worth less than no check, because it also spends the reader's trust. The second hole is the sharper one — `practices.md`'s wrapped sentence is the **only** live statement of that count on the page, so the phrase was registered, stated, wrong-able, and unwatched. `git grep` being line-based is not an implementation detail when the artifact under inspection is wrapped prose.

⚠️ **The fix produced a third defect of the same family within the hour, and running it is what caught it — reading it did not.** The new sliding-window scan advanced its cursor past the match START rather than its END, so a correct *"43 system-wide decisions"* matched a second time as *"3 system-wide decisions"*. First run: nine findings, six of them fabricated, across three of the five registered phrases. A gate inventing failures on correct prose fails in the opposite direction to the one it was just fixed for, and the same run surfaces both. `grep -o` consumes an occurrence; so does this now. Fixture 13f pins it, and `off = st` still kills 13f **and** case 1, the real-repo pin.

⭐ **A historical count is WORDED, not allowlisted — because a `count` exemption matches by file, not by line.** CLAUDE.md states the runbook count twice, two lines apart: *eight*, true on 2026-08-17, and *eleven*, true today. Allowlisting the first would have exempted the second, retiring the real check to spare a sentence about the past. This is the same shape as dropping backticks off a dead command, and it now has the same standing: word the historical sentence so the number is not adjacent to the registered phrase (*"eight runbooks that were purely operational"*), and leave the phrase to the claim about today. The failure message says so, and the gate's own header follows the rule — it describes its two examples instead of quoting them, because quoting them turned the gate red on itself.

⚠️ **`/sitrep` produced a section in which every line was true and the section was useless, which no gate can catch.** Step 7 said *read every ⚠️ in `## Next`*. The run did: six findings pasted from the two newest entries — four already closed or already tracked as open issues — and the other 103 reported as "not read". Nothing in it was wrong. It was still worthless, because a ⚠️ marks a finding an entry PRODUCED, not an open defect, and the reader could not tell which was which. Step 7 now sorts each ⚠️ into **closed / tracked / UNFILED** against `gh issue view`, and prints UNFILED in full. That class — a live defect the record names and no issue tracks — is the only thing in the whole report the issue counts cannot surface, and it is the reason to read the record at all. The two it surfaced on the 2026-08-20 run were this gate's markup hole and an `api-tests (3/4)` flake.

⚠️ **Authored, fixed and judged in one session, which is the structural rule this repo keeps breaking.** `docs/agents/practices.md` says the actor that builds a check may not be its only judge, and here that actor wrote the gate fix, the fixtures, the convention and this entry. The mitigation is that every claim above is a command someone else can re-run: the falsification is `git stash` + two `perl -0pi` edits + one gate run, and each of the four mutants is named with the case it kills. That is evidence, not a substitute for a second judge.

**Shipped 2026-08-19 — three commands restored against the bar, and the restore reproduced the defect it was about** (no issue — a continuation of the 2026-08-17 burn, recorded here rather than filed after the fact; PRs [#5346](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5346) @ `d34ea7e07` and [#5347](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5347) @ `8d95201e0`) — `/reset`, `/sitrep` and `/tidy` come back, taking `.claude/commands/` from eight to eleven. They were **re-derived, not restored**: run against [`practices.md`](../../docs/agents/practices.md)'s own test — *is the enforcement an external system that fails loudly, or was it your own diligence?* — and two of the three failed as written. `/reset` passed, and gained preconditions it never had. `/sitrep` passed on its inputs and **failed on its output**: every field is read from `gh`/`git`/`npm`, but it closed with strategy rendered in the same tables as the readings, and hard-coded a four-row package table while five packages publish. `/tidy` failed outright — it judged which issues had shipped and closed them, wrote the ROADMAP entry for work it had just done, and approved its own edits, all in the session that did the work. It is restored in two lanes, APPLY and PROPOSE, split on *does a printable fact decide the action* rather than *is it risky*.

⚠️ **THE RESTORE SHIPPED A STEP THAT VERIFIED NOTHING, INTO THE COMMANDS ABOUT STEPS THAT VERIFY NOTHING — and the method that missed it is the finding.** `/reset` Step 3 shipped `git rev-parse --short HEAD origin/main`, labelled *"expect two equal SHAs"*. `--short` takes **one** revision; two exit 128 with *"fatal: Needed a single revision"*, so the comparison never ran once. It reached `main` because the three commands were verified **in parts** — every fenced bash block was extracted and passed `bash -n`, and the summary said so. **A parse is not a run.** `bash -n` answers *is this syntactically a command*; it never answers *does this command exist, accept these arguments, or mean what its label claims* — and the distance between those questions is invisible in a green result. Caught on the command's first real invocation, by the step that exists to catch it, which is the only reason it was caught at all. Generalizable, and it is the burn's own diagnosis one level down: **a verification method has a reach, and a method that reports clean without stating its reach is indistinguishable from one that checked.** Fixed with a comparison that states its verdict instead of printing two SHAs for a reader to match by eye — *a check that asks the reader to compare degrades the moment anyone skims.*

⭐ **A guard that COULD NOT PASS, which is a check-that-cannot-fail with the sign flipped — and it was confirmed live on four branches.** The first `/tidy` draft blocked branch deletion on `git log main..<branch>` being empty. This repo squash-merges, so a merged branch keeps its original SHAs and that range **never** empties. Caught in review on `fix/5233-bridge-window-object-cmp-trace` (merged as PR [#5315](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5315), landed under a new SHA, still listed as unmerged), then confirmed by the first real `/tidy` run: **all four `[gone]` branches carried a merged PR, and the old guard would have refused every one of them** — 1, 1, 5 and 1 "unmerged" commits respectively. The guard would have deleted nothing, forever, while reading as careful. Generalizable: **a safety check that always fires and a safety check that never fires are the same defect** — both are decoupled from what they claim to measure, and the always-fires direction is harder to notice because its output looks like diligence. ⚠️ **The same run also falsified the obvious replacement.** `git cherry` compares patch-ids and got the single-commit branches right, but `fix/5326-producer-reads-every-member` was **five commits squashed into one**, so it has no patch-equivalent either and `git cherry` reported `+5`. The merged-PR query is the only sound signal; `git cherry` covers only branches GitHub never saw. Both the failing form and this caveat are recorded inline in the command so neither is simplified back.

⚠️ **A registered count phrase does not survive bold markup, so a claim in emphasis is unchecked.** `check-agent-doc-paths.sh` matches `<number>[[:space:]]+<phrase>`, and CLAUDE.md carried *"eight **operational** runbooks"* — the `**` sits between the number and the phrase, so the gate read past it while correctly flagging both unbolded copies in `practices.md`. Verified directly: `nine **operational** runbooks` does not match, `nine operational runbooks` does. The prose is corrected; **the gate is deliberately NOT patched in the same PR**, on this page's own structural rule — the actor that found the hole should not also be its only judge. The fix is a tolerant per-phrase pattern (spaces to `[*_[:space:]]\+`, leading `[*_]*`) plus a fixture asserting the bolded form goes red. Generalizable: **a gate's blind spots are found by the prose that walks through them, so an evaded gate is a finding about the gate and not only about the sentence.**

⭐ **One rule was dropped rather than carried across, because the artifact it governed had already overruled it.** The old `/tidy` capped ROADMAP bullets at *"≤ 2 sentences / ~240 chars"* and told the author to trim file paths and issue numbers out of them. This file abandoned that long ago — every entry around this one is multi-paragraph with ⭐/⚠️ markers and named SHAs — so the command and the file had disagreed for months, with the command losing silently every time. Replaced by *"match the entries around it"*, which cannot rot the same way. Generalizable: **when a rule and the artifact it governs disagree, the artifact is the measurement and the rule is the claim** — restoring the rule unexamined would have re-imported a dead one under the banner of preserving it.

⚠️ **One flaky shard, recorded because a re-run to green is a claim.** `api-tests (3/4)` failed on #5347 — a **one-file markdown diff** — with `src/lib/db/__tests__/migrate-pg-with-auth.test.ts` red. The same shard passed on `main` at `d34ea7e07` minutes earlier and passed on re-run of the identical SHA. Diff inertness was established before the re-run, not after: `git diff origin/main...HEAD --name-only` returned one `.claude/commands/*.md` path, which no test imports. Recorded rather than filed, because one observation does not separate infrastructure flake from a real order-dependence in that suite — but a second sighting should be filed rather than re-run.

⚠️ **The prod verification found a SECOND breadth defect one layer down, filed as [#5329](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5329).** `buildSnapshotSql` emits `SELECT … FROM <table> LIMIT n` with **no `WHERE` clause and no way to add one** — the enrollment says which entity and which dimension, never which ROWS. `organization` is soft-deleted (`deleted_at`, `workspace_status`), so a churned customer is asserted as a current fact forever. The entity YAML already carries `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` in every query pattern and defines an `is_active` dimension; the producer runs no query pattern and reads neither. **The knowledge is in the layer and is unreachable from the one consumer that mints durable facts.** Generalizable, and it is #5326's shape one layer up: *the producer reads more broadly than any human reading the layer would mean* — breadth across a group's members there, breadth across a table's rows here, and both store cleanly and look exactly like success. A name-shaped heuristic is explicitly NOT the fix, on the same ground `buildWarehouseClaims` refuses to guess a subject column.

**Shipped 2026-08-19 — warehouse material is an OBSERVATION, never a published belief; and the Company Keystone is asked for, never researched** ([#5330](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5330); [ADR-0042](../../docs/adr/0042-warehouse-material-is-an-observation-never-a-published-belief.md) + [ADR-0043](../../docs/adr/0043-the-company-keystone-is-asked-for-never-researched.md); PR [#5333](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5333) @ `ae0d53e88`) — docs only, no implementation code, and the largest reframe the warehouse arc has taken. The producer minted claims like `<org-id> / plan_tier / business` and routed them through **belief** machinery — draft status, a human review gate, publication, decay, corroboration, correction. These are not beliefs: they are true iff the row says so, right now, and `executeSQL` answers them **live and fresher**. The output is now an observation — stays in `brain_facts`, discriminated on `provenance.source`, refused at `classifyFactForPromotion`, **never reviewed, never published, never served.** Amends ADR-0036 §T9, ADR-0039 and ADR-0040; **ADR-0037 is untouched in its entirety**, so §4's emission contract, §5's live-read prohibition, `subject_cmp`, the entity store and all of M4's collision arbitration survive verbatim. Spawned [milestone #99](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/99) — *The Producer Stops Publishing*.

⭐ **The strongest defence of the old design was disagreement detection, and the measurement showed publication was not what bought it.** A person says "Dharma is on pro", the warehouse says trial, and that tension must surface — querying alone cannot do that. But of the five statements that make it happen, **exactly one filters `status`**: `CORROBORATION_LOOKUP_SQL` no, `TENSION_CANDIDATES_SQL` no, `loadTensions` has **no `mode` parameter at all**, `WAREHOUSE_EVIDENCE_SQL` no and says why — only `buildFactQuery`, the serving path. So an **unpublished** warehouse row already corroborates, already earns `in-tension-with` edges, already surfaces to the agent as a counterpart inside a published fact's cluster, and already counts as observed evidence on the Coverage Surface. **Publication bought exactly one thing: the serving path** — the one `executeSQL` serves fresher, and the one `SEARCH_BRAIN_TOOL_DESCRIPTION` already routes away from (*"Don't use this for quantitative state"*). Generalizable: **before defending a gate on what it protects, measure which statements actually consult it — a gate that four of five readers ignore is buying one thing, not five.**

⭐ **ADR-0041 reached the same conclusion independently, one subsystem over, for coverage.** Two subsystems converging on *derivable material is queried, not stored as belief* without either citing the other is the strongest available evidence that the boundary is real rather than convenient.

⭐ **If derivable material is queried, the fact store is for the durable and non-derivable — and the largest body of that is what the company IS and does, which nothing in the corpus produces.** Nobody says it in Slack; it is not a warehouse column. So Atlas **asks**: a guided interview where answers land as `human` episodes that **do** extract (`DRAIN_EPISODES_SQL` filters no source — the pre-stamp is `correct_fact`'s choice, not a class property), and the wizard's confirmation screen **is** the review gate. That makes the cold start happen in the onboarding session rather than by Friday. ⚠️ **Web research drives the QUESTIONS and never mints a claim** — research-as-evidence would need a new source class, a trust tier for material that is neither warehouse nor testimony, an ACL answer, and would resolve PRD condition 2's *"point at the person"* to **nobody**. ⭐ **A company's public copy is the most confidently-worded stale thing about it.** Also rejected: a fifth `assert` correction verb — a second promotion writer with no reviewer, which is the shape `check-brain-fact-promotion.sh` exists to refuse.

⚠️ **Consequences recorded rather than deferred, and one of them exposed a live defect on the spot.** Corroboration must exclude observations, which surfaced [#5332](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5332) — a person agreeing with a warehouse row produces no reviewable draft, so their testimony is **swallowed**. `WAREHOUSE_ROW_CAP`'s stated justification evaporates and needs re-grounding: its doc says the bound is *"expressed in units of review rather than of database load"* and refuses a knob because one would *"loosen the product's differentiating gate"* — **there is no review gate downstream any more, so 1,000 is a number in a unit that no longer applies.** PRD condition 2 is strengthened rather than waived, and the two published rows stranded on prod get `retract`, narrowly ([#5331](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5331)).

⚠️ **A rewrite of an open ticket's BODY, because the code defect was unchanged and the reason to care was not.** [#5329](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5329) was re-authored against ADR-0042 with the prior framing preserved at the bottom — its original justification (a reviewer publishing a plausible fact about a churned customer) describes a reviewer who **no longer exists**, and would have sent someone to fix it for a reason that had evaporated. Three costs survive and are the ones to fix against: the comparison surface is polluted, the entity store is fed dead entities, and the row cap is spent on rows that do not count. Generalizable: **when a decision removes a ticket's rationale but not its defect, rewriting the ticket is cheaper than the wasted fix — and leaving it is how a correct patch gets built for a dead reason.**

⭐ **Vocabulary is where a reframe like this actually lands.** `docs/contexts/company-atlas/CONTEXT.md` gains **observation** and **comparison surface** as terms, each with its own `_Avoid_` list — notably *"tier-1 fact"* for a stored row, since **tier 1 is the live read** and *"authoritative by construction"* is a property of that query, never of the snapshot it produced. Also: **Company Keystone** as a surface, `human` episodes recorded as extraction **per writer rather than per class**, the warehouse authority arm relocated to enrollment, and observations never reaching `/admin/brain/facts`. ADR count 41 → 43 across `CLAUDE.md`, `CONTEXT-MAP.md` and `docs/agents/domain.md` — the phrase `check-agent-doc-paths.sh` checks, one week after that guard was built to catch exactly this.

**Shipped 2026-08-19 — the producer reads EVERY member of a connection group** ([#5326](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5326); PR [#5327](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5327)) — found while verifying #5314 on prod `us`, one layer under it. The producer snapshotted the group's `primary`, and `primary` is `members.sort()[0]`: **alphabetical**, with no designated-primary concept anywhere in the product. `g_prod` = `apac-prod` / `eu-prod` / `us-prod` holding **1 / 1 / 2** organizations; the run read `apac-prod` alone because "a" sorts first, and the Company Atlas held facts derived from **1 of 4 organizations** asserted unqualified as the company's — `refusals: 0`, run log clean. The run now reads every member and describes their union, refusing the entity outright when two members hold one primary key, and `detail.connection` records which member each claim came from. `loadVisibleGroups`' `primary` is deliberately untouched: alphabetical is right for an amendment's evidence and for `executeSQL`'s group bound, and wrong only for the one caller that mints durable facts.

⭐ **The issue's own recommendation was REVISED by measurement, and the revision inverted it.** Filed, it recommended refusing a multi-member group outright and named "snapshot every member" as the trap — two shards sharing a primary key would file as one subject, a false `same` with no inverse. Then the shards were read: the keys are 32-char random ids with **zero overlap**, so the group is not ambiguous, it is the intended union, and refusing would have answered a question nobody asked. Generalizable: **a hazard that rules out an option is a claim about the data, and it is worth measuring before it decides the design.** The remedy keeps both — read every member, refuse the collision — because the producer cannot know another workspace's shards are not sequentially keyed.

⭐ **The guard was already written one scope down, and the fix is that same rule one scope up.** `buildWarehouseClaims` enforces at most one candidate per `(subject, predicate)` with an explicit first-writer-wins arm, because *"nothing guarantees a declared primary key is unique"*. That arm is also why claims are built **per member and merged after** rather than over concatenated rows: concatenated, the second shard's row is silently DROPPED by the very guard that exists to stop the merge — the safe-looking shape routes the hazard through the safety.

⚠️ **A cell in the checked-in mutation table fell to 0, and it was repaired rather than accepted.** The new union row cap sits beside the per-member one, and the union of a single member is the same number with the same refusal reason — so deleting the per-member check left the producer suite green. Only the message distinguished the arms. Generalizable: **a new guard beside an old one can silently stand in for it, and the coverage evidence reads as unchanged**; the mutation table is what made that visible, and the falsifier now asserts the arm's own wording. A second count is recorded as MOVED, not published silently: a respelled anchor stopped submitting a `null` the shipped defect never produced, so two logging cases that were failing on that artifact correctly stopped.

⭐ **A review pass caught the fix normalising at the REQUEST and not at the RECORD.** `"default"` is a documented entity YAML value and the flat root's implied group name, so it is collapsed to `undefined` before a snapshot request is frozen — and the new `detail.connection` was built from the RAW member, so a hinted entity stored `"default"` where its ungrouped neighbour, reading the same datasource, stored `null`. Two spellings of the flat scope, in the durable record, introduced by the change that exists to make the record answer *"which database"*. Generalizable, and it is #5314's lesson recurring inside #5314's own fix: **normalising a value on the path that EXECUTES does not normalise the copy that is STORED, and the stored one is the copy nobody re-reads until an audit.**

⚠️ **BREAKING for multi-member workspaces, and it is the specified behaviour rather than an oversight.** Three populations change: a group of **replicas or a prod-cloned staging** (identical keys) now refuses every enrolled entity `subject-collides-across-members` where it silently produced from one member before; a group of **prod + staging** with disjoint keys now mints company facts that include staging rows, with `detail.connection` the only tell; and a multi-member group whose members are individually under `WAREHOUSE_ROW_CAP` but collectively over it now refuses. The first and third are the fail-closed direction and the refusals name the members; the second is the direction to watch, and it is the same shape as the defect being fixed with the sign flipped. The union premise came from measuring the workspace that motivated the issue — regional prod shards, 32-char random ids, zero overlap — and CONTEXT.md still defines a group as *interchangeable* connections, which is the replica reading. **Atlas cannot tell the two apart**, which is why the collision refuses rather than guessing, and why this belongs in the release note for the tag that ships it.

⚠️ **The standing "this should ship before the cadence does" warning is discharged**, on #5233's precedent — recorded in `brain-warehouse-cadence.ts`, which is what a person turning `ATLAS_BRAIN_WAREHOUSE_CADENCE_ENABLED` on actually reads. **Prod verification is NOT met by this PR**: the re-read of `organization` against all three shards happens after the release on the [prod-verification-is-post-release](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/3974) precedent, so #5326 stays open until those counts exist. Milestone #96 (The Coverage Surface) inherits the same shape — a denominator computed from one member of a sharded group reports high coverage of a population that is a quarter of the real one.

**Shipped 2026-08-19 — the supersession stamp's attribution, and the producer's provenance group** ([#5324](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5324) + [#5314](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5314)) — two Architecture-Backlog residues of the same day's work, unrelated to each other except in provenance: both are records that disagreed with the behaviour they describe.

⭐ **#5324 was filed as a DECISION and the decision was to close it, not to re-accept it with a bigger number.** The stamp's collision re-check was per **target** — *"does ANY offered draft still collide with this row?"* — so with two same-slot drafts and one rival, a de-merge that broke one pair while the other held stamped the rival correctly and let `promoteBrainFacts` record a `supersedes` edge for **both**. `SUPERSEDE_STAMP_SQL` now evaluates the collision per disclosed **pair** in a CTE and `RETURN`s the draft whose arbitration did the retiring. **The stamped row set is unchanged in the direction that matters and narrower in the other** — nothing is stamped the old form would not have stamped, which is the invariant the guard is measured against.

⭐ **It had to stay ONE statement, and that is the whole design constraint.** The obvious shape — ask which pairs are live in a `SELECT`, then stamp — re-opens the exact READ COMMITTED window #5024 closed, because each statement takes a fresh snapshot. A `WITH` gives every arm one snapshot, so the pair list and the stamp decision cannot disagree. Generalizable: **a fix that splits an atomic check into two statements re-creates the race it was written to close, while reading like a clarification.**

⭐ **The LEFT JOIN is a guard, not a widening.** A stamped row with no surviving pair cannot happen — a surviving pair is *why* it was stamped — so an inner join would drop it silently and leave a belief retired with no `supersedes` edge, which is precisely the silent supersession #4912 forbids. Left-joined it arrives with a NULL and the publish rolls back. **Model the impossible row as arriving rather than as absent: the join that hides it is indistinguishable from the join that never saw it.**

⚠️ **Four places said "accepted, per target" and all four had to move together** — the stamp's header, `entity-comparable-retire.ts`'s, the promotion guard's allowlist entry and the register in `content-mode.md`. The ticket named that as an acceptance criterion in its own right, which is the right instinct: a fact recorded in four places is four things to keep true, and the one that rots is always the one a reader happens to reach first.

⭐ **#5314 is the smaller half of the same shape: a fix that corrected behaviour and left the record contradicting it.** #5284 corrected which connection a snapshot *executes* against; `detail.connectionGroup` kept reading `plan.entity.connection`, the YAML hint that is `null` for every entity on a DB-backed semantic layer — measured on prod `us`, where two facts from a real group recorded `"connectionGroup": null`. The resolved group is now a **required** parameter of `buildWarehouseClaims`, so no call site can reach the old silence by omitting it, and `null` still means the flat scope with `brain_enrollment`'s single translator left as the only one. Nothing published was ever wrong — `detail` is non-load-bearing by construction — and the cost was entirely in the audit trail, **which is where nobody looks until it matters.**

⚠️ **#5314's prod-verification criterion was NOT met by this change**, ✅ **met 2026-08-19 16:50 UTC and the issue is CLOSED.** Its fourth AC asks for a group-scoped enrollment read on prod per the #5197 pattern; what shipped here is the fix plus a unit pin and a run-level pin on the emitted provenance, so on the [prod-verification-is-post-release](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/3974) precedent the read waited for `v0.2.11`. It came back clean on prod `us` — `is_active` recording `g_prod` while the two pre-fix rows still read `null`, all three under **one** enrollment group, so "the enrollment changed" is ruled out by construction rather than by argument. ⭐ **The two attempts it took are the reusable part, and the failed one is the more useful.** First: **a re-run does not verify an emit-time fix on a workspace whose values are static** — unchanged values take the `corroborate` arm, corroboration does not rewrite `provenance`, and the run looks entirely healthy while verifying nothing; the read needs a *newly minted* fact, which means enrolling a pair that has never emitted. Second: the pair to enrol is one **structurally incapable of being NULL** (`is_active`'s `CASE … ELSE false`), not one believed to be populated — `region` was picked off the entity's `sample_values`, which describe the column's **domain** and not any row's value, and the pair emitted nothing at all: `enrolled=3 entities=1 created=0 corroborated=2 refusals=0`, with **no refusal and no counter**. Generalizable: **a verification that cannot distinguish "the fix works" from "nothing happened" has not run yet.** That silence is filed as [#5349](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5349) — absent cells get a bare `continue` while the sibling arm counts unsurfaceable ones per dimension.

**Shipped 2026-08-19 — the store's reaper, the rename reconciliation and comparable retirement, and MILESTONE #95 CLOSES** ([#5319](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5319) + [#5320](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5320) + [#5321](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5321), closing the parent [#5233](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5233); PR [#5323](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5323) @ `0ecbb5b76`) — three tickets on one PR because all three land in the minting transaction. **[Milestone #95](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/95) is closed**: #5197 closed on prod counts 2026-08-18, #5233 was the whole remaining open set, and its five ACs are ticked with where each was met. **The standing "#5233 should ship before the cadence does" warning is discharged** — `ATLAS_BRAIN_WAREHOUSE_CADENCE_ENABLED` no longer starts a reaper-less store accumulating on a schedule.

⭐ **The `object_cmp` question #5043 deferred is answered YES, by a trace rather than an assertion.** [#5315](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5315) reproduced it against a real Postgres: two facts about ONE entity whose object comparables carry different ids supersede each other **even when the object surface is byte-identical** — `object_key` is `acme corp` on both rows, and a fact is retired by a fact asserting the identical claim, autonomously, with no reviewer. Triage's partial trace held; what it could not confirm was that two such facts actually meet at the join, and they do.

⭐ **The remedy went upstream of the join, because the two readings are indistinguishable AT the join.** `object_key` equal + `object_cmp` different is genuinely ambiguous: an id **re-mint** must not supersede, a true **homonym** should, and nothing in the two rows separates them. So `supersessionCollisionJoin` is deliberately untouched — narrowing it would break the homonym case it gets right — and the pair is made to ABSTAIN at the moment the second id is minted, by the writer that knows it happened. Generalizable: **when two readings are indistinguishable at the point of comparison, the fix belongs where they were still distinguishable, not at a predicate that has already lost the information.**

⭐ **A wide rule turned two REVERSIBLE admin actions into an irreversible corpus write, and the justifying claim was simply false.** The retirement first gated on *"this id is no longer in the store"*, on the reasoning that an id no entry carries can never be produced by a future resolution. But `warehouseRowId` digests `(workspace, entity, primary key)` and **not** the canonical surface — so blanking a row's name drops its entry and re-typing it re-mints the identical id, and un-naming a *dimension* blanked an entire entity's facts in one transaction. Caught in review. The narrow rule that shipped fires only on a proven re-mint: **a comparable is provably stale when the SAME warehouse row answers to a DIFFERENT id, and merely absent when the row stopped being described** — absence is recoverable and over-matches nothing.

⭐ **The cross-region poisoning is NOT fixed at the region import, reversing the ticket's own framing.** The imported entries *are* the bridge, and the importer has no standing to say which name is current — the destination cannot read its warehouse yet, by construction, which is the entire reason the entries are exported. So the poisoning is **self-clearing on the first producer run** rather than unreachable, and `entityEdges.ambiguous` reports it while it exists. `admin-migrate.ts` carries the argument at the site where the next person will be tempted to fix it there.

⭐ **A gate fired correctly and the answer was still a carve-out — but the argument had to run OPPOSITE to the neighbouring one.** `check-brain-fact-promotion.sh` refuses an identity-column UPDATE outside the alias-approval seam, and `SET object_cmp = NULL` is one. The seam it protects **re-keys**, so it can create *and* destroy collisions — hence its advisory namespace and reviewer preview. This writer only ever **NULLs**, and at the object a proven difference is what *drives* supersession, so it can only subtract a trigger: the gated hazard (*stamping `valid_to` on a pair nobody arbitrated*) is unreachable from it. Registered as the register's **fourth** carve-out. Generalizable: **"my case is safe" and "the gate is wrong" are different claims, and only the first survives review.**

⚠️ **An allowlist entry exempts a FILE, not a column — so the carve-out had to buy its own gate.** `entity-comparable-retire.test.ts` parses the guard's own `UPDATE_GATED_COLUMNS` rather than restating it, and pins `object_cmp` as the only gated column the SET clause names; a column added to the guard later is picked up automatically instead of silently passing. Same shape #5024 gave the decide seam. **A recorded cost is a policy, not a gate.**

⚠️ **One follow-up filed rather than folded: [#5324](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5324)**, ✅ **closed 2026-08-19 — see the entry at the top of `## Next`** — the carve-out rests on `SUPERSEDE_STAMP_SQL`'s collision re-check, which is per **target**, not per **pair**. That acceptance was written when the only de-merger was the human-paced decide transaction holding namespace 5024; #5321 adds a second that runs on a producer run holding **no identity lock**. No belief is retired without a live collision either way — the residue is a `supersedes` edge with stale attribution. **The premise changed, not the verdict: "accepted" was a judgement about frequency, and the frequency moved.** Filed to the Architecture Backlog, deliberately not into #95, since it is a question about `promoteBrainFacts`' report contract rather than identity activation.

**Shipped 2026-08-19 — the rename verdict, a per-entity success record, and the disagreement census** ([#5316](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5316) + [#5317](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5317) + [#5318](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5318), three of #5233's tickets; PR [#5322](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5322) @ `c4dd848d8`) — the three inputs #5233's remedy had to be chosen against, landed together because each is small and all three answer to the same parent. **440 files, 440 passed** (a schema change pulls a wide graph).

⭐ **A semantic-layer rename re-mints every entity id and orphans the old name's store entries permanently — inside one region, with no migration involved.** Measured through the shipped `runWarehouseProducer` against a live schema with the entity's name as the only moving variable: `wh_3b9386f1…` → `wh_700d4644…`, both rows live, `resolvableIds` **empty** (wider than the ticket asked — `acme corp` *and* `1` are both poisoned). The control — the same two runs with no rename — holds the id fixed and keeps the store answering; **without it a producer whose store path was simply broken would satisfy every falsifier while proving nothing.**

⭐ **The ticket's own prediction was REFUTED, and the refuted row is what moved downstream scope.** #5316 framed the rename as reaching PR #5315's `valid_to` stamp. It does not and it **cannot**: the id lands in `subject_cmp`, and at the subject position a proven difference is a **suppressor** in `supersessionCollisionJoin` rather than a driver — pinned byte-for-byte in `collision-sql-pinned.test.ts`. #5315's stamp came through `object_cmp`, which an *extracted* fact reaches by resolving its object through the entity store, and after a rename the store resolves nothing, so that path **abstains instead of stamping**. One root cause, two costs, and the rename produces the quieter one: not a fact retired by its own twin, but an entity nothing can resolve any more while claims about one warehouse row split across two comparables and stop corroborating. Generalizable: **recoverable, invisible and unbounded in time is a worse profile than loud and wrong.**

⭐ **The failure direction is made safe by ROLLBACK rather than by a SET list there is no way to get wrong.** Migration 0206's `brain_warehouse_entity_success` is written **inside that entity's reconcile transaction**, so *"a failure never advances the marker"* is structural — a rollback takes the row with the facts it would have described. It ships as a prefactor with **one writer and no reader**, so #5233's reaper ticket is about the reach rule rather than about a migration, and it is **append-only** because the rule says *the last N successful runs* and a single timestamp answers only N = 1. Growth cost (~438k rows/workspace/year at hourly × 50 entities) and the pruning owner are stated in the migration header rather than left to be discovered.

⚠️ **It keys on the entity NAME, so it inherits the rename hazard measured one ticket over in the same PR.** Any other key makes the reaper's join to `brain_entity` inexpressible; the failure direction is safe (no recorded success means no reaping) and 0206's header says so rather than hiding it.

⭐ **A census, deliberately not a prohibition — and the wrong test is the one that gets written later.** *"No pair is both same and different"* is **false by design**: a genuine homonym is legitimately both and superseding is correct there, so a prohibition fails on that row the day it is written and the repair anyone reaches for is weakening the homonym handling — trading a visible disagreement for the silent merge the veto exists to prevent. The region turned out to have clean structure: the sameness evidence's second disjunct can never contribute (`a = b` against `a <> b`), so every disagreement enters through `object_key`, leaving exactly two mechanisms — `id-behind-a-shared-name` and `sign-the-key-discards` (`lexicalNorm` collapses `[ \t\n\v\f\r_-]+`, so a leading `-` is not in the key while the parser keeps it). `entity-remint` now sits beside `entity-homonym` as **the same shape with the opposite verdict**, with an assertion pinning that nothing in the columns separates them — which is why the remedy cannot live in `object-cmp.ts` at all.

⚠️ **Four falsifiers ship SKIPPED and un-inverted.** They assert what the remedy must deliver and they fail today; inverting them would pin the hazard as correct. Two controls are active and green. ⚠️ A mutation note initially claimed a `0` was read honestly; the measurement says **32**, and the note now says what that 32 means — the unit suites reach `internalQuery` with no pool configured and die on a thrown connection error, **a kill that says nothing about whether the row outlived the transaction** and one that would vanish the moment a suite happened to have a pool.

**Shipped 2026-08-18 — the Coverage Surface's composition layer, with the honesty rules in the SHAPE** ([#5214](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5214); PR [#5312](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5312) @ `0ea4ee9a4`) — ADR-0041's `lib/brain/coverage.ts`, a sibling of `oversight.ts` and never folded into it, joining oversight's numerators with #5213's dated denominators and #5212's per-class contract answers into the wire shape #5215 renders. 15 source mutations, **every row biting**; `coverage-snapshot-pg` 61/61 against real Postgres.

⭐ **Every failure this surface can have is in the FLATTERING direction, so the rules are structural rather than reviewed.** A dropped roster row shrinks a denominator and *raises* a ratio; a lost map edge makes the map read complete; a missing snapshot read as zero says *"nothing to see"* about a class nobody looked at. Hence: `Record<EpisodeSourceClass, …>` so a class added without a coverage answer is a **compile error** (in a list, an absent class and a class with nothing to say are the same row, and those are opposite statements); a ratio **carries the unit it is counted in** and no two classes share one, so blending means adding quantities whose `unit` differs and *"no single number, permanently"* becomes a property of the shape; and *"never enumerated"* is its **own arm with no counts on it**, with `asOf` non-null only on the arm carrying numbers so an empty roster there is a *measured* emptiness. Generalizable: **when every way a metric can break makes it look better, review cannot be the control — the shape has to refuse the flattering reading.**

⭐ **The unit list holds only namable units, so the wire has NO FIELD a mailbox address could occupy** — strictly stronger than the oversight buckets, where a withheld bucket still ships an ordinal. The label decision is re-derived at **read** time from stored disclosure facts, so a stored label cannot outlive the clause that admitted it.

⚠️ **`current` rested on a reading of unbounded age, and only review caught it.** `CHAT_ACTIVITY_PROBES_PER_CYCLE` is 20 per hourly cycle and the upsert carries an unprobed unit's reading forward, so a 5,000-channel workspace re-probes every **~10 days** — a ten-day-old vendor answer was compared against a 24-hour threshold and returned as a present-tense all-clear. Fixed by a `reading-expired` arm plus `current`'s own `checkedAt`, which needed a clock: `composeCoverage` now takes `at: Date`, **required and never defaulted**. The staleness ladder is a sequence of refusals with the word licensed last and only by a measurement: sick pipe → class cannot ask → never probed → **reading expired** → quiet → lag vs cadence.
