⭐ **Two mutations read `0` first time and BOTH fixes were outside the suite.** *"Green is read off the evidence date alone"* was absorbed by a redundant second `evidenceAt !== null` at the use site — so a rule the module header calls load-bearing had a test that **could not see it removed**; the derivation became single-point and now kills 7. Generalizable: **a duplicated condition at the use site is a mutation sponge, and it hides exactly the rule the header says matters most.** The runtime totality guard was **deleted** rather than fixed — both loops iterate `EPISODE_SOURCE_CLASSES` and `EpisodeSourceClass` *is* its member type, so it could not fire, and it threw an error asserting a different diagnosis. The cadence property is nested **inside** `class-contract.ts`'s `reports` arm so a class cannot declare the capability without the number, and is deliberately **not** read from `ATLAS_KNOWLEDGE_SYNC_INTERVAL_HOURS` — a knob an admin could use to define staleness away.

**Shipped 2026-08-18 — an untested Layer's outcome arms, one spelling of a refusal payload, and TWO measurements that were vacuous on their first run** ([#5305](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5305) + [#5303](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5303); PR [#5311](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5311) @ `1846187e5`) — a four-issue batch of which **two were already on `main`**: #5299 and #5306 shipped in `888a0d7db` with gates and fixtures and were simply never closed. Verified on the branch rather than taken on trust (both gates green, 30/30 and 24/24 fixtures) and left for the maintainer to close.

⭐ **"The wrapper's own tests cover it" was not the same claim.** `layers.test.ts` punted `ImplementationStatusOverrideLive`'s arms to the wrapper, but the wrapper's tests pin what the *wrapper* returns, and every gates-pass arm is the **Layer's translation** of that return into a Tag value. `no-config` is the clearest case — the wrapper calls it a skip and the Layer promotes it to `outcome: "error"`, so **no wrapper test can see the promotion**. Six mutations measured RED. Generalizable: **a delegation defence is only valid where the delegate's assertions and yours are about the same value.**

⚠️ **Both measurements in this PR were VACUOUS on their first run, in two different ways.** The `catchAll` mutation reported green and it was the **harness** lying: `error: errorMessage(err),` occurs five times in `layers.ts` — once per Layer with a `catchAll` — and a non-global `s///` mutated the first, belonging to a Layer this suite does not cover; the mutations are now line-targeted and the runner **refuses to print a verdict for a pattern that did not match**. The ninth-required-field mutation reported zero errors because `@useatlas/types` resolves through `exports` to `./dist/*.d.ts`, so **editing `packages/types/src` changes nothing a consumer sees until the package is rebuilt**; re-measured with a rebuild in the loop it produces 14 compile errors in three places at once. Generalizable: **a mutation that reports green has two readings — the test is weak, or the mutation never landed — and they are indistinguishable without checking that the edit took.**

⭐ **The issue's stated premise for a type change was FALSE, measured rather than reasoned, and the honest answer was to keep the shape and add the test.** #5305 proposed a discriminated union to make `{ ...zeroCounts, outcome: "applied" }` illegal; under such a union it **still type-checks**, because `zeroCounts` carries both fields at exactly those types. The headline mutation is a wrong *value*, not a wrong *shape*, and no arrangement of variants rejects it. What the union *would* buy is narrower and real — `{ ...zeroCounts, outcome: "error" }`, an error outcome with no message, is legal today — and is **not taken**, because every producing arm of `error` is now pinned scrubbed and the cost lands on the future `/health` consumer. Recorded beside the shapes in `layers.ts` rather than only in the PR. ⭐ **`satisfies z.ZodType<VocabularyRefusalDetail>` is strictly stronger than an inference pin**, because it cannot be defeated by an optional member — and the whole `screenRefusalDetails` describe in `migrate.test.ts` passes **unchanged, not one case edited**, which is what makes "contract preserved" a measurement instead of a claim.

**Shipped 2026-08-18 — the pre-customer clean-break window CLOSED, and the embeddable widget joined the code surface** (no issue — the two maintainer calls PR [#5309](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5309) deferred; PR [#5310](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5310) @ `23df44301`) — both turned out to have their answer already written down somewhere in the tree.

⭐ **A standing authorization and an enforced gate contradicted each other for two months, and the gate wins because it is the thing that actually runs.** The posture is stamped 2026-05-19 and grants *"schema migrations can hard-drop"*; on 2026-06-18 `check-migration-rename-discipline.sh` landed (#3686) and CI has rejected single-phase `DROP COLUMN`/`RENAME COLUMN` on every new migration since — that gate's own header names migration `0133` as the last clean break it was willing to authorize, **citing this posture as what authorized it**. Closing the window ratifies what CI already does. ⭐ **A posture keyed on an unrecorded fact can never be re-verified**: *"no external customers"* is operational and no file records it, by design, so no gate can ever check that section — leaving a standing authorization for destructive change that nothing measures, which is the defect class `practices.md` was written about sitting **inside the document that authorizes destruction**. ⭐ **The risk is asymmetric, and that settles the residue**: acting as though the window is open when it is closed hard-drops a column out from under a real customer, unrecoverable; acting as though it is closed when it is open costs one shim you did not need, and CI was going to make you write it anyway.

⚠️ **Rows 2 and 3 are a POLICY CHOICE, not ratification, and the file marks them as such.** Row 1 (schema) writes down what CI has enforced since June; unversioned contract changes and no-deprecation-shims have **no equivalent gate**, so closing them is a decision — reopening either is a dated one-line edit that does not touch row 1.

⚠️ **The six citing ADRs stand as written, and the one flagged as changing turned out to be stale in the other direction.** 0007, 0015, 0022, 0024, 0027 and 0035 keep their decisions — a decision made under a window that was open at the time does not become wrong when the window shuts; only the authority stops being available to *new* work, which is exactly the re-verification ADR-0022 asked for "at build time". ⚠️ **ADR-0027 was flagged for a re-read of its raw-SQL off-switch deferral, and that deferral had already been REVERSED**: #4095 was greenlit 2026-06-29 and shipped 2026-07-01 as the `raw_sql` gate-1 category, tests and all. Filed as [#5350](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5350). ⭐ Generalizable, and the sharper half of this entry: **auditing whether a rationale still holds is not the same as auditing whether its conclusion is still the code's behaviour** — the sweep checked six premises carefully and never checked one subject, then attached a correct-sounding correction to a claim that was already false, lending the stale half its credibility.

⭐ **The widget question was already answered in the package's own stylesheet.** `@useatlas/react` shipped `dark ? oneDark : oneLight` over `bg-zinc-100 dark:bg-zinc-800` — the #5306 defect byte for byte, **reaching customers rather than us**. #5309 deferred *does the widget follow the host page or the Atlas brand?*; the stylesheet opens *"The embedded chat is a **product surface**"*, and Design Principle 5 defines the code surface **by element, not by app**, so the `dark` prop stays a **chrome** seam and no longer reaches the code pane — because "identical on every surface **and mode**" is a statement about mode. ⭐ **Three copies of `--code-*` are sanctioned rather than drift**, since the package installs into somebody else's `node_modules` and cannot symlink a repo file; the guard therefore enforces **value-equality across all three** — a symlink where one is possible, equal values where it is not, and any *other* file defining `--code-*` still refused outright. Generalizable: **when a copy is structurally unavoidable, gate the values rather than the copy count.** ⚠️ This reaches customers only on the next `@useatlas/react` publish (0.2.0 → 0.3.0), a separate deliberate act — which landed the same day at `27d961988`.

**Shipped 2026-08-18 — three harness fixes, each shipping its own enforcement because what all three had in common was a documented rule that nothing measured** ([#5299](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5299) + [#5306](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5306) + the first [#5302](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5302) extractions; PR [#5309](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5309) @ `888a0d7db`) — the spin-outs from the harness burn, closed with gates rather than with prose. Five mutations applied and observed red on the doc-path guard; 31 fixtures across the two new gates.

⭐ **Each of the guard's three requirements comes from a way the HAND check failed**, which is the only reason to trust the shape: *never truncate a finding* (the manual grep piped through `cut -c1-140`, **did match** `deps-update.md:96` and `release.md:117`, and the visible prefix was declared a false positive); *scan the whole repo* (the manual scan covered `.claude/**` + `docs/agents/**`, while three of six live references were in `docs/development/**` and one was in shipped TypeScript); *commands and counts, not only paths* (five of the six were references to a **deleted command**, which a path-only guard misses entirely).

⭐ **The separator needed sharpening and the measurement is why.** *"Only check a backticked path whose first segment is a real top-level directory"* produced **230 findings** once the scan widened past `.claude/**`, because `plugins/`, `scripts/`, `docs/` and `public/` are also **sub-directory** names — so `plugins/secrets.ts` is a repo-rooted-*looking* abbreviation of a five-segment path. A reference now also resolves if a tracked path ends with it at a segment boundary, which is **self-tuning**: a 2-segment abbreviation matches easily while the audit's own `packages/api/src/lib/semantic.ts` would need all five and stays red. ⚠️ Counts are a **short registry of 5 phrases** and the gate's summary says so — an unregistered count is not checked, so the audit's *"14 chat components"* against 42 would still ship. One registered phrase kills a hand-maintained census that had been rewritten five times across two PRs, and it caught both of this PR's own edits.

⭐ **The fourth commit is the interesting one: both new gates were green before commit and RED immediately after.** While their fixture suites and allowlist were untracked, `git ls-files` skipped them — then the doc-path guard reported 12 findings **in its own test data**, and the brand ratchet went 1118 → 1121 because the SQL-pane falsifier has to name `bg-zinc-100` in order to assert its absence. Same shape `check-gate-fixtures-wired.sh` documents. Fixed **scoped, not loosened**: a file-scoped exemption for input files, with a fixture proving it does not leak to a second file. Generalizable: **a gate's own fixtures are its subject, so it is untested until it has run on a tree that contains them.**

⚠️ **The brand ratchet is a ratchet because zero-tolerance was unaffordable, and the header states the limit that leaves.** `packages/web/src` holds 1,117 hardcoded neutrals; a zero-tolerance rule needs an allowlist longer than the code it guards, so the count may only go **down**. The ground was then measured in a browser off the **shipped stylesheet** rather than off the source: light `lab(99.43 +0.15 +1.52)` (b\* positive → warm), dark `lab(4.16 -2.36 +1.04)` (a\* negative → forest), where it had been `#fff` and `#09090b`. ⚠️ **The deployment-posture context came out marked STALE on extraction** — the finding PR #5310 then acted on the same day.

**Shipped 2026-08-17 — the agent harness burned to one page, and the day's findings were one defect in six costumes** (no issue — a continuation of [#5251](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5251), recorded here rather than filed after the fact; PR [#5298](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5298) @ `3c1b6dfba`; spin-outs [#5299](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5299), [#5302](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5302), [#5306](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5306)) — 26 of 34 commands, all five review agents and `docs/agents/` deleted, **~6,250 net lines**, replaced by one page: [`docs/agents/practices.md`](../../docs/agents/practices.md). Eight **operational** runbooks survive (`release`, `publish`, `deploy`, `ci`, `verify-mcp-cli`, `verify-prod-signup`, `dev`, `deps-update`) on a line worth reusing: **delete the practice whose enforcement was your own diligence; keep the sequence whose enforcement is an external system that fails loudly.** Two are cited from live code — `scripts/ci-local.sh` and `ci-local-report.sh` defer to `ci.md` for the launch-and-watch protocol, ADR-0008 names `release.md` the canonical tagging path — so the first burn had already left a script and a ratified ADR pointing at nothing.

⭐ **The diagnosis is nominal compliance, and two of the three obvious suspects were ruled out by measurement rather than argument.** The recurring *round N finds defects inside round N−1's fixes* pattern is **not** the vendored reviewers: diffed against `pr-review-toolkit`, all four keep upstream's full methodology and add the Atlas standards on top, and are shorter only because they drop upstream's fake `<example>` dialogues and its Sentry/`errorIds` references. It is **not** the round machinery either — the ratio stop and the 3-round cap fired correctly on [#5148](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5148) and [#5170](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5170). What actually happens is that **each countermeasure is satisfied *nominally* and the defect moves up one level**: [#5027](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5027) shipped fixes with no falsifier (eleven probed, eleven zeros) → Step 6 required one → #5170's mutation battery measured **five of the author's own assertions inert** → [#5289](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5289) shipped **four falsifiers that could not fail** (a ROLLBACK-count assertion whose fixture rolls back *successfully*; a mock failing both its COMMIT and its ROLLBACK so neither indeterminacy condition was independently falsifiable). Every rule was obeyed. **More prose was the move that had already failed three times.**

**The fix is a seam, not a fourth rule.** Step 6 establishes that a falsifier *exists*; nothing established that it can *fail*, and those two claims came apart every time anyone measured. `fix-vs-finding` gains a second **object** rather than a second mode — a falsifier that cannot fail is *closed the instance, left the class* applied to the instrument, which is the question that agent already asks — dispatched from a new **Step 6b**. It holds no `Bash` either, so it **names** the mutation and the author runs it: the same panel-names/author-measures split [#5267](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5267) created after a panel agent's restore reverted the author's uncommitted work on [#5260](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5260).

⭐ **`/review-panel` Step 6 contradicted itself in FOUR places, and the retired rule was the one most readers would hit.** Its body retired the *"name every round, build in the closing one"* split after #5037 measured that deferral is a bet on which round is last — while its **heading**, its cost paragraph, its RUN-the-mutant rule and the Rules summary all still stated the retired version. This is the *"a prose sweep can half-apply its own correction"* shape from [#5031](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5031), occurring in the file that documents it.

⭐ **A truncated check output becomes a confident false negative.** A dangling-reference grep was run before the restore commit and **did match** `deps-update.md:96` and `release.md:117` — then its output was piped through `cut -c1-140`, which truncated away the `/pr`, `/reset` and `/changelog` references, and the visible prefix was declared a false positive. Its scan set was also too narrow: `docs/development/` was never scanned, and three of six live instances lived there. Six dangling pointers shipped past a check that had already found two of them. Both failure modes are acceptance criteria on #5299: **never truncate findings, scan the whole repo rather than a hand-listed set, and catch deleted *commands*, not only paths** — a path-only guard would have missed five of the six.

⭐ **`AGENTS.md` had drifted into contradicting CLAUDE.md about a MERGE-BLOCKING gate.** The 32 KB Codex-facing parallel copy claimed type-aware linting was *"available but off by default"*, while `ci.yml` runs `lint-type-aware` under the `ci` umbrella and its own comment states that a type-aware regression blocks merges. An agent trusting the stale copy skips a required gate — the [#5083](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5083) failure CLAUDE.md itself cites. Cut to a pointer plus the eight Codex/WSL lines that exist nowhere else (Windows Git cannot checkout Linux symlinks; put the Linux bun binary first in PATH). ⚠️ The same shape re-fired **during the merge**: [#5300](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5300) bumped the local-gate count 36 → 38 in `CLAUDE.md` and in `ci.md`'s frontmatter and left the **body of that same file** at *"all 35 of them"*, with three real gates missing from the roster (`brain-settings-doc`, `gate-fixtures-wired`, `lighthouse-report-paths`). One fact in three homes, two updated, disagreeing within twenty minutes.

⚠️ **The brand ships in `brand.css` and the product surface bypasses it** ([#5306](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5306), found while generating `packages/web/DESIGN.md`). The token layer is **correct**; the components do not use it. `layout.tsx:61` hardcodes `bg-white text-zinc-900 dark:bg-zinc-950 dark:text-zinc-100`, and Tailwind utilities beat the `@layer base` rule, so the ground is pure white instead of paper-lite and **pure neutral zinc** instead of the forest-tinted dark ADR-0023 §4 specifies as explicitly *"NOT pure gray"* — while the skip-link on **line 62** does it correctly with `focus:bg-background`. `sql-block.tsx:46` renders `bg-zinc-100 dark:bg-zinc-800`: a light **grey** pane where PRODUCT.md's code surface says *"always-dark terminal windows … identical on every surface and mode"*, with `--code-*` existing only in `apps/www`. Neither Sora nor JetBrains Mono is loaded anywhere in `packages/web`, so Principle 4's font pair is unimplemented on the surface customers use. All three values are already specified in PRODUCT.md, which makes the fix mechanical and the drift unforced.

⭐ **Same version string ≠ same contents, one level below npm.** `impeccable update` reported **v4.1.1** — exactly what the tracked copy already claimed — then rewrote 8 files (+319/−55, `serve-question.mjs` alone +238). #5251 had updated that directory *"to v4.1.1 in place"* and the version matched afterwards, which is precisely why nobody looked again. Four real defects in the refreshed vendor code (a click-race that can kill the question daemon under a live page, a 15s-vs-600s grace disagreement, two unguarded scan paths) are **deliberately not patched**: a local patch to vendored code is the fork #5251 spent 153 files deleting, and the next update clobbers it silently. Report upstream, do not carry.

**The record moved, which is why this entry is longer than usual.** The user-level memory store (249 files, 75 of them already unreachable from their own index) was **deleted outright** after a verified tarball restore-test; `MEMORY.md` was rewritten with a bar rather than a backlog. `practices.md` names this file as *the record* — so from today ROADMAP.md is the only durable home for findings that used to live in memory, and an arc that goes unrecorded here is genuinely lost. Also this session: `.impeccable.md` deleted (a stale predecessor still describing the dark-teal-on-black identity **retired 2026-06-23**, read by nothing in the current skill); `PRODUCT.md` brought onto the current schema with **positioning confirmed as the data-grounded company brain** (ADR-0038) over the validated-SQL and deploy-anywhere framings, the **developer confirmed still primary** despite the KB/chat/dashboards build-out, and an `Evidence on Hand` section that names three real proofs and then **states the absence** — no customer names, logos, testimonials, case studies or usage counts are confirmed, so no surface may invent one; and `CONTEXT-MAP.md` adopting the multi-context layout while **pointing every one of its 18 rows at the un-split root `CONTEXT.md`**, because writing the aspirational tree would have reproduced the defect the same hour it was diagnosed ([#5302](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5302) tracks the split).

**Shipped 2026-08-17 — bundle 3: an unobserved seed Layer and a refusal that outlives its log** ([#5297](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5297) = [#5273](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5273) + [#5112](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5112); PR [#5301](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5301) @ `2d53ce756`) — `rounds: 1 (code reviewers 7m / 7m / 12m in parallel; fix-vs-finding 3m / 3m; comment sweep 12m of reported agent time)`, one commit per member. **28 findings, all NEW SURFACE, zero defect-in-prior-fix** — the loop closed after one round because the round's own fixes were then audited by `fix-vs-finding` (two REPRODUCED, both fixed in the same round) and by the comment sweep (eight more), leaving nothing outstanding.

⭐ **Widening review scope to the enclosing FILE found a live production defect in a Layer the issue never named.** #5273 was scoped to two boot-seed Layers; reading `effect/layers.ts` whole turned up a THIRD, `OpenApiDatasourceCatalogSeedLive`, filling its documented-as-scrubbed `error` field straight from the boot wrapper's message — the #5239 asymmetry (*one field, two producers, two guarantees*) for the **third** time, after #5239 fixed it once and #5273's own tests pinned it twice. Fixed, covered, and **mechanised**: a lexical guard now fails on that spelling. Its NEGATIVE control earned its keep immediately — the first matcher also flagged `MigrationLive`, whose `error` is *deliberately* raw because it feeds the #1988 boot-failure line, and a guard that flags a legitimate producer gets switched off within a week.

⚠️ **A scrubber can be a floor rather than a filter, and `fix-vs-finding` is what said so.** #5112's new throw interpolated a caught DB error into `region_migrations.error_message`, which `admin-residency.ts` returns VERBATIM to a workspace admin. The first fix scrubbed at the persistence site; the check answered **REPRODUCED**, because `errorMessage` rewrites `scheme://user:pass@` and *nothing else* — `connect ECONNREFUSED 10.0.3.7:5432`, a pg `DETAIL: Key (…)=(…)` row fragment and internal column spellings all survive it. The throw site now carries no driver text at all (raw to `log.error`, generic on the customer-served field), which is the same split the import routes' 500s already use. Generalisable: **scrubbing downstream is not a substitute for not putting infrastructure detail on a customer-served field.**

⚠️ **A VALUE export added to `@useatlas/types` breaks every scaffold, and only `Deploy Validation` can see it.** `prepare-templates.sh` copies `packages/api/src` into the scaffold template, which installs the PUBLISHED package — pinned `^0.7.0`, so it cannot even reach the workspace's `0.10.0`. A **type** import is erased and free; a **value** import resolves at runtime against a version predating the symbol (`Export VOCABULARY_REFUSAL_DETAIL_CAP doesn't exist in target module`). `Standalone Example Build` passed on the same commit because it compiles against the local build — which is exactly what hides it. The constant moved to `packages/api`; the wire type stayed published. Distinct from the [types-scaffold gotcha](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5203) recorded at T3: that one was about `@useatlas/types` vs `@useatlas/schemas`, this one is about **value vs type** in either.

⚠️ **Nine of my own comment claims were measured FALSE and corrected rather than shipped**, which is the counterfactual-comments lesson arriving at scale: a shared Layer does NOT leak the first test's stub (each `Effect.provide` builds its own MemoMap); the fixture's distinct LENGTHS do not defeat a permutation (`toEqual` on contents does); the new pg case does not pin the `::jsonb` cast (Postgres implicitly casts); `toContain("COMMIT")` asserts presence, not position; `v != null` kills a test by REJECTING `null`, not by admitting `undefined`; a named coverage file did not exist; the lexical guard makes a fourth instance *less likely*, not impossible; its runtime-assembled literal defended against nothing (the guard cannot read its own file); and the docs claimed a count appears on a status surface that [#5304](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5304) exists to build. The sweep also found three **structural** defects the new code introduced — a docstring severed from its declaration by an inserted constant, a function's docstring left on a return-type interface, and a colon opening a list a rewrite had deleted.

⭐ **A self-satisfying assertion, from the quotation trap, twice in one PR.** A lookup for `"WILL DROP"` matched the post-COMMIT confirmation — which *quotes* the phrase to explain it — so the join-key assertion compared the confirmation to itself and passed while the per-refusal line was absent entirely. Root cause was a STATIC import of the new constant hoisting `lib/brain/vocabulary.ts` above the logger `mock.module`, the same module-scope-`createLogger()` trap `cleanup.test.ts` documents from the other side. **A lexical matcher cannot tell a quotation from the thing it quotes** — match wording only the real line has.

⚠️ **Both halves of a feature's coverage can be hand-written statements ABOUT the same columns.** #5112's mock suite grepped `capturedQueries` for a substring and never executed the SQL; its pg falsifier seeded the row with its own `INSERT`. Renaming the writer's column left every test green while failing **every migration carrying a refusal** in production. `recordVocabularyRefusals` is now exported and driven against the migrated schema — the only thing that confronts a statement with the real table.

**Also measured:** 36 mutations across both members, every one RED but one (`Array.isArray` in the payload screen kills nothing, and now says so beside itself); `bundle-identity.md` regenerated (suite size 15 → 18, every kill count unchanged) and `vocabulary.md` byte-identical despite `vocabulary.ts` gaining a required parameter; two arity pins added because *neither* function taking the new required token had its requiredness pinned; and `--affected` reported 13 red locally of which **10 were parallel `-pg` contention** on one scratch database — green one at a time. Follow-ups: [#5303](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5303) (one spelling of the refusal type), [#5304](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5304) (the record exists; the human cannot reach it), [#5305](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5305) (`ImplementationStatusOverrideLive` still has no Layer test).


**Shipped 2026-08-17 — bundle 2: the `scripts/` gate lane, and six of the author's own comments asserted guarantees the code did not deliver** ([#5296](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5296) = [#5097](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5097) + [#5174](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5174) + [#4738](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4738); PR [#5300](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5300) @ `e6362e0fe`) — three items on `ci.yml`'s `drift` job, one commit per member so a bad one drops without the others. ~40 falsifiers built and measured RED. ⚠️ **The panel left on the 3-round cap, not on a clean round**, disclosed in the PR body rather than discovered later.

⭐ **`--check` compares BYTES, so a cell recording "this measured nothing" becomes the expected output forever.** #5077 closed one member of that class and **removed the detection for the rest**, so a mutated run that skipped tests rendered a deflated count as an honest number with no warning anywhere. `Cell` becomes a discriminated union and `unmeasuredRows()` the single refusal, keyed on the **discriminant rather than on any cause** — which is what refuses a fifth member by construction. Generalizable: **a byte-comparison gate blesses whatever it is first shown, so the thing to key a refusal on is the shape that makes a cell meaningless, never the list of ways it got that way.**

⭐ **In every shell falsifier the exit code stayed unchanged and only the OUTPUT assertion discriminated — five independent confirmations.** That is the whole argument for `check()` requiring a discriminating phrase, and it is why three suites gained more fixtures than the findings asked for. The sharpest instance: deleting `mutate.ts`'s refusal entirely left exit 1 intact and only the phrase missing, so without the phrase assertion the fixture would have reported `ok` against a runner carrying no refusal at all — **exactly how #5077 measured the predecessor vacuous and deleted it.** Generalizable: **a gate's exit code is the weakest thing about it; assert the sentence, not the status.**

⭐ **A ratchet must parse as the READER parses.** #5174's retired-wording ratchet runs over **rendered bodies, never over source**, because the renderer's comments *name* each retired wording in order to explain why it is retired — a lexical source scan flags its own documentation, and the only ways out are deleting the explanation or exempting the file. Every ratchet case also carries a positive control, since a ratchet alone is satisfied by a renderer returning the empty string.

⚠️ **#4738's gate could not fail for the class it was filed for, and the reason is structural: it SKIPS `COPY . .`, and that one line bundles every package in the repo.** It reported *"All 54 COPY sources covered"* and exit 0 while `packages/okf-bundle` was copied into the api runtime image **by name** and watched by nobody — `deploy/docs` watched it; the api, which imports `@atlas/okf-bundle/wire` at runtime, did not. Membership is now by **NAME, not by the `workspace:*` protocol**, because every plugin declares `@useatlas/plugin-sdk` as a peerDependency *range* while the image copies it in and bun links by name ignoring range satisfaction. Coverage is probed with a **synthetic source file** rather than the bare directory, since `packages/**/package.json` matches the manifest and nothing else — a bare `packages/foo` entry watches none of its source and reads as coverage against a directory test.

⚠️ **Six of the author's own comments asserted guarantees the code did not deliver, and none was caught by the author.** Both `fix-vs-finding` passes came back REPRODUCED — round-1 fixes each reproducing the defect they fixed, one layer over, with the closing spelling already a few lines away in code the same commit had written (`pgHint: boolean` making `{ kind: "empty", pgHint: true }` legal while the sibling arm pinned the literal four lines below; a vacuity floor that *counted* the vacuity and still printed `All 0 COPY sources covered`). The closing sweep then found the same class in the guard the PR added **last**: `check-gate-fixtures-wired.sh` claimed its grep distinguished a `run:` line from a comment, it did not, and the hole was **live** — two workflow comments carry full repo-relative paths, so deleting either suite's `run:` step left the gate green off the prose alone. ⭐ **On a change whose whole subject is guards that make false claims about what they measured, the author's own false claims are the most useful measurement in it.**

**Kicked off 2026-08-13 — `v0.2.x` — Brain M5: Identity Activation** ([milestone #95](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/95), renamed from *Retrieval Depth*) — five issues, strictly sequenced: the enrollment surface ([#5196](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5196)) → the tier-1 warehouse producer ([#5042](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5042)) → the entity store ([#5043](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5043)) → prod-verification tracking ([#5197](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5197)), on [ADR-0039](../../docs/adr/0039-the-warehouse-producer-emits-only-what-a-human-enrolled.md) and an ADR-0036 §T10 amendment. The kickoff was a grill rather than a ROADMAP read, because the milestone's **name and its contents were different milestones**: #95 was called *Retrieval Depth* and contained only #5042 + #5043, which are identity activation, and **no T7 retrieval issue existed anywhere in the repo**.

⭐ **Retrieval depth left the cut, and the thing that decided it was a test ADR-0036 does not own.** [`docs/prd/company-atlas.md`](../../docs/prd/company-atlas.md) hands the cut back to §T10 by name while supplying one criterion — *"a milestone that advances none of the eight finish conditions is a milestone worth questioning."* Retrieval depth advances none, and that is a **position rather than an oversight**: the PRD declines a retrieval-quality benchmark as a finish condition at all, on §T1's *the adoption gap is trust, not leaderboard score*. Identity activation advances condition 4 (recognition — the arc's originating bug); the coverage surface advances condition 6, the only **unbuilt** one. **Nothing renumbered** — a removal, not an insertion, so M6 write-back and M7 /ee keep their numbers after #5044 already discharged one renumber ([#5198](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5198) tracks re-justification). What left is only the *deepening*: T7's FTS-first floor **shipped in M1** and is what `searchBrain` runs on today.

⭐ **The producer is enrollment-scoped, and the reason is arithmetic.** Every fact lands as a `draft` needing human publish — `reconcile.ts:777`, where migration 0180's default **is** the review gate (#4769) — and there is no auto-approve arm for facts. The warehouse producer is the first source in the arc emitting at **machine cadence**: ten thousand accounts × eight dimensions is eighty thousand drafts, and `reconcile.ts:856` mints a **fresh draft** per re-observation, so a changed number costs a draft *and* a tension edge. Auto-publish was rejected — ADR-0036 §T9 says warehouse facts are *"gated by the same core review gate"* and PRD condition 2 admits no exceptions — as was bulk-approve, which keeps every invariant's letter and empties condition 2 of meaning. So the scope moved instead of the gate: **a human enrolls `(entity, dimension)` pairs; the producer never widens its own reach.** [ADR-0032](../../docs/adr/0032-amendments-refine-never-grow.md) is the precedent, not a new pattern — *amendments refine coverage that exists; enrich is the human-initiated act with consequences.*

⚠️ **#5042's "resolve before building" blocker was RETIRED, not answered — it had been stale since it was written.** The issue called warehouse↔warehouse re-emission a known unresolved decision to settle before any code. ADR-0037 §4 already rules it *"tension-only, which is the Fog #5008 records rather than a gap"* — a recorded, accepted consequence. It was a **volume problem wearing a specification problem's clothes**, and enrollment is the volume answer. Worth generalizing: an issue body is a claim about the tree *at authoring time*, and a blocker written before its resolving ADR shipped will still read as live.

⚠️ **M5 closes on prod row counts, not merge** ([#5197](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5197), on #5000's precedent) — because enrollment-scoping makes activation **proportional to what someone enrolled**, and a producer nobody enrolls anything into leaves ADR-0037's four dormant mechanisms exactly as dead as today with every test green. That is **M1's failure restated**: *"flag on" and "source connected" are two facts, the sync reports green on either, and only a row count separates them.* The entity store sharpens it — `absent-means-abstain` is its **designed** behaviour, so an empty store and a working one are indistinguishable from inside the code.

**Frontier as of 2026-08-16: the mechanism cut is DONE** — #5196, #5042 and #5043 all shipped, so every ADR-0037 mechanism now has a producer and a reader. What remains is [#5197](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5197) (prod row counts, which is what closes the milestone), plus one follow-up that is not on the closing path: [#5233](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5233) (the entity store's four deferrals); [#5249](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5249), [#5248](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5248) and [#5257](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5257) shipped 2026-08-15, [#5255](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5255), [#5277](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5277), [#5229](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5229) (the producer's checked-in mutations spec) and [#5228](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5228) (the cadence trigger, under a run lock) on 2026-08-16 — leaving #5197 and #5233 as milestone #95's whole open set. ✅ **#5197 is now UNBLOCKED** — `v0.2.9` shipped its migrations to prod (`prod` @ `e47d4655c`, six migrations 0198–0203 in 47 ms), so the tables its row counts read now exist. It was structurally unsatisfiable until that release, on the [prod-verification-is-post-release](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/3974) precedent; what it needs now is a human reading prod, not a merge. [#5230](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5230) shipped ahead of #5228 as its coupling required. **Updated 2026-08-17: that prod read HAPPENED, and it paid for itself immediately** — every enrolled pair refused, which is [#5284](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5284) (fixed, PR [#5285](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5285)); #5197 stays open because the counts still have to be re-read against the fix, and milestone #95's open set is now **#5197, #5233 and [#5286](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5286)** — the last of which makes staging unable to falsify the fix, so the re-read is a prod read again. ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-18 — the re-read produced counts, and every criterion is met on prod `us`.** `v0.2.10` (`87562eb3a`, migration 0205 applied 21:06 UTC) cleared both blockers: the backfill resolved **2/2** enrollments to a real connection group (`still_flat = 0`), and `organization` is published under exactly one group in that workspace, so #5286's ambiguity never bit. ⚠️ **The zeros before the Run were the ENABLEMENT gate, not the producer** — `ATLAS_BRAIN_WAREHOUSE_CADENCE_ENABLED` was unset (platform default `"false"`) with no workspace row either, so both halves of the AND were off and the cadence fiber had never started; the counts moved only after a human pressed the [#5287](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5287) Run control at 21:30:46 UTC. **This is M1's failure caught BEFORE it was misread** — the issue's own warning is what stopped a zero being written up as a falsification. Row counts, prod `us` (`eu` and `apac` are 0 across the board — no enrollments there): **enrolled 2 · entity_store 0→1 · facts 10→12 · warehouse facts 0→2 · `subject_cmp` non-null 0→2 · `object_cmp` 0**. Both warehouse facts (`organization/name = Atlas`, `organization/plan_tier = trial`) landed `draft` and reached `published` at 21:35:26 UTC through the ordinary content-mode gate — `mode.publish` in `admin_action_log`, human actor with id, email and request id, `promotedBrainFacts: 2`. ⭐ **The tier guard reported itself rather than being asserted**: the same audit row carries **`supersessionsHeldBack: 0`** and `supersededFactCount: 0` — a *measured* zero, which is what criterion 5 asked for in place of an unexercised guard called "working". The entity store's one row resolves a real surface to a stable id (`organization` · key `DWBVFIhx…` · canonical `Atlas` · `wh_1ba69d69…`). **Milestone #95's open set is now #5233 alone.** ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-19 — #5233 shipped as [#5319](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5319)/[#5320](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5320)/[#5321](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5321) (PR [#5323](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5323) @ `0ecbb5b76`), so milestone #95 is closed — see the entry at the top of `## Next`. [#5314](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5314) stays open and moved to the Architecture Backlog.** ⚠️ **One finding, filed not folded: [#5314](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5314)** — the producer's `detail.connectionGroup` records the YAML `connection:` hint (always null on a DB-backed semantic layer) rather than the resolved group, so a group-scoped fact's provenance reads as flat. A **residue of #5284**: the fix corrected which connection *executes* and not the value *recorded*, and the correct `group` is already threaded two functions away at `warehouse-producer.ts:3753`. `detail` is explicitly non-load-bearing and every structural provenance key is correct, so no published fact is wrong and no #5197 criterion was affected — which is why it is a separate issue rather than a reason to hold #5197 open. Generalizable: **a fix that corrects behaviour and not the record leaves the record contradicting the behaviour**, and the audit trail is where nobody looks until it matters. ⚠️ **[#5233](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5233)'s risk profile changed on 2026-08-18** — it was a reaper bug on a store with **0** rows, and the store now has a live row in prod. It stays theoretical only while the cadence is off, which it still is; turning `ATLAS_BRAIN_WAREHOUSE_CADENCE_ENABLED` on starts a reaper-less store accumulating on a schedule, so **#5233 should ship before the cadence does**, whichever milestone it is filed under.


**GRILLED 2026-08-14 — the denominator grill milestone #96 was gated on, resolved in one session; [ADR-0041](../../docs/adr/0041-the-coverage-surface-counts-what-it-can-see.md), five issues [#5212](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5212)→[#5213](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5213)→[#5214](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5214)→[#5215](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5215)→[#5216](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5216), strictly sequenced.** The denominator is **evidence-side** (survey units: channels, mailboxes, enrolled entity-dimensions — the org-chart reading refused unless a human authors the mapping), every count **credential-relative** ("of what Atlas's credentials can see", never "of the company"), and every unit in one of **three states** — surveyed / enumerated / **unenumerable, which is a mark and never a number**. ⭐ **The single coverage number is refused permanently** — the layers are incommensurable, and a gauge rewards narrowing the denominator while punishing scope-widening (a ratio legitimately *drops* when the map grows). Stale is a **measured lag** (vendor activity newer than our newest evidence by more than the class cadence; no knob, quiet ≠ stale) with **"unverified since \<date\>"** as the says-less fallback; "thin" gets no computed badge. Labels split from counts: counts always disclosable (the #4825 sanction extended from facts to survey units), labels only by **deliberate act** or **per-class vendor-public, default closed**. The page is the **evolved `/admin/brain` overview** (admin-gated, deliberately no new permission flag — the #5188 seeded-roles class), fed by dated snapshot cycles, composed by a sibling of `oversight.ts` inheriting its fabrication discipline. Closes on the #5197 pattern: a human reads the page on prod and every part of the spoken statement checks against ground truth ([#5216](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5216)). PRD conditions 6 and 7 got their clarifying edits. **M5 stays the in-flight milestone** — #5213's warehouse half depended on [#5196](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5196), which landed first, so #5213 shipped both halves together on 2026-08-15. [#5214](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5214) (the read-time label policy and the page) is next on the #96 sequence.

**The coverage surface got its own milestone and deliberately no M-number** ([milestone #96](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/96)) — PRD condition 6, the only unbuilt finish condition and *"the one a person meets first."* It derives from the PRD rather than any T-decision, so an M-number would misrepresent it as part of ADR-0036's mechanism cut. **Not yet issue-scoped: it needs its own grill first**, because *coverage of what* has no denominator anywhere in the tree — `oversight.ts` computes numerators only, and the denominator collides with the ACL asymmetry that module exists to manage. PRD conditions 1 and 3 (unproven rather than unbuilt, and *cheap to test*) are held for that milestone rather than scattered into M5. ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-20, 5/5 — see the entry at the top of `## Next`.** The grill happened ([ADR-0041](../../docs/adr/0041-the-coverage-surface-counts-what-it-can-see.md), 2026-08-14) and the five issues [#5212](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5212)→[#5216](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5216) shipped in sequence, closing on a prod read rather than a merge. ⚠️ **PRD conditions 1 and 3 were NOT closed by this milestone** — they were *held* here, and condition 6 is what shipped; they stay open and unscheduled, so the next milestone that claims them has to say so explicitly rather than inherit them from this line.

**Framed 2026-08-13, grilled to resolution 2026-08-14 — the class-major ingest contract** ([#5200](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5200) wayfinder + eight decision tickets [#5201](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5201)–[#5208](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5208), on the [#4755](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4755) pattern). **The Atlas is not a fifth pillar — it stands on the four.** ADR-0006's pillars (Datasource / Chat Platform / Action Target / Knowledge) are mutually exclusive by `plugin_catalog` CHECK, one admin surface each; the brain has no pillar value yet was built with its own connectors, installs and admin surface. The claim to test: **connecting a source is the only act a person performs, and what the Atlas does with it is a property of its CLASS** — different mechanisms per class, one act by the user. Not the same as "all sources ingest alike"; conflating those two is what made the taxonomy look load-bearing in the first framing.

⭐ **The bug class already fired, and the evidence is structural rather than operational.** `register.ts:453` registers `catalog:slack-history` as **its own catalog row and install step**, distinct from the Slack chat-platform install and surfaced through `admin-knowledge.ts` — so **Slack installs twice**. That is the cause of M1's four green days: Slack live as a chat platform in all three regions, extraction on, and zero episodes, because the second install was never made. The frame predicts that bug; the recorded lesson (*"only a row count separates them"*) treated it as a miss.

**T3 SHIPPED 2026-08-14** ([#5203](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5203) ✅, PR [#5209](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5209) @ `bb484809f`, 3 review rounds; the remaining tickets resolved the next day, below) — `catalog:slack-history` retired; ingest scope is the bot's channel membership minus an admin exclusion list, dispatched per workspace off the chat install via the new required `BrainSourceConnector.scope`. Both hand-checks held: migration 0198 captures retired installs' channel sets with lazy reconcile at first sync (the empty-config case is an admitted state, not a skip), and the 50-channel cap's removal is recorded on [#5205](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5205). ⚠️ Its first CI run re-fired the **types-scaffold gotcha**: the sources tuple had been made a `@useatlas/types` value export, which the registry-installed scaffold cannot see — it now lives in `@useatlas/schemas` per `brain.ts`'s own convention, pinned exhaustive.

**GRILLED 2026-08-14 — all seven remaining tickets resolved in one session** (resolution comments on [#5201](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5201) / [#5202](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5202) / [#5204](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5204)–[#5208](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5208); [ADR-0040](../../docs/adr/0040-the-class-major-ingest-contract.md) + CONTEXT.md vocabulary in PR [#5211](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5211), which closes #5200 on merge). ⭐ The sharpening the session produced: **every class has two arms that never merge** — the **availability arm** (automatic: episode stream, extraction, grant derivation; live tier-1 for warehouse) and the **authority arm** (human: the review gate; enrollment + review for warehouse). *The contract automates availability and never automates authority* — the statement that makes ADR-0039 an **instance, not an exception**. Headlines: classes aren't pillars, installs are — the Knowledge pillar charter widens to "content corpora Atlas reads" (no fifth pillar, no CHECK migration, ADR-0006 amended in place); the **`ATLAS_BRAIN_*` env namespace goes to zero** (5 knobs die into the contract, webhook flag → capability detection, 3 → settings registry, alias auto-approve pair → workspace-scoped policy off-by-default fail-closed); steady-state extraction automatic under a per-workspace daily budget (**defer-not-drop**, observable before the cap bites) with **backfill a priced choice at connect**; audience derivation a **prerequisite** of private-source ingest (no fact without a derivable grant; missing scopes → visible public-only degrade); one install, **two landings** — ADR-0028 §1 confirmed verbatim, uploaded bundles stay document-only. Implementation issues follow the PR's merge; reshapes M3 (source breadth), never M5.

**The tree already agrees and is under-executed rather than wrong:** `EPISODE_SOURCE_SPECS` carries a CLASS axis separate from vendor, ADR-0036 §T10's M3 is explicitly *"class-major, plugin-shaped"*, and ADR-0028's amendment already gives one class no ingest at all (*"tier 1 … resolves live through the semantic layer"*). **What is missing is the contract** — what a pillar install triggers, per class. ⚠️ **[ADR-0039](../../docs/adr/0039-the-warehouse-producer-emits-only-what-a-human-enrolled.md) is a consistency check, not a casualty** ([#5208](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5208)): connecting a Postgres already works with zero config via live tier-1, and enrollment governs only the narrow copy-in that makes values *collidable*. Conflating *"the Atlas can use this source"* with *"the Atlas has materialized claims from it"* is what makes the two documents look contradictory. **Reshapes M3, not M5** — #95 is unblocked.

**Shipped 2026-08-14 — a five-issue `/ship-batch`, then the CI gate the batch exposed** ([#5158](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5158), [#5212](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5212), [#5173](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5173), [#5196](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5196), [#5061](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5061); PRs [#5218](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5218) @ `db3398a90`, [#5219](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5219) @ `2ae2732fd`, [#5220](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5220) @ `1c05eb57f`, [#5221](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5221) @ `5150922e4`, [#5222](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5222) @ `7cfdda12c`) — five parallel workers, all five merged. **M5's enrollment surface** (#5196) and **the Coverage Surface's class contract** (#5212) both landed, which unblocks [#5042](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5042) and [#5213](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5213) — both now on the frontier.

⭐ **#5061 retired the last six hand-measured mutation tables, and the runner immediately proved the case for itself:** `vocabulary-decide-pg` had **11 of 53 cells wrong**, under a docstring arguing no cell *could* have fallen ("killed by more tests, never fewer"). Five had. The reasoning holds only while tests are *added* — a rewritten test can stop reaching a mutation, and a docstring cannot notice its own escape clause was falsified. ⚠️ **`bun run type` in a worktree can type-check the PRIMARY checkout's files** — `tsgo` realpaths `@atlas/api/*` through symlinked `node_modules`, so every parallel worker's type pre-flight is partly blind; use `bun x tsgo --noEmit -p packages/api/tsconfig.json` (written up in `docs/development/testing.md`).

**Shipped 2026-08-14 — the tier-1 warehouse producer, the component ADR-0037 designed protections for** ([#5042](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5042); PR [#5226](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5226) @ `69ed180158`) — `rounds: 2 + closing comment sweep (32m / 42m / 9m of reported agent time)`, **stopped on the ratio rule**, curve **35 new-surface → 22 defect-in-prior-fix vs 12 new-surface**. M4's dormant machinery finally has a producer: `subject_cmp` is populated, the tier guard has rows it can hold back, and `warehouse_structural` cardinality is proposed. Reach is ADR-0039's enrollment set, and the snapshot query names the enrolled columns only, so an unenrolled column never leaves the warehouse. `POST /api/v1/admin/brain-enrollment/produce` is the one trigger; a cadence is deferred on `alias-proposal.ts`'s terms.

⭐ **Three of round 1's fixes REPRODUCED the defect they fixed, one layer over — and `fix-vs-finding` caught all three before round 2 did.** The SQL gate moved out of the shipped runner and into a seam that was equally skippable (`async () => ({valid: true})` satisfied it); the post-commit response absorbed a serialization failure by returning `refusals: []`, a confident all-clear *worse* than the 500 it replaced; and the absent/unsurfaceable cell split filed `''` — what a CSV or ETL load writes for an empty text column — as an enrollment mistake. The general shape: **a fix aimed at an instance lands, and the class re-forms one member over**, which is why the check belongs in the round that WROTE the fix rather than the next one.

⚠️ **A "positive control" that could never reach the thing it controlled.** `test-setup.ts` strips every `ATLAS_*` var, so `detectDBType()` throws and `validateSQL` returns *"No valid datasource configured"* before the empty-query check, the pattern regex, the parser and the whitelist. The gate test then asserted inside `if (!result.valid)` against a regex that string can never match — so it passed for `DROP TABLE users; SELECT 1`, and **deleting the production SQL gate entirely left every suite green**. Two lessons worth carrying: an assertion inside `if (!ok)` reports *pass* for exactly the case it excludes, and a suite-wide env strip silently disarms any test that needs the env to reach its subject.

⚠️ **The permanence split was inverted at the arm that mattered, twice.** Round 1 created `snapshot-rejected` because *"one message cannot carry both retry and retrying will never work"* — then round 2 routed a validator THROW into it (a module-init failure is transient, and the message told the admin to un-enroll a healthy pair), while a dropped table thrown from the RUNNER kept the arm promising a retry. A gate that checks SELECT-only/whitelist does **not** check existence. Also reversed late and deliberately: a transaction failure now REFUSES its entity instead of 500ing the run, because the 500 arrives after earlier entities have committed and the retry doubles the review queue — the argument the same PR had already written into `checkedRun` one layer up.

Falsifiers: **15 mutations measured, 14 RED**; the survivor is explained (switching the response schema to `z.discriminatedUnion` independently repaired the drift diagnostic). Comment sweep: **12 false claims**, five of them rot from the two late reversals, and one the accidental-equality defect *recurring* — a comment asserting every counter differs over a fixture where the two counters that ARE the split it pins were both `1`. Follow-ups **filed**, not left in the PR body: the cadence trigger ([#5228](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5228) — ADR-0039 decided it and only the manual `POST /produce` shipped; carries the concurrency question, since two runs take two snapshot instants and `ON CONFLICT` dedupes nothing), a checked-in `warehouse-producer.mutations.ts` spec ([#5229](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5229)), and branding the snapshot REQUEST rather than the verdict ([#5230](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5230) — the brand closes literals, not ordering or replay; **coupled to #5228**, which is the second call site and the one that runs unattended).

**Shipped 2026-08-15 — bundle 1, six small CI / scripts / docs items in one review** ([#5247](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5247), [#5242](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5242), [#5243](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5243); PR [#5252](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5252) @ `d07f86845`) — `rounds: 2 + closing comment sweep (9m / 17m / 13.5m of reported agent time)`, **stopped on the ratio rule**, curve **~15 findings / ~15 new-surface / 0 defect-in-prior-fix → ~18 findings, ~4 new-surface vs ~14 defect-in-prior-fix**, then 7 false claims in the sweep. Six items bundled because none shares logic with another and two land in `ci.yml`; one commit each, so a bad one could be dropped. `apps/docs` onto an isolated per-file runner; `lint:type-aware` into the documented pre-flight (11s measured, against the ~4min round-trip its absence cost on [#5083](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5083)); the `scripts/` fixture step renamed because it runs the SHIPPED repo-wide lint commands and reds on a diagnostic anywhere; `storableErrorText` branded on the OUTPUT with a source scan pinning the writer set; `check-docs-brain-snippets.ts` given a process-wide throw boundary (#5243); and `deploy/docs/Caddyfile` validated as a CONFIG for the first time (#5242).

⭐ **THE GATE WRITTEN TO TELL TWO FAILURES APART COULD NOT TELL THEM APART, THREE TIMES RUNNING.** `check-caddyfile.sh` first used `if ! docker run …`, so every docker fault — dead daemon, registry outage, Docker Hub rate limit — was reported as *"your Caddyfile is not a valid Caddy config"*, naming a tracked file that was fine. The fix branched on the status per docker's documented "125 for its own faults" rule; **its own new fixture immediately went red**, because a dead daemon exits **1** here, identically to `caddy validate` refusing a config. The second fix decided on positive evidence that caddy ran (`"msg":"using config from file"`, emitted on success AND on a config error) — and applied it to the FAILURE arm only, leaving exit 0 short-circuiting to `PASS` on the exact reasoning the header spends twenty lines rejecting, i.e. a false GREEN. Generalizable: **a documented exit-code convention is a claim about a version, and the reasoned fix for a mislabelling defect is the one most likely to reproduce it.**

⚠️ **Two fixture suites that could not fail, both caught by building the assertion that says so.** `check-caddyfile.test.sh`'s `mutate` returned 2 on a setup fault and `check_invalid`'s `|| return 0` swallowed it — with `sed` forced to exit 1 the suite reported **`5 passed, 0 failed`, exit 0**, all five adversarial cases silently gone (live trigger, not hypothetical: BSD/macOS `sed -i` needs a backup-suffix argument). Separately, `dest="$(mutate …)"` ran the helper in a command-substitution **subshell**, so its `fail` incremented a copy of the counter and the suite printed a FAIL line while exiting 0. And the marker rule — the centrepiece of the whole exit-1-vs-exit-2 argument — had **no fixture of its own**: three redundant guards stood behind one input, so neutering the marker left the suite at 11/11. Closed with a `docker` PATH stub, which isolates it with no seam in the gate.

⚠️ **Nine bypasses of one brand scan, across two rounds.** Uppercase `SET LAST_ERROR` (Postgres is case-insensitive, the regex was not); a quoted literal `SET last_error = ''` — the single value the CHECK refuses — which the value class could not match at all; a quoted identifier `SET "last_error"`, which is live production idiom in this repo; a `withBrainTransaction` re-bind adding no new SQL; a second minter; a **cast-free** minter that just declares the return type; `db.update(brainCoverageCycle).set({ lastError })`, which spells neither column nor table; a caller anywhere outside `lib/brain/**`; and a non-literal call argument. The header's original *"there is no way to add one that moves neither"* is now an itemised list of what IS pinned and five shapes that are not — plus the note that the REMOVAL direction is safe in all of them and only ADDITION is missed. Generalizable: **an exhaustiveness claim in a guard's docstring is an experiment, and the guard's own author is the worst-placed person to run it.**

⚠️ **A completeness guard that could not fire.** The docs runner's `.catch` was chained AFTER `.then`, so it also caught rejections from the recursively-returned `scheduleNext()` — every ancestor pushed a SECOND result for a file that had already passed, the count met `files.length` with a file genuinely missing, and the set difference the block exists to compute never ran. Keyed storage plus an unconditional membership check; the two-arg `.then(onFulfilled, onRejected)` keeps the handler off its own continuation. Its siblings: unknown flags silently dropped (so `--affected` — the flag this same PR's docs teach agents to type — printed a full run as an affected one), a second positional silently overwriting the first, and a filter matched against the ABSOLUTE path, so `docs` ran all nine suites and reported them as filtered.

⚠️ **The comment sweep found seven false measured claims, two of which contradicted other files in the same PR** — a fixture asserting "docker exits 125 for daemon faults alike" sitting directly above the fixture that proved otherwise, and a guard header claiming "there is no third case" while the suite added in the same commit stated the falsifier in plain words (a parse error exits 1, because the module never loads and the boundary was never installed). Also: `19 write sites, measured` reproduced under none of six readings; `~200 lines` of oxlint baseline was true of `lint:type-aware` (258) and wrong for `lint` (12); and a comment labelled the filter fix "REPO-RELATIVE" when `ROOT` is `apps/docs`, inverting the fix it described. One was a real gap behind the overstatement: the type-name containment scan stopped at `packages/api/src` while **96 files under `ee/src` import `@atlas/api`** — now covered, and falsified.

Deferred, each named in the PR body with its reason: `recordClassScanFailure` still returns `0` whether the class has no workspaces or the UPDATE matched none (a return-type change threading through the scheduler); no fixtures for the docs runner itself; six other packages still on bare `bun test <dir>`, which wants a grep ratchet rather than six edits; and the Caddyfile's new directive-order claim, which `caddy validate` cannot check.

**Shipped 2026-08-15 — a four-worker `/ship-batch`: five issues, four PRs, all merged CI-green** ([#5257](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5257) → PR [#5258](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5258) @ `bf16b1b49`; [#5239](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5239) + [#5240](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5240) bundled → PR [#5260](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5260) @ `0a977f8da`; [#5180](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5180) → PR [#5259](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5259) @ `7069d1bc0`; [#5176](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5176) → PR [#5261](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5261) @ `42dbeac72`). Four worktree-isolated workers, disjoint surfaces, no cross-worker collision. Three panels stopped on the **ratio rule**, one at the 3-round cap.

⭐ **ALL FOUR WORKERS FOUND ONE OF THEIR OWN FALSIFIERS THAT COULD NOT FAIL — independently, in a single batch.** #5180: a per-character loop that stripped lowercase before comparing, so it iterated **zero** times for 2 of 5 fixtures. #5239: an `S1` mutation that landed on a **header comment** rather than the SQL, plus an `unexamined` counter measuring nothing. #5176: an `assertPlainIdentifiers` that was **deletable with zero test failures** — added in the very commit headlined *"deletable with zero failures"* — and a vacuity pin that **passes against an empty registry**. #5257: 22 falsifiers held, but one labelled honestly as a **pin, not a falsifier** (the revoked-Proxy array was green pre-fix; no mutation REDs it). A guard that cannot fire is indistinguishable from one that works, and reading cannot tell them apart — only mutating and watching can. #5176's fix generalizes: `assertPlainIdentifiers` was **removed rather than tested**, both builders now taking a `ViaParentTableName` so injection is *unrepresentable*.

⚠️ **TWO OF #5257'S OWN MEASUREMENTS WERE FALSE, and the fix measured them instead of inheriting them.** The issue claimed eight escape classes each producing a whole-run 500 — seven did. The eighth, a bare string, produced a **phantom entity outcome**: not a crash anyone notices but a fabricated result nobody questions, which is the worse failure. It also claimed the compiler "encourages `validated.table.slice(0,200)`, which typechecks" — there is no `table` on that type at all. An issue body is a claim about the tree at authoring time, including its measurements.

⚠️ **#5180 overrode an acceptance criterion on measurement, and was right to.** AC-1 said reuse `maskSecret`; two reviewers independently measured that mask leaking **8 of 9 characters** of a short secret (`hunter2!!` → `hunt••••r2!!`) into a retained log stream. It is a recognition affordance for an admin viewing their own key — correct for a display surface, wrong for a log sink. Shipped `[withheld:secret-setting]`, deliberately colliding with neither pino's `[Redacted]` nor `plugins/secrets.ts`'s `MASKED_PLACEHOLDER`, the latter being a **round-tripping sentinel** that `restoreMaskedSecrets` reads back as "keep the stored value". Round 1 had concluded *"no runtime assertion can catch this leak, so the check must be a type"*; round 2 falsified it — `getSettingDefinition` returns the live registry object, so flipping `secret` for one test makes the leak an ordinary assertion. Both halves shipped, covering **disjoint** edits.

⚠️ **The `--affected` blind spot has a second instance.** #5239 reddened CI after a clean local pre-flight on `lib/auth/__tests__/migrate.test.ts` — a second hardcoded migration-list fixture. Neither enumeration is reachable from a changed source graph, so `--affected` structurally cannot see either.

**Batch-level process findings, all measured:** a bare `bun test` with **two files** in one process spins at 100% CPU indefinitely (reproduced with `admin-revoke` + `admin-sessions`, which pass in 1.4s each **alone** — generic `mock.module()` interference, nothing to do with the diff); a hung test holds its wrapper shell open, so four workers accumulated ~60 live shells until the spinner was killed. ⭐ **A blanket `node_modules` symlink into a worktree makes `@atlas/api` resolve to the SHARED checkout's source, so `tsgo` silently type-checks the wrong tree** — and `bun install` inside such a worktree writes *through* the symlinks and corrupts every sibling. The only sound check is a sentinel whose derived identifier exists nowhere on disk (`zzzStaleCheckProbe`, `ThisTypeDoesNotExist5257`): if the compiler's error text names it, the compiler read your bytes. **Back a claim with a RED you produced, never a green you received** — a green means "correct" or "never read the file", and those are indistinguishable.

Residue, each filed rather than left in a PR body: [#5262](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5262) (filed as the best find of the batch — `ATLAS_LOG_LEVEL` raised to `error` silencing the audit line #5180 added — ⚠️ **that mechanism was FALSIFIED on 2026-08-16 by the #5271 panel**: the route already writes an unsuppressible `admin_action_log` row, and the drop past an open circuit breaker emits nothing at *any* level, so the log level was never the lever; see the 08-16 entry below), [#5263](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5263), [#5264](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5264), [#5265](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5265), [#5266](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5266) (the datasource seeder reports a **blocked** row as `preserved` — the sibling-producer pattern, worse than the defect #5239 just fixed) and [#5269](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5269) (orphan `stripe_webhook_events` rows survive a purge reporting `complete: true`). ✅ **All six shipped by 2026-08-17** — PRs [#5271](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5271), [#5291](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5291) and [#5292](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5292); see the 08-16 and 08-17 entries below.

**Shipped 2026-08-16 — a checked-in mutations spec for the warehouse producer** ([#5229](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5229) → PR [#5282](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5282) @ `95144a046`) — #5042's fifteen falsifiers were hand-measured during review and lived only in commit messages, so the strongest evidence the producer's tests could fail was prose. Now 22 generated mutations over five suites, **no survivors**, every column killing at least one row. Rounds: **2**, **stopped on the RATIO rule**, curve **32 findings / 32 new-surface / 0 defect-in-prior-fix → ~17 / ~5 / ~12** — the total fell but defect-in-prior-fix exceeded new surface, which is the stop. (Wall clock ~9m and ~12m, measured only as launch→transcripts-quiet through a 90s quiet poll, so ±90s; per-agent durations not retained.) Both rows the issue was filed over are falsifiable again, verified not argued: `predicate: dim.name → dim.sql` (4 `producer` / 2 `pg`) and the production SQL gate deleted (2 / 2 `mint`).

⭐ **ROUND 1's FIX REPRODUCED ROUND 1's OWN FINDING, ONE ROW APART, IN THE SAME COMMIT.** A reviewer showed the episode-reader mutant did not type-check, and the fix added a second edit with the argument *"a row measuring a state the type-checker already forbids is weaker evidence than its number reads as"* — while a new row added in that same commit spelled its mutation `if (false)`, deleting the only narrowing of `validated` and reddening `tsgo` with `TS2345`. Two rows in one file holding opposite standards, the larger count on the weaker one. Measured, not reasoned: `if (false)` errors, `if (validated === undefined)` is clean, so the fix drops only the `!==` disjunct — which is what the source's own comment invites by calling the survivor *"Redundant"*. The count moved 23 → 20 and the DROP is the evidence: the case that stopped failing is *"the verdict's request is undefined"*, now correctly routed through the surviving arm. ⚠️ **A `0` was never the risk here; a number that cannot be produced by a tree CI would accept is.**

⭐ **THE SPEC TRIPPED THE GUARD IT EXISTS TO PROTECT, AND THE ANCHOR IS A DOCSTRING BECAUSE OF IT.** `warehouse-producer-bypass.test.ts` scans `packages/api/scripts/` and fails on any file merely NAMING one of the five guarded SQL-gate types — so the obvious anchor for the tag mutation (the `export type` line under the tag) reddened the baseline, which then aborts the runner. Anchored on the docstring ABOVE the declaration instead, with a note saying why, so nobody repairs it downwards. **Reworded, never exempted** — the same resolution the guard's own file already uses on itself, splitting the five names with `+`. ⚠️ Two anchors in this spec deliberately carry comment text and both are load-bearing: that one, and the `log.error`-demotion anchor, whose trailing `// ⚠️ These two` is the ONLY thing distinguishing five payload lines that are byte-identical to a sibling log further down the same function.

⭐ **PROSE ABOUT A TABLE ROTS FASTER THAN THE TABLE, AND A THROWAWAY CHECKER CAUGHT A CLAIM WRITTEN SECONDS EARLIER.** Six false claims across two rounds, every one a sentence about the measurements three lines below it: a positional *"the row above"* that a later insertion re-pointed at a cell measuring 0; *"the only edit the bypass suite can see"* contradicted by the same commit; an episode-reader note borrowing its neighbour's consequence; a gate note inverting the survival mechanism (the branch was always TRUE — the defect was a NEGATIVE assertion that *"No valid datasource configured"* satisfies trivially). A 15-claim script parsing the generated `.md` immediately flagged a sixteenth: a new row had made a FOURTH logging-only row under a bullet saying *"all three"*. **A standing version is the ratchet this earned and was deliberately deferred** — it is new machinery and would have entered the diff with no round left to review it. Also deferred with reasons: rows for `ID_SEPARATOR` and `WAREHOUSE_CARDINALITY_SOURCE`.

**Cost, measured on CI rather than estimated:** `mutation-tables (2/4)` 2m29s against sibling shards at 42–57s, so **~110s marginal**; ~113s locally for 22 mutations × 5 suites. The spec sorts to position 14, lands on shard 2 by round-robin, and the totals move `87/53/91/35 → 87/75/91/35` — the long pole is unchanged at 91 and FALLS from ~34% to ~32% of the sweep. ⚠️ **Mutation COUNT is the wrong unit for this spec** (five targets per mutation, four of them sub-second, against a `vocabulary-decide` whose 53 rows are minutes) and both copies of that census — `ci.yml` and `check-mutation-tables.sh` — now say so and point at each other, because #5229 updated one and left the other stale within the same hour. Five suites also gained the `MUTATIONS THIS CATCHES` pointer every sibling spec's suite already had; without it the newest table was unreachable from the code it vouches for.

⭐ **The real validation was #5228 landing on top of it hours later** — four shards green, no cell moved, no anchor died, so the spec survived first contact with a branch that reshaped its target. That is better evidence than either PR's argument, and it is the reason to land a falsifier spec BEFORE the change that will exercise it rather than after.

**Shipped 2026-08-16 — the warehouse producer's cadence trigger, under a run lock** ([#5228](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5228) → PR [#5283](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5283) @ `0c6564b29`) — ADR-0039 answered *when it runs* and #5042 shipped only the operator's button. Rounds: **2 (round 1 panels 6m05/7m19/— , round 2 6m/7m16/— of reported agent time; one fix-vs-finding pass 1m58; closing comment sweep 6m02 — several agent durations not retained, session wall clock not measured)**, **stopped on the YIELD rule**, curve **~30 findings / ~30 new-surface / 0 defect-in-prior-fix → ~30 / ~16 / ~14**. The count did not fall, which is the stop.

The five ACs were questions, and two answers are worth carrying. **Cadence is a setting with a CONSTANT FLOOR** — `ATLAS_BRAIN_WAREHOUSE_CADENCE_INTERVAL_HOURS` (platform, default 24) is unbounded in the LENGTHENING direction and stopped at one hour in the other, which is `WAREHOUSE_ROW_CAP`'s *"a claim about how much a human can review"* applied to time; the row cap has no safe direction, this knob does, which is why one is a constant and the other is a floored setting. **On-enrollment took the recorded-decision branch, and it is MEASURED rather than argued**: `planWarehouseEmission` reads ADR-0037 §4's fail-closed ambiguity set off the reach it is handed, so a plan narrowed to the newly enrolled pair EMITS what the full reach REFUSES — two tests are that pair of verdicts. A narrowed plan is not a cheaper way to compute the same answer. ⚠️ **The two-tier gate is an AND, and for a DEFAULT-OFF key that is not the neighbours' sentence** — the boot gate reads the key with no workspace, so with no platform value the fiber never starts and a workspace toggling itself on changes nothing; the registry comment copied from the default-ON siblings said the opposite, a customer-facing lie in the direction where the feature silently does nothing.

⭐ **Round 1's fix CLOSED THE INSTANCE, NOT THE CLASS, and round 2 caught it.** `WAREHOUSE_CADENCE_DEFAULTS` pinned the defaults table; the cycle resolved seams with `deps.x ?? DEFAULTS.x`, and nothing required the RHS to be the constant — so replacing one fallback with a pass-through (**the scheduled trigger taking no lock, the issue's entire subject**) stayed green *including* the three tests asserting the table. One object spread closes it. ⭐ **A wrong `UNLOCK` key was invisible in BOTH lanes**: the unit fixture answered `released: true` for any statement, and in `-pg` the false answer poisons → destroys the socket → the server drops the lock anyway, so every overlap assertion still held. Correctness rested silently on connection destruction. The recorder now models Postgres. ⚠️ **A session advisory lock survives `ROLLBACK`** (measured), so "we saw an error, therefore nothing was locked" is false — two paths pooled a possibly-held client, and the tests *asserted* that behaviour. Falsifiers: **16 mutations, 16 RED**, every one named by a reviewer as surviving an earlier tree; two of the author's own new tests were no-ops on first measurement (`"24h"` cannot separate `parseFloat` from `Number` when the NaN branch falls through to a 24h default — accidental equality inside a test *about* falsifiability). Comment sweep: **9 false claims**, including one that named this PR as its own expiry condition (*"This decision EXPIRES when #5228 lands … Whoever builds the cadence owns this line"*) and three `internal.ts` namespace enumerations still asserting *"all eight are pairwise distinct"* while omitting two live namespaces — the same defect the PR had documented in its new file and not fixed in the three it edited. Deferred with reasons in the PR body: a pool-slot bound for concurrent presses on distinct workspaces, a discriminated per-workspace outcome, a 503 for lock-unavailable, and a checked-in mutations spec for the lock/cadence.

⭐ **A `-pg` lane cannot see a cleanup bug whose failure path reaches the same end state**, and that is the general form of the `UNLOCK`-key survivor above: the wrong key makes the release report `false`, which poisons → destroys the socket → the server drops the lock anyway, so every integration assertion still holds. **The precondition for the hole is a row whose ONLY killer is `pg`** — #5229's spec has none today (both its non-zero `pg` rows also die in `producer`), so it cannot bite there yet, but rollback, `valid_to` not being stamped, and episode cleanup are exactly where such a row would first appear, and ADR-0037's tension-only re-emission lives in that neighbourhood.
