The fix generalises: make the UNIT fixture model the real semantics (releasing a key you do not hold answers `false`) instead of answering `true` unconditionally, so the unit lane sees what teardown hides from the integration lane.

⚠️ **Never run a test suite while `check-mutation-tables.sh` / `mutate.ts` is live — a NEW symptom for the known cause.** The repo already forbids *committing* in that window, because the runner rewrites source in place; the documented symptom is a sabotaged commit. The other symptom is a **phantom failure**: a suite that is `71/71` alone reported `70 pass 1 fail` because the gate had a mutant applied underneath the read. It is strictly nastier than the commit case — a bad commit is visible in review, while a phantom failure sends you debugging code that is fine. `ps -o pid=,args= -C bun | grep 'mutate\.ts'` before believing any red in that window.

**Shipped 2026-08-16 — three residue issues in one PR, and two of them were wrong about their own mechanism** ([#5266](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5266) + [#5270](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5270) + the re-scoped [#5262](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5262) → PR [#5271](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5271) @ `6f8525007`). #5266 is #5239's sibling one file over: the DATASOURCE catalog seeder used an unqualified `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and derived `preservedSlugs` by SUBTRACTION, so a row blocked by a foreign-id slug collision was *positively reported as preserved* — worse than #5239, which merely omitted it from an ambiguous count. Now `ON CONFLICT (id)`, per-row statements, SQLSTATE classification, `blockedSlugs` as its own outcome; `PG_UNIQUE_VIOLATION` consolidated from **six** definitions into `lib/db/pg-errors.ts`. #5270 built one seam for the settings audit row — redaction behind a branded `AuditedValue`, an explicit `scope: "platform"`, and `logAdminActionAwait`. Rounds: **2 (8m / 8m panels, 9m closing comment sweep — agent-reported durations; session wall clock not measured)**.

⚠️ **THE HEADLINE FINDING: TWO OF THE THREE ISSUES WERE FALSIFIED BY THE PANEL, AND ONE OF THEM WAS WRITTEN THE SAME SESSION.** #5270 claimed writing a `secret: true` key put plaintext in a DB row — false: both settings verbs 403 a secret key *before* the write, and that guard was already on `main`. All three reviewers found it independently. The issue was filed twenty minutes after #5262 was sent back to `needs-triage` for **exactly that class of error**, by the same reader, on the same call site — tracing a write path to `logAdminAction` without reading the forty lines above it. #5262's own mechanism was false too: there is no `log.warn` for `ATLAS_LOG_LEVEL` to silence, because past an open circuit breaker `internalExecute` emits *nothing at any level*, and before it opens the drop logs at `error` (which only `fatal` suppresses — and `fatal` is in that setting's own dropdown). Both fixes are still right; both rationales were rebuilt. **#5262 stayed OPEN deliberately** — its acceptance criteria were written against the falsified premise, and closing it would have retired a ticket nobody satisfied. It was then REWRITTEN on 2026-08-16 against the finding that survived and closed by PR [#5292](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5292) on 08-17.

⚠️ **THE PROSE WAS WRONG NINE TIMES; THE CODE WAS WRONG ONCE.** Every false claim was caught by a reviewer, never by the author. Round 1 corrected #5262's mechanism *in the header and left three restatements of the retracted version behind* — one opening "The residual #5262 is **actually** about", presenting itself as the correction while being the correctee. Round 1's own correction then cited two line numbers **its own commit had already moved**. Its replacement claim ("which `ATLAS_LOG_LEVEL` does not silence") was false at `fatal`. Its census recipe returned 8 lines, not 1; the *fixed* recipe returned 2, because the comment quoting it matches it. A `platformTier` docstring advertised the spelling round 2 had rejected fifteen lines away. And round 2's own delta table justified a correct fix with a false consequence — it claimed `=== undefined` re-exposes a global write on the org-scoped read API, but `requireOrgContext` 400s on a falsy orgId, so the row is *misfiled, not disclosed*. The lesson generalises past this PR: **a comment asserting a mechanism is an experiment, and this diff ran none of them until a reviewer asked.**

⚠️ **ROUND 1'S NEW GUARD WAS ITSELF A SILENT FAILURE.** The definition↔key mismatch check — added because `securitySensitiveAuditLine` has one and this module did not — recorded the mismatch in a single JSONB field nothing greps, and on the `reset_to_default` path (no value → the whole metadata block is skipped) it computed `mismatched`, found it true, and **discarded it**. A clear filed against the wrong registry entry was indistinguishable from a correct one. This is the `fix-vs-finding` pattern arriving through a fix written *for* a silent-failure finding.

Panel stopped at round 2 on the **ratio rule**, not the cap: ~10 of ~16 findings were defects inside round 1's own fixes vs ~6 new surface (round 1 was ~19, all new surface). **17 falsifiers measured RED plus one compile-error fence**; the `hasInternalDB` guard reddened all 12 tests in its file when added — unit runs have no `DATABASE_URL` — which is the proof it guards a reachable state. The `-pg` file seeds a **real** squatting row and pins the counterfactual: the unqualified target still swallows exactly that collision. 537/537 affected files green.

Deferred, each filed rather than left in a PR body: [#5272](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5272) (four sites read a FLAT SQLSTATE off `internalQuery`, which wraps the driver error under `.cause` once the Layer boots — so four 409/duplicate arms may be dead in production; two of the four had been given the shared import and *no* hedge, so this PR's own edit made them read as audited-and-fine) and [#5273](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5273) (neither catalog seed Layer has any test, so `blockedSlugs`' entire production route is unobserved — replacing the forwarded fields with `...zeroCounts` costs nothing).

**Shipped 2026-08-16 — the unique-violation classifiers read a shape `internalQuery` never rejects with** ([#5272](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5272) → PR [#5279](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5279) @ `9a4571e9a`) — once the Effect Layer has booted, `internalQuery` takes the `_sqlClient` branch and rejects with a `FiberFailure` whose own properties are `message` / `name` / `stack` **only**; the `Cause` hangs off a **symbol**, and under it `SqlError.cause` is the pg `DatabaseError`. Four sites classified `23505` on a flat read, so every duplicate 500ed instead of returning its 409 / typed error. `pgErrorLinks` unwraps and then walks; `asUniqueViolation` stays flat and unchanged, because the two catalog seeders hold a raw `Pool` and a chain walk there would classify an unrelated layer's wrapped violation as a benign slug collision.

⚠️ **THE ISSUE'S PROPOSED FIX DOES NOT WORK, AND THE SITE IT CITED AS THE CORRECT PRECEDENT WAS THE FIFTH DEAD ONE.** #5272 asked for *"a classifier that walks `.cause`"* — a `.cause` walk starting at the `FiberFailure` reads `undefined` and stops at **depth 0**, and no amount of inspection shows that; only running it does. `routing-id-conflict.ts` shipped exactly that naive walk under a docstring claiming it covered the `internalQuery` paths *"regardless of how deeply wrapped"*, so #3167's concurrent-install detection was dead too — reached via `persist-form-install.ts`'s promise-level `.catch`, which receives the `FiberFailure`. Measured with the test index deliberately renamed so the constraint check could not be what failed: **`false` on the real wrapped rejection, `true` on the hand-built fixture its own tests use.** ⭐ *Fixtures that agree by construction*, five arms deep — every pre-existing fixture built `{ code: "23505" }` by hand, which is why a green suite covered five dead recovery arms (the #5000 / #5068 class); the new `-pg` suite seeds a **real** conflicting row through the seam.

⚠️ **Two sessions fixed the same issue in parallel, 45 minutes apart** — PR [#5276](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5276) got there first, was red on a barrel import and had no live owner; #5279 supersedes it and carries across its `Runtime.isFiberFailure` guard, its `persist-form-install` trace and its symbol-reachability assertion. ⚠️ **`Cause` / `Runtime` must be imported by SUBPATH, not from the `effect` barrel** — the barrel form type-checks, passes `lint:type-aware` and passes this module's own tests, then reds `admin-marketplace.test.ts` with a runtime `SyntaxError: Export named 'Cause' not found` when a partially-mocked module graph links the file. Caught only by running suites outside the `--affected` set.

**Shipped 2026-08-16 — the SQL-gate allowlist's completeness direction, and `EntityEdgeOutcome`** ([#5255](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5255) + [#5277](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5277) → PR [#5278](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5278) @ `fba91e6ee`) — two issues on ONE branch because both edit `warehouse-producer.ts`, and because the interaction is the point: #5277 adds a new exported type to the exact file #5255's new completeness guard polices. Rounds: **2 (panels 6m02/7m20/7m51, then 7m32/10m10/4m31 of reported agent time; closing comment sweep 9m — session wall clock not measured)**, **stopped on the YIELD rule**, curve **24 findings / 24 new-surface / 0 defect-in-prior-fix → 27 findings / 13 new-surface / 12 defect-in-prior-fix**. The count did not fall, which is the stop; the ratio was near-level rather than inverted.

#5255: `GUARDED_NAMES` was hand-maintained, so a sixth brand-carrying export needed no file to change for the guard to go stale. A `@sql-gate-guarded` tag now makes membership reviewable at the definition site — but ⭐ **a tag is something a person has to remember, so tags alone would have RESTATED the hole rather than closed it.** The enforcing half is a structural closure over the module's top-level exported type declarations of *"names the brand symbol, or names something already in the closure"*; both are asserted set-equal to the assembled array. `ROOTS` went 3 → 8 (`cli/bin`, `cli/lib`, `api/scripts`, `mcp/src`, `plugins` walked whole), on an argument the OLD docstring already made — it named `cli/bin/brain-paraphrase-eval.ts` as brain-touching code outside its own scan. #5277: `EntityEdgeOutcome` replaces three parallel report fields; partial progress is representable via a nested `EntityEdgeProgress` phase discriminant. **AC-1 took the recorded-decision branch, not the detector** — the sketched predicate is UNSOUND for `rejected` (a human rejection between runs moves no `snapshot_at`), and a detector needs a persisted watermark that does not exist; the decision names #5228 as its expiry.

⚠️ **THE PROSE WAS WRONG THIRTEEN TIMES ACROSS THREE ROUNDS, AND ONE CLAIM WAS WRONG FOUR TIMES RUNNING.** "Every number on this report is distinct" was the fourth version of a single comment (4 → 5 → 7 → "every"), written in a sentence that *boasted about having measured the previous three* — `rows` is 4 too. A parser-floor count went 19 → 20 → 21. Three sites said "four disjoint reasons" where there are three reasons and a denominator. "Written once, so they cannot disagree" described a head with a **third un-hoisted copy**. "Borrowed verbatim" described a string differing by the word `wiring`. ⭐ **Where a number was not load-bearing it is now ABSENT rather than guessed** — four miscounts in one PR is the argument, and `grep -c` undercounts a phrase split across string concatenations, which is how two of them survived a check.

⭐ **THREE FIXES CLOSED THE REPORTED INSTANCE AND NOT THE CLASS, each caught by `fix-vs-finding` in fresh context and never by the author** — a pre-validation read narrowed on one field with its nested sibling two lines up untouched; a regex alternative given a fixture while the optional group beside it stayed unfixtured; a `positive()` that closed `0` and left every other wrong count. Two further fixes were written with **no falsifier at all** and measured GREEN under deletion before being given one. ⚠️ **A guard's own branches can MUTUALLY MASK**: `checkCensus`'s three arms each fired on a fixture another arm also refused, so every one was individually deletable with the suite green — closed by moving the invariants into the schemas package, where a test can name *which* message fired instead of collapsing onto one `reportComplete: false`. **36 falsifiers measured RED across the three rounds**, including the AC-1 mutation in three spellings (untagged, tagged, and `abstract class` — which matched none of the three matchers until round 2).

⚠️ **A round-2 reviewer invocation did not return and was re-dispatched**; its findings landed and were fixed, and the PR body records which reviewer and on what diff. A silently-dropped reviewer is not a clean one.

**Shipped 2026-08-17 — every group-scoped workspace sent every snapshot to the default datasource, and the page that would have shown it had no button** ([#5284](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5284) + [#5287](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5287) → PR [#5285](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5285) @ `d9aba7bb4`) — `warehouse-producer.ts` resolved the snapshot's connection from `entity.connection`, the YAML `connection:` hint, and read null as *"the default connection"*. On a DB-backed semantic layer that field is null for **every** entity, because the scope lives in `semantic_entities.connection_group_id` — `admin-source.ts` names the two as distinct while this module's own doc comment called them the same thing. So each entity refused with `relation "…" does not exist` while its pairs sat in the enrollment list looking live; on a stock SaaS deploy the `default` is the demo database. Now resolved through `resolveGroupPrimaryConnectionId`, the resolver semantic amendments already use for this question ([#4513](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4513), *"evidence runs where the change lives"*); the YAML hint still wins, and a name in more than one group is **omitted rather than guessed**, since `getAdminEntity` refuses to choose between them too. The gate is handed the same id as the runner, because its table whitelist is per-connection and validating one datasource while reading another would clear a table the target does not have.

⭐ **FOUND BY RUNNING THE PRODUCER AGAINST PROD FOR #5197 — WHICH IS THE #5197 PREMISE WORKING AS DESIGNED.** `defaultValidateSnapshotSql`'s own header predicted this exact failure: a test workspace has no whitelist, so the gate rejects on the table regardless, and *"a producer that refuses every entity in production look[s] from here exactly like one that works."* The unit suite drives the seam and the **caller** handed the seam the wrong id, so nothing at that layer could have seen it. ⚠️ **The second half is why it survived to prod at all.** The enrollment page ended two notices with *"Run the producer to apply it"* and afforded no way to do it — nothing in `packages/web` called `POST /produce`, and the run report is the **only** record of a refusal anywhere (nothing persists it), so every refusal reached nobody but the API logs. The new panel refuses to render a bare zero: `created: 0` has two causes that are identical from inside `brain_facts` — an empty reach and every pair refused — which is the central invisibility ADR-0039 names, so `enrolled` and `refusals` are shown side by side and the `reportComplete: false` arm renders no numbers at all rather than a confident all-clear.

⚠️ **Filed rather than fixed in flight: [#5286](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5286) — an enrollment cannot name a connection group.** `brain_enrollment`'s key is `(workspace_id, entity, dimension)` with no group column, while an entity name is unique only *within* a group — so where a name exists in two groups the enrollment cannot express which it means: it stores cleanly and reaches nothing, verbatim the failure the page's own copy claims it prevents. **It is live on staging**, where `test_orders` is the only producible entity and is ambiguous across three groups — which is why staging structurally cannot falsify #5284's fix, and why the re-read for #5197 is a prod read again.

**Shipped 2026-08-18 — the enrollment key gained the connection group, and the picker stopped presenting two entities as one choice** ([#5286](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5286)) — migration 0205 puts `connection_group_id` in `brain_enrollment`'s primary key and in `uq_brain_enrollment_naming`; the producer places each entity from the group its enrollment NAMED rather than inferring one from the catalog, and passes that group to `getAdminEntity` so the lookup that used to throw `AmbiguousEntityError` resolves. ⭐ **The picker's own de-dup comment was the defect, argued correctly from a premise that this change falsifies** — it collapsed same-named rows *"because the pair this surface stores is `(workspace_id, entity, dimension)` with no group column, so the duplicates are not a distinction the storage could record even if they picked one."* True when written; the fix is the column, not the collapse.

⚠️ **`null` had to mean ONE thing across the route and the producer, and the first cut gave it two.** The producer's loader was written to map a null group onto `getAdminEntity`'s `undefined` (unique-or-throw) so pre-0205 rows kept resolving, while the route scoped the same `null` to the flat scope — two readings of one stored value, in the two places that must agree about which entity a pair names. Closed by making `null` the flat scope everywhere and having **the migration resolve what each legacy row meant**: an `UPDATE` joins `semantic_entities` and backfills the group where the workspace publishes that name under exactly one, leaving `''` where it does not. Without it every backfilled row in a group-scoped workspace refuses `entity-not-published` — telling an admin to publish an entity that already is, forever.

⚠️ **Not all of it is addressable, and the producer says so rather than merging.** A name enrolled under two groups is refused outright (`enrolled-in-two-groups`, both sides, on `ambiguous-dimension`'s rule): `brain_entity.entity_id` hashes `(workspace, entity, primary key)`, the fact subject carries the name, and the coverage evidence join recovers it from `warehouse:<entity>@<instant>` — **none of those carries a group**, so producing both would file two subjects as one, which is a false `same` at the publish gate with no inverse. The coverage roster is name-keyed for the same reason and now de-dupes its units. Enrolling the pair in ONE group produces normally, which is the AC. ⚠️ **The PK swap is single-phase**: during a deploy overlap the draining container's `ON CONFLICT (workspace_id, entity, dimension)` is a 42P10 and its un-enroll over-deletes across groups — stated in the migration header rather than discovered, and accepted because the two-phase form ships a release N that does nothing (the fix *is* the key) on a surface that has never produced a row on prod.

⭐ **THREE DEFECTS THE AUTHOR DID NOT SEE, all found by one `/code-review high` pass and each a case where the fix was right and its REACH was not.** (1) An unplaceable entity's refusal was only reported from inside the emit loop, which a not-published entity never reaches — so the commonest post-0205 shape (a legacy row whose name has two published groups) was refused *"Publish the entity"* for one published twice, and the author's own comment asserting the placement arm fired first was **false**. Moved to a `WarehouseEntityLookup` arm ahead of every structural check, and the read is skipped entirely. (2) The declared-group short-circuit was gated `group !== null`, so the picker's *flat* option still took the inference path and refused `ambiguous-group` on every run — store-cleanly-reach-nothing, still live, for the one option the fix had not covered. `null` is genuinely two states here (the flat scope, and "predates the column") and they pick different databases, so it stays refused — and the **picker now withholds that option** instead of offering a choice that cannot work, which is AC1's own second branch. (3) `validateBundle` called the shared normalizer without the new third argument, under the comment recording that this exact drift had happened before — a bundle with a NUL in the group passed validation and aborted the cutover as a 22021 mid-transaction. ⚠️ **The general form: a field added to a key is not done when it type-checks — every reader that ORDERS refusals, every caller that passes a subset of the arguments, and every default that means two things have to be revisited.**

**Shipped 2026-08-17 — `Image Scan` was red on every PR while `main` showed green, because main's last run predated the advisory** (PR [#5288](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5288) @ `ea311884f`) — a required check failing on work that introduced nothing. `CVE-2026-53615` is a util-linux HIGH disclosed after the 2026-08-12 baseline pass, living in the `oven/bun:1.3.13` base Atlas **pins but does not build** — exactly the gap `.trivyignore` documents itself as existing to cover. ⭐ **No pin bump fixes it, and that is measured rather than argued**: under the gate's own invocation the current pin gives 8 distinct CVEs / 2 CRITICAL, `1.3.14` and `1.3.14-slim` give 3 / 0, and the only two clean images are prerelease **bun 1.4.0** canaries — not a released version, since npm `latest` and the newest GitHub release are both `1.3.14`. The file's own precondition (*"if the second command starts returning findings, the upgrade has stopped working and that is the bug"*) was checked directly: `apt-get upgrade` lands `util-linux 2.41.5-0+deb13u1`, precisely the version Trivy names as fixed, so the **shipped** images never carried this CVE and only the bare base did. One entry with the standard `exp:2026-11-01` — the expiry is the forcing function, not a bulk date extension. ⚠️ Two stale claims fixed while there, one of them costly: `image-scan.yml` described the baseline as *"currently EMPTY"* when it has held ten entries since 2026-08-12, and that sentence sent this investigation down a wrong path before the file itself was read.
**Shipped 2026-08-17 — three settings-audit residue items, and SIX of the author's own comments turned out false** ([#5290](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5290) bundling [#5263](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5263) + [#5262](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5262) + [#5264](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5264) → PR [#5292](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5292) @ `1c7a01493`) — bundled because two of them write the same object, so one commit each in sequence. Rounds: **2 (round 1 panels 9m/8m/7m + 12m comment sweep, round 2 7m/7m/10m, one fix-vs-finding pass 2m, then TWO closing sweeps 13m and 12m — agent-reported durations; session wall clock not measured)**, **stopped on the RATIO rule**, curve **~41 findings / ~41 new-surface / 0 defect-in-prior-fix → ~19 / ~5 / ~14**. The total fell and defect-in-prior-fix was three quarters of round 2, which is the stop. 22 falsifiers measured; **three came back GREEN and had to be strengthened**.

⚠️ **#5263'S PREMISE HAD HALF-MOVED BEFORE THE WORK STARTED, which is why #5290 ordered the re-location first.** The issue names the `logAdminAction` metadata in `api/routes/admin.ts`; #5271 had already routed that write through `redactAuditValue`, so the durable-table half was closed on `main` and only the **200 response body** still echoed the request verbatim. An issue body is a claim about the tree at authoring time, and this one was half-true.

⭐ **SIX COMMENTS THE AUTHOR WROTE IN THIS PR WERE FALSE, each caught by a reviewer and never by the author.** (1) *"the echo and the row cannot disagree"* — they could; the seam discarded a wrong-key definition and the new builder did not, so what actually held them together was `SETTINGS_MAP` being keyed by `def.key`, in a third file and stated nowhere. (2) *"nothing can close this"* — branding the 200 schema **does** make the raw echo `TS2322`; it just kills `extract-openapi.ts` with `UnknownZodTypeError`, so it is a trade and not an impossibility. (3) a **miscounted sibling grep** — claimed a third instance of a stale pino description; there were four, and the survivor then disagreed with the three that were fixed. (4) *"`judgement` present exactly on the clear path"* — it needs a **sensitive** key, as the PR's own test asserts. (5) *"`RuleFields | Record<never, never>` checks the field name"* — **0 errors**; excess-property checking is skipped when ANY union member is an empty object type, and `| undefined` is what gives `TS2561`. (6) *"`Partial<RuleFields>` removes the bare-`judgement` state"* — it does not; `Partial` is homomorphic, so both meaningless states still compile, probed directly. **The general form: a comment asserting a type-level or measured property is an experiment, and none of these was run until a reviewer asked.**

⭐ **THE FIX FOR ONE FINDING REPRODUCED ANOTHER FINDING'S DEFECT ONE BRANCH OVER, caught by `fix-vs-finding` in fresh context.** Folding the rule flags into the `!hasInternalDB()` warn — itself a round-1 fix for flags computed-then-discarded — emitted the `false`/`false` exoneration pair with **no** `judgement` caveat, on the one path that has no durable row to correct it, under a comment written minutes earlier calling that branch the sole trail that "should carry the most, not the least". Fixed structurally rather than at the second site: one `ruleFields` binding carries the flags **and** the caveat, so a third sink cannot take one without the other because there is nothing else to take.

⚠️ **A FALSE EXONERATION THIS PR CREATED, THEN CLOSED — and the elevation is the point.** Moving the judgement into the durable row made a **clear** record `disablesControl: false`: accurate about what the rule answered, and read as "nothing weakened". Clearing a `"10"` override on `ATLAS_TRIAL_IP_RATE_LIMIT_RPM` while the env var holds `"0"` turns the per-IP limiter **off**. The false negative pre-existed in the pino line, but nobody queries `admin_action_log` for a judgement it does not carry — after this PR they would, and get a wrong answer from the system of record. Closed with an additive `judgement: "reverted_value_not_evaluated"` marker; the reviewer's preferred fix (judge the clear on its reverted value) was **declined** because it remaps `reset_to_default → "set"` and deletes the mapping #5262's own criterion exists to pin.

⚠️ **A TEST WRITTEN TO CATCH ACCIDENTAL EQUALITY CONTAINED ACCIDENTAL EQUALITY.** The mismatch-discard falsifier used the `secret: true` fixture, which reaches the withheld placeholder through its **own** arm whether or not the discard exists — so deleting the discard left the suite green. Measured, not noticed by reading, in the same round a reviewer named that class as the one to hunt. Two more falsifiers came back green: `resetPerTest` shipped with none (the same unfalsifiable-guard sin this PR had just deleted a `try/catch` for), and the schema↔type tie was **one-directional** — `A extends B` let an optional field added to the TS type pass with 0 errors, so it needed `Equal<>` in invariant position with both sides normalised for `readonly`.

⚠️ **FILING FOLLOW-UPS WAS THE WRONG CALL AND THE HUMAN CAUGHT IT.** ~10 deferred items went into [#5293](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5293) / [#5294](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5294) / [#5295](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5295); the repo's rule is **fix inline, don't farm follow-ups**, and none of these was new machinery someone else should scope — they were defects in this PR's own surface. All three closed as done. Taking them inline paid: it surfaced a **regression** (deriving the mismatch warn from `redacted.maskReason` breaks the CLEAR path, because with no value the decision never consults the definition — so a wrong-key DELETE silently lost its warn, caught by an existing test), and the `satisfies Partial<typeof import(...)>` ratchet immediately found **six** mock stubs whose shapes had drifted from the real exports. One item is deliberately **declined**: a `try/catch` around `securitySensitiveAuditFields`, because all three rule arms are total over `string | undefined`, so it would be an unfalsifiable guard against an unverified state — exactly what this PR removed from the `COMMITTED` line.

⚠️ **`gh pr checks --watch` EXITED 0 WITH SEVEN CHECKS RED.** A sustained GitHub **Partial System Outage** (`Actions` + `API Requests` at major_outage) 429'd and 503'd `oven-sh/setup-bun` and `github/codeql-action` at `Set up job`, before checkout, across seven jobs over four attempts. The exit code is not the verdict — re-read the check list. Two recovery notes worth keeping: `gh run rerun --failed` fixes workflow-file jobs, but **CodeQL default setup (`event: dynamic`) refuses it** and needs a fresh push to re-trigger; and the status page lagged reality, so probing `codeload.github.com` directly for a `200` was what actually said the outage had cleared. No `--admin`: merged normally once green (**36 checks**), with the human asked first at the point where forcing it was the only remaining option.

**Shipped 2026-08-17 — two purge-path residue items, and a DPA erasure receipt that told the operator something false in each of two rounds** ([#5289](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5289) bundling [#5265](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5265) + [#5269](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5269) → PR [#5291](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5291) @ `519e4f697`) — both in `hardDeleteWorkspace`, one commit each in the order #5289 specified, so item 2's new `stripe_teardown_pending` read inherits item 1's actionable abort instead of shipping behind the bare 500 item 1 removes. Rounds: **2 + comment sweep**, **stopped on the RATIO rule**, curve **~26 findings / ~14 defect-in-round-1's-own-fixes**. #5265: a bare re-throw made `runEffect` answer `500 Service error (ref: …)` on the GDPR erasure endpoint, so `purge_rolled_back` is now a catch-all `PurgeAbortCode` mapped to **409** — 4xx is not cosmetic, because `classifyError` replaces a 5xx domain error's message with the opaque reference and a 500 would reintroduce the defect. The raw pg message stays out (a Postgres error can echo column values, and this is an erasure path); the SQLSTATE ships after a shape check, while the 42P01/42703 arm keeps interpolating because a relation name is not customer data.

⭐ **THE FIX TRADED AN OPAQUE FAILURE FOR A CONFIDENT FALSE ONE, TWICE, IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS.** `COMMIT` sits inside the same `try` as every DELETE, so a socket dying between "COMMIT sent" and "COMMIT acknowledged" reaches the same catch with the backend possibly committed — and round 1's 409 asserted four things there ("rolled back, nothing deleted, the org row still exists, re-run it"), all possibly false, with a remedy leading to a 404. Strictly worse than the 500 it replaced, which asserted nothing. Round 2 then found the correction over-reached one state over: `if (commitAttempted)` called **every** commit-time failure indeterminate, but a COMMIT that Postgres *refuses* answers with a SQLSTATE, which proves it did not commit — a false *uncertainty* claim replacing a false *confidence* claim on the same receipt. The stop was to enumerate rather than patch a third time: `commitAttempted && !backendAnswered(code)`, with all seven input rows pinned and **an assertion on `abort.code` in each** — the missing one, since both arms render an unshaped code as "no SQLSTATE", so swapping their order had been green.

⭐ **A WIDENING SOURCE GATED THE ERASURE IT EXISTED TO WIDEN.** `stripe_teardown_pending` is `retained` — the purge deletes nothing from it and reads it only to extend the id set — so leaving it unprobed meant a region behind migration 0141 raised 42P01 and could no longer purge **at all**: refusing to erase a customer's data because a billing outbox is missing. It now probes, degrades to the pre-#5269 reach, and says so; a skipped *source* is a third consequence with its own reason string, neither a skipped delete (rows survive) nor a skipped tombstone (cleared rows regrow). ⚠️ **And the source the triage precedent named was measured to be a cross-tenant delete.** `stripe_purged_subscriptions` carries no org column, so "tombstoned for this org" is not expressible from it; the rows it holds for this org are inserted in the same transaction from the very subquery the ledger DELETE already uses (zero rows added); and what remains belongs to **other** purged workspaces.

⚠️ **A GUARD, TWO COMMENTS AND A TEST RESTED ON A CLAIM ABOUT JAVASCRIPT THAT IS FALSE.** "JS `$` without `m` matches before a final newline" is Python/PCRE — `/^[0-9A-Z]{5}$/.test("42P01\n")` is `false` in ECMAScript. So the `length === 5` conjunct was redundant, the comments asserting otherwise were wrong twice, and the trailing-newline test had been passing on the regex alone. Conjunct and justification both deleted. Two more of the same shape: two arms of one catch disagreed about one state (the 42P01 arm reports "Nothing was deleted" for the state an early draft called indeterminate — one of them is lying), and `sqlStateOf` now validates rather than trusting "has a `code`", because `Object.assign(new TypeError(...), { code: "ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE" })` is a real Node shape that was being answered with a 409 asserting a database transaction rolled back.

⭐ **EVERY DEFERRAL WAS TAKEN RATHER THAN FILED — the correction from #5292 applied one PR later.** The one that mattered was invisible in the SQL text: the tombstone and the ledger DELETE built **identical** statements from one declaration, but read two READ COMMITTED snapshots, and `stripe_teardown_pending` has concurrent writers — so a row arriving between them was deleted **without** being tombstoned, regrowing exactly the row #3468 exists to prevent on the next cancellation webhook. One `WITH` now emits both, making the invariant a property of the statement instead of an argument about it (and subsuming the review's paired-factory suggestion, since two builders are no longer representable). ⚠️ **Four of this round's own falsifiers could not fail** — a ROLLBACK-count assertion whose fixture rolls back *successfully*, and three absent cells; plus a mock that failed both its COMMIT and its ROLLBACK, making both indeterminacy conditions true at once so neither was independently falsifiable. Also caught by measurement rather than reading: `delViaParent`'s guard regex had silently stopped recognising the one call that passes options, and was reporting `stripe_webhook_events` as purged with no delete at all.


**Shipped 2026-08-15 — the review panel was specified read-only and enforced nowhere** ([#5267](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5267); PR [#5268](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5268) @ `ba06bf63f`) — `/review-panel` says "read-only" in five places across the command and the agent definitions, and four of the five agents held **`Bash`** — `sed -i`, `>`, `git checkout`, `scripts/mutate.ts` — handed back immediately after `Edit`/`Write` were withheld, appearing only in frontmatter with no agent body explaining what it was for. ⚠️ **It fired during #5260: a panel agent mutated a file to measure a falsifier, and its restore reverted the AUTHOR'S uncommitted in-flight edits along with its own mutant**, leaving the mutant behind — caught by `git status` before any commit, but only by luck of looking. `git` cannot distinguish a reviewer's mutant from an author's unstaged edit; both are just unstaged changes, and **only the author knows what is uncommitted**. ⭐ **The motive was legitimate, so the fix states a split rather than only removing the capability** — you cannot prove a test is *able* to fail by reading it, and the panel's own standard demands measurement ("a `0` in a mutation table is a CLAIM"). Now: **the panel names the mutation, the author runs it.** A named mutation is worth more anyway — it lands in a `scripts/mutations/*.mutations.ts` spec and keeps working, while a measured-and-reverted one is gone on restore. Anything unmeasurable is reported **UNVERIFIED** with the one experiment that settles it. ⚠️ **Unverified in the way it now requires of others** — the experiment is the next panel run, specifically whether `pr-test-analyzer` names a mutation cleanly rather than going quiet about failability. If it hedges, sharpen the instruction; do not restore the grant.

**Shipped 2026-08-15 — SQL digests on the snapshot mismatch log, and an alert that was wrong about its own worst case** ([#5248](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5248); PR [#5256](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5256) @ `e1fd075b5`) — `rounds: 2 + closing comment sweep (4.8m / 5.2m / 5.8m → 9.1m / 9.9m / 10.3m → 13.2m of reported agent time; parallel within each round)`, **stopped on the ratio rule**, curve **10 findings / 10 new-surface / 0 defect-in-prior-fix → ~24 findings, ~4 new-surface vs ~20 defect-in-prior-fix**, then 8 false claims + 1 code defect in the sweep. The gate's identity check refuses a forged statement and a benign replay on one arm and logged them identically; digests of the validated SQL now separate them. ⚠️ **Round 1 shipped a line that was WRONG ABOUT ITS OWN ALERT, and that is the finding.** `buildSnapshotSql` emits no workspace and no connection, so two tenants on the same table build byte-identical SQL — a cross-tenant mint lands with `sqlDigestMatch: true`, and the comment told operators to alert on `false`. The alert key is now `returnedRequestMatch`. ⚠️ **The same principle was violated THREE times in three spellings** — `undefined.slice`, a hostile getter, and `Array.isArray` throwing on a *revoked Proxy* (so the `isRecord` guard was itself the escape, sitting one line above the `try` meant to cover it). The ratchet is structural, not another comment: the seam's return value is read in exactly one block inside the `try`, so the arms below cannot reach it and the pre-existing double-read of `validation.error` is no longer expressible. ⚠️ **Round 2's fix produced round 3's defect** — an `Error` arm added to `seamString` let `seamSqlDigest` return an attacker-chosen 16-hex string as `returnedSqlDigest`, caught by the comment sweep rather than another code round. 31 mutants; every round-2 survivor now RED; 2 survivors recorded as redundant-but-kept rather than papered over. Deferred: the same ratchet for the `runSnapshot` / `loadEntity` seams and the transaction `.catch`'s `instanceof` (8 measured escape classes, none reachable with shipped implementations, all pre-existing).

**Shipped 2026-08-15 — the SQL-gate bypass guard moves from line-adjacency to whole-file name occurrence** ([#5249](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5249); PR [#5254](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5254) @ `68dd188ae`) — `rounds: 2 + closing comment sweep (6m / 10.5m / 9.5m of reported agent time)`, **stopped on the ratio rule**, curve **~15 findings / ~15 new-surface / 0 defect-in-prior-fix → ~12 findings, ~2 new-surface vs ~10 defect-in-prior-fix**, then 2 false claims in the sweep. `warehouse-producer-bypass.test.ts` pinned the sites allowed to assert a branded snapshot type by matching the cast's own LINE; four spellings compile and walk past it — a local alias asserted through, the angle-bracket form, a line break before the name, and an import renaming a guarded type OUT. Matching on file-wide name occurrence closes all four at once. The cost is that an entry now means *"may name"* rather than *"may cast"*, so `kind: "bypass" | "annotation"` splits the two, counts are pinned, and **both kinds are checked against the tree rather than believed** — the annotation arm is vacuous today at zero entries and load-bearing the moment the widening invites the first one. Deferred to [#5255](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5255): a sixth branded type is still invisible (needs a tag convention in production source), and `plugins/<name>/src`, `cli/bin`, `cli/lib`, `api/scripts`, `mcp/src` stay unscanned. **Both shipped 2026-08-16 in PR #5278 — see the 08-16 entry above.**

⭐ **THE FIRST FIX MADE THE GUARD WEAKER THAN THE ONE IT REPLACED, AND ALL THREE REVIEWERS MEASURED IT INDEPENDENTLY.** Assembling the five names into one array — needed so the matcher's own definition would not put the file in its own result set — made that array the sole source of BOTH the regex and every fixture exercising it, so **deleting three of the five names left the whole suite green**. The pre-diff file had been stronger by accident: the regex literal spelled the names contiguously while each control assembled its own, two independent spellings that had to agree. *Fixtures that agree by construction, arriving as a consequence of a self-match fix.*

⚠️ **Then each round-1 fix closed the reported instance and left the class open — four times, all measured green.** The tuple pinned arity but not identity, so **substituting** one name for a copy of another kept length 5 and silently dropped an arm (a reviewer paired it with a real unlisted mint using that arm: still green). Stripping `//` comments left `/* */` bodies intact, because continuation lines carry `*` only by convention — so commenting out a declaration during a rename satisfied the export check, the same vacuity one syntax over. `kind:"bypass"` was checked and `"annotation"` — the quieter word — was not. And the `minFiles` floors were calibrated at *half of today's count* and verified on ONE root; the number that matters is the largest CHILD directory, so api (1612 vs 1000) and cli (35 vs 30) both passed a repoint while only `ee`, the root that had been measured, red. Sentinels replaced the proxy.

⚠️ **The machinery that closed round 1 had no falsifier of its own — which is exactly how its blind spot shipped.** Replacing the comment stripper with the identity function, or dropping the `^` anchor, each left the suite green. Hoisting the predicate and driving it with fixtures made both deletions red and caught the block-comment hole in the same move. Generalizable: **a checker added to close a finding is new surface, and new surface with no falsifier is where the next round's findings come from.** `fix-vs-finding` returned REPRODUCED on the same principle one array over — `GUARDED_NAMES` got its arity pinned while the adjacent `ROOTS` kept the identical defect, its floor test iterating the very list it validated. **17 falsifiers built and run across three commits, every one measured green beforehand.** Two process notes: a reviewer subagent's mutations collided with an edit in the shared worktree and silently reverted it (commit before dispatching reviewers), and `check-mutation-tables.sh` **SKIPS and exits 0** when `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is unset — a local green there is not a result.

**Shipped 2026-08-15 — three stacked copies of the same skills collapse onto the upstream plugin** (PR [#5251](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5251) @ `9a5d2eeff`) — no issue; found while reconciling, recorded here rather than filed after the fact. A vendored fork of the Matt Pocock skills lived under `.agents/skills/` and was symlinked into `.claude/skills/`, loose copies sat in `~/.claude/skills/`, and as of Claude Code v1.2 an upstream plugin replaces both. ⭐ **The fork had drifted a full rename cycle behind, leaving six symlinks pointing at deleted targets — and two of them were load-bearing: `/kickoff` invokes `to-issues` and `/investigate` invokes `to-prd`, so both commands had been calling NOTHING.** A symlink farm fails silently in exactly the direction a missing file does not. `.agents/` (153 files), all 29 symlinks and `skills-lock.json` are gone; `.claude/skills/` is now the three Atlas-owned skills (`impeccable`, `operator-commands`, `publish-package`); the four live renames are rewritten across 10 files and the full table is in `docs/agents/workflow.md`, verified against the upstream v1.2 notes — including two entries the first pass got wrong. Also retired a hardcoded `/home/msywu/oss/atlas/3` in `AGENTS.md`, which is always wrong for someone when checkouts run in parallel.

**Shipped 2026-08-15 — the Coverage Surface's denominators, both classes** ([#5213](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5213); PR [#5238](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5238) @ `68c82c9fd`) — `rounds: 2 + closing comment sweep (~18m / ~25m / 12m of reported agent time, plus four fix-vs-finding passes at 3m / 5m / 5m / 5m)`, **stopped on the ratio rule**, curve **~12 new-surface → 25 findings, 11 new-surface vs 14 defect-in-prior-fix**. Migration `0202` (renumbered mid-review after [#5082](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5082) took 0201) lands two tables, and the split is the design: a roster and the cycle that wrote it answer different questions, and only the cycle survives a failure — so "enumeration unavailable since \<date\>" is expressible where a per-row `stale` flag could not be, because the units are exactly the ones nobody could re-observe. **`state` is CHECK-derived from perimeter AND observed evidence** — ADR-0040 rule 3 in the schema, as an equality so neither direction can be stamped. Chat enumerates public channels beyond membership through a new `fetchConversationsListPage` (`listChannels` caps at 5 pages, drops archived, and absorbs `missing_scope` — fine for a picker, understatement for a denominator); warehouse crosses the published semantic layer with each entity's dimensions, enrollment as the perimeter. Warehouse half shipped rather than split: [#5196](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5196) had already landed. Residue named in the PR body: `CoverageClassSnapshot` stays flat for [#5214](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5214) to shape.

⭐ **Two of round 2's findings were the same failure the subsystem exists to prevent, in the subsystem itself.** An unparseable Slack `ts` collapsed onto the value the code's own comment defines as *"quiet, and quiet is not stale"* — so a `ts` format change would have stored "asked just now, never had a message" for every channel, with `page.ok` true so no arm was raised and nothing logged. And the UPSERT's `COALESCE` discriminated on `vendor_activity_at` while the reader discriminated on `vendor_activity_checked_at`, so an emptied channel kept its OLD reading under a fresh check time — a **fabricated freshness no later probe could correct**, since only a probe that FINDS a message produces a non-null. Both are M1's shape (green while nothing is happening) rebuilt inside the module written to end it. Generalizable: **a self-referential freshness reading is decorative** — the vendor must be asked, or the lag is structurally zero.

⭐ **Four fixes landed in a field nothing read, and the mutation script hid it.** Round 2 populated `CoverageSnapshotCycleResult.error` — but `registerPeriodicFiber` `asVoid`s the result and no span carried it, so the only consumer was the test suite: a fix **green by construction wherever it was observed**. Same shape for the collapse escalation (log-only, so ratio-vs-zero was unfalsifiable) and two tests that asserted their own arithmetic rather than the module's answer. The rule that came out of it: **when a falsifier survives, check whether the FIX has a reader before assuming the test is weak** — three of seven survivors this run were the script's error, not the tests'.

⭐ **A test can pass for the wrong reason in a way only a real server shows.** "the probe rotation is SCOPED and BOUNDED" named three mutations one line apart; the `workspace_id` one survived, because the neighbour row sorted after the `LIMIT` and was cut before the predicate mattered. The reviewer proved it against Postgres rather than reasoning about it — and the fixture's replacement comment was *itself* measured false (`C0AA` sorts between `C0A` and `C0B`, not before `C0A`). Ordering claims in a fixture are collation facts; run them.

**Shipped 2026-08-15 — the entity store, and M5's last mechanism** ([#5043](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5043); PR [#5232](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5232) @ `d63f963c2`) — `rounds: 2 + closing comment sweep (24m / 28m / 9m of reported agent time)`, **stopped on the ratio rule**, curve **20 new-surface → 9 defect-in-prior-fix vs 1 new-surface**. `surface → stable id` for both `_cmp` columns plus the vocabulary edge that puts a surrogate-keyed warehouse row in the same slot as a human's spelling of it. Deferrals filed as [#5233](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5233).

⭐ **A canonical surface has exactly one non-guessing source, and it is a HUMAN.** The issue never says where the name comes from; the semantic layer marks which dimension identifies a row and marks nothing as its NAME. So the alternatives were a `name`-ish heuristic — the homonym `subject-cmp.ts` calls a **confidentiality** limit, because corroboration has no grant arm and publish widens `visible_to` — or a person. `brain_enrollment` gains a `naming` flag on ADR-0039's own pattern, which also inherits the bound for free. **Scope the AC list does not name**, argued in the PR rather than smuggled.

⭐ **A fail-CLOSED guard became fail-OPEN by filtering its own input.** Round 1 dropped rows with unmintable ids *before* computing ambiguity — so a forged row stopped poisoning its norm, its twin became unambiguous, and an edge the rule had refused was emitted: two entities merged workspace-wide, no inverse. Generalizes past this store: **a guard that filters its input before the safety computation is not a guard** — poison over everything, restrict only the answer.

**Shipped 2026-08-15 — a five-issue `/ship-batch`, all five merged** ([#5230](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5230), [#5213](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5213), [#5172](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5172), [#5083](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5083), [#5082](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5082); PRs [#5234](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5234), [#5238](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5238), [#5237](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5237), [#5236](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5236), [#5235](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5235)) — five parallel workers, no halts, every panel stopped on the ratio rule rather than the cap. #5082 and #5213 both carried migrations, collided on `0201`, and resolved it by renumbering mid-review; per-issue detail below.

**Shipped 2026-08-15 — branding the snapshot REQUEST, not just the verdict** ([#5230](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5230); PR [#5234](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5234) @ `38be5597c`) — `rounds: 2 + closing comment sweep (15m / 11m / 11m of reported agent time, plus two fix-vs-finding passes at 2m / 8m)`, **stopped on the ratio rule**, curve **12 new-surface → ~15 defect-in-prior-fix vs ~4 new-surface**, then 6 more false claims in the sweep. #5042's brand made a passing verdict unforgeable by an object literal and left two doors that are not literals: a **cached** verdict authorized any later statement, and `WarehouseSnapshotRunner` took a bare request, so validate-then-run was enforced by statement order — the convention the brand replaced. The brand moves onto the request; the runner's parameter is the ordering invariant; the run loop closes the replay half at runtime. Landed before [#5228](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5228), whose cadence trigger is the second call site and the one that runs unattended.

⭐ **"The gate saw THIS statement" needed THREE mechanisms, and each round found the previous one insufficient by a different channel.** Identity (`verdict.request === request`) kills a cached or copied token. It does not stop the object CHANGING under it — `readonly` is erased, so a validator can validate, `Object.assign(request, {sql})`, and hand the same reference back; hence `Object.freeze`. And freezing does not stop the verdict ALIASING it: `validation.request` was read four times across two paths, so a getter answers the guard with the honest request and the runner with another — worse than the hole beside it, because the swapped object carries its own `workspaceId`/`connectionId` and `defaultRunSnapshot` picks the pool from those, making the residual a **cross-tenant read**. `reconcile.ts` already carried the rule (*"read ONCE… a second read of a getter or a Proxy can return a different value than the one the guard approved"*), one module over, unapplied.

⚠️ **A comment asserting what a COMPILER or a REGEX does is an experiment, and this PR lost that bet nine times** — three in the code rounds, six in the closing sweep, several of them introduced by the fix for a previous one. Two were *inverted*, i.e. they described a guard as WEAKER than it is, which is the direction that gets a guard deleted: *"the module names these types in prose, so it would keep matching even if its cast were removed"* (it matches on the cast's line alone) and *"an indexed lookup of the runner's parameter escapes"* (the assertion form matches; only the annotation form escapes, and an annotation asserts nothing). Two more were enumerations of one fact that disagreed with the OTHER copy added in the same PR. The generalizable half: **the stated CAUSE is a separate claim from the stated behaviour, and it is the one nobody measures** — *"the one-parameter mint compiles because `valid` widens to `boolean`"* was wrong four ways, and it mispredicted the fix (pinning `valid` with `as const` does not close it). The real mechanism is that a brand only ADDS a property, so `as` succeeds whenever *either* direction is comparable — which is why the bypass matcher now takes five names rather than two.

**Shipped 2026-08-15 — the fixture suite that rewrote tracked source stops needing to** ([#5172](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5172); PR [#5237](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5237) @ `286c60106`) — `rounds: 2 + closing comment sweep (23m / 25m / 13m of reported agent time, plus two fix-vs-finding passes at 4m / 5m)`, **stopped on the yield/ratio rule**, curve **20 findings / 20 new-surface / 0 defect-in-prior-fix → 28 findings, 16 new-surface vs 12 defect-in-prior-fix**, then 11 false claims in the sweep. `check-docs-brain-snippets.ts` read both roots from module constants, so its suite could only regress the guard by rewriting `atlas-connector-authoring.mdx` and `brain/ingest/types.ts` in place. #5169's two rounds had hardened that into a `restore()` with a trap, an idempotence guard, git-object verification and an `|| exit 2` escalation — and none of it closed the destructive direction: **`restore()` wrote the START-OF-RUN backup at the END, so a concurrent edit made during the run was reverted and reported as a successful restore, under `17 passed`.** `--root` / `--source-glob` / `--docs-glob` (defaults byte-identical, re-verified every round) retire all of it plus the probe page, its `.gitignore` entry, and every line-anchor helper; the page is emitted fence by fence, so a fence cannot drift out from under an anchor. Fixtures **21 → 40**, wall clock ~33s → ~20s, and a fixture now asserts `git status --porcelain` after every case. `gate-fixtures` STAYS in ci-local Stage 2: four other suites still write into the tree Stage 1 reads, and naming them corrected a comment that had listed three. Deferred as [#5243](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5243): `die()`'s exit-1/exit-2 taxonomy covers three I/O boundaries, not the process.

⭐ **Round 1's two fixes landed in one commit and one silently falsified the other.** Finding 8 kept a failing fixture's tree and printed `tree KEPT for inspection: …`; finding 7 installed `trap 'rm -rf "$TMPROOT"' EXIT`, and `$TMPROOT` is that tree's parent. The suite told an operator the evidence existed and deleted it milliseconds later — **strictly worse than the shape it replaced, which at least did not claim otherwise.** Its sibling: finding 6 checked `seed_tree`'s writes and spelled every failure `exit 2`, which kills the script before the caller can say WHICH fixture died or add "(TMPDIR full or read-only?)". The check fired and the message that makes it actionable was unreachable. **Two fixes to the same helper, in one round, are two things that can contradict each other — and nothing in the round reads them together.**

⚠️ **A COVERAGE claim was false in the commit whose message called it exact** — the third time in this arc that the assertion, not the code, was the defect. Round 1's message read *"the claim was false for six branches and is now exact"*; that same commit added three reachable `die()` arms and fixtured none, and its replacement note said the `__tests__`/`__mocks__` filter *"no input in either tree reaches"* — measured, it fires **1,376 times per CI run**. The true statement was narrower and about CONTENT rather than reach (no test file exports a `Brain*` name), which is exactly the kind that changes without anyone noticing. The header now names **four** unfixtured sites with the argument at each, and the fixture COUNT was moved out of it entirely: it had been wrong every time it was written down, because a header is the copy nobody re-measures.

⚠️ **A marker that PREFIXES a longer message is a fixture that cannot fail, and it took a mutation to see it.** Two vacuity-floor fixtures asserted `"…compared for: BrainSourceConnector"`, which also matches the two-name message — so with the docs side reading NOTHING they stayed **green** while the third correctly went red. One character each (the message's own full stop) fixed it. The same class bit the exit-2 runner: `grep -qF -- ""` matches any non-empty output, so an empty marker there is structurally vacuous rather than skipped — and `die()` now shares exit 2, so three parser mutations leave the status at exactly 2 and only the marker tells them apart.

⭐ **Two obvious ways to make a file unreadable were measured NOT to work**, which is why the arm they were meant to cover nearly shipped with a fixture that proved nothing: `Glob.scanSync` yields neither a directory named `types.ts` nor a broken symlink, so both breakages remove the file from the scan and reach a *different* arm. `chmod 000` is what remains and root ignores it — so the helper asserts its own precondition and fails loud rather than quietly covering nothing under a root CI container. The residue is recorded the same way: one fix (the root label's `root === null` half) is **unfalsifiable by construction** — reaching it means making this repository's own directory unreadable — and the mutation table says `nothing red` rather than omitting the row.

⚠️ **"A comment asserting a dataflow the code does not honour" hit ELEVEN times in one issue, four of them inside the fix for a previous instance.** Each was individually plausible: ids "already on the wire" (the importer NULLs them), refusals "reported by the caller" (it read only `length`), dropped ids that "reach `subject_cmp`" (that caller never emits one). The ratchet took the checkable member — a test that serializes every proposal to prove no id rides along — and the general class **did not mechanise**: it needs dataflow analysis the repo lacks, which is a finding about the design rather than a clean close.

⚠️ **Three CRITICALs were all previously GREEN, and the sharpest was cross-tenant.** Deleting both id guards left 197 unit + 8 `-pg` tests passing; transposing the INSERT's two norm columns survived everything (nothing fed `entityEdgeProposals` the output of `loadEntityStore`); and the store's DELETE could lose its `workspace_id` predicate — one workspace's run wiping every other's — while the isolation test passed **for the wrong reason**, never asserting the other workspace's row survived.

⚠️ **The local mutation check is not just redundant against the sharded remote — it is less trustworthy.** It runs against a working tree that may hold mutants and a Postgres under concurrency 32, and both fabricate staleness: a STALE verdict off this session's own leftover mutants cost a full regeneration cycle that came back `(unchanged)`. Remote reports the check; only regeneration must be local. Blocking on it in the foreground also risks a SIGTERM mid-run, which leaves rewritten source behind.

**Shipped 2026-08-15 — the Company Atlas guides take Company Atlas URLs** ([#5083](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5083); PR [#5236](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5236) @ `12ff5eea7`) — `rounds: 2 + closing comment sweep`, **stopped on the ratio rule**, curve **~19 findings (1 CRITICAL, 6 HIGH) / ~19 new-surface → ~29 findings, ~7 new-surface vs ~22 defect-in-prior-fix**, then 5 false claims in the sweep. ADR-0038 Layer 1 gave the guides the new noun in titles and prose and left the filenames, so `/guides/brain-sources` served a page titled "Company Atlas — Sources". Two premises in the issue were wrong and both changed the fix: **seven guides, not six** — `brain-vocabulary` landed after #5083 was filed ([#5158](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5158) / PR [#5218](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5218)) carrying the same defect, so renaming the six the issue named would have satisfied every AC and left a live `/guides/brain-*` URL — and **two mounts per guide**, because `content/shared/` feeds both loaders, so each rename retires two URLs and `meta.json` orders the sidebar without gating the route. 28 `redir … 308` lines, a new SSOT in `apps/docs/src/lib/redirects.ts`, and AC-5 verified pre-merge against a real `caddy:2-alpine` over real `next build` output: **44/44 URLs**, re-run after every round. Merge is the deploy — Railway watches `deploy/docs/**` and `apps/docs/**`, and docs does not track the prod tag. Filed as [#5242](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5242): nothing validates the Caddyfile *as a config*, so a syntax error passes every gate and fails the container at boot.

⭐ **`apps/docs` was never type-checked by anything, and 39/39 remote checks were green over a real `TS2345`.** Root `tsconfig.json` excludes it and root `type` never filtered it back in. Two reviewers predicted `Built image (docs)` would catch it; **it passed on the broken SHA** — a container build is not a type gate, and "the build would have caught it" is a claim to measure rather than assume. Now wired in.

⚠️ **One `lint:type-aware` error reds two CI jobs, and the second one lies about where.** It is titled *"scripts/ type + lint gate"* while printing a `packages/web` diagnostic — nothing in `scripts/` was wrong. That gate shells out to the shipped command and so needs a clean baseline repo-wide. `lint:type-aware` is not in the documented cheap pre-flight; on this evidence it should be. A round-1 falsifier in the same PR **held only by lexical accident** — the assertion and the thing asserted were the same substring — which is the review instrument exhibiting the defect class it exists to find.

**Shipped 2026-08-15 — the ingest catalog rows renamed as a migration, because a constant edit renames nothing** ([#5082](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5082); PR [#5235](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5235) @ `792c08ca2`) — `rounds: 2 + closing comment sweep (6.5m / 7.3m / 9.9m → 9.1m / 9.8m / 13.0m, sweep 15.0m)`, **stopped on the ratio rule**, curve **24 findings (5 HIGH) / 24 new-surface → ~26 findings (2 CRITICAL, 4 HIGH), ~5 new-surface vs ~21 defect-in-prior-fix**. The issue's premise held: `seedBuiltinKnowledgeCatalog` is `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on a stable id, so editing the constant would have given new installs the new label and left all three prod regions on the old one, with nothing reporting the divergence. **Two rows, not three** — [#5203](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5203) had already deleted `catalog:slack-history`. Migration `0201` guards **per column** so an operator's own edit survives, proves idempotence with `xmin` rather than timestamps, and emits a per-column breadcrumb plus a residue `WARNING`. 15 mutations, every one killing a named assertion. `fix-vs-finding` returned the breadcrumb fix **REPRODUCED**, fixed in-round. Follow-ups filed: [#5239](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5239) (the seeder reports success for a row an unqualified `ON CONFLICT` never wrote) and [#5240](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5240) (the `config_schema` "this brain source" string, deferred because a JSONB in-array rewrite needs its own guard semantics; CONTEXT.md records the exemption and a test pins the wording). AC-3 is prod verification on an upgraded region, not a merge gate.

⭐ **Three tests were green against real defects, and each was green for a different structural reason.** The copy lock was **tautological for exactly the two rows the issue is about** — it interpolated them from the constants it was locking, so it could not disagree with them. The id-scoping decoy existed **for Zoom only**, so a scoping defect on the Outlook twin passed 58/58. And the breadcrumb reported **per row** while the guard is **per column**, so the operator got *success* while the description still read "company brain". Coverage that is generated from the thing under test, coverage that exists for one instance of a pair, and coverage whose granularity disagrees with the guard's — three ways to be green, none of them detectable by counting tests.

⚠️ **"Concurrency" is a hypothesis, not a diagnosis — and here it was hiding a repo-wide flake source.** `api-tests` red on `migrate-pg`'s 0195 assertion was written off as `-pg` contention by a second worker and nearly by this session too. It was real: the `pg_constraint` query was not schema-scoped while its sibling column query was, and `pg_constraint` is **database-wide** — so any concurrent scratch schema at ≤0194 failed it on a correct tree. Scoped to `current_schema()`, both falsifiers measured. The fix cleared the other worker's failure as well. The seeder's neighbouring blind spot is the **arity** of a CHECK, not its semantics: any multi-column constraint is invisible to column-at-a-time generation, a merely correlational one included.

⚠️ **Two workers each asserted a merge claim about a tree that did not support it, and each caught the other's rather than their own.** #5082 reported the two migrations "fine together" from `792c08ca2` — its own merge, which holds `0201` and not `0202`. #5213 then named `dd46e302e` as the first SHA with both; it is the roadmap commit stacked above. **`68c82c9fd` is the first**, confirmed by `git ls-tree` plus a one-commit `git log 792c08ca2..68c82c9fd -- migrations/`. The substantive half: `ci.yml` 679–682 runs `--affected "$BASE_SHA"` only under `if [ "$EVENT_NAME" = "pull_request" ]` and `--all` otherwise, and neither PR touched a mutation spec — so **the full `mutation-tables` sweep genuinely first covered the pair post-merge**, where it passed 18/18. A concurrent batch needs the combined tree named explicitly; neither PR's own green says anything about it.

**Shipped 2026-08-14 — `mutation-tables` was the whole critical path, on a premise that had expired** (PR [#5224](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5224) @ `d961828e4`) — `rounds: 2 + closing comment sweep`, stopped on the ratio rule. The job justified its cost by finishing inside `Built image (docs)` at ~25 min; that job is path-gated and **skips on an ordinary PR**, so nothing was covering it. Now sharded four ways, divisor from `strategy.job-total` so the matrix list is the only place the count is written. ⭐ **The measurement in the first cut was wrong about its own provenance** — two of four figures were push-to-`main` runs and both "PR" figures came from #5222, the one PR that touched every spec and widened to `--all`; on an ordinary PR the job is under a minute. The cost is real but **concentrated**: every push to main, and any PR touching a spec's targets. ⚠️ **Round 1's fix reproduced its own defect**: ownership moved to range over `SPECS`, but the empty-shard guard kept comparing to the *selected* count, so a CORRECT partition exited 1 on any PR with ≥4 affected specs missing a residue class — and was inert on the common 1–3 spec PR where a mangled divisor still exited 0. Fixtures 17 → 21. Follow-ups recorded, not filed: exit 1 now carries usage errors *and* staleness (splitting onto 2 touches `ci-local.sh`'s contract), and nothing reads workflow YAML, so re-hardcoding the divisor is still a free mutation.

**Shipped 2026-08-14 — `comment-analyzer` reports adjacent de-slop candidates, advisory only** (PR [#5223](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5223) @ `406e4fd93`) — the sweep now also looks at comments in the **enclosing block** of each hunk, capped at five, never a must-fix, style-only. Bounded deliberately: unbounded file scope trades one problem for diff noise, rounds that cannot close, and parallel workers colliding on churn. `/review-panel` states that adjacent candidates never count toward the verdict, so the two files cannot drift on whether it gates — the failure #5077 already paid for once.

**Shipped 2026-08-14 — the final-round comment sweep now polices prose style** (PR [#5210](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5210) @ `8d3bf5e8b`) — `comment-analyzer` gains a de-slop check adapted from `/blog`'s AI-tell rules for the comment register; style-only findings rank as Improvement Opportunities, never Critical, and scope stays the diff. Process-adjacent to [#5078](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5078) (the deferral-rule weighing), which stays open.

**`v0.2.9` — Identity Activation** (tagged @ `e47d4655c` 2026-08-16, `prod` fast-forwarded from `dbec5c205`, all four services asserted `SUCCESS` on the tagged commit with the `v0.2.8` builds `REMOVED`): M5's mechanism cut reaches prod — the enrollment surface, the enrollment-scoped tier-1 warehouse producer, and the entity store, so ADR-0037's dormant `subject_cmp` and tier guard finally have a producer. Also one Slack install instead of two (#5203), the Coverage Surface's class contract and denominator snapshots, and the settings audit line that no longer carries a secret verbatim. **Six migrations (0198–0203) applied in 47 ms** — all new tables, nothing altering a populated one. **This unblocks [#5197](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5197)**, which could not be satisfied before: the tables its row counts read did not exist in the prod database.

⚠️ **`ci-local.sh` reported `RESULT: FAIL` while exiting 0 again — a different mechanism from `v0.2.8`'s, same lesson.** `test-discipline` named nine tracked test files, every one of them inside `.claude/worktrees/agent-*/` — this session's own live worktree. The gate globs the filesystem, the worktree is gitignored but physically present, and its duplicated copies carry a path prefix `scripts/test-discipline-allowlist.txt` cannot match, so **every allowlisted file is re-reported as a violation**. Proven spurious rather than argued away: `git archive HEAD | tar -x` into a scratch dir and re-running the gate gave *pass, 1759 files scanned, exactly 9 allowlisted offenders* — the same nine. ⭐ **An agent worktree under the repo root reds the primary checkout's gate, and the failure names tracked files, so it reads as real.** Also skipped locally with `TEST_DATABASE_URL` unset: `mutation-tables` (13 specs) and every `-pg` suite — and this range *did* reshape mutation anchors, so the skip mattered; remote CI ran all four `mutation-tables` shards and all four `api-tests` shards green on the tagged SHA, which is what actually covered it.

**`v0.2.8` — Dashboards Leave the Admin Perimeter** (tagged @ `dbec5c205` 2026-08-13, `prod` fast-forwarded from `3e50c3573`, all four services asserted on the tagged commit with the `v0.2.7` builds `REMOVED`): the 24-commit security arc — the first permission flags enforced outside the admin perimeter, the GDPR purge reaching brain and KB data, and the image/org-delete pass. Migrations 0196–0197 applied cleanly in all three regions (two `Applying migration` lines each, no errors). ⚠️ **`ci-local.sh` reported `RESULT: FAIL` while exiting 0** — two `-pg` files failed against a Postgres left orphaned by a deleted worktree, because `migrate-pg`'s `pg_constraint` lookup is **not schema-qualified** and matched a stale scratch schema; a clean-DB re-run was 134/134.

**Shipped 2026-08-13 — the residue sweep the runbook already prescribed** ([#5185](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5185), PR [#5187](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5187) @ `1fd342340`) — `rounds: 2 + fix-vs-finding + closing comment sweep`, stopped on the **ratio rule** (~7 new surface vs ~11 defect-in-prior-fix). `atlas-operator ops sweep-residue --region <us|eu|apac>`, DRY RUN by default, EXECUTE triple-gated (`ATLAS_RESIDUE_OK=1` + `--confirm` + a `--pg-dump` path checked to exist, be non-empty and be **under 6 hours old** — last month's dump of another region cleared the first two). Candidates derive from `purge-scope.ts` filtered to `decision: "purged"`, so `anonymized`/`retained` tables are unreachable by construction; `classifyScopeValue`'s every arm can only **withhold**, never sweep.

⭐ **`fix-vs-finding` returned REPRODUCED on all three round-1 guards** — each carried, one layer over, the defect it was written to fix. The blast-radius cap exempted DRY RUN nine lines below a docstring arguing a preview of a broken premise is *worse* than none; `to_regclass` fixed relation visibility and left `information_schema.columns`, which is privilege-filtered per **column**, so a role without the scope column still got "no scope column — reached through a parent": confident, specific, false, exit 0. And the DELETE's new `NOT EXISTS (organization)` clause is **vacuously true on an empty `organization`** — protecting nothing in exactly the state it exists for.

⚠️ **The unit fakes answer from a fixture, not from the query's `WHERE`** — reverting the `relkind` filter left them **49/49 green**; only a real relation (a VIEW planted under a purged-class name, a restricted role with a column-level grant) can falsify a SQL predicate. Two `-pg` tests exist because of that. ⚠️ **And `process.exitCode = undefined` does not clear bun's exit code**: the CLI suite reported *52 pass, 0 fail* and exited **1**, reddening `test-others` on a file where nothing failed. A green summary line plus a non-zero exit is exactly the pair the isolated runner exists to catch — the pre-flight has to be `bun run test`, or an explicit `echo $?`.

**Shipped 2026-08-13 — the PUBLIC share link gets its own flag, and #5190's residue is cleared** ([#5192](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5192), [#5191](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5191), PR [#5193](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5193) @ `7aaa23333`) — `rounds: 2 (13m / 13m) + closing comment sweep (11m)`, stopped on the **ratio rule**: round 1 was 21 findings, **all new surface**; round 2 was 19, of which **13 were defects inside round 1's own fixes**. The composition inverting is the signal, not the total.

⚠️ **#5190 widened a capability nobody decided to widen.** `POST /dashboards/{id}/share` mints a token served by `publicDashboards` with **no auth at all**, and an **absent or empty body defaults to `shareMode: "public"`** — so moving the route from `adminAuth` onto `dashboards:write` took "publish workspace data to the unauthenticated internet" from admin-only to **any `member`**, on every non-EE self-hosted deploy, with a bare `POST`. Caught before it left staging (`main` deploys to staging; `prod` was 21 commits behind). `dashboards:share` is a third flag, admin-only, enforced on the **public branch inside the handler** — because the regression is the empty body, and a gate keyed on an explicit `shareMode: "public"` would miss it entirely.

⭐ **The route-coverage test was EXTENDED, not weakened.** It asserts each route enforces exactly one flag; a route legitimately consulting two could have been "fixed" by relaxing it to `toContain`. Instead the table declares a conditional second flag, so the assertion stays an exact ORDERED set and an *undeclared* second gate still reddens. ⚠️ **And its `VALID_ID` was a ULID** — which every dashboards handler 400s on via `UUID_RE`, so **no handler body in that suite had ever been reached**. Invisible while every assertion was about middleware; it made the new in-handler check unmeasurable. Fixing it fixed the instance, not the class: round 2 found `c-1`/`s-1` were still non-UUIDs on five more routes, and two fixture *bodies* still 422'd. The exact-set loop now also asserts the fixture was ACCEPTED.

⚠️ **`migrationWriteLock` had never been mounted on ANY router**, and three comments — plus the issue's own framing — said `adminAuth` "omits it on purpose" and the workspace router "inherited the absence". `git grep` on the parent commit returns only the definition. A restored invariant reads safer than a first mount, which is exactly why the false version had to go. Round 1 then mounted it **router-wide**, which 409'd the read-classified POSTs (`…/render`, `…/export`) — so a region migration would blank every card of a dashboard anybody merely *opened*, an outage worse than the lost write. It rides on the write GATE now.

⭐ **Two round-1 fixes were worse than what they replaced, in the same shape each time.** The `does not exist` catch-narrowing fails **open** on five other error classes (`column … does not exist` — the partially-applied-migration case its own comment cited as justification — plus `operator`/`schema`/`database`/`role`), losing a write during an active migration, at `debug`, silently. And the fix's test could not tell: the only fixture matched **both** predicates, so restoring the broad match stayed 12/12 green. Five negative controls now fail it. Likewise the response-code spread **silently shadowed 13 of 34 declarations** — object spread never warns on a collision — so `POST /draft/publish` told clients a migration 409 meant "a teammate published first", remedy rebase-and-retry, which cannot succeed. That class had now bitten twice, so it got a **mechanical guard** over the emitted OpenAPI rather than a third comment.

⚠️ **A `-pg` fixture can delete its own coverage while closing a gap.** Round 1 planted a stale `viewer` row so the backfill met a real row — and thereby made every built-in *present*, so the seeder's INSERT arm was exercised by nothing (deleting the seeder call stayed 9/9 green). Its replacement idempotence assertion, `>=` on a timestamp, is satisfied by equality; removing the second seeder run entirely stayed green while the comment claimed otherwise. Now `ORG_FRESH` for the insert arm and **`xmin`** for "a write happened" — no clock in it.

⚠️ **THREE CI jobs each found something the local tree structurally cannot.** `api-tests` found a SECOND hardcoded applied-migrations fixture (both are `--affected`-blind: nothing in their source graph changes when a migration is merely *added*). **Symlink Stub Build** found the new `-pg` suite statically importing `@atlas/ee` — it type-checks locally because the api tsconfig maps the path, and `check-ee-imports.sh` passed because its allowlist is about source files.
