And **Deploy Validation** falsified [#5191](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5191)'s premise outright: *"no npm publish is required to land this"* is true of the monorepo and **false of the scaffold lane**, which builds `packages/api` against the PUBLISHED `@useatlas/types`. `PERMISSIONS` → `@useatlas/types` was implemented, measured, and split out to [#5194](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5194), sequenced after a `/publish`. Its most valuable half shipped anyway: all **seven** stale mocks (the issue said six) now spread the real tuple, and `permissionLabel` goes through a `Map` — indexed, it returned a **Function** for `"toString"` past a `?? p` fallback, from a function declaring `: string`.

⭐ **The closing comment sweep found four false claims, every one an un-swept sibling of a correction already made in the same commit** — including the "the brand cannot be minted outside this module" line, corrected in the test file and left standing in the source it documents. 37 falsifiers built and run; two recorded as unfalsifiable rather than hidden.

**Shipped 2026-08-13 — dashboards gate on workspace permissions, not admin role** ([#5188](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5188), [#5189](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5189), PR [#5190](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5190) @ `79fd4801e`) — `rounds: 2 + fix-vs-finding + closing comment sweep`, stopped on the **ratio rule** (round 1: 22 findings, all new surface; round 2: ~21, ~13 defect-in-prior-fix vs ~8 new). A fresh SaaS owner opening `/dashboards` looped `/login` → 403 → `/login` every ~5s in US prod, because the page read **403 as "not signed in"**. The cause underneath: every dashboards route was built through `createAdminRouter()`, and `requirePermission` REFINES `adminAuth` — so **RBAC could only subtract from admin, never grant to a non-admin**. An `analyst`, a built-in role whose whole job is querying data, was 403'd before `checkPermission` ever ran. `createWorkspaceRouter()` + `requireWorkspacePermission()` is the third option; `dashboards:read`/`dashboards:write` are the first flags enforced outside the admin perimeter. Three decisions were taken and recorded on the issue before building — two flags not three, **no MFA on any dashboard route** (the gate only ever enforced on admin/owner/platform_admin, so keeping it would protect the caller's org role rather than the action), and `member` gets both flags on non-EE deploys.

⭐ **The change would have shipped a regression worse than the bug.** `seedBuiltinRoles` was insert-if-absent while `resolvePermissions` RETURNS the stored `custom_roles` set and `updateRole` refuses `is_builtin` rows — so a built-in role's permissions froze at first seed, unreachable from every supported surface. Adding two flags silently **denied** them to every already-seeded workspace: invited admins lost dashboards, while `owner` (no row is seeded) was fine — so the founder's account, the one most likely to be used for QA, looked healthy. Fixed by an upsert plus backfill migration 0196.

⭐ **`fix-vs-finding` returned REPRODUCED on three of four principles**, each caught in the round that produced it. The sharpest: `GET /{id}/draft` FORKS — two INSERTs behind a `read` flag — and the round-1 sweep comment had listed the three read routes that *don't* write while omitting the one that does. ⚠️ **And the round-1 open-redirect guard was measured wrong twice over**: `startsWith("/") && !startsWith("//")` admits `/\evil.com` and `/<TAB>/evil.com` (WHATWG normalizes `\` and strips tabs *before* authority detection), and it sat on the one path that could not be exploited while two that could were unguarded. Resolving against a base and comparing origins has no arms to enumerate.

⚠️ **Fail-closed on unknown deploy mode was implemented, measured, and WITHDRAWN** — it reddened four suites covering the *supported* self-hosted no-auth configuration. `mode: "none"` means no auth is configured: a documented self-hosted posture, and a catastrophically broken SaaS region. The real defect was never the answer but the silence, and every arm now warns with its reason. **40 mutations built and run across both rounds; three started GREEN** and each produced a new test — the legacy mapping was untested because the route suite mocks `checkPermission` above it, the deploy-mode third state was invisible until the WARNING was captured, and the roles-page fallback had coverage one layer below the decision. ⚠️ One measurement harness was itself wrong first: a re-run reported 0 failures because it ran a repo-relative path from inside `packages/api`, so bun matched no files and a run that tested **nothing** reported clean.

**Shipped 2026-08-12 — the GitHub security surface: a stale Caddy pin hiding under 1,000 duplicate alerts, and the scaffolds nobody was patching** ([#5181](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5181), [#5175](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5175), partially [#4903](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4903), PRs [#5182](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5182) @ `1a6667139`, [#5183](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5183) @ `00ec2d59f`, [#5184](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5184) @ `369f6902c`) — started as "1,059 alerts, I don't like that". The count was the *symptom*; the reason it mattered is that it was **concealing** a real finding.

⭐ **1,055 of the 1,059 were Trivy's deliberate report pass, deduping to 272 unique CVEs** — inflated ~4x because code scanning refuses a shared SARIF category, so each shared base-layer CVE is counted once per image. Only 4 were CodeQL. Under that pile, `deploy/docs` had pinned `caddy:2.10-alpine` a full minor behind, carrying **4 CRITICAL and 43 HIGH fixable** CVEs. Nothing ever went red because the gate is `--pkg-types os` and every Caddy finding is a **library** finding in the compiled Go binary. ⭐ **The rule that falls out: when a base image looks stale, read the LIBRARY findings in the SARIF, not the gate** — the gate is green on a badly outdated Caddy by design.

⭐ **The upgrade, not the bump, was the load-bearing half — and reading advisories would never have shown it.** `apk add --no-cache curl` is a **silent no-op** when the base already ships curl at a lower version: `caddy:2.11.4-alpine` has `8.19.0-r0`, the fix for CVE-2026-5773/6276 is `8.20.0-r0`, sitting in the `v3.23/main` repo the image already points at. **Without an explicit `apk upgrade` the bump ADDS two gate-blocking CVEs instead of clearing them.** Measured with the pinned Trivy 0.72.0: docs 128 unique → 10, 6 CRITICAL → 0; api/web 107 → 75, 6 → 4.

⭐ **`oven/bun` was deliberately NOT bumped, and the measurement is why.** 1.3.14 produces an **identical** 75/4/0 to staying on 1.3.13 with the upgrade — the fixes live in the distro security repo, not the base image. So there was never a security argument for moving that pin, which matters because `engines.bun` holds it at `<1.3.14` for the [#2811](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/2811) `--isolate` TLA regression: the "obvious" bump would have broken the isolated test runner for nothing.

⭐ **The real exposure was the scaffolds.** Five Dockerfiles had no package upgrade at all — including all three `create-atlas/templates/docker/*`, which customers scaffold projects from, so a bare-base CVE propagated into every generated project. `image-scan.yml`'s own header names that exact risk ("several of them customer-facing scaffolds") and nothing had closed it. The nsjail **build** stages deliberately still don't upgrade — they're discarded, and `list-runtime-base-images.sh` already excludes build stages on the grounds that gating them would be red for something that cannot reach a customer.

⚠️ **A correction mid-flight, kept visible because the mistake is cheap to repeat.** The first commit emptied `.trivyignore` on a measurement taken against the **built** images. CI failed both base-image legs within a minute — the base tier scans the **bare** base, where nothing has upgraded yet. A green built-image scan says nothing about the bare-base gate; the two numbers are not interchangeable. Baseline restored at 10 entries, and they are **not** the original 11 minus one: the caddy bump retired 4 Alpine findings and introduced 2 curl ones 2.10 never had.

**Reporting narrowed twice, losing zero unique findings** — base-image legs stopped uploading SARIF (of their 113 unique CVEs, **zero** were absent from the built-image scans, since the base set *is* the set of bases those images are built from), and unfixed CVEs stopped becoming alerts (116 of 272 had no published fix — no version to move to, no decision to record), relocated to a census printed in full to the job log. The base job was **kept**, because deleting it would have traded away the discovery property that makes a new Dockerfile with an unknown base enter the matrix at all. ⭐ **Falsified by mutation:** removing the census label takes the suite 11/0 → 10/1. And test (9) deliberately asserts only that the census RUNS — measured, *neither* fixture has a single unfixed finding, so the set-difference assertion it would be natural to write **cannot fail**, and is documented as absent rather than added as coverage that isn't.

**[#5175](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5175) — `organization/delete` orphaned every workspace table.** Where [#5160](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5160) was "the purge misses ~34 tables", this was "a self-service path misses **all ~95**", reachable by any workspace **owner**. ⭐ **The posture turned on reading the implementation, not the docs.** `beforeDeleteOrganization` *does* abort — but better-auth 1.6.25 runs `setActiveOrganization(token, null)` immediately **above** the hook call, so a hook-based refusal still nulls the caller's active organization on every attempt: a persistent side effect from a *refused* operation. `disableOrganizationDeletion: true` throws at the top of the handler, before the session is read. That ordering also makes the HTTP test falsifiable with **no session at all** — flag-on gives 404 `ORGANIZATION_DELETION_DISABLED`, flag-off gives 401 UNAUTHORIZED; flipping the flag returns exactly the predicted 401.

⭐ **[#5184](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5184) — the residue runbook found nothing because it was hand-written.** Its four-table query returned **0 rows in all three prod regions**, while a discovery-based sweep over the same databases found `purged`-class residue in **all three**. ⚠️ **And the far more dangerous half: of 9 rows the sweep flagged, ONE was genuine residue.** The rest were **sentinel scope values** — `_default` (`sla_thresholds`, all three regions: the deployment-wide default tier), `<atlas-operator>` (`crm_outbox`), and empty `org_id` (`admin_action_log`, which is `anonymized` and retained **by design**). A sentinel is not an organization id, so it matches no `organization` row and reads identically to real residue — the same failure shape #5160 recorded when broadening `DELETE FROM settings` to `OR org_id IS NULL`. The old text asserted the opposite (*"the orphan signal really does mean the organization row is gone"*) directly above an instruction to delete. **AC-4 answered: no region shows any evidence the self-service path was ever used** — the single orphan carried a logged `workspace.delete` → `workspace.purge` pair from 2026-06-25, and a self-service deletion would have orphaned ~95 tables, not one.

**Spin-outs.** [#5185](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5185) — the runbook prescribes a double-gated operator sweep command that **does not exist**, and `hardDeleteWorkspace` structurally cannot cover residue (it requires the org row that residue is defined by the absence of); shipped the next day, above. [#4903](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4903) keeps the 4 multi-major npm packages: ⭐ **`bun update` is the wrong tool and fails like success** — it does not re-resolve a stale transitive behind a narrow parent range, it **adds a second copy** and promotes it into root `dependencies` (undici 4 versions → 5, brace-expansion 3 → 4). Scoped overrides re-confirmed ignored on bun 1.3.13.

**Also:** the 4 CodeQL alerts were **fixed, not dismissed** — `startsWith` treated `useatlas.dev.attacker.com` as the marketing origin and filtered it OUT of the set the assertion required to be empty. Two GitHub settings could not be enabled: `secret_scanning_validity_checks` and `non_provider_patterns` are paid **Secret Protection** features, and ⚠️ **the API returns 200 and silently leaves the flag `false`** on the free org plan.

**Shipped 2026-08-12 — four human decisions, decided then built: alias-knob visibility, the settings fail-open, the SLA figure, the Annex I rule** ([#5161](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5161), [#5162](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5162), [#5163](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5163), [#5164](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5164), PR [#5178](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5178) @ `1dc0414bf`) — `rounds: 2 (9m / 10m) + 11m closing comment sweep` (per-round figures are the max of three parallel reviewer durations), stopped on the **ratio rule** at round 2 (~11 defect-in-prior-fix vs ~9 new surface), not the cap. Four issues that were each blocked on a *decision* rather than an edit; each was decided and recorded on its issue first, then built together because the changes are small and two of them touch the same key pair.

**#5161** — `saasVisible` defaults to **true**, so the two `ATLAS_BRAIN_ALIAS_AUTO_APPROVE_*` keys were readable AND writable by a Cloud workspace admin while the reference page promised all eleven Company Atlas keys were hidden. The decision turned on one misreading: `scope: "workspace"` never meant "a workspace admin writes it" — `ATLAS_BRAIN_AUDIENCE_SYNC_ENABLED` is workspace-scoped *and* hidden, so the two alias keys were the outliers among all three, not the nine. Code moved to the promise, which is also the reversible direction. ⭐ **The guard is the deliverable, and it checks a PREDICATE rather than a count** — #5159 corrected nine→eleven and left the claim riding on it false, so a count-comparing guard would have passed it unchanged.

**#5162** — the recorded deferral argued for a general repair and rested on the path being "dormant until #5034's producer". #5034 shipped six days earlier; what actually kept it dormant was `ATLAS_BRAIN_EXTRACTION_ENABLED`, which the comment never named. ⭐ **A deferral whose stated trigger is not its real gate expires silently.** The general repair was declined — *"a failed settings load fails closed"* has one general meaning (fail boot), trading a narrow security edge for a whole-product availability failure. Instead `settingsCacheEverLoaded()` names the one window that exists (`_cache` swaps atomically, so only the FIRST load after boot can leave it empty) and `autoApproveEligible` refuses while the tier is unreadable — the rule it already applied to unreadable *inputs*, applied to an unreadable *tier*.

**#5163** — 99.9% was contractually live rather than marketing: `/terms` warrants performance *"as described in the documentation"*, so a docs table cell is warranted by reference. Withdrawn rather than backed, because per-region evidence does not exist for two of three regions sold and `platform-sla.ts` is in-process so it cannot observe its own region's outage. The sibling sweep found the internal `sla-runbook.md` still committing 99.9%/99.95% for tiers absent from `PLAN_TIERS`, with a customer-facing incident template promising credits "calculated and applied automatically".

**#5164** — the Annex I rule stated rather than inferred: Annex I lists third parties Atlas discloses data **to**; a system Atlas reads FROM under customer-granted credentials is a source, not a sub-processor. Slack was always in because Atlas *posts into* it — unstated, which made the row read as "connected platforms get listed". Applying the rule found the real gap, opposite to the one the audit proposed: **Teams, Discord, Telegram, WhatsApp** were missing. ⭐ **A retention claim was written, checked, and RETRACTED before shipping** — "deleting a source removes what it ingested" is false: KB documents are *archived* (restorable by re-ingest) and removing a brain source touches neither `brain_episodes` nor `brain_facts`, which stay live and searchable.

⚠️ **Round 2's headline is round 1's own lesson one rule over, and it is the clearest ratio-stop evidence yet.** Round 1 fixed the alias threshold to *"parse exactly as the reader parses"* and left the SIBLING rule in the same object untouched — instance, not class. Two reviewers independently measured `abuseThresholdRule` against `parseRpm` and found it wrong in **both** directions: `parseRpm` FLOORS, so `"0.9"` disables the limiter (`limit === 0` short-circuits) while the audit reported `disablesControl: false`; and it falls back to the shipped default on non-finite, so `"off"` left the limiter running while the audit cried disable — **with a test pinning that as correct.**

⭐ **A fix-vs-finding pass returned REPRODUCED on the round-1 fix itself:** the new registry→doc closure loop hardcoded an `ATLAS_BRAIN_` prefix with no vacuity floor — the same silent-locator defect as the `indexOf` heading match it had just fixed. Closed in the same round with a floor and a fixture that mutates the prefix inside the guard. ⭐ **The new adversarial fixture suite caught a real guard bug on its first run** — `indexOf("## Company Atlas")` matches the prefix of `"## Company Atlas Settings"`, so a renamed section kept passing.

⚠️ **The closing comment sweep found TEN factual errors, seven of the class it exists for — and one of them was this PR's own round-2 fix**, which silently inverted a "review measured" claim 90 lines below it. Also a named test file that does not exist, `"two hundred lines up"` for a 1,947-line distance, and a negative claim (*"nothing captures the log stream around this module"*) that was false: `vocabulary-rekey-logging.test.ts` already mocks the logger and drives that very module. The survival conclusion held; the reason was wrong, and the right reason makes the gap cheap to close.

Falsifiers measured rather than reasoned, all restored from backup files (never `git checkout`, which reverts your own uncommitted fix alongside the mutant): the latch mutation kills exactly the opt-out test; adding a key to `SECURITY_SENSITIVE_KEYS_LITERAL` without a rule is a **compile error** (TS2741); reverting `abuseThresholdRule` kills six rows across both divergence directions. **Gate count 35 → 36**, reproduced as 1 `type` + 31 launches + 2 tree-writing + 1 test before the +1. ⚠️ **Coverage caveat:** #5162's security behaviour is falsifiable only in the pg lane, which self-skips without `TEST_DATABASE_URL` — a green local `bun run test` has not exercised it.

**Shipped 2026-08-12 — the GDPR purge reaches the brain and KB data it claimed to remove** ([#5160](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5160), PR [#5171](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5171) @ `33ba76df6`) — `rounds: 2 (14m / 12m) + 10m closing comment sweep` (per-round figures are the max of three parallel reviewer durations), stopped on the **ratio rule**, not the cap. `hardDeleteWorkspace` issued 57 `DELETE FROM` statements and touched none of `brain_facts`, `brain_edges`, `brain_episodes` or `knowledge_documents` while the endpoint answered *"All data has been irreversibly removed"* and `/dpa` promised deletion of all Personal Data. `brain_facts` is invalidate-never-delete by design — `schema.ts` says *"Nothing DELETEs"* — so there was **no other mechanism behind the claim at all**. ⭐ **The structural reason it could not self-correct: NO table in `db/schema.ts` has an FK to `organization`**, so the `DELETE FROM organization` cascades to nothing and a table is purged only if named explicitly. Enumerating mechanically rather than from memory found **34** unreached workspace-scoped tables, not the four the issue named — including an encrypted credential store (`knowledge_sync_credentials`, the #3425 miss one pillar over) and two tables scoped by a THIRD column name (`reference_id`, migration 0051), which a guard checking only `org_id`/`workspace_id` reports as unscoped. That is exactly how they slipped. `lib/db/purge-scope.ts` is now a per-table decision registry over all 101 tables on `bundle-scope.ts`'s tripwire pattern, with deliberately **no "reached by cascade" kind** — an inherited cascade is the mechanism everyone assumed was working. Delete ORDER is load-bearing: two brain FKs are `RESTRICT`, so the reverse order aborts the whole transaction and leaves a workspace soft-deleted but unpurgeable.

⚠️ **Round 2 inverted rather than shrank — 19 → ~19 findings, but ~13 of them were defects inside round 1's own fixes** (vs 19/0 new surface in round 1), which is the ratio stop. Four of round 1's headline fixes closed the instance and left the class, and each is a category worth keeping: the tripwire **counted COMMENTS as statements** (`DELETE FROM scim_group_mappings` appeared twice, once in prose *this PR added* — deleting the real statement passed 15/15, and `-pg` skips silently without `TEST_DATABASE_URL` so that tripwire is the entire local gate), then its fix was anchored to line-start so a *trailing* comment still satisfied it; the wire contract **shipped the bug just removed** (`skippedTables`, an array, spread into a `Record<string, number>` — bit for bit the CLI defect fixed two commits earlier, re-exported to every consumer); the operator CLI still **exited 0 on an incomplete purge** because `hardDelete` returned a bare count; and the region-drift error was built, logged, and thrown as a plain `Error`, which `classifyError` does not match — so the operator got a generic 500 while two comments claimed otherwise. ⭐ **19 mutations measured, and FIVE started green.** The RESTRICT-order swap passed 9/9 because the seed left both FK columns NULL, so nothing could restrict; the `metadata` scrub was unfalsifiable because the column is nullable and nulling NULL proves nothing; `target_id`'s residue check was satisfied by the SET clause that writes the same string; the NULL-scope exclusion held for 1 of 23 eligible tables (broadening `DELETE FROM settings` to `OR org_id IS NULL`, which destroys the deployment-wide settings tier, passed everything); and `session`/`account` **cannot fail here at all** because they cascade from `"user"(id)` — recorded in-file with the tripwire that does catch it, rather than papered over. ⚠️ **The comment sweep found the retracted *"includes the record of THIS purge"* claim in FOUR copies, not the two round 1 fixed** — including `purge-scope.ts`'s decision SEMANTICS, the definition a future author applies to the next table — plus six wrong numbers, each re-measured rather than taken from the reviewer (`56`→57 in three files, `35`→34, *"three encrypted credential stores"*→one, *"the six that would cascade"*→eight-with-no-scope-column). ⭐ **And it found a false premise under a destructive runbook**: the residue sweep justified a production DELETE with *"`hardDeleteWorkspace` is the only code path that deletes an `organization` row"* — **verified false**, Better Auth's `organization/delete` is reachable by any workspace owner and runs no Atlas cascade, so an orphan does not prove a purge happened and that path orphans all ~95 tables. Filed as [#5175](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5175); type-level derivations that would retire three runtime tripwires into compile errors are [#5176](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5176). Also widened the per-user F-36 erasure, which was clearing strictly LESS than the bulk purge — the stronger Article 17 obligation, doing less.

**Shipped 2026-07-28 — SaaS default model picker off the live gateway catalog** ([#4869](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4869)): PR [#4870](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4870) replaced the three hardcoded Anthropic options in `/admin/billing` with the live Vercel AI Gateway catalog (~300 entries, server-cached with a TTL + bundled fallback, capability-filtered to the ~190 that can drive the agent loop) and moved the curated shortlist out of source into the hot-reloadable `ATLAS_RECOMMENDED_MODELS` setting; PR [#4871](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4871) dropped the output-equivalent token weighting entirely (`gateway_cost_usd` measures cost exactly, so the haiku/sonnet/opus approximation had nothing left to add) and priced the operator spend view from the same live catalog; PR [#4873](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4873) repinned the scaffold templates once **`@useatlas/types@0.7.0`** published (tag `types-v0.7.0`, cut from `46e29b2cf`).

An adversarial review of both PRs (four specialist reviewers per diff, every empirical claim re-derived against the live catalog) found the live-catalog tier **never activated for the models it was built for**: the background warm sat below the static family table in BOTH ladders, and the table's `claude` rule matches every Anthropic id — so a workspace on `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5` sized compaction at 200k against a real 1M window indefinitely, and Opus priced at a 3x-stale $15/$75 against a live $5/$25. Both were pinned as intended behavior by tests. Also fixed: a gateway outage told operators to "prune or replace" 8 of 9 *correct* shortlist ids; the bundled fallback silently drove compaction sizing while reporting `contextWindowSource: "catalog"`, and had drifted a full model generation behind the shipped shortlist; unknown model types failed OPEN to `language` (the one value that passes the picker's gate); widening `GATEWAY_MODEL_TYPES` broke older web bundles via `z.enum` inside `z.array`; `isSelectable` was a browser-only filter while `PUT /admin/model-config` took any string; and the billing page discarded catalog fetch errors. Every fix carries a regression test verified to FAIL without it — including a real-Postgres test for `logUsageEvent`'s INSERT, which mocks structurally cannot cover (fire-and-forget write; the circuit breaker disables all metering after 5 failures).

**Deploy-order constraint for the next promote: web before api.** `GatewayCatalogModelSchema.type` gained `.catch("other")`, but that only protects bundles built from `46e29b2cf` forward — an older deployed web bundle rejects the three new enum members and, because the object sits inside `z.array()`, discards the WHOLE catalog response (blank picker, no error). The 0.7.0 publish also unblocks consolidating the capability predicate, currently duplicated across api and web behind matching tests.

**Shipped 2026-07-28 — the gateway catalog is awaited once per turn** ([#4872](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4872), PR [#4875](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4875)): the spin-out above, taken. `agent-compaction.ts` claimed the trigger "runs inside `prepareStep` on every step and cannot await" — false; `resolveCompactionSettings` is reached once per turn from an already-`async` scope in `runAgent`, and `prepareStep` only consumes the resolved value. That false premise was the entire justification for the `peekModelContextWindow`/`warmGatewayCatalog` pair, and it is what let the warm drift below the static table. One `await` retires all four moving parts: `lookupModelContextWindow` now fetches on a cold cache (live window on turn **1**, not turn 2+), air-gap gated on the `/` id shape, racing a **1.5s hot-path budget** against the static fallback so a slow or unreachable gateway can never delay or fail a turn — the fetch's own 10s ceiling stays where it belongs, in front of the admin picker. The pricing path keeps its sync per-row reader and awaits one `primeGatewayCatalog` before the fold instead.

Review (two rounds, four specialists each) surfaced three things worth recording. `contextWindowSource` reported `"catalog"` for BOTH the live gateway and the static family table — so the same model could resolve 1M on one turn and 200k on the next and be indistinguishable in telemetry or in a test; split to `"gateway"`/`"static"` and now logged, which is what makes the tier ordering unable to regress silently again. The catalog was being awaited even with compaction **disabled** — the shipped default — so every default deploy would have paid up to 1.5s on the hot path for a number `shouldCompact` short-circuits past; the network tier is now gated on `enabled`. And retiring the fire-and-forget warm silently removed the only `log.warn` on the caller side of the load, so a merely SLOW gateway would have reinstated the original bug invisibly; budget-elapsed and uncatalogued-model faults are now warned, deduped per cache-fill attempt and per model id. Separately, `platform-demo.test.ts` was found making a live call to `ai-gateway.vercel.sh` and pricing an assertion off Anthropic's published rate — green only because the live haiku price happened to equal the static family rate. Every guard was verified by breaking the implementation and confirming the specific test goes red, including the per-STEP regression (6 fetches vs 1).

Shipped 2026-07-18 from the [Architecture Backlog](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/49) — **region-migration export bundle scope drift closed** ([#4460](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4460) → PR [#4724](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4724)): the bundle (v2) now moves every content pillar — dashboards (cards + per-user drafts, share tokens re-minted by the owner, `next_refresh_at`/`next_run_at` recomputed at import), knowledge documents (link graph + review status, FTS regenerates), scheduled-task definitions, and durable agent session memory — while integrations are recreated (per-region ciphertext + region-bound OAuth callbacks; re-connect checklist in `data-residency.mdx`) and `agent_runs` checkpoints stay (region-local resume leases). One bundle producer for the executor **and** `atlas-operator export`; a per-table `exported`/`stays`/`platform` registry (`lib/residency/bundle-scope.ts`) + Drizzle-enumerating tripwire test makes the next pillar fail CI instead of silently missing the bundle. **Unblocks #4458's destructive cleanup: `STAYS_TABLES` is its deletion scope; `platform` tables must never be touched.**

Shipped 2026-07-18 from the `v0.1.0` milestone: **scheduled internal-DB backups are real** ([#4457](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4457) → PR [#4723](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4723)) — the dead `startScheduler` cron loop (zero production callers; a sold Business entitlement + DPA promise with no backing mechanism) replaced by a `scheduled_backup` periodic fiber reached through the `BackupsManager` Tag, a `BackupStorage` driver seam (local fs default + Bun-native S3 for Railway buckets, per the maintainer's option-ii decision), an atomic cross-replica cadence-window claim (partial UNIQUE index on `backups.scheduled_window`, migration 0177, proven against real PG), error-level degraded logging for header-only verification, and a `/api/health` `backups` tripwire component that degrades — never 503s — when the newest verified backup outages the cadence window. Docs reconciled (backups.mdx rewritten; SaaS env-vars page + `.env.example`). **Operator follow-up (post-merge, in the PR checklist): per-region buckets + `ATLAS_BACKUP_S3_*`, per-region scratch Postgres + `ATLAS_BACKUP_VERIFY_SCRATCH_URL`, then verify the fiber start log + first `verified` row + healthy `/health` component per region.**

Shipped 2026-07-18 from the [Architecture Backlog](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/49) — **region-migration Phase 4 cleanup now executes** ([#4458](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4458) → PR [#4725](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4725)): the previously-unconsumed `getCleanupDueMigrations` is driven by a new `region_migration_source_cleanup` periodic fiber (hourly, `registerPeriodicFiber`, span + due/cleaned/skipped result attributes), so a migrated workspace's source-region data is actually **deleted** after the 7-day grace period — closing the residency/PII promise gap in `data-residency.mdx`. The deletion scope derives from #4460's bundle-scope registry (`EXPORTED_TABLES ∪ STAYS_TABLES`; `platform` never touched) via a per-table rule map (`lib/residency/cleanup.ts`) locked to the registry by a tripwire test (Drizzle-validated columns, `chat_cache` scoped by `value->>'orgId'`, parent-first delete ordering for the FK-less `slack_threads`). Each migration's cleanup is one transaction (deletes + `source_cleaned_at` stamp, migration 0178) — partial failure rolls back to "still due" and retries; a cutover guard refuses to delete a workspace homed back in the source region; the real-PG roundtrip suite now runs every generated DELETE against the live schema.

Shipped 2026-07-27 (launch day) — the **post-M1 follow-up wave closed out**, all unmilestoned, all riding `v0.2.0`: the nsjail pin reports the backend that will actually build ([#4834](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4834) → PR [#4850](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4850)); a widened fact no longer discloses its first episode's channel/actor/timestamp ([#4836](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4836) → PR [#4848](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4848)); `/admin/sandbox` treats fail-closed as an outage rather than a backend id ([#4837](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4837) → PR [#4849](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4849)); the SaaS boot guard derives its fail-closed rule from `planSandboxSelection` instead of hand-mirroring it ([#4838](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4838) → PR [#4847](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4847)); and a deployment can now declare it expects no datasource, so `/health` stops degrading forever on a knowledge/brain-only deploy ([#4854](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4854) → PR [#4855](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4855)). Riding along untracked: the hotfix lane documented + CI wired to `hotfix/**` (PRs [#4845](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4845), [#4846](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4846)), the `/audit-*` command-family rename (PR [#4841](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4841)), the README grounding-surfaces lead (PR [#4843](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4843)), `@useatlas/types` 0.6.0 (PR [#4852](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4852)), and the M1 soak manifest marked done (PR [#4853](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4853)).

Shipped 2026-07-27 from the [Architecture Backlog](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/49) — **two CI-reliability fixes, both root-caused past their reported symptom**: `--affected` normalises git's repo-root-relative paths at one boundary, and additionally learned to see cache-busting template-literal imports — 32 test files were invisible to it via that edge independently of the path bug ([#4851](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4851) → PR [#4856](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4856)); and the migration advisory lock is scoped to `current_schema()` instead of a database-wide constant, which was making every `-pg` suite serialize on one lock — 8 concurrent suites took 30.5s against their 30s `beforeAll` budget, so a random one failed per run ([#4844](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4844) → PR [#4857](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4857)). The reported "shard 3/4" framing was falsified — round-robin shard membership shifts on any test-file add, and each matrix leg has its own Postgres.

Shipped 2026-07-27 from the [Architecture Backlog](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/49) — **both halves of the security-claim gap #4821 hedged are now earned back**: container-image scanning is real, gating built images and every Dockerfile's runtime base on fixable HIGH/CRITICAL OS packages, with a deliberately-vulnerable fixture proving the gate can go red ([#4822](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4822) → PR [#4859](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4859)); and the incident-response runbook exists, organized around the DPA's 48-hour clock and grounded in mechanisms that ship today ([#4820](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4820) → PR [#4862](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4862)). Both moved out of `/dpa` Annex II's roadmap clause into the in-place TOM list, leaving only third-party pen testing ([#1928](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/1928) Track 3) hedged. Two follow-ups the scan surfaced: an 11-entry `.trivyignore` baseline of upstream base-image CVEs expiring **2026-09-01** (no pin bump fixes them — the pinned `oven/bun:1.3.13` digest *is* the current tag), and 34 fixable npm advisories in the shipped api image, reported to code scanning but left to Dependabot by design. The runbook lands with four `DECISION NEEDED` callouts — severity tiers, on-call tree, notification authority, incident-record retention — deliberately unanswered rather than invented.

**Tagged `v0.1.1`** (2026-07-28, prod-verified across all three regions) — a security hotfix closing all four better-auth advisories, cut from the `v0.1.0` tag rather than `main` in the [hotfix lane](../../docs/development/release-process.md#when-main-is-not-a-safe-hotfix-source)'s first real use, since the unreleased Brain M1 arc sits on `main`. GHSA-j8v8-g9cx-5qf4 (personal SCIM providers unbound to their creator) needed code beyond the 1.6.25 bump: `providerOwnership` plus migration 0184, which seals pre-existing ownerless rows to a reserved owner rather than deleting a live IdP connection ([#4863](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4863), [#4864](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4864); backport [#4865](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4865)). Pre-flight found zero `scimProvider` rows in us/eu/apac, so 0184 sealed nothing on Cloud.

Shipped 2026-07-28, unmilestoned, riding `v0.2.0`: the **launch-week blog drip restored** (PR [#4867](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4867)) — a future `isoDate` does not hold a post back on a static export, so Wed–Fri were publicly readable on day two; a request-time gate is impossible because post data also ships in Next's RSC hydration payload, so the three are held off `main` as revert-able per-day commits. Plus the **passkey-tile re-auth flake**, root-caused to a fire-and-forget promise racing testing-library's 1000ms wall-clock default and proven closed with an injected-delay case rather than a green-run loop ([#4866](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4866) → PR [#4868](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4868)).

Shipped 2026-07-28, unmilestoned, riding `v0.2.0`: **12 npm advisories cleared** (PR [#4879](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4879)) — `next` to 16.2.12 and `js-yaml` to 5.2.2, the directly-fixable slice of the ~34 the #4822 image scan surfaced. The two structural reasons the rest persist were split out rather than papered over: the Dependabot handoff `image-scan.yml` assumes has never worked because the dependency graph doesn't parse `bun.lock` ([#4878](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4878)), and the api runtime image ships the whole monorepo, putting 68% of the npm findings in packages the api never loads ([#4880](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4880)).

Shipped 2026-07-29, unmilestoned, riding the next tag: **both #4879 spin-outs closed.** The api runtime image now ships only `@atlas/api`'s production closure ([#4880](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4880) → PR [#4881](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4881)), and [#4878](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4878)'s never-working Dependabot handoff is replaced by a real one — Trivy detects, a Dependency Submission snapshot makes alerts fire on `bun.lock`'s closure, remediation is hand-authored root `overrides` (PRs [#4884](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4884), [#4885](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4885)). `bun` is a separate Dependabot ecosystem with no security updates, so no configuration could ever have fixed it.

Shipped 2026-07-29: **Dependabot's bun version-updates config removed** ([#4890](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4890) → PR [#4892](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4892)) — its grouped PRs could never pass the required `drift` gate, which compares the `create-atlas` templates a run rooted at `/` cannot reach, while `/deps-update`'s `syncpack fix` pass covers them by construction. The **first Group A sweep since #3952** then landed server-side only (35 packages, PR [#4893](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4893)); the web/docs half is held on [#4891](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4891)'s runner SIGILL ([#4896](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4896)), with [#4894](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4894), [#4895](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4895) and [#4889](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4889) spun out for real breaks the sweep surfaced.

Shipped 2026-07-29: **the runner SIGILL root-caused — `next build` was running on bun-as-node** ([#4891](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4891)). Not a flake, not a dep, not a CPU-feature mismatch, and not really SIGILL: the raw job log carries a full bun crash report the buildx summary line hides, and it decodes to `napi.ThreadSafeFunction.enqueue` (`napi.zig:1654`) under `RunAsNodeCommand`. `SIGILL`/132 is bun's panic handler trapping *after* a SIGSEGV — the issue's original title was right. `next` is a `#!/usr/bin/env node` bin and **`bun run` honours that shebang**, so the first `node` on PATH builds; the `oven/bun` image ships only a shim (`bun-node-fallback-bin/node` → `bun`), which is why dev machines and the CI `build` job — both with real node — always passed while the image crashed. Upstream and open across bun 1.3.2→1.3.13 ([oven-sh/bun#25864](https://github.com/oven-sh/bun/issues/25864), [#24829](https://github.com/oven-sh/bun/issues/24829)), so the AC's "test the previous pin" experiment was moot. Fix: a digest-pinned real `node` copied into the builder stage of `deploy/web`, `deploy/docs` **and `create-atlas/templates/docker`** — the scaffold had the identical exposure, uncaught because no customer had lost the race yet — each followed by a `typeof Bun` assertion, since deleting the `COPY` would restore a bug that passes locally and only fails on the deploy path. **Not reproducible locally** (five pre-fix runs passed, incl. `--cpus 4` / `--cpuset-cpus 0-1`), so the runner jobs are the only verdict. Two things surfaced and left open: nothing *required* builds the web or docs image (`Deploy Validation` covers api only; `Image Scan` covers all three and is advisory), and `> Build error occurred` vs `panic:` + a `bun.report` URL is the discriminator that separates a genuine type break from this bug in the raw log.

Shipped 2026-07-30: **the four held dependency chores cleared** ([#4889](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4889), [#4894](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4894), [#4895](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4895), [#4896](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4896) → PR [#4901](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4901)) — neither "security-relevant" hold was what its issue suspected: `@hono/zod-openapi` 1.5.x exposed our own missing `defaultHook` on two routers (10 tests had codified the leak as expected behaviour), and `just-bash` 3.2.0 fails **closed** under bun while still reporting a working backend, so it stays pinned at 3.1.0. `/deps-update` absorbed the seven constraints the #4893 sweep taught, including the one-sided playwright hold and the `apps/docs` gap in `bun run type` (PR [#4897](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4897)).

Shipped 2026-07-30: **the PR-facing CI workflows consolidated** (PR [#4898](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4898)) — the required `ci` umbrella now `needs:` the four `api-tests` shards and `ee-stub-build` so a reshard stops being a silent branch-protection break, failed PR evals stopped rendering as green checks nobody opened, and ~14 copy-pasted setup preambles collapsed to one composite action. Its deferred operator step was **applied 2026-07-30** during `/tidy`: branch protection went from nine required contexts to five (`ci`, `Deploy Validation`, `Image Scan`, `Analyze (javascript-typescript)`, `fork-pr-gate`), dropping the shard/`Symlink Stub Build` duplicates and making `Image Scan` block for the first time since #4822 built it — which turns the `.trivyignore` baseline's **2026-09-01** expiry into a merge-gate event.

Shipped 2026-07-30: **19 of the 26 npm advisories on `bun.lock` cleared** ([#4903](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4903) → PRs [#4904](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4904), [#4905](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4905)) — the first cohort ever visible, since #4878's Dependency Submission snapshot is what made alerts fire on bun's closure at all, and one Dependabot structurally cannot fix. Most were not distinct bumps: all seven `undici` alerts were **one** stale nested copy (`@vercel/sandbox` declares a caret, not a pin), and the reflex top-level override would have dragged the healthy 8.x copies *down*, since bun overrides cannot be path-scoped. The two tools are not interchangeable — deleting a *hoisted* entry re-resolves the whole graph (172 packages moved, four documented holds broke incl. the #4897 `stripe` API-version trap), while deleting a *nested* one is surgical; overrides pin without re-resolving. The 7 that remain are blocked with named constraints, not unfinished: `brace-expansion` at three simultaneous majors, two better-auth advisories whose only fix is a `1.7.0-beta.4` prerelease (one already mitigated in code by `v0.1.1`), and `js-yaml` v5 having dropped the default export `fumadocs-*` needs. **A green local battery proved nothing here** — the `js-yaml` break passed `type`, api 990/990 and test:others 38/38, and failed only in `generate:api` during the build job, exactly the `apps/docs` gate gap #4897 had documented three days earlier.

Shipped 2026-07-30 — **Company Brain extraction enabled in prod, and the two Slack bugs turning it on exposed.** `ATLAS_BRAIN_EXTRACTION_ENABLED=true` went on all three api regions (the M1 dogfood obligation `v0.2.0` deferred), and the Slack reinstall that followed added `message.channels`/`message.groups` event subscriptions for the first time. That single config change surfaced **two latent bugs, both multi-workspace-only, both silent, both from one root pattern**: `@chat-adapter/slack` populates instance state inside `initialize()`, which runs *solely* on the single-workspace `defaultBotToken` path — so anything depending on it is dead in SaaS/BYOT while passing every self-hosted test.

Shipped 2026-08-02→03 — **the M3 review-panel residue, each finding taken as its own change rather than widened into the PR that surfaced it**, all untagged: a credential-bundle parse failure no longer echoes decrypted plaintext through `err.message` ([#4984](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4984) → PR [#4988](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4988)); a brain source and its audience re-verifier now register as one unit, so the ingests-then-goes-invisible half-state is unrepresentable instead of merely guarded ([#4985](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4985) → PR [#4992](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4992)); Outlook's block-vs-skip became a discriminated union instead of a boolean plus a hand-incremented out-param, the #4965 outage class in its silent direction ([#4986](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4986) → PR [#4990](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4990)); and the "in tension (N)" badge stopped counting settled counterparts ([#4961](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4961) → PR [#4989](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4989)) — the fix whose leftover the badge work below then completed.

Shipped 2026-08-02 — **the brain review surface's two honesty gaps, both found by review panels rather than by use** ([#4994](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4994) → PR [#4996](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4996); [#4995](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4995) → PR [#4997](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4997), both untagged). Neither was a wrong answer — both were a signal that **asserted more than it had established**, which is the failure mode a queue built for human trust cannot afford twice.

The Outlook stall detector declared `skips: MessageSkips` once per *pass* but ran once per *mailbox*, gating on `skips.blockedAudience > 0` — so the first mailbox to block armed it for every mailbox after it, and any later one that merely ran out of budget or dropped a page was named as stuck having blocked nothing. Over-reporting, i.e. the safe direction, which is exactly why it survived #4986's review: this is the only signal separating a retryable block that never clears from a transient one, and a detector that cries wolf on innocent mailboxes teaches operators to skip the line that is supposed to be unignorable. Gated on a per-mailbox delta against the SSOT counter rather than a second hand-maintained one, so a future block arm cannot forget to increment it.

The list badge was the mirror image. #4961 correctly stopped counting arbitrated rivals, but the row it left behind was indistinguishable from a claim nothing ever contradicted — the arbitration vanished from the list and the stats tile's "N in tension" had no row to point at. A muted "Conflict resolved" badge restores it. The load-bearing part is what it refuses to say: `TENSION_FANOUT_CAP` is page-wide and biased to the tail, so a row can arrive holding a partial rival list, and a settled-looking partial list would assert an arbitration a dropped rival contradicts. The predicate therefore takes the **row**, not the rival list, so omitting the cap check is inexpressible — and the walk's null-endpoint drop now raises the same page-incomplete flag the cap does, since #4995 turned that previously-cosmetic arm into a false affirmative. Understating the violet count under the cap stays accepted; asserting a resolution that is not established does not.

Shipped 2026-08-12 — **the #4479 email recipient-domain deprecation completed, and a phase-2 drop whose own tests could not see it** ([#4663](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4663) → PR [#5168](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5168), untagged). Phase 1 shipped 2026-07-16 and then rode **23 tags** — `v0.0.55` (2026-07-17) through `v0.2.7`, per `git tag --contains cce2ee05d | grep -c '^v[0-9]'` — rather than the one release the two-phase discipline assumes, so the drop was long overdue: the retired env-only knob, its fallback branch and both of its once-per-process warn latches are gone, and `ATLAS_EMAIL_ALLOWED_RECIPIENT_DOMAINS` is the gate's only domain source. Producers and consumers were swept as **two separate claims** (the #5028 lesson) across tracked *and* gitignored files, and — since deployed env is not in the repo — across all four live Railway api services via `list_variables` on 2026-08-12: `api`, `api-eu`, `api-apac`, `api-staging` set **neither** knob, so prod agent email was already workspace-members-only and this merge is a runtime no-op.

**The transferable finding is about the falsifier, not the code, and it survived a round of being "fixed".** A removal's replacement tests asserted the *end state* (unset survivor ⇒ members-only) — which is identical before and after the change — so re-adding the fallback left **56/56 green**, and the mutation that appeared to prove them was red only because the retired var had been injected from the shell rather than established by the test. All three code reviewers found it independently. **Restating it as a rule: when a change removes an input's influence, the only test that can tell before from after is one that SUPPLIES that input and asserts it has no effect** — which is why the retired name deliberately survives AC-1's grep in the three suites that set it. Round 2 then found the repair over-claiming in the same direction three ways: two "this is the tree's only mention" comments, one of them falsified by a file the same commit added; a "three tests catch this" claim where each of the three catches a *different* reintroduction shape and none catches all; and a premise assertion built on a module-global warn latch that measured nothing on any repeat execution (`--rerun-each 4` → 3 fail) and could be satisfied by a same-substring warn from `email/delivery.ts`. Replaced with a latch-free premise read off `hasInternalDB()` and the gate's own verdict. Rounds: **2, closed on the ratio rule** — round 1: 19 findings, all new surface; round 2: ~22, of which ~13 were defects inside round 1's fixes. Panel wall-clock 8.1m then 15.6m.

Both panels' most valuable finding was the same class and neither was in the new code: a **pre-existing comment that documented the bug as intended design** (`runMessage`'s block-arm block, found independently by three reviewers) and two wire-type docs that the API change silently falsified. In a codebase where the comments *are* the design record, that is how a fix gets reverted by the next reader.

**`v0.2.1` — Slack Loop Fix + Supply-Chain Hardening** (tagged @ `31e08c64e`): Atlas answered its own Slack posts in an unbounded loop in prod — ~26 turns in 90 seconds, one a 144k-token cross-region query ([#4907](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4907) → PR [#4908](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4908)). `saveInstallation` never persisted `botUserId`, which disables the adapter's *entire* `isMessageFromSelf` check: of its three tests, two read the null instance fields and the third reads a request-context value sourced from the `chat_cache` row Atlas wasn't writing. `lib/slack/store.ts`'s own header had documented the gap and deferred it to "a future `auth.test` round-trip" by the adapter — a round-trip that does not exist, since the adapter only ever *reads* the field and its sole writer is the SDK OAuth callback Atlas bypasses. Both install paths now persist it (OAuth `bot_user_id`; BYOT `auth.test` `user_id`, which the route was already fetching and discarding), migration `0185` backfills existing installs so nobody reconnects, and a per-thread reply circuit breaker gated on `author.isBot` bounds any future loop cause-agnostically. Review panel caught that the **DM path had no breaker at all** and that the first fix protected only *new* installs. Also in the tag: 19 of 26 npm advisories cleared, the api image scoped to its production closure, `next build` on real node in the bun images, CI shards folded into the `ci` umbrella.

**`v0.2.2` — Slack @-Mention Detection Fix** (tagged @ `092ac1a0c`): with `app_mention` *and* `message.*` both subscribed, Slack sends two events per mention sharing a `ts`; the SDK dedupes them and the plain `message` event won every time, arriving with `isMention` unset ([#4909](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4909) → PR [#4910](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4910)). The text fallback could never match: the adapter defaults `userName` to the literal `"bot"` and, being truthy, **shadows** `Chat`'s — so setting only the `Chat` side (which the code already did) looks right and does nothing — while `resolveInlineMentions` strips the bot's user id from the text, killing the id patterns. Name matching is the only remaining route, so the handle now comes from one shared constant passed to both. Proactive keeps its `message.*` subscriptions; the alternative fix would have traded one pillar for the other. Live-verified in prod: one `New mention received`, one `agent finished`, **zero** `Follow-up message received` — the second count is what proves #4907 closed. Residue [#4911](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4911) — `skipSelfMention`, the same class, leaving mention detection on a single mechanism — **closed 2026-08-02** (PR [#4991](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4991), untagged): a vendored `bun patch` swaps the dead instance field for the ctx-aware `botUserId` getter the adapter already resolves per request, so the id patterns work again and the display-name match is no longer load-bearing alone. The patch is a **standing obligation on every `@chat-adapter/slack` bump**, and `bun install` is not the gate for it — measured on bun 1.3.13, a stale `patchedDependencies` version key is silently ignored and a non-matching hunk is applied fuzzily without warning. A pinning block in `plugins/chat/src/adapters/slack.test.ts` is the only real gate; two of its six cases redden on a reverted patch, one of them through the real signed-webhook path.

The pattern is now written into [chat-plugin-atlas-contract.md](../../docs/architecture/chat-plugin-atlas-contract.md) as its own ⚠ row — that doc exists to catch exactly this recurring class, and these are rows 4 and 5.

**Shipped 2026-08-01 — Brain M2 merged to `main`** ([milestone #92](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/92) CLOSED 19/19, PR [#4950](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4950) @ `b01c8b48a`, 70/70 green) — ADR-0036's T4 complete; untagged, riding the held `v0.2.x` train. The two closing issues shut the tool-surface gaps M1 left: `runAgent`'s `tools` is now **compiler-required** with the five bug shapes pinned as `@ts-expect-error` fixtures ([#4943](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4943)), and `buildRegistry`'s "fatal" misconfiguration finally fails boot instead of being swallowed by all five callers ([#4940](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4940)). Detail + the closeout review in [`ROADMAP-archive.md`](./ROADMAP-archive.md).

Shipped-milestone detail (v0.0.43, v0.0.45, v0.0.46, the staging track) lives in [`ROADMAP-archive.md`](./ROADMAP-archive.md); one-line hooks stay in [History](#history).

**`v0.2.3` — Brain M2 + M3, and the first real prod soak** (tagged @ `1618237be` 2026-08-03, all four services verified on the tagged commit): two milestones in one tag — M2 ([#92](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/92), 19 issues) and M3 ([#93](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/93), 9 issues) — both merged untagged and riding the held `v0.2.x` train. One migration (`0186`). Also in the tag: **web signup had been broken on every cross-origin deployment since launch day** ([#4998](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4998)) — the Turnstile token's `x-captcha-response` header was missing from the CORS allow-list, so the browser failed the PREFLIGHT and the POST never left the page. It fails only for a user whose captcha *works*: local dev is same-origin (no preflight) and automation passes when the widget never loads, which is the inverse of the usual "green locally, red in CI" shape. MCP `start_trial` was unaffected — a headless door with no browser and no CORS.

Then the connector was installed and **the brain ingested real data for the first time** — 16 episodes, 10 facts, correct ACL split (private channel → `audience:` grant, public → `{org}`), and audience membership resolving to a real person. Three messages of deliberate soak produced three outcomes: a Series A conflict flagged correctly, cross-channel corroboration strengthening one row with three provenance edges, and **one silent miss that is the finding of the day** — [#5000](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5000): collision detection is exact-string on `subject`+`predicate`, so an extractor that emitted `is priced at` where it had earlier emitted `priced at` left two contradictory prices coexisting, both draft, both publishable, nothing flagged. **M2's entire conflict apparatus is gated on an LLM reproducing a byte-identical predicate across independent calls, and every M2 test hand-writes both sides of the collision so the predicates agree by construction.** Staging holds the same claim under a third phrasing, which is independent confirmation rather than a one-off. Arbitration then worked end-to-end: retraction on the record, and #4823's grant widening firing at a channel boundary with `pre_widening_visible_to` preserved. Two more found by review rather than use: [#5001](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5001) (the pending-pill count went stale after publish — whose own fix introduced a modal-unmount regression the review caught before merge) and [#5002](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5002) (same class, reject path). [#4999](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4999) filed: Atlas-published Zoom + Microsoft apps so Cloud customers install without registering their own — both M3 connectors are BYO-app for everyone today. **Still untested: Zoom and Outlook entirely — M3's actual thesis.**

**`v0.2.4` — Pending-Changes Counter Accuracy** (tagged @ `2fb2af65d` 2026-08-03, `prod` fast-forwarded): the two admin-pill staleness fixes the v0.2.3 soak review found — PR [#5001](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5001) (publish path) and PR [#5003](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/5003) closing [#5002](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/5002) (reject path) — so the badge refreshes on every draft-queue mutation rather than only on publish. Display-only: the drafts, and what publishing did to them, were always correct.

Before it, **`v0.2.2` — Slack @-Mention Detection Fix** (tagged @ `092ac1a0c` 2026-07-30). Before it, **`v0.2.0` — Brain M1: Thin Wedge Slice** (tagged @ `ba98acad6` 2026-07-29). Before it, **`v0.1.0` — Public Launch** (tagged @ `c4703ed87` 2026-07-24) was the first minor out of the `v0.0.x` train. Its [milestone #74](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/74) closed **0 open / 35 closed** on 2026-07-24 — the last hard blocker, the live security pass ([#3351](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/3351)), shipped with its LOW residue ([#4785](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4785)); #1936 (OpenStatus Starter) closed as a deliberate stay-on-free-tier decision. **Public launch day is 2026-07-27** ([#2919](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/2919)).

**The launch-week ship freeze was lifted early on 2026-07-29** — prod had been stable since the `v0.1.1` hotfix, and shipping during launch week is itself the signal worth sending. `v0.2.0` was cut the same day rather than waiting for 2026-08-03, so the freeze note above applies only to Jul 27–29. The hotfix-branches-from-`prod` rule that freeze implied is now moot: `prod` tracks the newest tag (`v0.2.4`), so `main` is once again a safe hotfix source — but re-check `git rev-list --count origin/prod..origin/main` before assuming it, since the M4 slices land on `main` untagged.

The **Staging environment** track ([milestone #57](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/57)) is **CLOSED 2026-06-22 ("good for now")** — the staging env is live and soaked end-to-end; the remaining HITL OAuth provisioning slices were deferred for unreleased/undecided integrations (detail in [`ROADMAP-archive.md`](./ROADMAP-archive.md)).

## Brain M3 — Source Breadth (Shipped, untagged)

**Brain M3: Source Breadth** ([milestone #93](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/93) — CLOSED 0 open / 9 closed 2026-08-02) — the third of [ADR-0036](../../docs/adr/0036-atlas-as-company-brain.md)'s six brain milestones: T6's class expansion, deliberately sequenced *after* trust. The brain goes from one source to three, on a seam built so the fourth is additive. **Merged to `main` 2026-08-02** via PR [#4987](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4987) @ `f86e61c90`, accumulated on **`milestone/brain-m3`** per the #91/#92 pattern (CI fires per #4795, CodeQL deferred to the integration PR — deferred, not waived; `Closes #N` won't auto-close). Untagged, riding the held `v0.2.x` train — the next `0.x.0` is the Brain-complete tag.

Cut: connector-seam class/vendor split ([#4963](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4963), foundation) → transcripts/**Zoom** ([#4965](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4965)) · email/**Outlook** ([#4966](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4966)) · chat webhook fast-path ([#4967](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4967)) · re-verifier tail starvation ([#4971](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4971)) · OOV-source quarantine ([#4964](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4964)) · docs ([#4969](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4969)) · e2e multi-source arc proof ([#4968](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4968)) · M2 tension-module test debt ([#4962](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4962)). **Scoped to the seam + two classes** — `docs/wiki/code/drive` deferred, the deliberate guard against M2's 7→19 growth under review. Both vendors were confirmed with the maintainer rather than inferred (#4966 recommended Gmail and got Outlook). Per-issue decisions → [`brain-m3-notes.md`](./brain-m3-notes.md); the seam win → [`architecture-wins.md` #103](./architecture-wins.md).

- **The seam proof is empirical.** #4965 and #4966 each added a whole source class with `episode-sync.ts`, `episodes.ts` and `ingest/types.ts` untouched; the one shared file either extended is `grant.ts`, additively.
- **Email is where the audience stops being enumerable** — BCC is invisible and forwarding leaves no trace, so the derived grant is knowingly a lower bound, and the posture (From+To+Cc, Bcc ignored *even on the sender's copy that exposes it*) is chosen for **determinism**, not safety: episodes dedupe cross-mailbox on the RFC 5322 Message-ID, so which copy wins is undetermined.
- #4971 is the only M3 item that degraded already-shipped code, and it had two inheritors: both re-verifier scans ordered on a timestamp only a *successful* reconcile advances, so a permanently-failing audience held a scan slot forever and everything else aged past the staleness bound into ACL suppression — facts stored and invisible while the cycle reported `degraded` at worst. Fixed with a separate table (0186) stamped **on selection**, which makes the omission unrepresentable across the connectors' ten no-reconcile branches.
- **The milestone-wide review panel changed the shape of the milestone** (PR [#4983](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4983)): reviewing the 14 commits *against each other* found three blocking defects that exist only in their interaction — a Slack-scoped early `return` silently retiring the Zoom and Outlook audiences, a decrypted client secret echoed by `JSON.parse`'s error message, and block-vs-skip documented backwards in both `outlook/client.ts` and the public authoring guide. A second pass over those fixes found the first pass had **reintroduced the milestone's headline bug through the fix for it**.
- Two accepted risks recorded rather than fixed: the periodic brain fibers assume `numReplicas: 1` (pinned in all three regions, load-bearing in `deploy/README.md`), and Zoom has no anti-substitution binding on its audience id (the ids are already minted onto append-only rows with no update path).
- Closeout follow-ups, all on the Architecture Backlog: [#4984](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4984) the same credential-leak class outside the brain diff · [#4985](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4985) make the source/re-verifier pair unrepresentable in types, not merely guarded at runtime · [#4986](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4986) Outlook's block-vs-skip as a discriminated union. Two PRs (#4980, #4981) shipped **without** the review panel — recorded in the notes rather than assumed covered.

## Brain M2 — Temporal & Conflict Depth (Shipped, untagged)

**Brain M2: Temporal & Conflict Depth** ([milestone #92](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/92) — CLOSED 0 open / 19 closed 2026-08-01) — the second of [ADR-0036](../../docs/adr/0036-atlas-as-company-brain.md)'s six brain milestones: T4 complete, hardening the trust claim ("staleness is the moat"). Scoped after the M1 dogfood obligation was discharged (extraction ON in all three prod regions). **Merged to `main` 2026-08-01** via PR [#4950](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4950) @ `b01c8b48a` (70/70 checks green). Per the 2026-07-30 versioning amendment it rides the held `v0.2.x` train and is **untagged** — the next `0.x.0` is the Brain-complete tag.

Cut, on the M1 scaffolding that already stores `predicateCardinality` and writes advisory `in-tension-with` edges: human-gated supersession at the publish gate ([#4912](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4912)) · `in-tension-with` conflict clusters, surfaced-both-with-provenance ([#4913](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4913)) · staleness decay surfacing, advisory-only ([#4914](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4914)) · `correct_fact` + four verbs ([#4915](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4915), depends #4912) · bi-temporal `asOf` reads ([#4916](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4916), depends #4912) · e2e temporal-loop proof ([#4917](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4917)) · docs ([#4918](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4918)). Accumulated on **`milestone/brain-m2`** (branch created at kickoff from `main` @ `c37978f82`; the #91 pattern — CI fires per #4795, CodeQL deferred to the `main` PR, `Closes #N` won't auto-close, green checks are discipline not machinery). No ADR amendment issue — T4 is fully specified in ADR-0036 and no in-flight ADR invariant broke.

- Shipped 2026-07-30: the parallel trio — #4912 (PR [#4922](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4922)) · #4913 (PR [#4920](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4920)) · #4914 (PR [#4921](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4921)).
- Shipped 2026-07-31: the dependent pair + the arc proof — #4916 (PR [#4923](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4923)) · #4915 (PR [#4924](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4924)) · #4917 (PR [#4925](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4925)) · #4918 (PR [#4931](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4931)).
  - Two things #4917's e2e surfaced that the per-stage suites structurally could not, both worth carrying into M3+: (a) a naive `asOf` assertion is **vacuous by default** — extraction-born facts carry `valid_from NULL` (the schema has no `validFrom`), so a point read admits them at any instant, and the temporal predicates only bite if a test brackets the gate-stamped `valid_to`; (b) `timestamptz` keeps microseconds while `parseBrainAsOf` round-trips through a JS `Date`, so **equality at the half-open bound is unreachable through that API** — bracket ±1ms rather than reading at the stamp. Both pinned with mutation-verified assertions.
  - Also corrected in #4925: `correction.ts` carried two comments claiming a retracted row "stays readable to as-of reads" — the opposite of #4916's implemented rule (`invalidated_at IS NULL` survives in **both** branches of `buildFactQuery`). The real carve-out is narrower and worth knowing: the **tension surfaces** deliberately don't filter `invalidated_at`, so a retracted claim is still surfaced — labelled — as a withdrawn rival, which is why content erasure has to be a separate operation from the `retract` verb.
- **Premature merge and revert, 2026-07-31.** M2 went to `main` early (PR [#4928](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4928)) and was reverted the same day (PR [#4930](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4930), `git revert -m 1`). That revert records the changes as intentionally undone, so re-merging the branch would **silently** bring nothing back — the branch carries a `Reapply` commit (`ca9ed6b81`) for exactly this reason. Verified at the real merge: `main` was an ancestor of the branch, `admin-brain-facts.ts` went 433 → 615 lines, `correct_fact` reached 20 files.
- Shipped 2026-08-01, the correctness wave the M1 tool-surface work left open: `runAgent` stopped defaulting to a write-carrying registry ([#4936](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4936) → PR [#4945](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4945)), then `tools` became **compiler-required** so a source-text scanner is no longer the gate ([#4943](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4943) → PR [#4959](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4959)) — the five bug shapes are pinned as `@ts-expect-error` fixtures, so re-widening to `tools?:` fails `bun run type` with exactly five `TS2578`. `buildRegistry`'s "fatal misconfiguration" throws, swallowed by all five callers, now actually fail boot through the existing guard mechanism, inheriting `relaxSaasGuardForDev` ([#4940](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4940) → PR [#4960](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4960)). Also: the retracted-rival labelling pair ([#4933](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4933), [#4939](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4939)), `correct_fact`'s audit row ([#4934](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4934)), headless degraded-tool relay ([#4941](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4941)), one `searchBrain` schema across api and mcp ([#4954](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4954)), and the M2 invariants that passed for the wrong reason ([#4938](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4938)).
- **Closeout review** (two axes over the combined 18-commit diff) found no hard standards violations and no wrong implementations — the spec axis mutation-verified rather than read. Three things carried forward: `lib/brain/tensions.ts`, the largest new M2 module at 360 lines, has no dedicated test file ([#4962](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4962)); the admin list view's "in tension (N)" counts settled counterparts so a row reads contested post-arbitration ([#4961](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4961)); and #4941 rode a **disclosure-policy widening** — `code`/`constraint` on the global pino `err` serializer — under a UX issue whose ACs never mentioned it. The widening itself is sound (it deliberately excludes `detail`/`where`/`internalQuery`, the fields that echo row values and statement text), but it never got reviewed *as* a disclosure change.

## v0.0.46 — KB Support Center Connectors (Shipped)

**`v0.0.46` — KB Support Center Connectors** ([milestone #85](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/85); PRD [#4395](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4395)) — **COMPLETE (closeout 2026-07-10; milestone #85 closed; TAGGED @ `02bdf38ab`).** Extended the connector seam (ADR-0030) to HTML help centers, unified by **one shared HTML→markdown converter** (`knowledge/support/html-to-markdown.ts`) built with the anchor slice and reused across the tier — each follow-on connector is a thin vendor client (`fetchChanges` + `fetchAll`) over it, no per-vendor fork. Anchor slices shipped early into the `v0.0.45` window: **Zendesk Guide** + the shared converter ([#4396](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4396) → PR [#4444](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4444)) and **Salesforce Knowledge** reusing the jsforce+OAuth install ([#4397](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4397) → PR [#4449](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4449)), both with docs guides via PR [#4477](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4477). The four token-auth followers then shipped via the `/ship-milestone` loop 2026-07-10, each rebasing serially over the shared additive-spine files (`KnowledgeCollectionSource` enum, catalog seed, `admin-knowledge` branch, `register` pairing, OpenAPI): **Front** (Bearer token, multi-KB + locales, delta-less reconciliation-diff; [#4400](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4400) → PR [#4526](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4526)), **Intercom Articles** (access token, reconciliation-diff, multi-locale; [#4399](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4399) → PR [#4527](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4527)), **Help Scout Docs** (Docs API key, multi-site, `updatedAt` watermark; [#4398](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4398) → PR [#4528](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4528)), and **Freshdesk Solutions** (API-key/Basic, category tree-walk, reconciliation-diff; [#4401](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4401) → PR [#4529](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/pull/4529)). The two OAuth slices — **Zoho Desk** ([#4402](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4402)) + **ServiceNow KM** ([#4403](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4403)), both `ready-for-human` (new vendor OAuth app registration first) — were **detached to the [Architecture Backlog](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/milestone/49)** at closeout as deferred enhancements (the shared seam already supports them). Closeout docs pass added the missing **Front + Freshdesk connector guides** (`connect-front.mdx` / `connect-freshdesk.mdx`). PRD [#4395](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4395) closed. HubSpot ([#4404](https://github.com/AtlasDevHQ/atlas/issues/4404)) remains blocked — no official KB API; Gorgias has none either.
